# PART II: FIRST THROUGH THIRD WEEKS OF THE GREAT FAST (CLEAN WEEK TO VENERATION OF THE CROSS)

## FIRST WEEK OF THE GREAT FAST

## Monday of the First Week
*Beginning of the Holy and Great Fast.*[^230]

*The paraecclesiarch sounds the klepalo before dawn, on account of the evening collation, and we all gather into church as usual. We sing the Midnight Office without prostrations, only at the end making three great prostrations, with the prayer: "O Lord and Master:" This takes place on the first day only. On the other days of the Holy and Great Fast we sing the entire Midnight Office with 16 prostrations, as usual.*[^231]

*Then the Priest, having arisen, makes the usual metania to the superior, and enters into the holy Altar; and taking the censer, stands before the holy Table, and censing crosswise, exclaims:*
**Priest:** Blessed is our God:
*And when we have answered:*
**Reader:** Amen.
*The Priest goes forth and censes. And the appointed psalm-reader says: Trisagion, always without metanias. After "Our Father," the Priest exclaims:*
**Priest:** For Thine is the kingdom:
*And after the Amen:*
**Reader:** Lord, have mercy, 12 times. O come, let us worship: thrice, without metanias. *And Psalms 19 and 20; at their conclusion, Trisagion, without metanias. After "Our Father:" the usual troparia. While these are being said, the Priest censes the holy Altar and the entire church. Then the litany:*
**Priest:** Have mercy on us, O God:
*Then:*
**Priest:** Again we pray for our pious Emperor:
*Exclamation:*
**Priest:** For a merciful and man-befriending God art Thou:
**Reader:** Amen.
*And he says:* In the name of the Lord bless, father.
*And immediately the Priest:*
**Priest:** Glory to the holy, consubstantial: *And the rest.*

*And the brother whose day it is, standing in the appointed place, says the Six Psalms with all attention, not rushing, but with the fear of God, as invisibly conversing with God Himself.*[^232]

*And no one has authority to whisper, nor to spit or clear the throat, but rather to give heed to the words spoken by the psalm-reader, having their hands folded upon the breast, heads bowed, and eyes cast down, looking with inward spiritual eyes toward the east, praying for our sins, remembering death, future torment, and eternal life.*

*At the beginning of the 120th Psalm, the Priest says the morning prayers. At the conclusion of the psalms: Glory, and now and ever, Alleluia, thrice. Then the Great Litany, and the exclamation.*

*And the appointed monk sings Alleluia in the Tone that happens to be in order from the Octoechos.*[^233]
*Verse 1: From the night my spirit awakes early unto Thee, O God, for Thy commandments are a light upon the earth.*
*Verse 2: Learn righteousness, ye that dwell upon the earth.*
*Verse 3: Envy shall seize an untaught people.*
*Verse 4: Bring evil upon them, O Lord, bring evil upon the glorious of the earth.*
*And after each verse, Alleluia, thrice.*

*Then the Triadica of the Tone: We sing them with compunction, in a loud voice and slowly with chanting. At the second verse: Glory: and at the third: Now and ever:*[^234]

*Then we recite one kathisma of the Psalter. After the antiphons, at Alleluia we make three prostrations, all equally, following the superior or the ecclesiarch.*

*After the first kathisma, the penitential sessional hymns of the Octoechos.*
*Then the reading.*

*Then the reading from Saint Ephrem; for from that day begins Saint Ephrem, and it is read until the Pascha of Palm Sunday.*[^235] *(Note).*

*After the second stichology, the Sessional Hymn of the Triodion, by Lord Joseph. Tone 2:*[^236]
*Model: Being the fountain of tender mercy:*
Let us acquire compunction with the divine beginnings of the Fast, crying aloud from the soul: O Master Christ, accept our prayer like chosen incense, and deliver us, we pray, from foul-smelling corruption and dreadful torment, as Thou alone art good and forgiving.

*Glory, the same. Now and ever, Theotokion. Model:*
O Mother of God, who art the fountain of tender mercy, vouchsafe us mercy; look upon a people that has sinned; show forth as ever thy power; for hoping in thee, we cry aloud unto thee: "Rejoice!" as of old did Gabriel, the supreme commander of the bodiless hosts.

*And again the reading in Saint Ephrem.*

*After the third stichology, the Sessional Hymn of Lord Theodore. Tone 2:*
*Model: Before Thine immaculate image:*
Let us begin the all-venerable temperance brightly, shining with the rays of the holy commandments of Christ our God, with the splendor of love, the flashing of prayer, the purification of purity, and the strength of courage; so that, bearing light, we may arrive beforehand at the holy and third-day Resurrection, which shines incorruption upon the world.

*Glory, the same. Now and ever, Theotokion. Model:*
Having acquired thine invincible prayer in grievous afflictions, I am marvelously delivered from those who oppress me beyond hope. O Mother of God, who ever anticipate those who faithfully ask thee, therefore we cry aloud unto thee with thanksgiving: Accept, O Sovereign Lady, brief thanksgiving; according to the nature of these, be unto me an aid in all things.

*Then the reading in the Lausaic History.*[^237] *Psalm 50.*
*And the usual prayer: "O God, save Thy people:" Then the exclamation.*

*After this we begin: "Let us sing unto the Lord:" in the Tone of the Canon of the Menaion, of the saint of the day.*[^238] *And we sing the verses of the canticles quickly, each choir its own verse, until they reach "They were troubled:" and from that point they begin to maintain the verses to 14. And we sing the Menaion with the Heirmos to 6, singing thus: The first choir sings the verse: "They were troubled:" and sings the Heirmos. The second choir sings the second verse: "The enemy said:" and after it the troparion of the canon. And in order the verses according to the choirs until "Then they hasted:" From the verse "Then they hasted:" we begin the Triodion of Lord Joseph to 8, and at "The Lord reigns:" we sing the other Triodion of Lord Theodore the Studite; and the first choir sings the troparia, and the second choir the second verse and troparion. Then, both choirs having united, they sing with a higher voice: Glory, Trinity Hymn; Now and ever, Theotokion. Then we sing with the highest voice: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. And we sing the other troparion of Lord Theodore. For the ode contains five troparia. And at the end we sing the Katavasia, the Heirmos of the second canon.*
*In this manner are sung the eighth and ninth odes. But the third ode we sing thus:*
*The first choir sings the verse: "The Lord is gone up into the heavens:" and the troparion of the canon of the Menaion. The second choir the verse: "And shall give strength unto our King." And the second troparion of the canon of the Menaion. Then, Glory, and now and ever, with the troparia of the canon. And the first choir sings the Heirmos of the Menaion of the third ode, at the end of the canon.*

*In this manner we sing also the sixth ode. But the fourth, fifth, and seventh odes we sing thus:*
*First say the Heirmos of the canon of the Menaion; then we sing the final two verses of the canticle to the troparia of the canon; and Glory, and now and ever. But we do not cover the ode with the Heirmos; for the Heirmos is said before the ode. The Heirmoi of the Menaion are sung after the canon only after the third and sixth odes, because for those odes the Heirmos is not sung first. On the other days of the week, except Monday, the first ode is sung in the same way as indicated for the eighth and ninth odes. And in whatever odes there is a Triodion, the Katavasia, the Heirmos of the Triodion, is sung at the end of the canon, as indicated above.*

*Be it known that the Octoechos is not sung throughout the entire Holy Forty Days, except on Sunday.*[^239] *Only the Menaion and the Triodion are sung; and in whatever odes there is no Triodion, only the Menaion is sung. After the third ode the Priest says the Little Litany. Then the Sessional Hymn of the saint, Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion; and reading in the Lausaic History. After the sixth ode the Little Litany. Then the Sessional Hymn, Martyrikon; but if the saint has a kontakion, say it here; but say the Martyrikon after the first kathisma with the sessional hymns, with the refrain: "God is marvelous in His saints:" And reading in the Synaxarion.*

*Canon and Triodion of Lord Joseph. Ode 1.*[^240]
*Tone 2. Heirmos: Come, ye peoples:*
*Here the first canticle is versified.*
*Troparion:*
How shall I now weep for the falling away of my soul? What beginning of my salvation shall I make, who have lived prodigally? O Compassionate One, by the judgments that Thou knowest, save me.

*Troparion:*
Behold the time of repentance, behold the saving day, the entrance of the Fast! O my soul, be watchful, and close the entrances of the passions, looking unto the Lord.

*Troparion:*
The tempest of sins storming against me drags me down into the abyss of despair; but I flee unto the ocean of Thy mercy: save me, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
I alone have enslaved myself to sin; I alone have opened the doors to passions, O forgiving Word: convert me, O Savior, by Thy tender-heartedness.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth to the Fountain of dispassion, O Maiden, heal me who am wounded with passions, and snatch me from eternal fire, O only God-graced One.

*Another Triodion of Lord Theodore, the same Tone and Heirmos:*
*Troparion:*
Come, ye peoples, today let us receive the gift of fasting, as a God-given time of repentance; therein let us appease the Savior.

*Troparion:*
Darkness has passed away, the time of ascetical labors has arrived, the course of the Fast; let us all eagerly begin this, bringing virtues unto the Lord like gifts.

*Glory, Trinity Hymn:*
O Simple, Thrice-Radiant Unity, most supreme Single One, All-Powerful, All-Living God and Lord, Father Almighty, Son, and Spirit, save those who honor Thee.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
Let us sing of the holy mountain of God, Mary the undefiled, from whom the Sun of Righteousness shone forth for those in darkness: Christ, the Life of all.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*

*Troparion:*
A fiery chariot received the marvelous Elijah, who was armed by fasting, and showed Moses to be a beholder of things ineffable; and having received this, we shall behold Christ.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
*Troparion:*
Through food Adam fell, and his intemperance cast him out from Paradise; but do Thou, O Lord, Who hast accepted fasting, show us who are worthy of repentance, O Lover of mankind.

*Katavasia:*
*Heirmos: Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt, for He is glorified.*

*After the sixth ode, the Martyrikon of the Tone.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos: Into the fiery furnace:*[^241]
*Troparion:*
Consumingly burned by pleasures and darkened in the eyes of my soul, renew me with the fire of Thy fear, O Christ, and illumine me with the light of salvation, that I may glorify Thee unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Having hated the satiety of the passions, feed upon the sweet food of good deeds, and delight thyself rather with the sweetness of the Fast, turning away from the bitterness of pleasures, O my humble soul, and live unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Blinded in soul and weighed down with the drunkenness of passions, I gaze not at all unto Thee, the only God; therefore have mercy upon me and enlighten me, and open unto me the gates of repentance.

*Theotokion:*
O Pure One, who by thy fervent prayer hast heavenly elevated our earthly and corruptible nature, make our prayers and supplications to be directed unto thy God and King, and the God of all.

*Another [Triodion]. Heirmos: Of old Who dewed:*[^242]
*Troparion:*
Let us brightly adorn the entrance of the Fast, ye faithful, and let us not mourn; but let us wash our faces with the water of dispassion, blessing and supreme-exalting Christ unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Having anointed the head of the soul with the oil of mercy, let us not use many words to our Father Who is in the heavens, sending up prayers, blessing and supreme-exalting Him unto all ages.

*Verse: Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
*Trinity Hymn:*
Let us the faithful sing of the Ever-Existing, Unoriginate Father, and the Co-Unoriginate Son, and the Spirit Co-Shining forth from the Father: consubstantial Persons, being one almighty origin and dominion.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
O Mary, called of God, truly the cleansing place of the faithful, for from thee forgiveness is richly granted unto all: cease not to pray to thy Son and Lord for those who hymn thee.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
*Troparion:*
The Lord, having fasted the measure of forty days, has now sanctified and cleansed these days, O brethren; having attained unto them, let us cry aloud: Bless and supreme-exalt Christ unto the ages.

*Verse: We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord.*
*Katavasia:*
*Heirmos: Of old Who dewed the Hebrew youths in the flame, and wonderfully consumed the Chaldeans therein, let us sing unto the Lord, saying: Bless and supreme-exalt Him unto the ages.*

*And the Magnificat is versified.*[^243]

*Ode 9. Heirmos: God the Word from God:*[^244]
*Troparion:*
Let the day of the Fast be unto thee, O soul, a casting away of sin, and at the same time an inclination and attachment unto God; so that, fleeing from the abyss of malice, thou mayest love only the paths that lead unto that rest.

*Troparion:*
I have slipped in thoughts and sinned in the flesh; I lament, and groan, and cry aloud: Save me, O Lord, save me, O Lover of mankind, O only One, and condemn me not into that fire of Gehenna, who am worthy of condemnation.

*Troparion:*
Having clothed ourselves in the bright garment of the Fast, let us strip off the dark and heavy garments of drunkenness; and becoming bright with divine virtues, let us behold with faith the radiant Passions of the Savior.

*Theotokion:*
O All-Pure Sovereign Lady, who didst give birth to the Healer Christ: heal my passionate soul, weakened by all the assaults of malicious demons, the deliverance of us who know thee as the Maiden, the Inviolable Virgin.

*Another [Triodion]. Heirmos: Her who supernaturally:*[^245]
*Troparion:*
Now has arrived the season of the holy fasts; let us begin it in good conduct; for it says that we must not fast in judgments and strifes.

*Troparion:*
On Mount Horeb Elijah, having cleansed himself by fasting, beheld God; let us also cleanse the heart by fasting, and we shall behold Christ.

*Glory, Trinity Hymn:*
I worship the one Essence, I sing the three Persons, the one God of all: the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Ever-Existing Principle.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
O Pure Maiden, thou givest birth to a Child, and as a Virgin thou dost suckle with milk: how hast thou effectively given birth in these two things? God is He that has wrought it: ask not of me the manner how!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
*Troparion:*
One day, it says, is the life of all mortals on earth; for those who labor out of love there are forty days of the Fast, which let us complete brightly.

*Katavasia:*
*Heirmos: Her who supernaturally conceived in her womb in the flesh the Word Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, in unceasing hymns we the faithful magnify.*

*Then: "It is truly meet:" One prostration. Litany: "Again and again:"*
*And the Photogogicon Trinity Hymn of the Tone.*
*At "Let every breath praise the Lord," we do not sing stichera, but we recite the verses: "Praise the Lord from the heavens:" the three usual psalms.*[^246]
*And if it happens in the Menaion that there are two verses in order: We sing stichera to the one in the evening, and to the other at the Praises stichera to 4, if there be any.*
*If not, the appointed brother says:*
To Thee glory is due, O Lord our God, and unto Thee we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

**Reader:** Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light.
Glory to God in the highest:
*And the rest. And we make three metanias, standing equally in our places. After this the Priest says: "Let us complete our morning prayer:" And the exclamation. And both choirs having gathered together, we sing:*

*At the Aposticha, self-governing sticheron, Tone 5:*[^247]
The Fast has arrived, the mother of chastity, the accuser of sins, the preacher of repentance, the citizenship of angels, and the salvation of men; ye faithful, let us cry aloud: O God, have mercy on us!

*Verse: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy: And we repeat the same.*
*Then the verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us: And the Martyrikon:*
Blessed is the army of the King of the heavens; for though the passion-bearers were earthly-born, yet they made haste to attain the angelic rank; disregarding their bodies, and through their sufferings they were accounted worthy of the honor of the bodiless hosts; by their prayers, O Lord, save our souls.

*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion:*
Thee, the Mother of God, the pure Virgin and holier than the Cherubim, we magnify in voices of songs; for in soul and body we confess thee to be the Mother of God, as having truly given birth to God incarnate: intercede, O All-Pure One, for our souls.

*Then: "It is good to confess unto the Lord:" twice. Trisagion, and three metanias. O All-Holy Trinity: Our Father: And when the Priest says: "For Thine is the kingdom:" we say the troparion: "Standing in the temple of Thy glory:" And we say: Lord, have mercy, 40 times. Then the priest steps aside and says: "Blessed is Christ our God, Who is:" "O Heavenly King:" After this, standing and praying within ourselves in our own thought, we say the prayer of our venerable father Ephrem:*[^248]
"O Lord and Master of my life:" *And three great prostrations, and 12 small ones, saying to ourselves at each one: "O God, cleanse me a sinner." Then again another single great prostration, saying with it the entire aforesaid prayer of Saint Ephrem.*
*And after we have risen, the superior begins: "O come, let us worship:" thrice, and three metanias. And we join:*

*First Hour:*[^249]
*And having concluded at the third psalm, we say: Alleluia, and three metanias.*
*(It is proper to know that on all Mondays of the Holy Forty Days we do not recite a kathisma at the First Hour. On the other days we recite a kathisma, and make three metanias at each antiphon).*
*(Likewise also on Friday, the First Hour and the Ninth are without a kathisma).*

*The appointed reader or the Priest sings in the midst of the church the troparion, in Tone 6: "In the morning give ear unto my voice:" And we sing the same loudly, and make a prostration to the ground. Likewise also at the other two verses. Verse 1: "Give ear to my words, O Lord, understand my cry." Verse 2: "For unto Thee will I pray, O Lord." And again: "In the morning give ear unto my voice:" Glory, and now and ever: "What shall we call thee, O Full of grace?" And three metanias. Then: "Order my steps according to Thy word, and let no iniquity have dominion over me." And we repeat the same. Then: "Deliver me from the false accusation of men, and I will keep Thy commandments." Again the same: "Make Thy face to shine upon Thy servant, and teach me Thine ordinances." Again the same. And this we say thrice, loudly and slowly: "Let my mouth be filled with Thy praise, O Lord:" Trisagion, and three metanias. O All-Holy Trinity: Our Father: The priest exclaims: "For Thine is the kingdom:" And after the Amen we say the troparion. On Monday, Tuesday, and Thursday say the Theotokion: "The most glorious Mother of God:" On Wednesday and Friday say: "Speedily anticipate, before we are enslaved:" Lord, have mercy, 40 times. And we say the prayer: "Thou Who at every season:" Lord, have mercy, thrice. Glory, and now and ever: "More honorable than the Cherubim:" And one prostration. In the name of the Lord bless, father. Priest: "God be compassionate unto us, and bless us, and cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us." And we make the prostrations, as prescribed above; and after this the superior or the reader says: Trisagion, and three metanias. Our Father: Lord, have mercy, 12 times. And the prayer: "O Christ, the true Light:" Priest: "Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee." And we: Glory, and now and ever: Lord, have mercy, thrice. Bless, and the Dismissal. Then, the Many Years. And we venerate the holy icons.*

*Then we go forth, making a litiya to the narthex.*[^250]
*And there the usual prayers are said; and the catechetical instructions of our venerable father Theodore the Studite are read by the superior or by the ecclesiarch, as prescribed, that is, on Wednesday and on Friday.*
*And the conclusion takes place, and the Dismissal.*
*This order takes place throughout the entire Holy Forty Days at Matins and at the First Hour.*
*After the dismissal, going forth from the church, we go in all silence to our cells, or to obedience; and it is not proper for us to hold conversation with one another on monastery grounds on the way, for this was restrained by the holy Fathers.*

*At the Third Hour the kindler of lamps strikes the klepalo sufficiently; and we gather into church, each of us making three prostrations before the holy doors before the beginning of the singing, and we venerate the holy icons. And to both choirs we make a small bow each. And when the priest has given the blessing, and we begin: "O Heavenly King:" Trisagion, and three metanias. After "Our Father:" Priest: "For Thine is the kingdom:" And after the Amen: Lord, have mercy, 12 times. "O come, let us worship:" thrice, and three metanias. And there is recited:*[^251]

*Third Hour:*
*as usual. At the end of the third psalm, at Alleluia, three metanias. And we recite the ordinary kathisma; and at each antiphon we make three metanias. And after the kathisma the reader or the Priest, standing in the midst of the church, says the troparion, Tone 6: "O Lord, Who didst send down Thy Most Holy Spirit:" And as we respond in antiphon, we make a prostration to the ground. Likewise also at the other two verses. Verse: "Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me." Verse: "Cast me not away from Thy presence, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me." Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion; and three metanias: "O Mother of God, thou art the true Vine:" And if we read in the Ladder. And from now on the appointed monk begins to rouse the brethren throughout the entire Holy Forty Days, and walks about quietly, and looks upon those that sleep, and raises them up to hearing, according to the prescribed form.*[^252]

*After the reading: "Blessed be the Lord God:" Trisagion, and three metanias. And after "Our Father," the priest exclaims: "For Thine is the kingdom:" And after the Amen we sing the troparia in Tone 8: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God:" Glory: "Grant speedy and steadfast consolation:" Now and ever: "Hope and protection:" Lord, have mercy, 40 times. And the prayer: "Thou Who at every season:" Lord, have mercy, thrice. Glory, and now and ever: "More honorable than the Cherubim:" In the name of the Lord bless, father. And the priest: "God be compassionate unto us:" We make the prostrations as prescribed above; the superior says the prayer: "O Master God, Father Almighty:" The kindler of lamps, having made the usual bow to the superior, goes forth and strikes the klepalo six times.*[^253]

*And upon the conclusion of the prayer: "O come, let us worship:" thrice, and three metanias. And we recite:*

*Sixth Hour:*
*And at the end: Glory, and now and ever: Alleluia, thrice, and three metanias. And we recite a kathisma, and at each antiphon, Alleluia, thrice, and three metanias.*
*After the kathisma the troparion, Tone 2: "Who on the sixth day and hour didst nail to the Cross:" Verse 1: "Give ear, O God, unto my prayer, and despise not my supplication." Verse 2: "As for me, I have cried unto God, and the Lord heard me." Making also three prostrations, as prescribed above. Glory, and now and ever: "Seeing that we have no boldness:" and three metanias. Then we sing the troparion of the prophecy, Tone 5:*[^254]
O Lord, O Lord, at Whom all things do tremble, and quake from before the face of Thy power, unto Thee we fall down, O Immortal One, unto Thee we pray, O Holy One: save our souls by the prayers of Thy saints.

*Glory, and now and ever: and we repeat the same.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4, Psalm 1:*
The Lord knows the way of the righteous, and the way of the ungodly shall perish.
*Verse: Blessed is the man that has not walked in the counsel of the ungodly, nor stood in the way of sinners.*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 1):*[^255]
The vision which Isaiah the son of Amoz saw, which he saw against Judea and against Jerusalem, in the days of Uzziah, and Jotham, and Ahaz, and Hezekiah, who reigned in Judea. Hear, O heaven, and give ear, O earth, for the Lord has spoken: I have begotten and brought up sons, but they have rejected Me. The ox knows his owner, and the donkey his master's crib; but Israel knows Me not, and My people have not understood Me. Ah sinful nation, a people full of sins, an evil seed, lawless children! Ye have forsaken the Lord, and provoked the Holy One of Israel; ye have turned backward. Why should ye be smitten anymore, adding iniquity? The whole head is in pain, and the whole heart in sadness. From the feet even unto the head there is no soundness in him: neither wound, nor bruise, nor burning sore; there is no plaster to apply, neither oil, nor bandages. Your land is desolate, your cities are burned with fire; strangers devour your land before your face, and it is made desolate, overthrown by strange peoples. The daughter of Zion shall be left like a booth in a vineyard, and like a lodge in a garden of cucumbers, like a besieged city. And except the Lord of Sabaoth had left us a seed, we should have been as Sodom, and we should have been made like unto Gomorrah. Hear the word of the Lord, ye rulers of Sodom; give ear unto the law of God, ye people of Gomorrah! To what purpose is the multitude of your sacrifices unto Me? says the Lord. I am full of the burnt offerings of rams, and the fat of lambs; and the blood of bulls and goats I desire not. Neither come ye to appear before Me; for who has required these things at your hands? Ye shall no more tread My court. If ye bring fine flour, it is vain; incense is an abomination unto Me. Your new moons and sabbaths, and the great day I cannot endure: fasting, and idleness, and your new moons, and your feasts My soul hates; ye have become a satiety unto Me, no more will I endure your sins. When ye stretch forth your hands unto Me, I will turn away Mine eyes from you; and though ye multiply supplication, I will not hear you; for your hands are full of blood. Wash yourselves, and be clean; take away the evil from your souls before Mine eyes; cease from your evils. Learn to do good; seek judgment, deliver the wronged, judge the orphan, and plead for the widow. And come, let us reason together, says the Lord: and though your sins be as scarlet, I will make them white as snow; and though they be as crimson, I will make them white like wool. And if ye be willing, and give ear unto Me, ye shall eat the good of the land; but if ye will not, neither give ear unto Me, the sword shall devour you; for the mouth of the Lord has spoken these things.

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7, Psalm 2:*[^256]
Serve the Lord with fear, and rejoice unto Him with trembling.

*Verse: Why did the heathen rage, and the peoples imagine vain things?*

*And the reading takes place in the Ladder. Then the troparion: "Let Thy tender mercies speedily anticipate us:" Trisagion, and three metanias. After "Our Father:" Priest, exclamation: "For Thine is the kingdom:" And the troparia in Tone 2: "Thou hast wrought salvation:" Glory: "Before Thine immaculate image:" Now and ever: "Being the fountain of tender mercy:" On Wednesday and Friday we say the Stavrotheotokion: "Most glorified art thou, O Virgin Mother of God:" Lord, have mercy, 40 times. "Thou Who at every season:" Lord, have mercy, thrice. Glory, and now and ever: "More honorable than the Cherubim:" In the name of the Lord bless, father. Priest: "God be compassionate unto us, and bless us:" And we make the prostrations as usual. And upon the completion of the prostrations the superior begins the final Trisagion, and three metanias. After "Our Father:" Lord, have mercy, 12 times. Then the Priest: "Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee." And the Dismissal takes place.*

*This manner and order is bound to be throughout the entire Holy Forty Days at the Third and Sixth Hours.*

*(Note: Where there is no dispersal to cells, after the prayer: "O God and Lord of hosts:" we immediately say: "O come, let us worship:" thrice, and the Ninth Hour).*[^257]
*(Where there is dispersal, at the ninth hour the kindler of lamps strikes nine times, and we gather into church. And the Priest having given the blessing, we begin: Trisagion, and three metanias. After "Our Father," the priest exclaims: "For Thine is the kingdom:" And after the Amen: Lord, have mercy, 12 times. "O come, let us worship:" thrice, and three metanias; and we recite)*

*Ninth Hour (with three psalms):*
*At the end of the third psalm, at Alleluia, three metanias. And we begin the recitation of the kathisma; and at each antiphon, three metanias.*
*Upon the completion of the recitation the troparion, Tone 8: "Who at the ninth hour for our sake didst taste death in the flesh:"*[^258] *Verse 1: "Let my prayer draw near before Thee, O Lord:" Verse 2: "Let my supplication enter before Thee:" And making prostrations at these. Glory, and now and ever: "Thou Who for our sake wast born of a Virgin:" three metanias. And reading takes place again in the Ladder.*
*And after the reading we say the verse: "Deliver us not up utterly:" Trisagion, and three metanias. O All-Holy Trinity: Our Father: And the priest exclaims: "For Thine is the kingdom:" And after the Amen we say the troparia, Tone 8: "Beholding the thief, the Author of life:" Glory: "Between two thieves:" Now and ever: "Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Savior of the world:" Lord, have mercy, 40 times. "Thou Who at every season:" Lord, have mercy, thrice. Glory, and now and ever: "More honorable than the Cherubim:" In the name of the Lord bless, father. Priest: "God be compassionate unto us:" And we make three great prostrations, saying to ourselves the aforesaid prayer of our venerable father Ephrem; but the other 12 small ones we do not make. And we say the prayer: "O Master Lord Jesus Christ our God:"*

*And immediately the choir whose turn it is to begin starts in Tone 8: "In Thy kingdom remember us, O Lord:" And we sing:*

*The Beatitudes:*[^259]
*with sweet singing and loudly and slowly, in the same Tone:*
Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.
*Refrain:* Remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom.
*And the other verses.*
Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:
*Refrain:* Remember us, O Lord.
Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.
*Refrain:* Remember us, O Lord.
*Then both choirs having come together, we sing with a higher voice in expansive tones:*
Remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom. *(One prostration).*
Remember us, O Master, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom. *(One prostration).*
Remember us, O Holy One, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom. *(One prostration).*
*And we say:*
The heavenly choir hymns Thee and says: Holy, Holy, Holy, Lord God of Sabaoth; heaven and earth are full of Thy glory.
*Verse:* Come unto Him and be enlightened, and your faces shall not be ashamed.
*And again:* The heavenly choir:
*Glory:* The choir of holy angels and archangels:
*Now and ever:* I believe in one God:
Remit, pardon, forgive: Our Father: *Then the kontakia of the day and of the temple.*[^260]

*If it be a temple of Christ, we say the kontakion of the temple first, then of the day and of the ordinary saint whose day it is, if he has one. Glory: "With the saints give rest, O Christ:" Now and ever, of the temple of the Mother of God, or: "O Protection of Christians unashamed:" Lord, have mercy, 40 times. Glory, and now and ever: "More honorable than the Cherubim:" In the name of the Lord bless, father. And the priest says: "God be compassionate unto us:" And we make the 16 Lenten prostrations with the prayer of Saint Ephrem, as prescribed above.*[^261] *And the paraecclesiarch, having made a bow to the superior and received a blessing, goes and rings the bell, and there begins:*

*Vespers:*[^262]
*Then the superior begins: "O come, let us worship:" thrice, and three metanias, and the reader says the psalm: "Bless the Lord, O my soul:" in a quiet and gentle voice. The Priest says the usual evening prayers before the holy doors. After the psalm: Glory, and now and ever: Alleluia, thrice, and three prostrations. And the Great Litany. And we recite the kathisma: "Unto the Lord when I was afflicted:" and at each antiphon: Glory, and now and ever: Alleluia, thrice, and three metanias.*

## Monday Evening of the First Week of the Holy Great Fast[^263]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 stichera, Tone 2. Composition of Lord Joseph:*[^264]
*Model: All things have passed away:*
I have committed every sin, I have surpassed all in prodigality; if I wish to repent, I have no streams of tears; if I live in slothfulness even now, I am guilty of torment; but grant me amendment, O only Good God, and have mercy on me.

*Model:*
Grant me showers of tears, O Christ, on the fair day of the Fast, so that I may lament and wash away the defilement that comes from pleasures, and may appear cleansed before Thee when Thou, O Lord, shalt come from heaven as Judge to judge mankind, as Judge and only Righteous One.

*Another [sticheron], Tone 5. Of Lord Theodore the Studite:*[^265]
*Model: O venerable Father:*
Come ye eagerly, having the strong armor of the Fast as a shield, let us the faithful turn back every snare of the enemy's deception; let us not be burned with the pleasures of passions, neither let us fear the fire of temptations, wherewith Christ the Lover of mankind crowns us with the honors of endurance; therefore praying with boldness, we fall down crying aloud, asking peace and great mercy for our souls.

*And in the Menaion, 3.*
*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion: "O gladsome Light:"*
*Then the prokeimenon, Psalm 3, Tone 6:*[^266]
Salvation is of the Lord, and Thy blessing is upon Thy people.
*Verse: Lord, why are they multiplied that afflict me?*

*The Reading from the Book of Genesis (Chapter 1):*[^267]
In the beginning God created the heaven and the earth. And the earth was invisible and unformed, and darkness was upon the abyss; and the Spirit of God was borne above the water. And God said: Let there be light, and there was light. And God saw the light, that it was good; and God divided between the light and between the darkness. And God called the light day, and the darkness He called night. And there was evening, and there was morning, the first day. And God said: Let there be a firmament in the midst of the water, and let it divide between water and water; and it was so. And God made the firmament, and God divided between the water which was under the firmament, and between the water which was above the firmament. And God called the firmament heaven, and God saw that it was good; and there was evening, and there was morning, the second day. And God said: Let the water which is under heaven be gathered into one gathering, and let the dry land appear; and it was so. And the water which was under heaven gathered together into its gatherings, and the dry land appeared. And God called the dry land earth, and the gatherings of the waters He called seas; and God saw that it was good. And God said: Let the earth bring forth the herb of grass, yielding seed according to its kind and according to its likeness, and the fruit tree bearing fruit, whose seed is in itself, according to its kind upon the earth; and it was so. And the earth brought forth the herb of grass, yielding seed according to its kind and according to its likeness, and the fruit tree bearing fruit, whose seed is in itself, according to its kind upon the earth; and God saw that it was good. And there was evening, and there was morning, the third day.

*Second Prokeimenon, Tone 5, Psalm 4:*[^268]
The Lord will hear me when I cry unto Him.
*Verse: When I called, the God of my righteousness heard me.*

*The Reading from the Proverbs (Chapter 1):*[^269]
The proverbs of Solomon, son of David, who reigned in Israel: To know wisdom and instruction, and to understand the words of prudence; to receive also the subtilty of words, and the interpretation of dark sayings; to understand true righteousness, and to direct judgment; that he may give subtlety to the simple, and to the young man knowledge and understanding. For hearing these things, a wise man will be wiser, and a man of understanding will gain direction; he will understand also a proverb, and a dark saying, the words of the wise, and their riddles. The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom, and good understanding have all they that practice it; piety toward God is the beginning of discernment, but the ungodly set at naught wisdom and instruction. Hear, my son, the instruction of thy father, and reject not the laws of thy mother; for thou shalt receive a crown of graces upon thy head, and a chain of gold about thy neck. My son, let not ungodly men entice thee, neither consent thou, if they exhort thee, saying: Come with us, share in blood; let us hide the righteous man in the earth unjustly; let us swallow him up alive as hades, and take away his memorial from the earth; let us acquire his precious substance, and fill our houses with spoils; cast in thy lot with us, let us all have one common purse, and let there be one pouch for us all. Go not in the way with them; turn away thy foot from their paths; for their feet run to evil, and are swift to shed blood. For not in vain are nets spread for winged fowls; for they, partaking in murder, store up evil for themselves, and the destruction of lawless men is evil. Such are the ways of all that commit iniquity; for by ungodliness they take away their own soul. Wisdom is praised in the exits, and in the broad places she speaks boldly.

*Vouchsafe, O Lord, this evening:*
*And three metanias.*
**Priest:** *Let us complete our evening prayer:*
*After the exclamation both choirs sing together.*
*At the Aposticha, self-governing sticheron of the day, Tone 3:*[^270]
Let us fast an acceptable fast, well-pleasing unto the Lord: true fasting is estrangement from evils, temperance of the tongue, laying aside of wrath, separation from lusts, slander, falsehood, and perjury; deprivation of these things is true and acceptable fasting.
*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes:*
*And we repeat the same.*
*Then the Martyrion. Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord:*
Great is the power of Thy martyrs, O Christ: for they lie in graves and drive away spirits, and have abolished the power of the enemy, having contended for piety through faith in the Trinity.
*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, protection of all that pray, through thee we take courage, in thee we boast, in thee is all our hope: pray unto Him Who was born of thee for thine unprofitable servants.
*Then the canonarch makes the customary bow to the choirs, likewise also both choirs. "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart:" Trisagion, and three metanias.*
**Priest:** *For Thine is the kingdom:*
*And after the Amen:*
*O Virgin Mother of God, rejoice, Mary full of grace:* *(One prostration).*
*Glory: O Baptist of Christ:* *(One prostration).*
*Now and ever: Pray for us:* *(One prostration).*
*Then: "Beneath thy tenderness of heart:" without a prostration.*
*Lord, have mercy, 40 times, in a gentle voice. Glory, and now and ever: "More honorable than the Cherubim:" In the name of the Lord bless, father.*
**Priest:** *He that is, Christ our God, is blessed: "O Heavenly King:"*
*And we say silently the prayer of the venerable Ephrem: "O Lord and Master of my life:" And we make 3 great prostrations, and 12 small ones. And again the prescribed prayer, and one prostration. Trisagion. Lord, have mercy, 12 times. And the prayer: "O All-Holy Trinity, consubstantial dominion:" Then: "Blessed be the name of the Lord from henceforth and unto the ages," thrice. And three metanias. Glory, and now and ever: "I will bless the Lord at all times:" After this: "It is truly meet:" and a prostration. Then the other choir: Glory, and now and ever: Lord, have mercy, thrice. Bless, and the dismissal according to custom.*[^271]

*When we set the table, we say also Psalm 144: "I will extol Thee, my God:" And the dismissal takes place. And going forth into the narthex, we sing according to custom the sticheron of the holy temple.*
*The customary prayers are also said by the priest, and he commemorates the departed. And the complete dismissal takes place.*[^272]
*This order of the Lamplighting service takes place throughout the entire Holy Forty Days when there is no Presanctified Liturgy; for we have not received the tradition of celebrating the Presanctified Liturgy until Wednesday, so that the entire brotherhood may fast according to tradition, and those who are able may continue fasting until Friday. The Typikon of the Holy Mountain commands not to eat at all on the first day.*[^273]
*On Tuesday, Wednesday, and Thursday, it is proper to eat one litra of bread, and water, and nothing else, except as needed salt with ukrop.*[^274]
*It is proper to know: That in the Lavra of our venerable father Sabbas, we do not sing compline in church, but each of us sings it in cells; for so have we received.*[^275]
*In the cenobitic monasteries of Palestine compline is sung thus:*

## Monday of the First Week of the Holy Fast at Great Compline[^276]

*At the tenth hour. The kindler of lamps enters, and having made a bow to the superior, departs, and strikes twelve times upon the klepalo. And we all gather into church, and each having made the appointed bows, we sit for a little while, until all, or most, are gathered. The priest, rising from his seat and having made the customary bow to the hegumen, gives the blessing. And we begin: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee:" "O Heavenly King:" Trisagion, and three metanias. Likewise also at "O come, let us worship:" three metanias. And immediately we say Psalm 69: "O God, attend unto my help:"*
*Then we begin the Great Canon, dividing it into four parts with the heirmos, so that it may be completed by Thursday evening.*[^277]
*A composition of our holy father Andrew of Crete, of Jerusalem.*[^278]
*Ode 1, Tone 6:*
*The heirmos twice. And at each troparion we make three metanias.*
*Heirmos:*
He is my Helper and Protector unto salvation, He is my God, and I will glorify Him, the God of my father, and I will exalt Him, for He is gloriously glorified.
*Troparion:*
Whence shall I begin to lament the deeds of my wretched life? What beginning shall I lay, O Christ, for my present lamentation? But as Thou art compassionate, grant me remission of transgressions.
Come, wretched soul, with thy flesh, make confession unto the Creator of all, and refrain henceforth from thy former irrationality, and offer unto God tears in repentance.
Having rivaled the transgression of Adam the first-formed, I have known myself stripped naked of God and of the eternal kingdom and delight, because of my sins.
Woe is me, wretched soul! Why hast thou become like unto the first Eve? For thou didst gaze evilly, and wast bitterly wounded, and didst touch the tree, and didst boldly taste of the irrational food.
In place of the sensory Eve, an intelligible Eve has arisen in me: the passionate thought in the flesh, showing what is sweet, yet ever tasting of the bitter potion.
Adam was justly cast out of Eden for not keeping one of Thy commandments, O Savior; but what shall I suffer, who am ever rejecting Thy life-giving words?
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
O Transubstantial Trinity, worshipped in Unity, take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art compassionate, grant me tears of compunction.

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, hope and protection of those that hymn thee, take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Pure Mistress, accept me in repentance.

*Ode 2, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^279]
Give ear, O heaven, and I will speak, and I will hymn Christ Who came in the flesh from the Virgin.
*Troparion:*
Give ear, O heaven, and I will speak; earth, give ear unto the voice that repents before God and hymns Him.
Give ear unto me, O God my Savior, with Thy merciful eye, and receive my fervent confession.
I have sinned more than all men, I alone have sinned against Thee; but as God, O Savior, be compassionate toward Thy creation.
I have formed the deformity of my passions, and by lustful impulses I have corrupted the beauty of my mind.
A storm of evils holds me fast, O compassionate Lord; but stretch forth Thy hand unto me, as unto Peter.
I have defiled the garment of my flesh, and have sullied that which is in Thine image and likeness, O Savior.
I have darkened the beauty of my soul with the pleasures of passions, and have turned my whole mind utterly into dust.
I have torn now my first garment, which the Creator wove for me from the beginning, and thence I lie naked.
I have clothed myself in a torn garment, which the serpent wove for me by his counsel, and I am ashamed.
Tears of the harlot do I also offer, O Compassionate One; cleanse me, O Savior, by Thy tender mercy.
I looked upon the beauty of the garden and was deceived in mind; and thence I lie naked and am ashamed.
All the rulers of passions have plowed upon my back, lengthening their iniquity against me.
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Thee, One in three Persons, the God of all do I hymn: the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit.
*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
O Most Pure Virgin Mother of God, alone all-hymned, pray fervently that we may be saved.

*Ode 3, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^280]
Upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, establish my mind.
*Troparion:*
Raining fire from the Lord, the Lord of old burned up the land of Sodom.
Save thyself upon the mountain, O soul, like Lot of old, and flee unto Zoar.
Flee the conflagration, O soul! Flee the burning of Sodom, flee the destruction of the divine flame!
I alone have sinned against Thee, I have sinned more than all, O Christ Savior: despise me not.
Thou art the Good Shepherd: seek me, the lamb, and despise me not that have gone astray.
Thou art sweet Jesus, Thou art my Creator: in Thee, O Savior, shall I be justified.
I confess unto Thee, O Savior: I have sinned, I have sinned against Thee; but remit, forgive me, as Thou art compassionate.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
O Trinity, Unity, God, save us from delusion, temptations, and tribulations!
*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Rejoice, acceptable womb; rejoice, Throne of the Lord; rejoice, Mother of our Life!

*Ode 4, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^281]
The Prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid, how Thou wast about to be born of a Virgin and appear unto men, and he said: I have heard the report of Thee and was afraid; glory to Thy power, O Lord.
*Troparion:*
Despise not Thy works, forsake not Thy creation, O Righteous Judge, though I alone have sinned as a man more than any man, O Lover of mankind; but as Lord of all Thou hast power to forgive sins.
The end draws near, O soul, it draws near, and thou art heedless, neither dost thou prepare thyself; the time is short; arise! the Judge is near at the doors; like a dream, like a flower, the time of life runs past: why do we toss about in vain?
Awake, O my soul! Consider the deeds that thou hast done, and bring these before thy face, and shed a drop of thy tears; declare thy deeds and thoughts with boldness unto Christ, and be justified.
There has not been in life a sin, nor a deed, nor an evil which I have not committed, O Savior; in mind and in word, by choice and intention, in thought and in deed have I sinned, as no other ever has.
Condemned and convicted am I from thence; I, wretched one, am confounded by mine own conscience, than which nothing in the world is more exacting; O Judge, my Redeemer and Knower, spare and deliver and save me, Thy servant.
The ladder which the great one among the Patriarchs saw of old is an example, O my soul, of active ascent and spiritual ascent: if then thou desirest to live in action, understanding, and contemplation, be renewed.
The Patriarch endured the heat of the day for the sake of want, and bore the frost of the night, making daily provision, pasturing, laboring, serving, that he might wed two wives.
Understand for me the two wives to be action and understanding in contemplation: Leah indeed as action, for she had many children, but Rachel as understanding, for she was acquired through much toil; for without labors, O soul, neither action nor contemplation can be accomplished.
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Undivided in essence, unconfused in Persons do I theologize Thee, Triune One Godhead, as of one kingship and enthroned together; I cry unto Thee the great hymn, thrice-sung in the highest.
*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Both thou bearest and remainest a virgin, and abidest in both natures a Virgin; He that is born renews the laws of nature, and the womb gives birth without travail; where God wills, the order of nature is overcome: for He does whatsoever He wills.

*Ode 5, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^282]
Early out of the night waking early unto Thee, O Lover of mankind, enlighten, I pray Thee, and guide me also in Thy commandments, and teach me, O Savior, to do Thy will.
*Troparion:*
In the night have I passed my life continually; for the night of sin was darkness and deep mist unto me; but make me, O Savior, a son of the day.
Rivaling Reuben, I, wretched one, have devised a lawless and transgressing counsel against God the Most High, defiling my bed as he did that of his father.
I confess unto Thee, O Christ King: I have sinned, I have sinned, as did of old the brothers of Joseph who sold the fruit of purity and chastity.
By his kinsmen was the righteous soul bound, the sweet one was sold into servitude as an image of the Lord; but thou, O soul, hast sold thyself wholly to thine evils.
Imitate the righteous and chaste mind of Joseph, O wretched and unproven soul, and defile not thyself with irrational impulses, ever transgressing the law.
Though Joseph once lived in a pit, O Master Lord, yet it was as a figure of Thy burial and resurrection; but what such thing shall I ever offer unto Thee?
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Thee, O Trinity, do we glorify, the One God: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O Father, Son, and Spirit, simple Essence, Unity ever worshipped!
*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
From thee did God, Who created the ages, clothe Himself in my clay, O incorrupt, unwedded Virgin Mother, and united human nature unto Himself.

*Ode 6, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^283]
I cried with my whole heart unto the merciful God, and He heard me from the lowest hades, and brought up my life from corruption.
*Troparion:*
The tears of mine eyes and sighs from the depth do I purely offer, O Savior, while my heart cries: O God, I have sinned against Thee, cleanse me!
Thou hast turned away from thy Lord, O soul, like Dathan and Abiram; but spare, cry out from lowest hades, lest the earth's chasm cover thee.
Like an unbroken heifer, O soul, hast thou become like Ephraim; preserve thy life like a gazelle from the snares, winging thy mind with action and contemplation.
Let the hand of Moses convince us, O soul, how God can whiten and cleanse a leprous life; and despair not of thyself, even though thou art leprous.
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
I am the Trinity, simple, undivided, divided in Persons, and I am the Unity united in nature, says the Father, and the Son, and the Divine Spirit.
*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Thy womb bore God for us, formed like unto us; Him, as Creator of all, pray thou, O Mother of God, that through thy prayers we may be justified.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*[^284]
My soul, my soul, arise! Why sleepest thou? The end draws near, and thou shalt be confounded; awake therefore, that He may spare thee, even Christ God, Who is everywhere present and fills all things.

*Ode 7, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^285]
We have sinned, we have transgressed, we have done unrighteously before Thee; neither have we kept, nor done, as Thou hast commanded us; yet deliver us not up utterly, O God of the fathers.
*Troparion:*
I have sinned, I have transgressed, and have rejected Thy commandment, for I was brought forth in sins, and have added wounds and sores unto myself; but do Thou Thyself have mercy on me as Thou art compassionate, O God of the fathers.
The secrets of my heart have I confessed unto Thee, my Judge; behold my lowliness, behold also my affliction, and give ear now unto my judgment, and do Thou Thyself have mercy on me as Thou art compassionate, O God of the fathers.
When Saul of old lost his father's asses, O soul, he unexpectedly found a kingdom as an addition; but watch, forget not thyself, preferring thy bestial lusts above the kingdom of Christ.
David the ancestor of God of old, though he sinned doubly, O my soul, shot through indeed with the arrow of adultery, and captured by the spear of the torment of murder; yet thou thyself art sick with heavier deeds, through thine own voluntary impulses.
David of old joined iniquity unto iniquity, mingling murder with adultery, yet immediately he showed double repentance; but thou thyself, most wicked soul, hast done deeds without repenting unto God.
David of old portrayed, composing a song as on an icon, whereby he exposes the deed that he had done, crying: Have mercy on me, for against Thee only have I sinned, the God of all; cleanse Thou me!
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
O Trinity simple, undivided, consubstantial, and One Nature: Lights and Light, and three Holies, and one Holy is God the Trinity hymned; but sing, glorify the Life and Lives, O soul, the God of all!
*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
We hymn thee, we bless thee, we venerate thee, O Mother of God, for from the undivided Trinity hast thou borne the One Christ God, and hast opened heaven itself unto us who are upon earth.

*Ode 8, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^286]
Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify, and before Whom Cherubim and Seraphim tremble, let every breath and creation hymn, bless, and exalt unto all ages.
*Troparion:*
Have mercy on me that have sinned, O Savior; raise up my mind unto conversion, receive me in repentance, be compassionate unto me as I cry: I have sinned against Thee, save me; I have transgressed, have mercy on me!
The charioteer Elijah, having mounted the chariot of virtues, was once borne aloft as unto the heavens, far above earthly things: consider, then, O my soul, this ascent!
Elisha once, having taken up the mantle of Elijah, received from God double grace; yet nevertheless, O my soul, thou hast not partaken of this grace because of intemperance.
The stream of the Jordan of old, by the mantle of Elijah through Elisha, stood on this side and on that; but thou, O my soul, hast not partaken of this grace because of intemperance.
The Shunammite woman once entertained the righteous man, O soul, with good intent; but thou hast not brought into thy house stranger nor traveler; therefore shalt thou be cast out of the bridal chamber, lamenting.
Thou hast ever imitated, O wretched soul, the foul mind of Gehazi; cast away his love of money at least in old age: flee from the fire of Gehenna, departing from thine evils!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
O Father without beginning, Son co-beginningless, O Comforter Good, Spirit Right: Begetter of the Word of God, Word of the Father without beginning, Spirit living and creating: O Trinity, Unity, have mercy on me!
*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
As from the dipping of purple dye, O Most Pure, the spiritual purple robe of Emmanuel was woven within thy womb; therefore as truly the Mother of God do we honor thee.

*Ode 9, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^287]
Ineffable is the childbearing of a seedless conception, incorrupt is the fruit of an unwedded Mother; for the birth of God renews natures; therefore all generations, as the Virgin Bride of God and Mother, orthodoxly magnify thee.
*Troparion:*
The mind is ulcerated, the body is diseased, the spirit is sick, speech is weakened, life is deadened, the end is at the doors; therefore, O my wretched soul, what wilt thou do when the Judge comes to examine thy deeds?
I have brought before thee, O soul, the creation of the world by Moses, and from thence all the covenant Scripture, telling thee of the righteous and the unrighteous; of whom the second, O soul, hast thou imitated, and not the first, having sinned against God.
The Law is weakened, the Gospel remains idle, and all Scripture is neglected in thee; the prophets have grown faint, and every word of the righteous; thy wounds, O soul, are multiplied, and there is no physician to heal thee.
I bring before thee examples of the New Scripture, leading thee, O soul, unto compunction: rival therefore the righteous, turn away from sinners, and appease Christ by prayers and fasts, and by purity and reverence.
Christ became man, calling robbers and harlots to repentance; repent, O soul! The doors of the kingdom are already opened, and Pharisees and publicans and penitent adulterers take it by anticipation.
Christ became man, partaking of my flesh, and voluntarily fulfilled everything that belongs to nature, except sin, showing forth unto thee, O soul, a pattern and image of His condescension.
Christ saved the Magi, called the shepherds, showed the multitude of infants as martyrs, glorified elders and aged widows; whom thou hast not rivaled, O soul, neither in deed nor in life: but woe unto thee when thou shalt be judged!
Having fasted forty days in the wilderness, the Lord afterward hungered, showing that which is human; be not sluggish, O soul: if the enemy attack thee, let him be repulsed from thy feet by prayer and fasting.
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Let us glorify the Father, let us exalt the Son, let us faithfully worship the Divine Spirit: the Undivided Trinity, the Unity according to essence, as Light and Lights, and Life and Lives, giving life and enlightening the ends of the earth!
*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Preserve thy city, O Most Pure Mother of God, for reigning faithfully in thee, in thee it is also established, and conquering through thee, it overcomes every temptation, captures warriors, and passes through obedience.

*To Andrew:*[^288]
O venerable Andrew, thrice-blessed father, shepherd of Crete, cease not to pray for those that hymn thee, that we all may be delivered from wrath, affliction, corruption, and immeasurable transgressions, who faithfully honor thy memory.

*Then both choirs sing the Heirmos:*
*The seedless conception:*

*And after the conclusion of the canon we say Psalm 4: "When I called, He heard me:" Likewise also the other psalms of compline; at Alleluia, three metanias. And immediately we begin the second stasis: "Unto Thee, O Lord, have I lifted up my soul:" and the other two psalms, saying at the end of these: Alleluia; and we make three metanias. Then the choir which had the beginning from vespers begins: "God is with us:" and we say these verses loudly and with melodious chanting, one choir speaking and the other responding. Likewise also: "The bodiless nature:" And: "I believe in one God:" in a low voice. Then the choir that began: "Most Holy Lady Mother of God, pray to God for us:" and the other choir makes a bow. And when the other choir rises and responds, the first choir makes a bow. Likewise also at all the other petitionary verses. And when we say: "O God, cleanse us sinners," both choirs sing equally, and three bows. Then Trisagion without bows, and we say the present troparia in Tone 2, which we also sing loudly and melodiously: "Enlighten mine eyes, O Christ God:" Glory: "Protector of my soul:" Now and ever: "Seeing that we have no boldness:"*[^289]

*There are also other troparia which are sung on alternate days, in Tone 8, that is, on Tuesday and Thursday evening. And these are: "The unsleeping vigilance of mine invisible enemies:" Verse: "Look upon me and hear me, O Lord my God:" "For Thy judgment is terrible, O Lord:" Glory: "Grant me tears, O God:" Now and ever: "Thy shameless protection, O Mother of God:"*[^290]

*Lord, have mercy, 30 times. A verse by the priest. And the prayer: "O Lord, Lord, Who deliverest us:" Then: "O come, let us worship:" thrice. And we say Psalms 50 and 101: "O Lord, hear my prayer:" and the prayer: "O Lord Almighty, God of our fathers:" Trisagion, and three metanias.*
**Priest:** *For Thine is the kingdom:*
*And after the Amen, we say these troparia quickly, in Tone 6: "Have mercy on us, O Lord:" Glory: "O Lord, have mercy on us:" Now and ever: "Open unto us the doors of tender mercy:" Lord, have mercy, 40 times. Glory, and now and ever: "More honorable than the Cherubim:" In the name of the Lord bless, father.*
**Priest:** *Through the prayers of our holy fathers:*
*And the prayer: "O Master God, Father Almighty:" "O come, let us worship:" thrice, and three metanias. "O God, attend unto my help:" and: "O Lord, hear my prayer:" and: "Glory to God in the highest:" "Lord, Thou hast been our refuge:" "Vouchsafe, O Lord, this night:" Then, Trisagion. "O All-Holy Trinity, have mercy on us:" "Our Father:"*
**Priest:** *For Thine is the kingdom:*
*Then we sing loudly and slowly, in Tone 6: "O Lord of hosts, be with us:" And the other choir responds with the same. And the choir that began says, Verse: "Praise ye God in His saints, praise Him in the firmament of His power." And again the troparion. Then the other choir the same troparion at each verse. And upon the completion of the five verses, both choirs say together the first verse and troparion. Then the choir that began starts: Glory: "O Lord, if we had not:" Now and ever: "A great multitude of my transgressions, O Mother of God:" "All-Holy Mother of God:" "All my hope:" Lord, have mercy, 40 times. And the prayer: "Thou Who at every season:" Lord, have mercy, thrice. Glory, and now and ever: "More honorable than the Cherubim:" In the name of the Lord bless, father.*
**Priest:** *God be compassionate unto us:*
*And we make 16 prostrations, as usual, with the prayer of Saint Ephrem; and we make final prayers, that all may be healed. Then the superior begins: Trisagion, and three metanias. After "Our Father:" Lord, have mercy, 12 times. And we say the prayer: "O undefiled, unstained:" And: "And grant unto us, O Master:"*[^291]

*It is proper to know:*
*That certain typika and customs command, after "And grant unto us, O Master," that all fall upon the earth, and the priest say loudly the prayer which is said at Great Vespers: "O Master great in mercy, Lord Jesus Christ our God:"*[^292]

*And thus having made a prostration to the ground, the superior says to the brethren:*
**Superior:** Bless, holy fathers; forgive me a sinner, whereinsoever I have sinned in word, in deed, in thought, and by all my senses.
*And we respond:*
**Brethren:** God forgive thee, holy father.
*And beginning from the left choir in order, one by one, they do likewise, asking and receiving forgiveness, until all have finished.*[^293]
*And thus the priest begins to say: "Let us pray for our most holy universal Pontiff [Name], Pope of Rome," and the rest.*[^294] *And after these as usual, a verse from the superior, and toward him from all likewise a bow; and thus we depart unto our cells.*
*This order takes place throughout the entire Holy Forty Days at compline. Be it known, when the superior says the verse, we respond: "Bless," having received forgiveness. And it is proper for the ecclesiarch to watch this diligently, that when they dismiss compline, there be the token of day, that is, before darkness.*[^295]
*This then, as was prescribed above, having diligently examined one day of the holy fasts, we have set before your love, the amount of chanting, and the prostrations portrayed in it from the holy Fathers, which it is not seemly to despise, not even for a great lawgiver.*[^296]
*And the number of day-and-night prostrations made in church is 300, besides the Midnight Office. We have received also in Palestine that each chant the Psalter in his cells: and those who are able accomplish it day and night; others thrice in the week, and others twice. And to make prostrations, as many as the superior shall determine for each according to his strength.*[^297]

## Tuesday of the First Week of the Holy Fast at Matins[^298]

*After the second poetic recitation of the Psalter, Sessional Hymns, Tone 2:*
*Model: Most blessed art thou:*
Most blessed is the grace of the most honorable Fast: for by it Moses was glorified, and received the Law written upon tablets; and the young youths showed themselves stronger than fire; having quenched therein the blazing carnal passions, crying aloud unto Christ the Savior: Grant illumination unto us all, and deliver us from Gehenna!
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Another [sessional hymn], the same Tone. Model: Being the fountain of tender mercy:*
The time of repentance has arrived: show the fruits of temperance, O my soul! Look upon those that repented before thee, cry aloud unto Christ: I have sinned, save me, even as Thou didst save, O Master, the publican who sighed from his heart, O Good One, Who alone art rich in mercy!

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
Fervent protection of Christians, pray unceasingly unto Thy Son, O Mother of God, to deliver us from every malice and fury of him that wars against us, and to grant us forgiveness wherein we have sinned, for the sake of the tender mercy of His compassions, through thy prayers, O Virgin Mother.

*After the 3rd Sticherology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*[^299]
*Model: O Lord, the cup of torment:*
O Lord, spending the second day in saving abstinence, we cry aloud unto Thee: Grant peace unto the hearts of us Thy servants, and receive our prayers offered with fear, granting unto us the well-traversed course of the fast, cleansing, and great mercy.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. The same. Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
*Model: The Word co-beginningless:*
O divine Blossom sprung from the Root, Ark, and Candlestick, and all-golden Jar, holy Table bearing the Bread of life: as Thy Son and God, implore Him together with the holy Forerunner, to show compassion and save those who confess thee to be the Mother of God.

*Canon from the Menaion and the Triodion. Ode 2, Tone 2:*[^300]
*And the second canticle of the Psalter is chanted.*
*Heirmos:*
See, see that I am He Who saved in the sea, and nourished the people of Israel in the wilderness, and brought forth water from the rock for men, that bearing him that fell of old into corruption, I might draw him unto Myself through ineffable mercy.

Be sober, be watchful, sigh, weep, cast off by fasting the whole burden of sin, O soul; flee the fire through fervent repentance, and tear the lamentable garment of passions by weeping, receiving divine vesture.

Let us all draw near unto the mountain by the fasting of excellent deeds, leaving below the burdensome assaults of pleasures; and let us enter into the darkness of venerable visions, that we may behold the sole desirable beauty of Christ, being divinely deified by mystical ascents.

Woe is me! What shall I become? What shall I do, having committed sin, and yet not fearing the unknown future? Therefore am I condemned before the judgment: O Good and Righteous Judge, convert and save me, who have provoked Thee to anger more than all men.

*Theotokion:*
O Untilled Earth, who didst bud forth the Nourisher of all, Who opens His hand and satisfies every living thing with divine strength according to His good pleasure: establish with the Bread of life the hearts melted away with the satiety of our grievous transgressions.

*Another [canon]. Tone 2. Heirmos: See, see:*
Come, let us descend into the mire of the soul, rendering prayers unto the Lord and crying aloud: Our Father, Who art in heaven, remit and forgive our debts, as Thou alone art compassionate.

Showing our soul serene toward the fast, let us not cloud the changes of daily life, that the rectifications of piety may blossom forth for us.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Unoriginate, uncreated, tripersonal Unity, Sovereign Lady, Kingdom of the ages: Thee, the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the multitude of angels and the whole nature of mankind glorify.

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
We hymn thee, the all-virtuous glory of our race; for through thee we have been deified, O Virgin: for thou didst bear for us the Son and God, Christ, Who released us from the curse.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Who quenched the fire? Who stopped the mouths of the beasts? Fasting, which delivered the youths from the furnace, and saved the Prophet Daniel from the lions: let us also embrace it, brethren!

*Heirmos:*
See, see that I am God, Who of His own will was clothed in flesh, that He might save Adam, who fell through deception into transgression by the serpent.

*Ode 8. Heirmos: In the bush to Moses:*[^301]
Cast off sleep, O soul, from grievous sloth, and keep watch zealously unto the divine commandments: the torch-bearing Bridegroom draws near, make haste to go forth to meet Him.

Grievously wounded by the weapon of pleasures, heal me, O Word, with the medicine of the compassions of Thy tender-hearted beckoning, that I may thankfully glorify Thee unto ages.

Abstain, O soul, from harmful passions, from envy and hatred, and from all malice, feeding upon foods that immaterially mediate heavenly nourishment.

*Theotokion:*
O Pure Mother of God, heal the festerings of my soul, the passions of my heart, and the aberration of my mind, as the only helper of sinners and wall of those that are overthrown.

*Another [canon]. Heirmos: Creator of creation:*
Having given wings to our souls by abstinence, let us all offer acceptable prayers unto the Lord in the heavens.

Having received the spirit of compunction, let us weep for the deliverance of our souls, singing praises unto Christ unto the ages.

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
*Trinity Hymn:*
Consubstantial Trinity and uncreated Unity, God of all, Thee do we highly exalt unto all ages.

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Offer supplication, O Most Pure Lady, for those that hymn thee, that we may be delivered from all temptations and tribulations.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Having spurned the food of the rich man, come, let us fast with Lazarus, that the bosom of Abraham may receive us also.

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord:*
*Heirmos:*
The Creator of creation, Whom the angels dread, hymn, O ye people, and highly exalt Him unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Who from among the earthborn has heard:*[^302]
Who from among the earthborn has stumbled thus, and ever so provoked God to anger? Who followed the impulses of malice, and became an abode of sin, as I, the wretched one? But, O God, Leader of mercy, do Thou show compassion upon me.

O angelic powers of godlike form, pray unto God Who is easily entreated, that He may save my soul, which is tossed upon the deep of the pleasures of this life and the surges of the passions, and is in danger from the assaults of the adversary spirits.

Come, O soul, winged with lightness, arise from downward-dragging malice through the fasting of virtues, and delight in the most radiant visions, mediating the food of virtues, becoming godlike through faith.

*Theotokion:*
Who can utter praise worthy of hearing for thee who didst bear, beyond understanding, the praised God the Word and Lord, Whom the angelic ranks praise? Implore Him, therefore, O unwedded Virgin, on behalf of the people that have sinned.

*Another [canon]. Heirmos: Rejoice, O Isaiah:*
An acceptable time, a day of salvation: let us offer unto God the gifts of virtues, wherein casting off the works of darkness, brethren, let us put on the armor of light, even as Paul cries aloud.

Come, even as the Lord slew the enemy by fasting, so let us also crush his arrows and snares, each saying: "Get thee behind me, Satan," whenever he seeks to tempt us.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
In consubstantiality I hymn Thee, O unoriginate, honorable Trinity, life-originating, indivisible Unity: unbegotten Father, and begotten Word and Son, and Holy Spirit: save us who hymn Thee.

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding is thy childbearing, O Mother of God; for without man was conception in thee, and virginal was the birth: for He that was born is God; Whom magnifying, we call thee blessed, O Virgin.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Having applied ourselves to prayer upon high through fasting, let us also with a pure heart behold God, receiving inwardly the tablets of the commandments, like Moses, gleaming with glory by the countenance of His love.

*Heirmos:*
Rejoice, O Isaiah, the Virgin held in womb, and bore a Son, Emmanuel, both God and Man, Dayspring is His name; Whom magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed.

*Exapostilarion. At the Aposticha, self-governing sticheron of the day, Tone 3:*[^303]
Let us begin, O peoples, the blameless fast, which is salvation unto souls; let us serve the Lord with fear, let us anoint our heads with the oil of almsgiving, and wash our faces with the water of purity;

let us not multiply words in prayers, but as we have been taught, so let us cry aloud: Our Father, Who art in heaven, forgive us our transgressions, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Verse: We were filled in the morning:*
*Again the same self-governing sticheron.*
*Verse 2: And let the brightness:*
*Then the Martyrion:*
The warriors of Christ rejected the fear of kings and tormentors, and with good courage and fortitude confessed Him, the Lord God of all and our King, and they pray for our souls.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, protection of all that pray unto thee, in thee we take courage, and in thee we boast, and in thee is all our hope: pray unto Him Who was born of thee for thine unprofitable servants.

*At the Sixth Hour, troparion of prophecy, Tone 1:*[^304]
As sojourners are we upon the earth, even as all our fathers; preserve our brief span of life without sin, O our Savior, and have mercy on us, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 5, Tone 4:*
Give ear unto the voice of my supplication, my King and my God.
*Verse: Give ear unto my words, O Lord; consider my cry.*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 1):*
Thus says the Lord: If ye be willing and give ear unto Me, ye shall eat the good of the land. But if ye be not willing, neither give ear unto Me, the sword shall devour you; for the mouth of the Lord has spoken these things. How is the faithful city Zion, full of judgment, become a harlot! In her righteousness lodged, but now murderers. Thy silver is unproved; thy tavern-keepers mix wine with water. Thy princes are disobedient, companions of thieves, loving gifts, following after recompense, not judging for the orphans, and not regarding the judgment of the widows. Therefore thus says the Master, the Lord of hosts: Woe to the mighty in Israel! For My wrath shall not cease against the adversaries, and I will execute judgment upon Mine enemies. And I will bring My hand upon thee, and will purge thee unto purity; but the disobedient I will destroy, and I will take away from thee all the lawless, and I will bring low all the proud. And I will establish thy judges as before, and thy counselors as at the beginning; and afterward thou shalt be called a city of righteousness, the mother of cities, the faithful Zion. For with judgment shall her captivity be saved, and with almsgiving. And the lawless and the sinners shall be crushed together, and they that forsake the Lord shall be consumed. For they shall be ashamed of their idols, which they themselves desired, and they shall be confounded for their gardens, which they coveted. For they shall be as a terebinth that has cast off its leaves, and as a garden that has no water. And their strength shall be as tow of flax, and their works as sparks of fire; and the lawless and sinners shall be burned up together, and there shall be none to quench them. The word that came from the Lord unto Isaiah, the son of Amos, concerning Judah and concerning Jerusalem: For it shall come to pass in the last days, that the mountain of the Lord and the house of God shall be manifest upon the tops of the mountains, and shall be exalted above the hills; and all the nations shall come unto it. And many nations shall go and say: Come, and let us go up to the mountain of the Lord, and to the house of the God of Jacob; and He will declare unto us His way, and we will walk in it.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 6, Tone 4:*
O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger.
*Verse: Have mercy on me, O Lord, for I am weak.*

## Tuesday of the First Week of the Holy Fast at Vespers[^305]

*At Lord, I have cried, Stichera, Tone 2:*
*Model from the Triodion: When He appeared:*
Having of old been cast out of paradise by bitter eating, let us make haste to enter in by abstinence from passions, crying aloud unto our God: O Thou Who didst stretch out Thy hands upon the Cross, Who didst drink vinegar and taste gall, and didst endure sufferings from the nails, nailing away all the most bitter pleasures from our souls: for the sake of the tender mercy of Thy compassions, save Thy servants!

Having once been driven out of paradise by the eating of the tree, we have been brought into it by Thy Cross, which we bring in supplication unto Thee, O Plenteous in mercy, and we all faithfully pray Thee: Send down upon us now, in the season of abstinence, fountains of tears that cleanse all the defilement of our passions and transgressions, that we all may cry aloud unto Thee diligently: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Another Sticheron, Tone 2. Model: For my deeds:*
