Grant unto me also, O Word, the enjoyment of abstinence, as once to Adam paradise: to taste of Thine every commandment, O our God, and ever to abstain from the fruit whereof Thou didst forbid sin, that having in joy anticipated Thy life-bearing Passion on the Cross, I may glorify Thee.

*And for the Menaion, 3. Glory, and now and ever, Stavrotheotokion.*
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 7, Tone 6:*
O Lord my God, in Thee have I hoped: save me.
*Verse: Deliver me from all them that persecute me.*

*The Reading from the Book of Genesis (Chapter 1):*
And God said: Let there be lights in the firmament of heaven, to give light upon the earth, and to divide between the day and between the night; and let them be for signs, and for seasons, and for days, and for years. And let them be for enlightenment in the firmament of heaven, so as to give light upon the earth; and it was so. And God made the two great lights: the greater light to rule the day, and the lesser light to rule the night, and the stars. And God set them in the firmament of heaven, so as to give light upon the earth, and to rule over the day and over the night, and to divide between the light and between the darkness; and God saw that it was good. And there was evening, and there was morning, the fourth day. And God said: Let the waters bring forth reptiles of living souls, and winged fowls flying above the earth across the firmament of heaven; and it was so. And God created great sea-monsters, and every living soul of reptiles which the waters brought forth according to their kinds, and every winged fowl according to kind; and God saw that it was good.

And God blessed them, saying: Increase and multiply, and fill the waters in the seas, and let the fowls multiply upon the earth. And there was evening, and there was morning, the fifth day.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 8, Tone 5:*
O Lord our Lord, how marvelous is Thy name in all the earth!
*Verse: For Thy magnificence is exalted above the heavens.*

*The Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 1):*[^306]
Wisdom is sung in the exits, and in the broad places she speaks boldly. Upon the tops of walls she proclaims, and at the gates of the mighty she sits, and at the gates of the city boldly she says: How long then will the simple hold fast to righteousness, and not be ashamed? But the foolish, being desirers of abuse, having become ungodly, hated understanding, and became liable to reproofs. Behold, I will set forth unto you the utterance of my breath, and I will teach you my word. Because I called, and ye did not give ear, and I stretched out words, and ye did not attend, but set at naught my counsels, and did not give ear unto my reproofs: therefore I also will laugh at your destruction, and I will rejoice when ruin comes upon you; and when tumult comes upon you suddenly, and overthrow comes like a whirlwind, or when tribulation and distress come upon you, or when ruin overtakes you. For it shall be that when ye call upon me, I will not give ear unto you; the evil shall seek me, and shall not find me. For they hated wisdom, and did not receive the word of the Lord, neither would they give heed unto my counsels, but mocked at my reproofs. Therefore shall they eat the fruits of their own ways, and be filled with their own ungodliness; for because they wronged infants, they shall be slain, and the inquiry of the ungodly shall destroy them. But he that hears me shall dwell in hope, and shall rest without fear from all evil.

*Then: "Vouchsafe, O Lord:"*
*At the Aposticha, self-governing sticheron of the day, Tone 8:*
Let us accomplish not only abstinence from foods, but estrangement from every material passion: that having enslaved the flesh that tyrannizes over us, we may become worthy of communion with the Lamb of God, the Son of God slain of His own will for the world, and spiritually celebrate the Savior's Resurrection from the dead: having ascended to the height of virtues, in the radiant splendor and enjoyment of excellent deeds, gladdening the Lover of mankind.

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes:*
*Again the same sticheron.*
*Verse 2: Have mercy on us, O Lord:*
*Martyrion:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, having forgotten the things of this life, and disregarding tortures for the sake of the life to come, were shown forth as heirs thereof; therefore they also rejoice with the angels: by their supplications grant unto Thy people great mercy.

*Glory, and now and ever, Stavrotheotokion:*
O marvelous wonder! O new mystery! O dreadful undertaking! cried the Virgin, when she beheld Thee hanging upon the Cross between two thieves, Whom she bore without pain in a marvelous manner; and she wept, saying: Woe is me, my most beloved Child! How has a fierce and ungrateful people nailed Thee to the Cross?

*And the remainder of the office takes place according to custom.*

## Tuesday of the First Week of the Holy Fast at Great Compline[^307]

*After the saying of the psalm: "O God, attend unto my help:"*
*We sing the Great Canon.*
*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos, twice: "A Helper and Protector:"*

*Troparion:*
Surpassing the murder of Cain, by deliberate choice I have become a murderer of the conscience of my soul, having quickened the flesh, and warred against her by my wicked deeds.

*Troparion:*
I have not become like unto the righteousness of Abel, O Jesus: never have I offered unto Thee an acceptable gift, nor divine deeds, nor a pure sacrifice, nor a blameless life.

*Troparion:*
Like Cain, we also, O wretched soul, have together offered unto the Creator of all defiled deeds, a blemished sacrifice, and a useless life: therefore we are also condemned.

*Troparion:*
Fashioning clay into living form, O Creator, Thou didst place within me flesh and bones, and breath and life; but, O my Maker, my Redeemer, and my Judge, accept me in repentance!

*Troparion:*
I make known unto Thee, O Savior, the sins which I have committed, and the wounds of my soul and body, which murderous inner thoughts have brigand-like inflicted upon me.

*Troparion:*
Though I have sinned, O Savior, yet I know that Thou art the Lover of mankind: Thou chastisest mercifully, and art warmly compassionate: Thou seest him that weeps, and runnest to meet him like a Father, calling back the prodigal.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
O Transubstantial Trinity, worshipped in Unity, take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art compassionate, grant me tears of compunction.

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, hope and protection of those that hymn thee, take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Pure Mistress, accept me in repentance.

*Ode 2, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^308]
Give ear, O heaven, and I will speak, and I will hymn Christ Who came in the flesh from the Virgin.

*Troparion:*
Sin has sewn for me coats of skins, stripping me of the first god-woven garment.

*Troparion:*
I am clothed with a vesture of shame, as with fig leaves, in condemnation of my self-willed passions.

*Troparion:*
I have put on a garment of shame, stained and defiled by the shameful flowing of a passionate and pleasure-loving life.

*Troparion:*
I have fallen into passionate ruin and into material corruption; and from then until now the enemy vexes me.

*Troparion:*
Having preferred a life loving of possessions and material things, through intemperance, O Savior, I am now burdened with a heavy load.

*Troparion:*
I have adorned the bodily image with a diverse vesture of shameful thoughts, and I am condemned.

*Troparion:*
I took diligent care for outward adornment alone, having disregarded the inner tabernacle fashioned in the image of God.

*Troparion:*
I have buried the beauty of the primal image by my passions, O Savior: which, as once the lost coin, do Thou seek and find!

*Troparion:*
Like the harlot I have sinned, and cry unto Thee: I alone have sinned against Thee: accept, O Savior, my tears also as myrrh!

*Troparion:*
Cleanse me, as the publican I cry unto Thee: O Savior, cleanse me! For none of those born of Adam has sinned against Thee as I have sinned.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Thee, One in three Persons, the God of all do I hymn: the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit.

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
O Most Pure Virgin Mother of God, alone all-hymned, pray fervently that we may be saved.

*Ode 3, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^309]
Establish, O Lord, my wavering heart upon the rock of Thy commandments, for Thou alone art holy and the Lord.

*Troparion:*
I have gained Thee, the Fountain of life and the Destroyer of death; and I cry unto Thee from my heart before the end: I have sinned, cleanse and save me!

*Troparion:*
I have sinned, O Lord, I have sinned against Thee, cleanse me: for there is none among men that has sinned whom I have not surpassed by transgressions.

*Troparion:*
I have imitated those who committed fornication in the time of Noah, O Savior, inheriting their condemnation of drowning in the flood.

*Troparion:*
Having imitated Ham of old, the mocker of his father, O my soul, thou hast not covered the shame of thy neighbor, turning back with face averted.

*Troparion:*
Flee the burning of sin, O my soul, like Lot: flee Sodom and Gomorrah; flee the flame of every irrational desire!

*Troparion:*
Have mercy, O Lord, have mercy on me, I cry unto Thee, when Thou comest with Thine angels to render unto all according to the worth of their deeds.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Simple, uncreated Trinity, unoriginate Essence, hymned in a Trinity of Hypostases: save us who with faith worship Thy dominion!

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Without father from eternity, in time, O Mother of God, thou didst bear the Son without knowing man: a strange marvel, remaining a virgin while suckling.

*Ode 4, Tone 6. Heirmos: The prophet heard:*[^310]
Keep watch, O my soul! Excel like the great Patriarch of old, that thou mayest acquire action with contemplation, that thou mayest be a mind seeing God, and reach the unsetting darkness in vision, and become a great merchant.

*Troparion:*
Having begotten the twelve Patriarchs, the great Patriarch mystically established for thee, O my soul, a ladder of active ascent: wisely setting the children as foundations, and the steps as ascents.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast imitated the hated Esau, O soul: thou hast given over to the deceiver the birthright of the primal beauty, and fallen from the father's blessing; and twice hast thou stumbled, wretched one, in deed and in contemplation: therefore now repent!

*Troparion:*
Esau was named Edom, because of his extreme frenzy for women: for ever burning with intemperance, and defiled by pleasures, he was called Edom, which is interpreted: the burning of a soul that loves sin.

*Troparion:*
Having heard of Job upon the dunghill, O my soul, who was justified, thou hast not emulated his fortitude; thou hast not had a firm purpose in all that thou knowest and wherein thou hast been tried, but hast shown thyself impatient.

*Troparion:*
He that was formerly upon a throne now sits naked upon a dunghill, covered with sores; he that had many children and was famous is suddenly childless and homeless: for he counted the dunghill a palace, and his sores as pearls.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Undivided in Essence, unconfused in Persons do I proclaim Thee in theology, O one Trinitarian Godhead, as consubstantial and co-enthroned: I cry aloud unto Thee the great hymn, triply sung in the highest.

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Both childbearing and virgin, thou remainest in both natures a Virgin: He that is born renews the laws of nature, and the womb brings forth without childbed travail. Where God wills, the order of nature is overcome; for He does whatsoever He wills.

*Ode 5, Tone 6. Heirmos: Early out of the night waking early:*[^311]
Thou hast heard, O soul, of the ark of Moses, borne by the waters and river waves, fleeing of old as in a bridal chamber from the deeds of Pharaoh's bitter counsel.

*Troparion:*
If thou hast heard of the midwives slaying of old the infant males, O wretched soul, suckle now wisdom, like the great Moses, the practice of chastity!

*Troparion:*
Like the great Moses, having wounded the Egyptian, thou hast not slain him in mind, O wretched soul: and how shalt thou dwell, tell me, in the wilderness of passions through repentance?

*Troparion:*
The great Moses dwelt in the wilderness: come then, imitate his life, that in the bush of the divine appearance, O soul, thou also mayest be in vision!

*Troparion:*
Picture to thyself the rod of Moses, O soul, striking the sea and congealing the deep into a type of the divine Cross, through which thou also canst accomplish great things.

*Troparion:*
Aaron offered unto God fire that was blameless and unbeguiling; but Hophni and Phinehas, like thee, O soul, offered unto God a strange, defiled life.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Thee, O Trinity, do we glorify, the one God: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, Father, Son, and Spirit, simple Essence, Unity ever worshipped!

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
From thee was He clothed in my substance, O incorrupt, unwedded Virgin Mother, God Who created the ages; and He united human nature unto Himself.

*Ode 6, Tone 6. Heirmos: I cried with my whole heart:*[^312]
The surges of my transgressions, O Savior, returning as in the Red Sea, have suddenly overwhelmed me, as once the Egyptians and their captains.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast had an irrational choice, O soul, like Israel of old: for thou didst irrationally prefer the pleasure-loving gluttony of the passions before the divine manna.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast preferred the wells of Canaanite thoughts, O soul, before the vein of the rock, whence as a cup the fountain of theology pours forth streams of wisdom.

*Troparion:*
Swine's flesh, and cauldrons, and Egyptian food, hast thou preferred before heavenly food, O my soul, even as the senseless people of old in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
When Moses Thy servant struck the rock with his rod, he prefigured in type Thy life-creating ribs, from which we all draw the draught of life, O Savior!

*Troparion:*
Search, O soul, and observe, like Joshua the son of Nun, what the land of promise is, and settle therein through good obedience to the law.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
"I am the Trinity, simple, undivided, divided in Persons, and I am the Unity, united in nature," says the Father, and the Son, and the Divine Spirit.

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Thy womb bore God for us, formed in our likeness: implore Him as Creator of all, O Mother of God, that through thy prayers we may be justified.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*
My soul, my soul, arise! Why sleepest thou? The end draws near, and thou shalt be confounded; awake therefore, that He may spare thee, even Christ God, Who is everywhere present and fills all things.

*Ode 7, Tone 6. Heirmos: We have sinned, we have transgressed:*[^313]
When the Ark was being carried upon the wagon, and the ox turned aside, Uzzah merely touched it, and made trial of the wrath of God: fleeing that man's presumption, O soul, reverence divine things with honor.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast heard of Absalom, how he rose up against nature; thou hast known his defiled deeds, whereby he polluted the bed of David his father: yet thou hast imitated his passionate and pleasure-loving impulses.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast subjected thine unenslaved dignity unto thy body: for finding another Ahithophel as an enemy, O soul, thou hast assented unto his counsels; but Christ Himself has scattered them, that thou mayest by all means be saved.

*At Great Compline:*
Marvelous Solomon, filled with the grace of wisdom, once did evil before God and departed from Him; unto whom by thy cursed life, O soul, thou hast become like.

*Troparion:*
Drawn away by the pleasures of his passions, alas for me, Solomon the lover of wisdom became a lover of wanton women and estranged from God; whom thou hast imitated in mind, O soul, through defiled voluptuousness.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast emulated Rehoboam, who gave no heed unto his father's counsel, and likewise the wickedest servant, Jeroboam, the apostate of old, O soul; but flee imitation of them, and cry aloud unto God: I have sinned, have compassion upon me!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
O simple, undivided Trinity, consubstantial and of one nature, Lights and Light, and three Holies, even so is the one Holy praised as God the Trinity; but hymn, O soul, and glorify Life and Lives, the God of all!

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
We hymn thee, we bless thee, we worship thee, O Birthgiver of God; for thou didst bear one of the undivided Trinity, Christ God, and thou thyself hast opened heavenly things unto us who dwell upon the earth.

*Ode 8, Tone 6. Heirmos: Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify:*[^314]
Emulating Uzziah, O soul, thou hast acquired his leprosy doubled within thyself; for thou thinkest unseemly things and committest lawless deeds: leave what thou hast, and hasten unto repentance.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast heard of the Ninevites, O soul, repenting before God in sackcloth and ashes; these thou hast not imitated, but hast shown thyself more wicked than all who sinned before the law and after the law.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast heard of Jeremiah in the pit of mire, O soul, crying out with lamentations for the city of Zion and seeking tears; imitate his sorrowful life, and thou shalt be saved.

*Troparion:*
Jonah fled unto Tarshish, foreseeing the conversion of the Ninevites; for as a prophet he understood the compassion of God, and therefore was zealous that his prophecy be not false.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast heard of Daniel in the pit, how he closed the mouths of the beasts, O soul; thou hast learned how the youths with Azariah quenched the burning flame of the furnace through faith.

*Troparion:*
All the examples of the Old Testament have I brought before thee, O soul: imitate the God-loving deeds of the righteous, and again shun deceitful sins.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Unoriginate Father, co-unoriginate Son, gracious Comforter, upright Spirit: Begetter of the Word of God, Word of the unoriginate Father, living and creating Spirit, Trinity and Unity, have mercy on me!

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
As from the sprinkling of purple dye, O Most Pure One, within thy womb was woven the spiritual purple robe of Emmanuel, His flesh; therefore as truly the Mother of God do we venerate thee.

*Ode 9, Tone 6. Heirmos: Seedless conception:*
Christ was tempted; the devil tempted Him, showing stones that they might become bread; he led Him up into a high mountain to see all the kingdoms of the world in an instant: fear the snare, O soul, be sober, pray at every hour unto God!

*Tuesday of the First Week of the Holy Fast*
The desert-loving turtledove, the voice of one crying aloud, the lamp of Christ, proclaimed repentance, while Herod committed lawlessness with Herodias: take heed, O my soul, that thou be not ensnared in the nets of lawlessness, but embrace repentance!

*Troparion:*
The Forerunner of grace took up his abode in the wilderness, and all Judea and Samaria hearing ran to him, and confessed their sins, being zealously baptized; whom thou hast not imitated, O soul.

*Troparion:*
Marriage is indeed honorable, and the bed undefiled; for Christ blessed both beforehand, eating in the flesh, and at the wedding in Cana turning water into wine, and showing His first miracle, that thou mightest be transformed, O soul!

*Troparion:*
Christ strengthened the paralytic carrying his bed, and raised the dead young man, the widow's son, and the centurion's servant; and appearing unto the Samaritan woman, He prefigured for thee, O soul, the spiritual worship.

*Troparion:*
The Lord healed the woman with the flow of blood by the touch of the hem of His garment; He cleansed lepers; He enlightened the blind and straightened the lame; the deaf, the dumb, and the bowed down to the earth He healed with a word, that thou mightest be saved, O wretched soul![^315]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Let us glorify the Father, let us exalt the Son, let us faithfully worship the Divine Spirit: the undivided Trinity, Unity in essence, as Light and Lights, and Life and Lives, life-creating and illuminating the ends of the earth.

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Preserve thy city, O pure Mother of God; for in thee does it faithfully reign, and in thee is it established, and through thee conquering, it overcomes every temptation, captures foes, and exercises dominion.

*To Saint Andrew:*
Honorable Andrew, most thrice-blessed Father, shepherd of Crete, cease not to pray for those who hymn thee, that we all may be delivered from wrath, affliction, corruption, and boundless transgressions, who faithfully venerate thy memory.

*Then both choirs sing the Heirmos: Seedless conception:*
*And the rest of the order of Compline.*

## Wednesday of the First Week of the Holy and Great Fast at Matins[^316]

*After the first stichology of the Psalter, Sessional Hymn of the Octoechos.*

*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2. Model: The noble Joseph:*
Thou Who by Thy Sufferings hast granted passionlessness unto all, O Lover of mankind, mortifying the passions of my flesh by Thy Cross, vouchsafe me to behold Thy divine Holies, pleasing Thy glory through fasting, that I may richly receive great mercy.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: the same. Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Stavrotheotokion, same Tone. Model: Being full of mercy:*
The Virgin and Thy Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee stretched out dead upon the Tree, weeping bitterly, said, O my Son: What is this fearful mystery? Thou Who grantest eternal life unto all, how dost Thou voluntarily die upon the Cross by a reproachful death?

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2. Model: Life-creating:*
The radiant season of abstinence which Thou hast now sanctified and granted unto us, O Lord: vouchsafe us all to pass through it cleanly in compunction and at peace, by the power of the Cross, O only Lover of mankind.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: the same.*

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Stavrotheotokion, same Tone. Model: Doors of mercy:*
Preserved by the precious Cross of Thy Son, O pure Sovereign Lady, Mother of God, we easily cast down every assault of the adversary; therefore as is due we call thee blessed, as the Mother of Light, the only hope of our souls.

*Then the reading from the Lausiac History.*

*Psalm 50. Canon from the Menaion, and the Triodion Three-Ode Canon, Tone 2.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos: The desert hath blossomed:*
Crucifying voluptuous desires through abstinence, let us be sober in prayers, as it is written, and let us walk in the footsteps of Him Who suffered and put the passions to death.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Having vomited forth bitter sin, let us strive to please Christ Who voluntarily tasted gall, casting down the author of evil by His Cross.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Habit having taken hold of sin draws me unto complete destruction; but deliver Thou me therefrom by Thy Cross, O Compassionate and Plenteous in mercy!

*Theotokion:*

*Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady, who rulest over all creatures, because thou hast borne the Master, free me from the servitude of the deceiver and only adversary.

*Another Ode, same Tone. Heirmos: Establish us:*
The tree of the Cross of abstinence has blossomed forth unto the world; embracing it with love, let us enjoy the fullness of fruits of Christ's divine commandments.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Bearing even now abstinence from passions, let us crucify our flesh unto the Lord, and let us show all its carnal wisdom to be dead unto divine life.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Three Persons of one form do I glorify: the Father, the Son, and the Spirit, one dominion of Godhead, the kingdom and brightness of all.

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Thy birthgiving, O Pure One, was fearful: for God is He Who became incarnate, unoriginately born of the Father, and born of thee at last without a man.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
I hymn the Crucifixion and the piercing of Thy divine ribs, from which I ever draw the drink of immortality, O Christ, and am sanctified.

*Heirmos:*
Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death; and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who hymn Thee.

*Ode 8, Tone 2. Heirmos: Once in Babylon the fiery furnace:*[^317]
The furnace of passions inflames my soul, but quench it with the dew of Thy mercy, O Savior, Who didst pour forth streams of dispassion from Thine incorruptible ribs, being crucified, O Benefactor, by reason of Thine abundant condescension.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Thou hast raised us up who fell into malice by Thine elevation upon the Cross, O Christ; therefore raise up him who has slipped into the abyss of sin, and establish me upon the rock of salvation, that I may glorify Thy dominion.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Having cleansed the purulence of passions from my heart with Thy spear, O Christ, heal all of me whom the serpent wounded with poisonous teeth, and grant me to walk unshaken in the divine paths.

*Theotokion:*
Enlighten those held by night in corruption, O Undefiled One, who art the radiant candle and lampstand wherein the Fire of the Godhead made its dwelling: we all honor and bless thy blessed Childbearing.

*Another Ode. Heirmos: In the bush to Moses:*
Hymn, bless, and exalt throughout all ages Him Who was crucified upon the Tree between the thieves, and pierced with a spear in His life-giving ribs!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Thou Who judgest all the earth didst stand before the tribunal, Thou wast beaten and reviled, Thou wast suspended upon the Cross, redeeming me throughout all ages from the corruption of ancient sin.

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*

*Trinity Hymn:*
One Godhead, Trinity, undivided Nature, distinct in Persons, unshakable Dominion, Father, Son, and Spirit: we hymn Thee in song throughout all ages!

*Now and ever:*

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
O pure Birthgiver of God, heavenly gate, saving door: receive the entreaties of all Christians who call thee blessed throughout all ages.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
O Cross of Christ, who didst guide the thief unto faith, vouchsafe me also by fasting to run the course with good strength, to anticipate thy veneration, and to be quickened with life.

*Let us praise, bless, and worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him throughout all ages.*

*Heirmos:*
Hymn, bless, and exalt throughout all ages Him Who once on Mount Sinai prefigured in the bush the wonder of the Virgin unto Moses!

*Ode 9, Tone 2. Heirmos: The unoriginate Father:*[^318]
The Fast shining brighter than the sun, a grace of many lights, proclaims unto all the rays of the Cross, the radiant gleams of the precious Sufferings, and the saving day of the Resurrection.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Let us love purity, let us flee fornication, let us gird our loins with chastity: that we may appear pure unto the Pure One, the Savior of our souls, Who alone among all seeks purity.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Nail my flesh to Thy fear, O Christ, Who didst nail the sin of Adam unto the Cross; loose the bonds of my evils, crush the arrows of the deceitful one with Thy spear, O Master, and deliver me from his harm!

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin who didst bear the most righteous Judge and only benevolent Christ the Lord: deliver me from the judgment, O Maiden, and from the fire and torment which the enjoyment of sin procures for me.

*Another Ode. Heirmos: Pure and most pure:*
O Thine abundant compassion! For Thou didst endure the Cross, the nails, and the spear, O Lord, for the sake of me who was condemned to corruption; therefore I hymn Thee, O Christ.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Worshipping the Cross, the reed, the nails, and the spear, Thy life-giving Sufferings, all we people hymn Thee in songs, O Christ.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Unity in three hypostases, sole Trinity, sovereign Origin, equally-glorified Nature, Father, Son, and Divine Spirit: save us all!

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Rejoice, propitiation of the world, O Mother of God, unto whom we sinners fleeing unto God ever find reconciliation!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
Strengthening me also by Thy Cross, O Lord, grant me to complete the choir of the fasts, easily finishing its course.

*Heirmos:*
Pure and most pure Mother and Virgin: in songs of hymns let all the faithful piously magnify thee as the Mother of God!

*Photagogicon.*

*Wednesday of the First Week of the Holy Fast. Matins*

*At the Aposticha, self-governing sticheron, twice, Tone 8:*
Come, let us strike down the passions of thoughts through fasting, covering ourselves with spiritual wings: that having easily traversed the hostile storm, we may become worthy of the veneration of the Cross of the Son of God, voluntarily slain for the world; and let us spiritually celebrate the Savior's Resurrection from the dead, having ascended the mountain with the disciples, and glorify the Lover of mankind, the Son Who received all authority from the Father.

*Martyrikon:*
Invincible martyrs of Christ, having conquered deception by the power of the Cross, you received the grace of eternal life; you feared not the threats of the torturers, and wounded with torments, you rejoiced; and now your blood has become healing for our souls: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Stavrotheotokion, same Tone:*
What is this visible sight which is seen by mine eyes, O Master? Thou Who upholdest all creation art lifted up upon the Tree, and put to death, Thou Who grantest life unto all!—the Mother of God, weeping, said, when she beheld crucified upon the Cross God and man, Who shone forth ineffably from her.

*And the First Hour with the stichology and usual bows; and the dismissal.*

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of prophecy, Tone 4:*
Thou knowest our frame, Thou knowest our weakness, O Lover of mankind: we have sinned, but we have not departed from Thee, O God, nor lifted up our hands unto a strange god; spare us through Thy goodness, O Compassionate One![^319]

*And we repeat the same.*

*Then the Prokeimenon, Psalm 9, Tone 4:*
**Reader:** I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart.
*Verse:* I will be glad and rejoice in Thee.

*The reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah. Chapter 2:*
Thus says the Lord: Out of Zion shall go forth the law, and the word of the Lord from Jerusalem. And He shall judge among the nations, and shall rebuke many people; and they shall beat their swords into plowshares, and their spears into sickles; nation shall not take sword against nation, neither shall they learn war any more. And now, O house of Jacob, come, let us walk in the light of the Lord! For He has forsaken His people, the house of Jacob, because from the beginning their country was filled with divinations, as that of the Philistines, and many children of foreigners were born unto them. For their land was filled with silver and gold, and there was no number of their treasures; their land was also filled with horses, and there was no number of their chariots. And their land was filled with the abominations of the works of their hands, and they worshipped that which their fingers made. And the common man bowed down, and the nobleman was humbled; and I will not endure them. And now enter into the rocks, and hide yourselves in the earth from the presence of the terror of the Lord, and from the glory of His power, when He arises to strike the earth! For the eyes of the Lord are lofty, but man is humble; and the loftiness of man shall be brought low, and the Lord alone shall be exalted in that day.

*Wednesday of the First Week at the Sixth Hour*
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 10, Tone 6:*
**Reader:** The Lord is righteous, and He has loved righteousness; His countenance has beheld uprightness.
*Verse:* In the Lord have I put my trust: how will you say to my soul?
*And the rest of the order of the Hours, as previously appointed; and the dismissal.*

## Wednesday of the First Week of the Holy and Great Fast at Vespers[^320]

*When the Presanctified Liturgy is celebrated, the priest makes a small litany at each antiphon of the stichology. At "Lord, I have cried", we set 10 stichoi, beginning with "Bring my soul out of prison": to the Tone of the self-governing sticheron of the day, repeating the same. Then the Martyrikon in the same Tone; then 3 prosomoia from the Triodion; and 3 from the Menaion, repeating one.*

*Self-governing sticheron of the day, Tone 8:*
Fasting bodily, brethren, let us fast also spiritually: let us loose every bond of unrighteousness; let us tear apart the knots of forced contracts; let us rip up every unjust record; let us give bread to the hungry, and lead the homeless poor into our houses: that we may receive great mercy from Christ God!

*Twice.*

*Then the Martyrikon:*
If there be any virtue, and if there be any praise, it is fitting for the Saints; for they bowed their necks to the sword for Thy sake, Who didst bow down the heavens and descend; they shed their blood for Thy sake, Who didst empty Thyself and take the form of a servant; they were humbled even unto death, imitating Thy poverty: through whose prayers, according to the multitude of Thy compassions, O God, have mercy on us!

*Then stichera of Lord Joseph, Tone 2. Model: As Thou hast appeared:*
Jesus, the truly spiritual Sun, having sent you into all the world as lightning-bearing rays of light, has driven away the darkness of deception by the brightness of your divine preaching, O God-beholding apostles, and has enlightened those miserably held in the gloom of ignorance; therefore entreat Him to send down upon us also illumination and great mercy.

*Another sticheron by the same. Model:*
Ascending upon the divine chariot through virtues, Elijah, having shone forth through fasting, was borne aloft to the heavenly height: emulate him, O my humble soul, and fast from all malice, envy, strife, passing pleasure, and voluptuousness: that thou mayest escape the grievous pains of eternal Gehenna, crying aloud unto Christ: Lord, glory to Thee!

*Another sticheron of Lord Theodore, Tone 5. Model: Venerable Father:*
Divine apostles, most fervent intercessors for the world, and defenders of the Orthodox, having boldness of dominion unto Christ our God: pray for us, we entreat you, O most honorable ones, that *Wednesday of the First Week*
we may brightly accomplish the good season of the fasts, and receive the grace of the Consubstantial Trinity: glorious, great heralds, pray for our souls!

*And 3 stichera from the Menaion.*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion.*

*And the Entrance is made with the censer, without the Gospel; [but when the Gospel is to be read, then it is made with the Gospel].*

***"O Radiant Light:"***

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 11, Tone 5:*
**Reader:** Thou, O Lord, shalt keep us, and shalt preserve us from this generation and unto eternity.
*Verse:* Save me, O Lord, for the godly man has ceased.

*The reading from Genesis. Chapter 1:*
And God said: Let the earth bring forth the living soul according to kind, four-footed beasts, and creeping things, and wild beasts of the earth according to kind: and it was so. And God made the wild beasts of the earth according to kind, and cattle according to their kind, and all creeping things of the earth according to their kind: and God saw that it was good. And God said: Let Us make man in Our image and likeness; and let them have dominion over the fish of the sea, and over the birds of heaven, [and over the wild beasts,] and over the cattle, and over all the earth, and over all creeping things that creep upon the earth. And God made man, in the image of God He made him: male and female He made them. And God blessed them, saying: Be fruitful and multiply, and fill the earth, and subdue it; and have dominion over the fish of the sea, [and over the wild beasts,] and over the birds of heaven, and over all the cattle, and over all the earth, and over all creeping things that creep upon the earth. And God said: Behold, I have given you every seed-bearing herb sowing seed, which is upon the face of all the earth; and every tree which has in itself the fruit of a seed that is sown, it shall be food for you; and to all the wild beasts of the earth, and to all the birds of heaven, and to every creeping thing that creeps upon the earth, which has in itself the breath of life, even every green herb for food: and it was so. And God saw everything that He had made; and behold, it was very good. And there was evening, and there was morning, the sixth day. And the heavens and the earth were finished, and all the host of them. And on the sixth day God finished His works which He had made; and He rested on the seventh day from all His works which He had made. And God blessed the seventh day, and sanctified it; because on it He rested from all His works which God began to make.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 12, Tone 6:*
**Reader:** Look upon me and hear me, O Lord my God.
*Verse:* How long, O Lord, wilt Thou forget me unto the end? How long wilt Thou turn away Thy face from me?

*And after this the deacon proclaims:*
**Deacon:** Command!
*The priest, taking the censer in both hands, and the candlestick with the candle, stands before the holy table facing east, and tracing the sign of the cross, says:*
**Priest:** Wisdom! Upright!
*Then turning west toward the people, he says:*
**Priest:** The Light of Christ illumines all!
**Reader:** *The reading from Proverbs.*
**Deacon:** *Let us attend!*

*Chapter 2:*
My son, if thou wilt receive the words of my commandment, and hide them within thee; thine ear shall listen unto wisdom, and thou shalt apply thine heart to understanding, and thou shalt apply it for the instruction of thy son. For if thou wilt call upon wisdom, and utter thy voice for understanding, and seek discernment with a loud voice; and if thou wilt seek it as silver, and search for it as for treasures: then shalt thou understand the fear of the Lord, and find the knowledge of God.[^321]

*At Vespers*
For the Lord gives wisdom, and from His face come knowledge and understanding; and He stores up salvation for the upright, and protects their course, that He may preserve the paths of ordinances, and He will guard the way of those who reverence Him. Then shalt thou understand righteousness and judgment, and shalt direct all good paths; for if wisdom enters into thy mind, and knowledge seems good unto thy soul, good counsel shall preserve thee, and holy understanding shall guard thee: that it may deliver thee from the evil way, and from the man who speaks nothing faithful. O you who have forsaken the upright paths to walk in the ways of darkness! O you who delight in evils, and rejoice in wicked perversity! Whose paths are crooked, and their courses winding: to make thee far from the upright path, and an alien from righteous understanding. My son, let not evil counsel overtake thee, which forsakes the instruction of youth, and has forgotten the divine covenant; for it has set its house near death, and its paths near Hades with the earthborn. And all who walk in it shall not return, neither shall they overtake the paths of the upright; for they shall not attain unto the years of life. For had they walked in good paths, they would have found smooth upright paths; the good shall be dwellers upon the earth, and the innocent shall remain upon it. For the upright shall dwell upon the earth, and the holy shall remain upon it; but the paths of the ungodly shall perish from the earth, and the lawless shall be cast out from it.

*And at the conclusion of the readings, the priest says:*
**Priest:** Peace be unto thee.
**Deacon:** Wisdom!
**Reader:** *Let my prayer arise:*
*Then both right and left choirs, and the people standing before them, both sides in the whole church, stand upon their knees, praying. And after the chanting of the reader, the first choir sings the same:*
**Choir:** *Let my prayer arise:*
*And the reader, and the other side of the choir, and the people standing before them, stand upon their knees, until they have sung.*

*Then the reader sings the 1st verse:*
**Reader:** O Lord, I have cried unto Thee, hear me:
*And after the verse is sung, the second choir sings:*
**Choir:** *Let my prayer arise:*
*Then the reader, and the first choir, and the people on that side, prostrate upon their knees.*

*Then the reader sings the 2nd verse:*
**Reader:** Set, O Lord, a watch before my mouth:
*And the first choir sings again:*
**Choir:** *Let my prayer arise:*
*And the reader, and the second choir, and the people standing before them, again bow their knees.*

*And again the reader sings the 3rd verse:*
**Reader:** Incline not my heart unto words of evil:
*And the second choir sings:*
**Choir:** *Let my prayer arise:*
*And the reader, the first choir, and the people bow their knees.*

*And again the reader sings:*
**Reader:** *Let my prayer arise, as incense before Thee:*
*And then both choirs, and all the people standing in church, prostrate upon their knees, and stand while the reader sings, and having arisen, finish chanting at the kleros:*
**Choir:** *The lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice.*
*And at the conclusion, we make 3 bows.*

*If it be the feast of the Finding of the Honorable Head, or of the Holy 40 Martyrs, then at the conclusion of "Let my prayer arise", the holy doors being immediately opened, we sing the Prokeimenon, the Apostle, Alleluia, the Gospel, and the rest according to custom. But outside of a feast, after "Let my prayer arise": the deacon recites the litany, and the priest the fervent prayers; and we make 3 prostrations. And the rest of the order of the Presanctified, according to the rank as set forth in the Sluzhebniks.*

*It should be known:*
*That at the completion of the procession of the Holy Gifts, at "Now the celestial powers": we make three prostrations, with bare heads.*
*Be it known also, that when the divine Mysteries are being carried, all the people make God-befitting adoration, and the chanters, falling prostrate, worship Christ God Who is in the Mysteries, for they are completely presanctified.*

*Kinonikon:*
**Choir:** Taste and see that the Lord is good. Alleluia, *three times.*
**Deacon:** Draw near!
*We make one bow, and sing:*
**Choir:** I will bless the Lord at all times, His praise shall continually be in my mouth. Alleluia, *three times.*
*After the exclamation by the Priest:*
**Priest:** Always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** We thank Thee, O Christ our God. Alleluia, *three times.*
*After the prayer behind the ambo:*
**Choir:** Blessed be the name of the Lord: *and 3 metanias.* I will bless the Lord at all times.
*And antidoron is distributed unto us; and the complete dismissal.*[^322]

*This order takes place throughout the entire Forty Days, whenever the Presanctified is celebrated.*

*Wednesday of the First Week*
*And where there is no Presanctified Liturgy, at Vespers we sing 6 stichera: prosomoia from the Triodion, and from the Menaion. Glory, now and ever, Theotokion, according to the Tone of the Menaion. At the Aposticha, the self-governing sticheron of the day from the Triodion, twice; and the Martyrikon which was at "Lord, I have cried". Glory, now and ever, Theotokion of the same Tone; and the usual conclusion.*

*Entering the refectory, we eat dry food, and drink juice with honey.*

*On Thursday of this week we do not set a table, but remain fasting until Friday: for then, entering the refectory, we eat.*

## Wednesday of the First Week of the Holy and Great Fast at Great Compline[^323]

*The beginning as on the preceding days, and we sing the present Great Canon of our venerable Father Andrew of Crete, of Jerusalem.*

*We make at each troparion 3 metanias, saying also the verse:*
*Troparion:*
*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos, twice: A helper and a protector:*
From my youth, O Christ, have I transgressed Thy commandments; completely passionate and careless, I have passed my life in sloth; therefore I cry unto Thee, O Savior: at least at the end, save me!

*Troparion:*
Cast down, O Savior, before Thy gates, at least in mine old age cast me not empty into Hades; but before the end, as Lover of mankind, grant me remission of transgressions!

*Troparion:*
My wealth, O Savior, have I squandered in wantonness; I am devoid of godly fruits, and hungry I cry: O Father of compassions, prevent me and have compassion upon me!

*Troparion:*
I am he who fell among thieves in my thoughts; all of me has now been wounded by them, and is filled with sores; but do Thou Thyself, standing by, O Christ Savior, heal me!

*Troparion:*
The priest, foreseeing me, passed by on the other side, and the Levite, seeing me naked in afflictions, despised me; but O Jesus, Who didst shine forth from Mary, do Thou stand by and have compassion upon me!

*To the Venerable Mother Mary:*
Grant me radiant grace from divine providence from on high to escape the gloom of passions, and to sing zealously, O Mary, the fair corrections of thy life!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
O Trinity above all essence, worshipped in Unity: take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art compassionate, give me tears of compunction!

*Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, the hope and protection of those who hymn thee: take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as pure Sovereign Lady, receive me in repentance!

*At Great Compline:*

*Ode 2, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Give ear, O heaven, and I will speak":*[^324]
*Troparion:*
I have slipped away, as David, in wantonness, and have been defiled; but wash me also, O Savior, with tears.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Neither tears nor repentance have I, nor compunction; do Thou Thyself, O Savior, as God, grant these unto me.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
I have lost my first-created beauty and my comeliness, and now I lie naked and ashamed.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Shut not Thy door against me then, O Lord, O Lord; but open it unto me as I repent before Thee.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Give ear unto the sighing of my soul, and receive the drops of mine eyes, O Savior, and save me!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
O Lover of mankind, Who desirest that all be saved, do Thou recall me, and receive me as good, who repent.

*Theotokion:*
O Most Pure Virgin Theotokos, alone all-hymned, pray fervently that we may be saved.

*Second Heirmos: "See, see, that I am God":*
*Troparion:*
"See, see, that I am God": give ear, O my soul, unto the Lord crying aloud, and depart from thy former sin, and fear Him as incorruptible, and as Judge and God.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Unto whom hast thou become like, O most sinful soul? Only unto the first Cain, and unto that Lamech, having stoned the body with evil deeds, and slain the mind by irrational strivings.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Having outrun all who lived before the Law, O soul! Unto Seth thou hast not become like, nor hast thou imitated Enos, nor Enoch by translation, nor Noah; but thou hast shown thyself destitute of the life of the righteous.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Thou alone hast opened the sluices of the wrath of thy God, O my soul, and hast drowned all thy flesh, as the earth, and thy deeds and life, and hast remained outside the ark of salvation.

*To the Venerable Mother Mary:*
*Refrain:* Venerable Mother Mary, pray unto God for us.
With all eagerness and love thou didst run unto Christ, having turned away from the first path of sin, and feeding thyself in trackless wildernesses, and purely fulfilling His divine commandments.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
O unoriginate, uncreated Trinity, indivisible Unity: receive me who repent, save me who have sinned, I am Thy creation, despise me not; but spare me and deliver me from the condemnation of fire!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
O Most Pure Sovereign Lady, Birthgiver of God, hope of those who run unto thee, and haven of those in tempest: make Thy merciful Son, the Creator, gracious unto me also by thy prayers.

*Ode 3, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Establish, O Lord, upon the rock":*
*Troparion:*
Thou hast not inherited the blessing of Shem, O wretched soul, nor a spacious possession like Japhs; thou hast appeared on earth as an abandonment.

*Wednesday of the First Week of the Holy Fast*

*Troparion:*
From the land of Haran, depart from sin, O my soul; come into the land pouring forth ever-living incorruption, which Abraham inherited.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Thou hast heard of Abraham, O my soul, who of old left the land of his fathers and became a pilgrim; imitate his purpose.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
At the oak of Mamre the Patriarch entertained the Angels, and inherited in old age the prize of promise.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Understanding Isaac, O my wretched soul, as a new sacrifice, mystically offered as a whole burnt-offering unto the Lord, imitate his purpose.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Thou hast heard of Ishmael, be sober, O my soul, driven out as the offspring of a bondwoman; take heed, lest thou suffer anything similar through wanton indulgence.

*Refrain:* Venerable Mother Mary, pray unto God for us.
I am held fast by tempest and the surging billow of transgressions; but do thou thyself, O mother, save me now, and lead me unto the haven of divine repentance!

*Another:*
Having offered a servant's supplication also now, O venerable one, unto the compassionate Mother of God by thy prayers, open unto me the divine entrances!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
O simple, uncreated Trinity, unoriginate Nature, praised in a Trinity of Persons: save us who worship Thy dominion in faith!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
The timeless Son of the Father, in time, O Birthgiver of God, without knowing man thou didst bear, a marvelous wonder, remaining a Virgin nursing a Child!

*Ode 4, Tone 6. Heirmos: "The prophet heard":*[^325]
*Troparion:*
My body is defiled, my spirit is soiled, I am all wounded; but as Physician, O Christ, heal both by my repentance; wash me, cleanse me, show me, O my Savior, purer than snow!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Thy Body and Thy Blood, when crucified for all, hast Thou laid down, O Word: Thy Body, that Thou mightest renew me; Thy Blood, that Thou mightest wash me; and Thy spirit hast Thou delivered up, that Thou mightest bring me, O Christ, unto Thy Father!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Thou hast wrought salvation in the midst of the earth, O Compassionate One, that we might be saved; willingly wast Thou crucified upon the Tree; Eden that was shut was opened; the creation above and below, all the nations being saved, worship Thee.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Let the Blood from Thy side be a font for me, together with the drink that poured forth the water of remission, that I may be cleansed by both, anointing myself and drinking, as anointing and drink, O Word, Thy life-flowing words!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
The Church has gained a chalice, Thy life-bearing side, from which it poured forth for us two streams of remission and knowledge, as a figure of the Old and New Covenants together, O our Savior!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
I am destitute of the bridal chamber, I am destitute of the marriage, and together of the supper; the lamp is quenched as having no oil; the bridal chamber is shut while I sleep, the supper is consumed; and I, bound hand and foot, am cast out!

*At Great Compline:*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Indivisible in essence, unconfused in Persons, I theologize Thee, O Trinity, One Godhead, as consubstantial and co-enthroned; I cry aloud unto Thee the great hymn, thrice-chanted in the highest!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Both thou bearest and remainest a virgin, and in both thou abidest a Virgin by nature; He that is born renews the laws of nature, and the womb that bore not brings forth. Where God wills, the order of nature is overcome; for He does whatever He pleases.

*Ode 5, Tone 6. Heirmos: "From night early morning":*[^326]
*Troparion:*
Being heavy in disposition, I have become unto bitter Pharaoh, O Master, like Jannes and Jambres in soul and body, and submerged in mind; but help me!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
I have been defiled with mire, wretched in mind; wash me, O Master, in the bath of my tears, I pray Thee, making the garment of my flesh white as snow!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
If I examine my deeds, O Savior, I see myself exceeding every man in sins; for with deliberate knowledge I have sinned, not in ignorance.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Spare, spare, O Lord, Thy creation; I have sinned, remit unto me; for Thou alone art pure by nature, and none other besides Thee is without defilement.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
For my sake, being God, Thou didst take form in me; Thou didst show forth miracles, healing lepers, and strengthening the paralytic; Thou didst stop the flow of the woman with an issue of blood, O Savior, by the touching of Thy hem.

*To the Venerable Mother Mary:*
*Refrain:* Venerable Mother Mary, pray unto God for us.
Having crossed the streams of the Jordan, thou didst find painless rest, having escaped the pleasures of the flesh; from which deliver us also by thy prayers, O venerable one!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Thee, O Trinity, do we glorify, the One God: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, Father, Son, and Spirit, simple Essence, Unity ever worshipped!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
From thee did God, Who created the ages, clothe Himself in my clay, O undefiled, unwedded Virgin Mother, and united human nature unto Himself!

*Ode 6, Tone 6. Heirmos: "I cried aloud with my whole heart":*
*Troparion:*
Arise and subdue, as Joshua did Amalek, the carnal passions, and the Gibeonites, ever conquering deceitful thoughts!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Pass over the flowing nature of time, as the ark of old, and take possession of that land of promise, O soul; God commands!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
As Thou didst save Peter when he cried out "Save me!", prevent me, O Savior, and deliver me from the beast, stretching out Thy hand, and lead me up from the depths of sin!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
I know Thee as a tranquil haven unto all, O Master, Master Christ; but prevent me and deliver me from the impenetrable depths of sin and despair!

*Wednesday of the First Week of the Holy Fast*

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
"I am the Trinity, simple, indivisible, divided in Persons, and I am the Unity united in nature," says the Father, and the Son, and the Divine Spirit.

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Thy womb bore God for us, fashioned in our likeness; Him, as the Creator of all, do thou implore, O Mother of God, that by thy prayers we may be justified.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*
My soul, my soul, arise, why sleepest thou? The end draws near, and thou shalt be confounded; awake therefore, that Christ God may spare thee, Who is everywhere present and fills all things!

*Ode 7, Tone 6. Heirmos: "We have sinned, we have committed iniquity":*[^327]
*Troparion:*
Thou hast gathered the sins of Manasseh by deliberate choice, setting up the passions as abominations, and increasing indignation, O soul; but emulating his repentance warmly, acquire compunction!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Thou hast rivalled the defilements of Ahab, O my soul, woe is me, thou hast been a dwelling-place of carnal abominations, and a shameful vessel of passions; but groan from thy depths, and confess thy sins unto God!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Heaven was shut against thee, O soul, and a famine from God overtook thee, when thou didst not submit unto the words of Elijah the Tishbite, even as Ahab of old; but become like the widow of Zarephath, feed the prophet's soul!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Elijah once burned twice fifty of Jezebel's men, when he destroyed the shameful prophets, unto the rebuke of Ahab; but flee imitation of both of them, O soul, and be strengthened!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
O simple, undivided Trinity, consubstantial, and one Nature, Lights and Light, and three Holies; and the one Holy is praised as God the Trinity; but hymn, O soul, and glorify Life and Lives, the God of all!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
We hymn thee, we bless thee, we worship thee, O Birthgiver of God; for thou didst bear One of the undivided Trinity, Christ God, and thou thyself hast opened heavenly things unto us who dwell upon earth.

*Ode 8, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify":*
*Troparion:*
O righteous Judge, Savior, have mercy, and deliver me from the fire, and from the threat which I am righteously to endure at the judgment; remit unto me before the end, through virtue and repentance!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
As the thief I cry unto Thee: Remember me! As Peter I weep bitterly: Remit unto me, O Savior! I call as the publican; I shed tears as the harlot; receive my lamentation, as once that of the Canaanite woman!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Heal the festering of my humble soul, O Savior; O only Physician, apply a plaster unto me, and oil and wine: deeds of repentance, compunction with tears!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Imitating the Canaanite woman, I also cry: Have mercy on me, O Son of David! I touch the hem of Thy garment, as the woman with an issue of blood; I weep as Martha and Mary over Lazarus!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Unoriginate Father, co-unoriginate Son, good Comforter, upright Spirit: Begetter of the Word of God, Word of the unoriginate Father, living and creating Spirit: Trinity, Unity, have mercy on me!

*At Great Compline:*

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
As from the dipping of crimson dye, O Most Pure One, within thy womb was woven the spiritual purple robe of Emmanuel, His flesh; therefore as truly the Mother of God do we venerate thee.

*Ode 9, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Seedless conception":*[^328]
*Troparion:*
Healing diseases, Christ the Word preached good tidings unto the poor; He cured the maimed, He ate with publicans, He conversed with sinners; the soul of the daughter of Jairus, which was dead beforehand, He brought back by the touch of His hand.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
The publican was saved, and the harlot was made chaste, but the Pharisee boasting was condemned: the one saying "Cleanse me!", the other "Have mercy on me!", but the last boasting cried: "God, I thank Thee!", and other foolish words.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Zacchaeus was a publican, yet nevertheless he was saved; and Simon the Pharisee was offended, while the harlot received the absolving remission of sins from Him Who had power to forgive sins; whom, O soul, make haste to imitate!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Unto the harlot, O my wretched soul, thou hast not been zealous, who having taken the alabaster jar of myrrh, with tears anointed the feet of the Savior, and wiped them with her hair, of Him Who tore up the handwriting of her ancient transgressions.

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Thou hast learned, O my soul, how the cities unto which Christ gave the gospel were accursed; fear the warning, lest thou become as they, whom the Master compared unto the Sodomites and condemned even unto Hades!

*Refrain: Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me...*
Lest thou appear worse through despair, O my soul, having heard of the faith of the Canaanite woman, whose daughter was healed by the word of God: "O Son of David, save me also!", cry aloud from the depth of thy heart, even as she unto Christ!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Let us glorify the Father, let us exalt the Son, let us faithfully worship the Divine Spirit: undivided Trinity, Unity according to essence, as Light and Lights, and Life and Lives, giving life and illuminating the ends of the earth!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Preserve thy city, O Most Pure Birthgiver of God; for reigning faithfully in thee, in thee it is also established, and conquering through thee, it overcomes every temptation, and takes captive the warriors, and directs obedience.

*To the Venerable Father Andrew:*
*Refrain:* Venerable Father Andrew, pray unto God for us.
Honorable Andrew, thrice-blessed Father, Pastor of Crete: cease not to pray for those who hymn thee, that we all may be delivered from wrath, and sorrow, and corruption, and measureless transgressions, who faithfully honor thy memory!

*Then both choirs sing the Heirmos: "Seedless conception":*
*Likewise the rest of the order of Great Compline.*

## Thursday of the First Week of the Holy Fast at Matins[^329]

*After the first stichology, the Apostolic Sessional Hymns, two idiomela, and Theotokion. Then the reading.*

*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Having made wiser" (Оу҆мꙋдри́вый па́че):*
O Word of God, Who didst show forth the disciples as luminaries throughout the ends of the earth by their preaching: illuminate our hearts with the light of virtues, and cleanse them through fasting, granting the repentance of conversion unto Thy servants, that we may glorify Thee, O Savior, Who alone art all-good!

*Glory, the same. Both now and ever, Theotokion, in the same Tone:*
O Mother of God, despise me not who need thy protection; for in thee has my soul hoped, have mercy on me!

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Brighter than the Cherubim" (Свѣтлѣ́йшаѧ Херꙋві́мъ):*
True abstinence, the brightest companion of virtues, do we implore thee, O apostolic twelve, pray that we may peacefully accomplish it, and receive the fruits of salvation; for ye are truly the bulwark of those on earth, and the refuge of our souls!

*Glory, the same. Both now and ever, Theotokion, in the same Tone:*
Brighter than the Cherubim, higher than the heavens, all-hymned Mother of God, confessing thee in truth, we sinners have salvation, and find protection in temptations; therefore cease not to pray for us, the bulwark and refuge of our souls!

*The Canon in the Menaion. And the Triodion by Lord Joseph. And the Biblical Ode is chanted.*

*Ode 4, Tone 2. Heirmos: "Thou hast come from the Virgin":*
Receiving the rays of abstinence, shine forth, O my soul, and flee the darkening of sin, so that the light of forgiveness may shine upon thee through the Holy Spirit.

Ensnaring me with the hook of pleasure, the deceiver took me captive; but O Apostles, who caught the world with the word, deliver me from his malice!

Ye appeared as the rays of the Sun of glory, O glorious Apostles, driving away the darkness of deception; illuminate me also, who am darkened by every malice!

*Theotokion:*
I will cry aloud unto thee in night and in day when afflicted, and I shall be saved and pass beyond pleasures, being protected by thy power, O Virgin, my wall and help!

*At Matins:*

*Another [canon], by Lord Theodore, Tone 5. Heirmos: "The works of Thy dispensation, O Lord":*[^330]
Being apostles of the Sun of righteousness, all-radiant luminaries, ye enlighten the earthly world, driving away the gloom of deception.

Being the Spirit-moved lyre of the Savior, O Apostles, ye became known on earth, singing unto us a sweet-sounding hymn, and turning the world unto God.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Glorifying the Trinity in one Essence, let us hymn the one Lord God: the unbegotten Father, the begotten Son, and the living Spirit.

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
A Virgin conceived in her womb, O Lord, and bore Thee, Emmanuel; for Thou didst go forth for the salvation of Thy people, to save Thy faithful, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
O divine and all-honorable apostolic twelve: pray Christ for us, we beseech you, that we may courageously traverse the forty-day course!

*Heirmos:*
The works of Thy dispensation, O Lord, struck awe into the Prophet Habakkuk; for Thou didst go forth for the salvation of Thy people, Thou didst come to save Thine anointed ones.

*Ode 8, Tone 2. Heirmos: "Unto the tyrant's decree":*
Let us fast from every pleasure, disciplining our senses through fasting, and let us drink warmly the drink of compunction, chanting: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Pray that I may be a partaker of the standing at the right hand, O Apostles, when ye sit to judge mankind with Christ, I who am guilty of many sins and of judgment!

Let us ascend the chariot of divine virtues, having cleansed ourselves by fasting, and let us give wings to our mind unto the exalted heights, chanting: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst remain unconsumed, having borne the Fire of the Godhead, O Virgin; but burn up the passions of the souls of those who faithfully chant unto thee the greeting of the angels, O only mediatrix of joy!

*Another [canon]. Heirmos: "Thy venerable children":*
As trumpets of the Spirit, hymning the disciples of Christ, we cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

As intercessors for the world and expellers of deception, we hymn the disciples of Christ, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
*Trinity Hymn:*
Hymning the all-holy Trinity, in the Father, and the Son, and the Spirit, we chant: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
All we men hymn thine ineffable childbearing, crying out piously, O Pure One: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*At Matins:*

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
O Apostles, pray that we may in peace finish the cleansing days, who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord:*
*Heirmos:*
Thy venerable children chanting in the furnace, O Christ, said: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9, Tone 2. Heirmos: "The uncontainable God":*[^331]
Cast into the deep abyss of pleasures, I call upon the deep of Thy compassion, O Helmsman, save me!

O Fountain of compassion, grant me now compunction and sighing, that I may weep for the measureless seas of my sins!

By the honorable prayers of Thy disciples, O Jesus, grant me to worship Thy precious Resurrection and Thy divine Sufferings!

*Theotokion:*
Thou who didst heavenize our earthly clay, O All-Immaculate One, by the dwelling of God within thee, deliver all from afflictions!

*Another [canon]. Heirmos: "We magnify, O Christ":*
Drawing water of immortality prophetically from the Savior's fountains, the Apostles ever give drink unto those who thirst through the traditions of life.

Appearing as princes of the Heavenly King, O Apostles, ye make the whole inhabited earth subject unto honoring, worshipping, and glorifying Him alone as God!

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. Trinity Hymn:*
Indivisible Trinity, all-effecting and almighty Unity, Father, Son, and Holy Spirit: Thou art my God, Lord, and Light, and hymning Thee, I worship Thee!

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Thee, O Pure Mother and Virgin, do generations of generations call blessed, for thou wast the cleansing-propitiation of the world, having ineffably born the Savior and Creator!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
O choir of Apostles, save those who hymn you, and vouchsafe that in compunction of heart we may pass through all the days of the light-creating fasts!

*Heirmos:*
We magnify, O Christ, Thy pure and all-immaculate Mother: for she bore Thee supernaturally in the flesh, Who deliverest us from all deception and corruption.

*Photagogicon of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 3:*
O Lord, Thou hast appointed repentance for me, a sinner, wishing to save me, unworthy though I am, through Thy measureless mercy; I fall down before Thee, praying: humble my soul through fasting, for unto Thee have I fled, the only abundantly merciful One!

*At Matins:*

*Martyrikon:*
As luminaries in the world ye shine even after death, holy Martyrs, having fought the good fight; having boldness, pray Christ that He have mercy upon our souls!

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion, in the same Tone:*
O Mother of God, mediatrix of all who pray unto thee, in thee we are confident, and in thee we boast, and in thee is all our hope: pray Him Who was born of thee for thine unprofitable servants!

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of Prophecy, Tone 1:*[^332]
Deliver us from enemies visible and invisible, O Lord, lest the nations ever say: "Where is their God?"; that they may understand, O Master, that Thou overlookest the sins of Thy people when they repent!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 13, Tone 1:*
When the Lord turns back the captivity of His people.
*Verse:* The fool hath said in his heart: "There is no God."

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (2:11–21):*
The Lord alone shall be exalted in that day. For the day of the Lord of Sabaoth shall be upon every insulter and proud one, and upon every high and haughty one, and they shall be brought low; and upon every cedar of Lebanon of the high and exalted, and upon every tree of the oak of Bashan; and upon every high mountain, and upon every high hill; and upon every high tower, and upon every high wall; and upon every ship of the sea, and upon every display of the beauty of ships. And every man shall be humbled, and the haughtiness of man shall fall, and the Lord alone shall be exalted in that day. And they shall hide all handmade idols, carrying them into caves, and into the clefts of the rocks, and into the caverns of the earth, from before the face of the dread of the Lord, and from the glory of His might, when He arises to crush the earth. For in that day a man shall cast away his golden and silver abominations, which they made that they might worship vanities and bats; to go into the caverns of the solid rock, and into the clefts of the rocks, from before the face of the dread of the Lord, and from the glory of His might, when He arises to crush the earth.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 14, Tone 2:*
Lord, who shall abide in Thy dwelling-place?
*Verse:* He that walks without blemish, and works righteousness.

## Thursday of the First Week of the Holy Fast at Vespers[^333]

*At the Lucernarium, the usual stichology of the Psalter.*

*On "Lord, I have cried", we sing prosomoia from the Triodion.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Our Enlightener" (Просвѣти́телѧ на́шего): by Joseph:*
Illumine me who am darkened by the deceits of hostility, O my Christ, Who hanging upon the Cross didst once darken the sun, and didst visibly shed upon the faithful the true light of forgiveness; that walking in the light of Thy commandments, I may purely reach the saving dawn of Thy Resurrection!

*Podoben:*
As a Vine hanging upon the Tree, O Savior, Thou didst give the ends of the earth to drink the wine of incorruption, O Christ; therefore I cry: having given drink through the sweetness of true compunction unto me who am ever grievously darkened by sinful drunkenness, strengthen me now to fast from pleasures, O Savior, as God and Lover of mankind!

*Another prosomoion by Lord Theodore, in the same Tone, Podoben: "O the great mystery" (Ѡ҆ вели́кагѡ та́инства):*
O the power of Thy Cross! It blossomed forth abstinence for the Church, having torn up by the roots Adam's incontinence of old in Eden: for that brought death unto men, but this in incorruption pours forth immortality unto the world, as from another fountain of paradise, through the outpouring of Thy life-flowing Blood together with the water, whence all things were given life; through which sweeten for us our fasting nourishment, O God of Israel, Who hast great mercy!

*And 3 stichera from the Menaion. Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 15, Tone 4:*
I will bless the Lord Who hath given me understanding.
*Verse:* Keep me, O Lord, for in Thee have I hoped.

*Reading from Genesis (2:4–9):*
This is the book of the origin of heaven and earth, when it came to be, in the day that the Lord God made heaven and earth. And every plant of the field before it was upon the earth, and every herb of the field before it sprang up: for the Lord God had not rained upon the earth, and there was not a man to till it. But a fountain went up out of the earth, and watered the whole face of the earth. And God formed man of dust (taken) from the earth, and breathed into his nostrils the breath of life; and man became a living soul. And the Lord God planted a garden in Eden in the east, and put there the man whom He had formed. And God made to spring up further from the earth every tree that is beautiful to the sight and good for food: and the tree of life in the midst of the garden, and the tree of knowing the distinction of good...

...and evil. And a river went out of Eden to water the garden; from thence it was parted into four heads. The name of the one is Pishon: that is it which compasses the whole land of Havilah, where there is gold; and the gold of that land is good: there also is carbuncle and the emerald stone. And the name of the second river is Gihon: the same is it that compasses the whole land of Ethiopia. And the third river is the Tigris: this is it which goes toward the front of Assyria; and the fourth river is the Euphrates. And the Lord God took the man whom He had formed, and put him into the garden of delight to till it and to keep it. And the Lord God commanded Adam, saying: Of every tree which is in the garden thou mayest freely eat; but of the tree of the knowledge of good and evil, ye shall not eat of it; for in the day that ye eat thereof ye shall surely die. And the Lord God said: It is not good that the man should be alone; let Us make him a helper fit for him. And God formed yet out of the ground all the beasts of the field, and all the birds of the air, and brought them unto Adam to see what he would call them; and whatsoever Adam called each living soul, that was the name thereof.[^334]

*Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 16, Tone 4:*
Keep me, O Lord, as the apple of Thine eye.
*Verse:* Hear my righteousness, O Lord.

*Reading from Proverbs (3:1–18):*
My son, forget not my laws, but let thine heart keep my words: for length of days, and years of life, and peace shall they add unto thee. Let not alms and faithfulness forsake thee, but bind them about thy neck, and write them upon the table of thine heart, and thou shalt find favor; and provide good things in the sight of the Lord (God) and of men. Trust in God with all thine heart, and be not exalted in thine own wisdom; in all thy ways acknowledge Her, that She may make straight thy paths. Be not wise in thine own eyes, but fear God, and depart from every evil: then shall there be healing unto thy body, and cure unto thy bones. Honor the Lord from thy righteous labors, and give Him firstfruits from thy fruits of righteousness: so shall thy barns be filled with plenty of wheat, and thy presses shall overflow with wine. My son, despise not the chastening of the Lord, neither faint when thou art reproved of Him: for whom the Lord loves He chastens, and scourges every son whom He receives. Blessed is the man that finds wisdom, and the mortal that gets understanding. For to purchase her is better than the treasures of gold and silver. She is more precious than costly stones; nothing evil resists her: she is well known unto all that draw near unto her, and all that is precious is not worthy of her. For length of days and years of life are in her right hand, and in her left hand are riches and glory; out of her mouth proceeds righteousness, and law and mercy she bears upon her tongue. Her ways are good ways, and all her paths are peaceful. She is a tree of life unto all that lay hold upon her, and leaning upon her as upon the Lord, she is steadfast.

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 1:*
Desiring to partake of the divine Pascha, not that which comes from Egypt, but that which comes forth from Zion, let us put away the leaven of sin through repentance; let us gird our loins with the mortification of pleasures; let us adorn our feet with sandals that protect from every evil work, and let us be strengthened by the staff of faith; not emulating the enemies of the Cross who make a god of their belly, but following through fasting our Savior Who showed us victory over the devil, the Savior of our souls.

*Martyrikon:*
Glorified in the memorials of Thy saints, O Christ God, being entreated by them, send down upon us great mercy!

*Glory, and now and ever, Cross-Theotokion, the same Tone [Tone 1], Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
When she beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, Thine ewe-lamb and Mother marveled, and said: What is this sight, O most desired Son? This do the unfaithful and lawless assembly repay Thee, which enjoyed Thy many wonders; but glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*And the rest of the office according to custom.*

## Thursday of the First Week of the Holy Fast at Compline[^335]
