*We sing the present Great Canon of our holy father Andrew of Crete and Jerusalem.*
*And at each troparion we make 3 prostrations, saying also the verse:*
**"Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me."**

*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos, twice: "A Helper and a Protector":*

*Troparion:*
O Lamb of God, Who takest away the sins of all, take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art compassionate, grant me tears of compunction.

*Troparion:*
Unto Thee I fall down, O Jesus: I have sinned against Thee, cleanse me! Take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art compassionate, grant me tears of compunction.

*Troparion:*
Enter not into judgment with me, bringing forth my deeds, inquiring into my words, and setting right mine impulses; but in Thy compassions looking past my terrible evils, save me, O Almighty One!

*Troparion:*
It is the time of repentance: I draw near unto Thee, my Creator! Take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art compassionate, grant me tears of compunction.

*Troparion:*
The wealth of my soul have I squandered in sin; I am empty of pious virtues, and hungering I cry: O Giver of mercy, Lord, save me!

*To St. Mary:*
Incline unto the divine laws of Christ, O saint, unto Him hast thou approached, having abandoned the unrestrained impulses of pleasures, and every virtue, most piously, hast thou fulfilled as one.

*Glory, Trinity:*
Super-essential Trinity, worshipped in Unity, take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as Thou art compassionate, grant me tears of compunction.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, hope and protection of those who sing of thee, take from me the heavy burden of sin, and as pure Lady, receive me repenting.

*Ode 2, Tone 6. Heirmos: "See, see, that I am God, Who rained manna, and poured forth water from the rock of old in the wilderness unto My people, by My right hand alone and by My power":*[^336]

*Troparion:*
I have slain a man, he says, to my wounding, and a young man to my hurt: Lamech wailing cried out. But thou tremblest not, O my soul, having defiled the flesh and polluted the mind!

*Troparion:*
A tower didst thou devise to build, O soul, and to erect a bulwark by thy lusts, had not the Creator held back thy counsels and cast down thy contrivances to the earth.

*Troparion:*
O how have I rivaled Lamech, the former murderer, having slain the soul as a man, the mind as a young man, and my body as my brother, like the murderer Cain, by lustful impulses!

*Troparion:*
The Lord rained fire from the Lord of old, burning up the Sodomites for their provoking iniquity; but thou hast kindled the fire of Gehenna, in which, O soul, thou art about to be burned!

*Troparion:*
I have been wounded, I have been pierced! Behold the arrows of the enemy which have wounded my soul and body! Behold the festering sores, the blackenings cry aloud, the wounds of my self-willed passions!

*To St. Mary:*
Thou didst stretch forth thy hands unto the compassionate God, O Mary, when sinking in the abyss of evils; and as unto Peter He lovingly stretched forth His divine hand, seeking in every way thy conversion.

*Glory, Trinity:*
Unoriginate, uncreated Trinity, undivided Unity, receive me repenting, save me who have sinned: I am Thy creation, despise me not, but spare me, and deliver me from the fiery condemnation!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
All-pure Lady, Mother of God, hope of those who flee unto thee and haven of those tossed by the tempest, with thy Creator and thy Son make the Compassionate One merciful also unto me by thy prayers!

*Ode 3, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Establish, O Lord, upon the rock":*

*Troparion:*
Thou hast rivaled Hagar of old, O soul, the Egyptian, having enslaved thy will and brought forth a new Ishmael, insolence!

*Troparion:*
Jacob's ladder didst thou understand, O my soul, shown from earth to the heavens: why hadst thou not a firm ascent of piety?

*Troparion:*
The Priest of God and solitary King, Christ's likeness in the world of life among men, do thou imitate!

*Troparion:*
Turn back, groan, O wretched soul, before the triumph of life receives its end, before the Lord closes the door of the bridal chamber!

*Troparion:*
Be not a pillar of salt, O soul, turning back! Let the example of Sodom terrify thee; high up in Zoar seek salvation!

*Troparion:*
The supplications of those who sing of Thee, O Master, reject not; but have compassion, O Lover of mankind, and grant forgiveness unto those who ask in faith.

*Glory, Trinity:*
Simple, uncreated Trinity, unoriginate Nature praised in Trinity of Hypostases, save us who worship Thy dominion in faith!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Timelessly from the Father a Son, in time, O Mother of God, without knowledge of man didst thou bear: a strange marvel, remaining a Virgin while nursing!

*Ode 4, Tone 6. Heirmos: "The Prophet heard":*[^337]

*Troparion:*
The time of my life is short and full of pains and wickedness; but receive me in repentance and call me unto understanding, that I may not become the possession nor the food of the stranger: O Savior, do Thou Thyself have compassion on me!

*Troparion:*
Arrayed in royal dignity, with crown and purple, a man of great possessions and righteous, abounding in wealth and herds, suddenly, being impoverished, was deprived of wealth and the glory of the kingdom.

*Troparion:*
If he was righteous and blameless above all, yet did not escape the hunter's snare and net, what wilt thou do, O wretched soul that lovest sin, if any unforeseen thing shall happen to befall thee?

*Troparion:*
Now am I boastful of speech and hard of heart, vainly and in vain: condemn me not with the Pharisee, but rather grant me the humility of the Publican, O only Compassionate and Righteous Judge, and number me with him!

*Troparion:*
I have sinned, I have dishonored the vessel of my flesh, I know it, O Compassionate One; but receive me in repentance and call me unto understanding, that I may not become the possession nor the food of the stranger: O Savior, do Thou Thyself have compassion on me!

*Troparion:*
I have made an idol of myself by passions, harming my soul, O Compassionate One; but receive me in repentance and call me unto understanding, that I may not become the possession nor the food of the stranger: O Savior, do Thou Thyself have compassion on me!

*Troparion:*
I gave not ear unto Thy voice, I disobeyed Thy scripture of the Lawgiver; but receive me in repentance and call me unto understanding, that I may not become the possession nor the food of the stranger: O Savior, do Thou Thyself have compassion on me!

*To St. Mary:*
Having been brought down into the depth of great transgressions, thou wast unrestrained; but thou didst ascend by better reasoning unto the highest virtue manifestly by deeds, most gloriously, having astonished the angelic nature, O Mary!

*Glory, Trinity:*
Undivided in Essence, unconfused in Persons, I theologize Thee, the Trinitarian one Godhead, as co-essential and co-enthroned: I cry unto Thee the great hymn, thrice-chanted on high!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Thou both givest birth and remainest a virgin, and abidest in both natures a Virgin: He Who is born renews the laws of nature, and the womb that bears not gives birth. Where God wills, the order of nature is overcome; for He does whatsoever He wills.

*Ode 5, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Watching early from the night":*[^338]

*Troparion:*
Imitate the woman bowed down, O soul! Come, fall down at the feet of Jesus, that He may straighten thee, and that thou mayest walk uprightly in the paths of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Though Thou art a deep well, O Master, pour forth water unto me from Thy pure veins, that like the Samaritan woman, no longer drinking I may thirst; for Thou pourest forth streams of life!

*Troparion:*
May my tears be Siloam unto me, O Master and Lord, that I also may wash the pupils of my heart, and behold Thee spiritually, the Pre-eternal Light!

*To St. Mary:*
With matchless desire, O all-wealthy one, longing to venerate the Life-giving Tree, thou wast granted thy desire: grant then that I also may attain heavenly glory!

*Glory, Trinity:*
Thee, O Trinity, do we glorify, the one God: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, Father, Son, and Spirit, simple Essence, Unity ever worshipped!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
From thee did God Who created the ages clothe Himself in my clay, O incorrupt and unwedded Virgin Mother, and united unto Himself human nature.

*Ode 6, Tone 6. Heirmos: "I cried with my whole heart":*

*Troparion:*
I am that royal drachma which of old Thou didst lose, O Savior; but having kindled the lamp, Thy Forerunner, O Word, seek and find Thine image!

*Troparion:*
Arise and wage war, as Joshua against Amalek, against the passions of the flesh, and the Gibeonites, deceptive thoughts, ever conquering!

*To St. Mary:*
That thou mightest quench the flame of passions, thou didst ever pour forth drops of tears, O Mary, burning in soul: the grace of these grant also unto me thy servant!

*To St. Mary:*
Heavenly passionlessness didst thou acquire by an exalted life on earth, O mother; therefore pray that those who sing of thee may be delivered from passions by thy prayers.

*Glory, Trinity:*
I am the Trinity, simple, undivided, divided in Persons, and I am the Unity united by Nature, saith the Father, and the Son, and the divine Spirit.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Thy womb bare God for us, formed like unto us: Him, as Creator of all, do thou implore, O Mother of God, that by thy prayers we may be justified.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*
My soul, my soul, arise! Why sleepest thou? The end draws near, and thou art about to be troubled: awake therefore, that Christ God may spare thee, Who is everywhere present and fillest all things!

*Ode 7, Tone 6. Heirmos: "We have sinned":*[^339]

*Troparion:*
My days have vanished as a dream of one awaking: therefore like Hezekiah I weep upon my bed that years of life may be added unto me; but what Isaiah shall stand before thee, O soul, if not the God of all?

*Troparion:*
I fall down before Thee, and I offer unto Thee my words as tears: I have sinned as the harlot never sinned, and I have transgressed as no other upon earth; but have compassion, O Master, upon Thy creation, and call me back!

*Troparion:*
I have buried Thine image and squandered Thy commandments; all my beauty hath been darkened, and by passions, O Savior, the lamp is extinguished: but having compassion, restore unto me, as David sings, rejoicing!

*Troparion:*
Turn back, repent, uncover hidden things, speak unto God Who knows all things: Thou knowest my secrets, O only Savior; but do Thou Thyself have mercy on me, as David sings, according to Thy mercy!

*To St. Mary:*
Having cried aloud unto the all-pure Mother of God, thou didst first drive away the rage of passions that violently afflicted thee, and didst put to shame the enemy who tripped thee up: but grant now help from affliction unto me also, thy servant.

*To St. Mary:*
Whom thou didst love, Whom thou didst desire, for Whose sake thou didst wear out thy flesh, O venerable one, pray now unto Christ for thy servants: that being merciful unto us all, He may grant a peaceful estate unto those who honor Him.

*Glory, Trinity:*
O Trinity, simple, undivided, co-essential, and one Nature, Lights and Light, and three Holies, and one Holy is God the Trinity chanted: but sing, glorify, life and lives, O soul, the God of all!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
We hymn thee, we bless thee, we worship thee, O Mother of God, for from the inseparable Trinity thou didst bear the One Christ God, and didst thyself open the heavenly realms unto us who are upon the earth.

*Ode 8, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Him Whom the hosts":*

*Troparion:*
Pouring out upon Thy head the vessel of tears as myrrh, O Savior, I cry unto Thee as the harlot who sought mercy: supplication I bring, and forgiveness I ask to receive.

*Troparion:*
Though no one hath sinned against Thee as have I, yet receive even me, O compassionate Savior, repenting with fear and calling with love: I have sinned against Thee alone, have mercy on me, O Merciful One!

*Troparion:*
Spare, O Savior, Thy creation, and seek as a shepherd that which was lost; anticipate him that is gone astray, snatch me from the wolf, make me a sheep in the pasture of Thy sheep!

*Troparion:*
When as Judge Thou shalt sit, as Compassionate, and show Thy dread glory, O Savior: O what fear shall be then, when the furnace is burning, unto all who dread Thine intolerable tribunal!

*To St. Mary:*
The Mother of the never-setting Light, having enlightened thee, loosed thee from the darkening of passions: therefore, having entered into spiritual grace, enlighten, O Mary, those who praise thee in faith.

*To St. Mary:*
Seeing a new wonder, the divine Zosimas was astonished in thee, O truly-venerable mother: for he beheld an angel in the flesh, and was filled with all astonishment, praising Christ unto the ages.

*Glory, Trinity:*
Unoriginate Father, co-unoriginate Son, good Comforter, upright Spirit: Begetter of the Word of God, Word of the unoriginate Father, Spirit living and creating, Trinity, Unity, have mercy on me!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
As from the dipping of purple dye, O All-Pure One, the spiritual purple robe of Emmanuel, His flesh, was woven within thy womb; therefore as truly the Mother of God do we honor thee.

*Ode 9, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Incorruptible Conception":*[^340]

*Troparion:*
Have compassion, save me, O Son of David, have mercy, Who didst heal the demoniacs by a word; and utter unto me the voice of compassion, as unto the thief: Amen, I say unto thee, with Me shalt thou be in paradise when I come in My glory!

*Troparion:*
A thief reviled Thee, a thief confessed Thee as God; for both were hanging upon the cross: but, O Compassionate One, as unto Thy faithful thief who acknowledged Thee as God, open unto me also the door of Thy glorious Kingdom!

*Troparion:*
Creation shuddered seeing Thee crucified; the mountains and rocks broke apart with fear, and the earth shook, and Hades was stripped bare, and the light was darkened in the daytime, beholding Thee, O Jesus, nailed unto the Cross.

*Troparion:*
Exacting not from me worthy fruits of repentance: for my strength hath failed within me; grant me ever a contrite heart and spiritual poverty, that I may offer these unto Thee as an acceptable sacrifice, O only Savior!

*Troparion:*
My Judge and my Knower, Who art about to come again with the angels to judge all the world: with Thine eye of mercy, seeing me then, spare and have compassion on me, O Jesus, who have sinned beyond all human nature!

*To St. Mary:*
Thou didst astonish all by thy strange life, both the ranks of angels and the assemblies of men, having lived immaterially and transcended nature, whereby, as with immaterial feet, having stepped forth, O Mary, thou didst cross the Jordan.

*To St. Mary:*
Make the Creator merciful unto those who praise thee, O venerable mother, to be delivered from mistreatments and afflictions that assail us round about: that being delivered from trials, we may unceasingly magnify the Lord Who hath glorified thee, O saint.

*To St. Andrew:*
O Andrew venerable and thrice-blessed father, pastor of Crete, cease not praying for those who praise thee: that we all may be delivered from wrath, affliction, corruption, and boundless transgressions, who faithfully honor thy memory.

*Glory, Trinity:*
Let us glorify the Father, let us exalt the Son, let us faithfully worship the divine Spirit, the undivided Trinity, Unity according to Essence, as Light and Lights, and Life and Lives, giving life and enlightening the ends of the world.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Preserve thy city, O all-pure Mother of God: for in thee it faithfully reigns, in thee also it is established, and through thee conquering, it vanquishes every temptation, leads captive the warriors, and exercises obedience.

*Then both choirs sing: "Incorruptible Conception":*
*And the rest of the office of Compline.*

## Friday of the First Week of the Holy Fast at Matins[^341]
*Trinity Hymns.*
*After the first stichology, we sing the Sessional Hymns of the Cross, Idiomela, and Theotokion.*
*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn, by Lord Joseph, Tone 2, Podoben: "The noble Joseph" (Благоѡбра́зный І҆ѡ́сифъ):*
Granting passionlessness unto all by Thy holy Sufferings, O Lover of mankind, having mortified the passions of my flesh by Thy divine Cross, vouchsafe me to behold also Thy holy Resurrection, O Lord.
*Glory, the same. Both now and ever, Cross-Theotokion:*
Preserved by the precious Cross of Thy Son, O pure Lady Mother of God, we all easily overcome every assault of the adversary: therefore as is meet we ever bless thee, as the Mother of God and the only hope of our souls.

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn, by Lord Theodore, Tone 2, Podoben: "Being the wellspring of loving-kindness" (Мѵ́рꙋ сꙋ́щи):*[^342]
Being the Fountain of purity, preserve us through fasting, O Merciful One; look down upon us who fall down before Thee; give ear unto the lifting up of our hands, Thou Who didst stretch out Thy palms upon the Tree, being crucified for all earthborn, O only Lord of bodiless hosts.
*Glory, the same. Both now and ever, Cross-Theotokion, Podoben:*
The Virgin and Thy Mother, O Christ, seeing Thee stretched out dead upon the Tree, weeping bitterly, said: My Son, what is this dread mystery? Granting eternal life unto all, how dost Thou die of Thine own will upon the Cross by a shameful death?

*Canon in the Menaion to 6, and the Triodia according to their order. We recite the fifth ode as usual.*

*Ode 5, Tone 2. Heirmos: "Enlightenment of those who lie in darkness":*[^343]

*Troparion:*
Beholding sin within me, the crafty one hastens and works with sin: he rejoices truly over my destruction; but do Thou grant me amendment, O Savior, as a refuge unto what is good, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
O Thou Who didst conquer the principalities of darkness upon Thy Cross, deliver me from their evil deeds, who have fallen into the depth of sin and into the pit of unlawful deeds, yet trust to be saved by Thy mercy.

*Troparion:*
O Thou Who wast put to death, stretched out upon the Cross, quicken my soul which is mortified by sin, and vouchsafe me in peace to reach Thy precious Resurrection, O Christ, zealously working Thy statutes.

*Theotokion:*
Enlightenment of my weakness, salvation of my darkened soul, O Pure One, save me, save me who am perishing, O Maiden, and clothe me in the garment of incorruption, who have corrupted myself with terrible transgressions!

*Another [canon], the same Tone. Heirmos: "The night having passed":*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast well pleased to be crucified in the flesh at the place of the skull, O only Immortal One, making the human race immortal and reforming it, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Seeing the insult of Thy Passion, all creation was transformed, O Lord, weeping over the Jewish murder; but Thou didst endure it, to save all things.

*Glory, Trinity:*
O all-holy Trinity! Thou art our worship, Thou art our refuge and dominion: unto those who praise Thee in one Nature, send down the cleansing of transgressions.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
O Maiden of many names, rejoice, Mother of God: Ark, and Jar of Manna, and Table, light-bearing Lampstand, bush unburned by fire, overshadowed Mountain of God!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
*Troparion:*
Having bidden the world and those in the world to rejoice, now being crucified with Christ, let us endure insult, mockery, and the rest, that with Him we may be glorified.

*Heirmos:*
The night having passed, the day hath drawn near, and the light hath shone upon the world: therefore the ranks of angels praise Thee, and all things glorify Thee.

*Ode 8, Tone 2. Heirmos: "Into the fiery furnace":*

*Troparion:*
Having humbled Thyself, Thou wast lifted up upon the Cross out of compassion, lifting up him who fell of old through eating of the tree: therefore, O only All-Good One, we praise Thee glorified unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Through the slumber of inattention I have succumbed to the heavy sleep of sin: but do Thou Who didst fall asleep upon the Cross for my sake, O my Christ, raise me up who have fallen, lest the night of death overtake me.

*Troparion:*
I have been blinded by pleasures, I night-wander with a darkened soul, and the crafty enemy laughs seeing me: therefore enlighten me, and deliver me from his malice, O Christ, unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*
In slothfulness have I spent my life, through the slumber of sin have I weighed down my soul: but I flee unto thy heavenly prayer; suffer me not, O all-pure Virgin, to fall asleep in death.

*Another [canon]. Heirmos: "In the bush to Moses":*

*Troparion:*
Crowned with thorns and robed in a purple garment, Thou didst appear most fair, O Christ, in beauty beyond all the sons of men, shining with glory.

*Troparion:*
Drinking gall and vinegar, Thou pourest forth two streams, of life and incorruption, from Thy divine side, unto those who sing of Thee in faith and glorify Thee, O Christ, unto all ages.

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
*Trinity:*
One Godhead, Trinity, indivisible Nature, but divided in Persons, imperishable Dominion: Father, Son, and Spirit, we hymn Thee unto all ages.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
O pure Mother of God, heavenly Door, saving Gate, receive the supplication of all Christians who bless thee unto all ages.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
*Troparion:*
O Cross, scepter of Christ, horn of the Church, victory of kings, keeper of Christians: thou art my enlightenment, thou art also my boast unto all ages.

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord:*
*Heirmos:*
In the bush to Moses on Mount Sinai, Him Who of old prefigured the marvel of the Virgin, sing, bless, and highly exalt unto all ages!

*Ode 9, Tone 2. Heirmos: "He Who before the sun":*[^344]

*Troparion:*
Cleanse my soul, leprous with unlawful thoughts, by the sprinkling of Thy blood, O Word of God, and make me a partaker of Thy glory, O Christ, Who for my sake didst endure the inglorious crucifixion.

*Troparion:*
I am wholly enfeebled by the weight of unlawful deeds, O Christ, and thence I cry unto Thee, O Lover of mankind: heal the incurable wounds of my soul by Thy precious Blood, that I may sing of Thy Godhead.

*Troparion:*
Making malice my food and negligence my drink, I remain in all things uncorrected, while rejoicing unseasonably over abstaining from food: for the Lord hath said that fasting is not to be such.

*Theotokion:*
O destroyer of the fierce ancient decree, and correction of the first mother, cause of our race being united unto God, bridge unto the Creator: thee, O Mother of God, do we magnify.

*Another [canon]. Heirmos: "Pure and all-immaculate":*

*Troparion:*
When Thou wast crucified upon the Tree, creation trembled; being God, Thou didst suffer in the flesh out of the compassion of Thy mercies, that Thou mightest save us.

*Troparion:*
Great is the power of Thy Cross, O Lord! For when we form its figure upon ourselves, immediately we strike down the might of demons.

*Glory, Trinity:*
O Tri-hypostatic Unity, simple Trinity, Sovereign Principle, equally-glorified Nature, Father, Son, and divine Spirit: save us all!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mother of God, propitiation for the world, unto whom all we sinners flee, ever finding reconciliation with God.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*
*Troparion:*
By the divine might of Thy Cross, O Lord, strengthen me to offer unto Thee the time of the fast blameless and pure.

*Heirmos:*
Pure and all-immaculate Mother and Virgin, with songs of songs, all we faithful piously magnify thee as the Mother of God.

*Exaposteilarion of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 8:*
Let us joyfully receive the bequest of the fast; for had our forefather kept this, we would not have suffered the fall from Eden: fair to look upon and good for food was the fruit that slew me. Let us not be seduced with our eyes, neither let our throat be delighted by esteemed delicacies which after ingestion become dishonorable. Let us flee intemperance, and when satisfied let us not yield to passions; let us sign ourselves with the blood of Him Who was led willingly to death for our sake, and the destroyer will not touch us, and we shall eat the most sacred Pascha of Christ, unto the salvation of our souls.

*Martyrikon:*
What shall we call you, O saints? Cherubim? For Christ rested upon you. Seraphim? For you unceasingly glorified Him. Angels? For you turned away from the body. Powers? For you work miracles. Many are your names, and greater are your spiritual gifts: pray that our souls be saved!

*Glory, and both now and ever, Cross-Theotokion, the same Tone [Tone 8], Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
When all creation saw Thee, O Savior, being crucified upon the place of the skull, it was wounded and shuddered, trembling unable to endure it. But the pure Virgin and Thy Mother, weeping, cried out unto Thee: Woe is me, O my Child, my most sweet Savior! What is this new and marvelous and strange sight?

*It is good to give praise to the Lord: and the rest of the order.*
*The First Hour is without stichology; likewise also on the other Fridays of the Holy and Great Forty Days.*[^345]

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion, Tone 5:*
Prevent us with Thy compassions, O Lord, for our life is full of sins and our disposition is unrepentant; for we know no other beside Thee Who rulest over life and death: save us, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Prokeimenon,* Psalm 17, Tone 7:*
I will love Thee, O Lord, my strength: the Lord is my foundation.
*Verse:* My God is my helper.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (3:1–14):*[^346]
Behold, the Sovereign Lord of Sabaoth will take away from Jerusalem and from Judea the mighty man and the mighty woman, the strength of bread and the strength of water, the giant, the mighty man, the warrior, the judge, the prophet, the diviner, the elder, the captain of fifty, the admirable counselor, the skillful architect, and the intelligent listener. And I will make youths their rulers, and mockers shall rule over them. And the people will fall upon one another, man upon man, and man upon his neighbor; the child will strike against the old man, and the dishonorable against the honorable. For a man will lay hold of his brother or of the kinsman of his father, saying: Thou hast a garment, be thou our leader, and let my food be under thee. And he will answer and say in that day: I will not be thy leader, for in my house there is neither bread nor clothing: I will not be the leader of this people. For Jerusalem is forsaken, and Judea is fallen, and their tongue with lawlessness does not submit unto the Lord. Therefore now their glory is brought low, and the shame of their face has stood up against them: and they have proclaimed their sin as Sodom, and manifested it. Woe unto their soul, because they have devised an evil counsel against themselves, saying: Let us bind the righteous man, for he is cumbersome unto us; therefore shall they eat the fruits of their deeds. Woe unto the lawless! For evils will befall him according to the works of his hands. O My people, your exactors glean you, and extortioners rule over you: O My people, they that call you blessed deceive you, and disturb the paths of your feet. But now the Lord stands up for judgment, and He will bring His people to judgment: the Lord Himself will come to judgment with the elders of the people and with their princes.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 18, Tone 7:*
O Lord, my Helper and my Redeemer.
*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God.

*Let it be known that the office of the Menaion of the saint occurring on Saturday and on Sunday is sung at Compline, or whenever the ecclesiarch wills.*

## Friday of the First Week of the Holy Fast at Vespers[^347]
*At the Lamp-lighting Psalms, after the customary stichology, on "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and we sing stichera: the Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 5:*
Come, ye faithful, let us work the works of God in the light, as in the day let us walk honestly; let us take away from ourselves every unjust writing against our neighbor, not placing a stumbling block before him unto scandal;

...scandal; let us abandon the voluptuousness of the flesh, let us increase the gifts of the soul, let us give bread to the needy, and let us draw near unto Christ crying out in repentance: Our God, have mercy on us!

*Then we sing also four Martyrika of the Oktoechos, of the occurring Tone.*
*Then four Idiomela of Saint Theodore, Tone 2:*[^348]

*Sticheron 1:*
Come, all ye lovers of martyrs, let us spiritually rejoice and keep festival! For today the Martyr Theodore sets forth a mystical table, gladdening us who keep festival, that we may cry unto him: Rejoice, invincible passion-bearer, who didst overthrow the threats of tyrants upon earth! Rejoice, thou who didst surrender thy frail body unto tortures for Christ God! Rejoice, thou who in diverse perils wast shown to be a proven warrior of the heavenly army! Therefore we beseech thee, adornment of martyrs: pray for our souls!

*Sticheron 2:*
The God-given grace of thy miracles, O Martyr Theodore, thou extendest unto all who flee unto thee in faith, through which we praise thee, saying: Thou deliverest captives, thou healest the sick, thou enrichest the poor, and savest those who weep; thou checkest the flight of fugitive servants, and causest the discovery of what was stolen by thieves, O passion-bearer; thou instructest that stolen horses be found; thou graciously grantest the petitions of infants; thou art found to be a fervent protector unto those who celebrate thy sacred memory: with whom do thou also ask great mercy from Christ for us, most sacred champion, who sing of thy martyrdom!

*Sticheron 3:*
A supreme gift of God hast thou been shown to be, O Martyr Theodore; for even after thy demise, as one alive thou grantest petitions unto those who flee unto thee. Therefore, when a woman's son had once been snatched away, captured by soldiers of an alien faith, the widow stood weeping and drenched thy temple with tears; and thou, compassionate as thou art, arriving upon a white horse, didst present this youth unharmed. And thereafter, and along with this, working miracles, thou hast not ceased: but pray unto Christ God that our souls be saved!

*Sticheron 4:*
Namesake of divine gifts, I honor thee, thrice-blessed Theodore! For shown to be an unwaning luminary of divine light, thou didst illumine all creation by thy sufferings; and shown to be stronger than fire, thou didst quench the flame and crush the head of the deceitful serpent. Therefore, bowing down over thy sufferings, Christ crowned thy divine head, O great-martyr and passion-bearer: as thou hast boldness unto God, pray earnestly for our souls!

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Using the apostate tyrant as his vessel, the enemy plotted with fierce malice to defile with abominable foods from impure sacrifices the pious people who were purifying themselves through fasting. But by a wiser plan thou didst destroy his foolish intrigue: thou didst appear in a dream to the archbishop of that time, revealing the depth of thy discernment and exposing the unlawful device. Therefore, offering thanksgiving unto thee, we depict the Savior and make annual memorial of the miracle that was wrought; and we pray furthermore, that we may be preserved unharmed from the devices of the evil one, and saved unto God through thine intercessions, O Martyr Theodore!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion of the Tone.*
*Entrance without Gospel. "O Gladsome Light":*
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 19, Tone 5:*
May the Lord hear Thee in the day of affliction.
*Verse:* May the Name of the God of Jacob protect Thee.

*Reading from Genesis (2:20–3:20):*[^349]
Adam gave names to all the cattle, and to all the birds of the air, and to all the beasts of the earth; but for Adam there was not found a helper fit for him. And God brought a trance upon Adam, and he fell asleep; and He took one of his ribs, and filled up flesh in its place. And the Lord God built the rib which He had taken from Adam into a woman, and brought her unto Adam. And Adam said: This now is bone of my bones, and flesh of my flesh; she shall be called Woman, because she was taken out of her Man. Therefore shall a man leave his father and his mother, and shall cleave unto his wife, and they two shall be one flesh. And they were both naked, Adam and his wife, and they were not ashamed.
Now the serpent was wiser than all the beasts which were upon the earth, which the Lord God had made. And the serpent said unto the woman: Why is it that God said: Ye shall not eat of every tree of paradise? And the woman said unto the serpent: Of every tree of paradise we may eat; but of the fruit of the tree which is in the midst of paradise, God said: Ye shall not eat of it, neither shall ye touch it, lest ye die. And the serpent said unto the woman: Ye shall not surely die. For God knew that in whatsoever day ye eat thereof, your eyes will be opened, and ye will be as gods, knowing good and evil. And the woman saw that the tree was good for food, and that it was pleasant to the eyes to behold, and fair to look upon to gain understanding; and taking of its fruit, she ate, and gave also unto her husband with her, and they ate. And the eyes of both were opened, and they knew that they were naked; and they sewed fig leaves together, and made themselves aprons. And they heard the voice of the Lord God walking in paradise in the afternoon; and Adam and his wife hid themselves from the presence of the Lord God in the midst of the tree of paradise. And the Lord God called Adam, and said unto him: Adam, where art thou? And he said unto Him: I heard the voice of Thee walking in paradise, and I was afraid, because I am naked, and I hid myself. And God said unto him: Who told thee that thou art naked, unless thou hast eaten of the tree whereof I commanded thee, of this alone not to eat? And Adam said: The woman whom Thou gavest to be with me, she gave me of the tree, and I ate. And the Lord God said unto the woman: What is this that thou hast done? And the woman said: The serpent deceived me, and I ate. And the Lord God said unto the serpent: Because thou hast done this, cursed art thou above all cattle, and above all the beasts of the earth; upon thy breast and thy belly shalt thou go, and earth shalt thou eat all the days of thy life. And I will put enmity between thee and between the woman, and between thy seed and between her seed: He shall watch against thy head, and thou shalt watch against His heel. And unto the woman He said: Multiplying I will multiply thy sorrows and thy sighings; in pain shalt thou bring forth children, and thy desire shall be unto thy husband, and he shall rule over thee. And unto Adam He said: Because thou didst listen to the voice of thy wife, and hast eaten of the tree whereof I commanded thee: Of this alone thou shalt not eat, yet hast eaten of it: cursed is the earth in thy works; in sorrows shalt thou eat of it all the days of thy life. Thorns and thistles shall it bring forth unto thee, and thou shalt eat the herb of the field. In the sweat of thy face shalt thou eat thy bread, until thou return unto the earth from which thou wast taken: for earth thou art, and unto earth shalt thou go. And Adam called the name of his wife Life [Eve], because she is the mother of all living.

*Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 20, Tone 7:*
Be Thou exalted, O Lord, in Thy strength: we will sing and hymn Thy powers.
*Verse:* O Lord, in Thy strength shall the king rejoice.

*Reading from Proverbs (3:19–34):*[^350]
God by wisdom founded the earth, and prepared the heavens by understanding. By His knowledge the depths were broken up, and the clouds dropped down dew. My son, let them not pass by thee, but keep my counsel and thought: that thy soul may live, and grace may be upon thy neck: (and there shall be healing unto thy flesh, and care unto thy bones:) that thou mayest walk relying in peace in all thy ways, and thy foot shall not stumble. For if thou sit down, thou shalt be without fear; and if thou sleep, thou shalt sleep sweetly: and thou shalt not be afraid of terror coming upon thee, nor of the rushing attack of the ungodly coming upon thee; for the Lord shall be in all thy ways, and shall establish thy foot that thou be not shaken. Withhold not to do good unto the needy, when thine hand hath power to help. Say not: Go away, and return, and tomorrow I will give, when thou art able to do good; for thou knowest not what the coming day will bring forth. Devise not evil against thy friend, who dwells near thee and trusts in thee. Be not at enmity with a man in vain, lest he work some evil against thee. Acquire not the reproaches of evil men, neither be envious of all their ways; for every transgressor of the law is unclean before the Lord, and he associates not with the righteous. The curse of the Lord is in the houses of the ungodly, but the dwellings of the righteous are blessed. The Lord resists the proud, but gives grace unto the humble.

*Then: "Let my prayer be set forth."*
*And the rest of the order of the Presanctified.*

*And after the Prayer behind the Ambo, we sing the present Supplicatory Canon of Saint Theodore.*[^351]
*And setting forth the kolyvo, we say Psalm 142:*
**"O Lord, hear my prayer."** *Then:* **"God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us."**

*And immediately the Troparion, Tone 2:*
Great are the accomplishments of faith! In the fountain of flame, as upon the water of rest, the holy Martyr Theodore rejoiced; for having been consumed by fire as sweet bread, he was offered unto the Trinity. By his supplications, O Christ God, save our souls!

*Glory, the same. Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
All beyond comprehension, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Mother of God: sealed in purity and preserved in virginity, thou wast acknowledged to be a true Mother, having given birth unto the true God: Him do thou entreat, that our souls be saved!

*And immediately Psalm 50. Then the present canon.*

*Ode 1, Tone 8. The creation of John of Damascus, of which the acrostic is:*[^352]
*"I sing of thee, namesake of God's gifts."*
*Heirmos: "Having traversed the water":*

*Troparion:*
Knowing thee to be a lover of God, O Theodore, and held fast by thine unavoidable love, I offer soul and body, and words of praise.

*Troparion:*
A God-abhorred decree was written, to offer sacrifice to idols and not to the living God; but thou wast brought forward, not having sacrificed, O passion-bearer, that thou mightest truly be brought unto God.

*Glory:*
Fastened by divine longing, thou didst enlist under the Only-Begotten Son of the Most High God, O Martyr Theodore, and didst not miss the marks of honor.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
The ranks of angels and of men unceasingly praise thee, O unwedded Mother; for their Creator, as an Infant, thou didst bear in thine arms.

*Ode 3. Heirmos: "Thou art the establishment":*

*Troparion:*
A hymn from my lips and a supplication from an aching soul do I bring, which do thou graciously receive, O passion-bearer Theodore.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst subject the flesh unto the sovereign mind, O glorious martyr, and by both thou dost please Him Who created thee.

*Glory:*
Thou didst stand before the tribunal of the tyrant, theologizing Christ the King; for thou didst refuse to sacrifice unto impure idols, O Theodore.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
All we Christians have acquired thee as a refuge and a wall forever: thee do we unceasingly glorify, O Unwedded One.

*Ode 4. Heirmos: "I have heard, O Lord":*

*Troparion:*
A witness of divine mysteries in understanding, O Martyr Theodore, thou didst confess the incomprehensible preaching of God's Nativity.

*Troparion:*
Those who served the passions supposed divinity to be subject to passion, whom thou didst convict in the Spirit, enlightened passion-bearer Theodore.

*Glory:*
By thy prayer unto the Redeemer, O blessed Theodore, we pray that we who sing of thee may be delivered from diverse afflictions and passions.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
O unplowed field that didst grow the life-creating ear of grain that gives life unto the world: O Mother of God, save those who sing of thee!

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "Watching early from the night":*

*Troparion:*
Love the Lord Who loved you! didst thou cry aloud, O passion-bearer Theodore, unto the martyrs who suffered with thee.

*Troparion:*
Inflamed in heart with zeal, O passion-bearer, thou didst burn the falsely named goddess along with her shrine.

*Glory:*
By invincible power, O passion-bearer Theodore, destroy the barbarian audacity and my passions!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
We sing of thee as Virgin after childbirth, O Mother of God; for thou didst bear in the flesh the God-Word for the world.

*Ode 6. Heirmos: "Cleanse me, O Savior":*

*Troparion (twice):*
How weak, how feeble is the raging of vain gods, to entreat men for their own avenging, vanquished by the steadfastness of true martyrs who proclaim the truth!

*Glory:*
Strengthened by the power of God, O glory of martyrs, Theodore, thou didst regard the tortures of the impious as the arrow-shots of infants, O blessed one, foreseeing the eternal things of the future.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
May we be delivered from grievous transgressions by thy supplications, O pure Parent of God, and may we attain, O all-pure one, the divine illumination of the Son of God Who was ineffably incarnate of thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*[^353]
Having received the faith of Christ within thy heart as a shield, thou didst trample down the opposing powers, O much-suffering champion, and hast been crowned eternally with a heavenly crown, O Theodore, as one invincible!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "Having arrived from Judea":*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast wounded in body for the Word Who was wounded in the flesh for our sake, and gratefully rejoicing thereat, thou didst cry out, O passion-bearer Theodore: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
As the Redeemer of all was laid in a tomb and sealed with a stone, so thou in a sealed prison didst rejoice, crying out, O passion-bearer Theodore: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Being put to death, and having by voluntary choice shaken off the carnal passions, not nourished by food, O passion-bearer, but fed upon divine love, thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Glory:*
In prison, O passion-bearer, as once the youths in the furnace, One of the manifest Trinity strengthened thee, being seen by thee as thou criedst out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
When Thou didst desire to accomplish our salvation, O Savior, Thou didst take up Thine abode in the womb of the Virgin, Whom Thou didst show to be an advocate for the world: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "The furnace sevenfold":*

*Troparion:*
Blinded in mind, the ruler of the lawless impiously threatened thee, the pious one: Why hast thou, senseless one, placed thy hope in a man who died by violence? But thou didst cry out: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
As an understanding and all-wise steward of grace, by the grace given unto thee from God thou didst cry out, O Theodore, unto him who commanded impiously: Let this shame be upon me and my champions! Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Glory:*
Youthfully didst thou contend, boldly didst thou convict: Why dost thou play the madman, O tyrant? leaping up, thou didst say, commanding to serve creatures falsely, having forsaken the Creator? But I sing: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
In bodily images, in diverse riddles, in symbolic and figurative utterances, the God-proclaiming prophets foretold thy childbirth, supernatural and marvelous, O Virgin: therefore, rejoicing, we piously praise thee, supremely exalting Christ unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Thee, unwedded":*

*Troparion:*
The fuel of thy martyrdom, O Theodore, was the desire for God; therefore thy body also became fuel for the material fire, through which, rejoicing, thou didst depart unto the divine fire, O passion-bearer, pleasing servant of God, Theodore!

*Troparion:*
All-honorable martyr, having burned, thou wast not consumed; for thou didst burn up error, and thou standest living before God, eternally rejoicing in Him, O passion-bearer and pleasing servant Theodore!

*Glory:*
We know Thee, Christ, as One, One of the Trinity, in two perfect natures: save Thy people, O Savior, whom Thou didst acquire by Thy precious blood, through the supplications of Theodore the passion-bearer!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Thee, who didst hold in thine arms the invisible God Who is hymned in the heavens by every power, and through whom salvation is ever granted unto us in distress, do we magnify.

*Heirmos:*
Thee, the unwedded Mother of the Most High God, thee who above understanding gavest birth by the Word truly unto God, higher than the all-pure powers, with unceasing hymns of glory do we magnify.

*And immediately: Trisagion. And the Troparion of the Saint:* "Great are the accomplishments of faith." *Glory, Kontakion:* "Having received the faith of Christ as a shield." *Both now and ever, Theotokion, the same Tone:*
As all we who are exceedingly sinful have acquired thy protection and mighty intercession, O All-pure One, thy fervent help, O Virgin Mother Mary, the salvation of Christians: therefore cease not to entreat the Son on our behalf, that forgiveness be granted us.

*And there takes place the blessing of the kolyvo.*[^354]
*Then:* "Blessed be the Name of the Lord," *and:* "I will bless the Lord at all times." *And the distribution of the blessed bread is given by the priest. And the dismissal.*
*And where the Presanctified is not celebrated, we conclude the Hours thus: after "Remit, forgive," and after the final prostrations, we say the Trisagion; after "Our Father": "Lord, have mercy," 12 times. Then the prayer: "All-Holy Trinity": and we begin Vespers.*

*"O come, let us worship," thrice. Psalm 103. Kathisma. And we sing on "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 6: three Martyrika from the Oktoechos in the Tone of the week, and to Saint Theodore, 3. Glory, to Theodore:* "Using the apostate tyrant as his vessel"; *Both now and ever, Theotokion in the Tone of the week. "O Gladsome Light," without entrance. Reading. At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyrikon, Tone 5:* "Blessed is the host"; *Glory, to Theodore, Tone 2:* "Namesake of divine gifts" *(written in the stichera);* *Both now and ever, Theotokion:* "All my hope"; *"Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart"; Trisagion; after "Our Father": Troparion:* "Great are the accomplishments of faith"; *Glory, and both now and ever, Theotokion:* "All beyond comprehension." *Then the litany:* "Have mercy on us, O God," *and 3 prostrations. And the priest vests in a crimson phelonion, and begins:* "Blessed is our God"; *and we sing Psalm 142:* "O Lord, hear my prayer," *and the rest as indicated before. The canon on 4. And after the completion of the canon, instead of "It is truly meet," we sing the Heirmos of Ode 9:* "Thee, unwedded." *Then Trisagion; after "Our Father": Troparion:* "Great are the accomplishments of faith"; *Glory, Kontakion:* "Having received the faith of Christ"; *Both now and ever, Theotokion:* "As all we have acquired thy protection." *Then the prayer over the kolyvo; censing of the kolyvo. After this:* "Blessed be the Name of the Lord," *and:* "I will bless the Lord at all times"; *and the dismissal.*

*And we enter the refectory, and partake of wine and oil on account of the saint. This we received in the Lavra of our venerable father Sabbas and the coenobium of our venerable father Euthymius the Great; but we do not do this now on account of the solemnity of the day. Nevertheless we eat cooked food with prunes, without oil and pickles [armea]; and those who wish eat dry food, even as on Wednesday.*[^355]

*It is fitting to know that on all Fridays of the Holy and Great Forty Days at Vespers and at Compline we do not make prostrations, except those appointed at the Presanctified, which is: after "Let my prayer be set forth," 3 prostrations; after the entrance of the Holy Gifts, 3 prostrations; and at "Blessed be the Name of the Lord," 3 prostrations. And we sing Great Compline, at which we sing the resting canons of the occurring Tone in the crypt [temple of the dead]. Be it known that the Synod held in the city of Lviv in the year 1740, following the Greek Typika, commands that on all Fridays of the Holy Forty Days Small Compline be performed. As for the canons in the Menaion of the occurring Saturday and Sunday, we sing them whenever the ecclesiarch wills: one indeed we sing at Compline on Friday, and the other canon we sing at Compline on Sunday. Thus we do on all Fridays, on the evening of the Holy and Great Forty Days.*[^356]

## Saturday of the First Week of the Holy Fast at Matins[^357]
*After the Six Psalms, we sing: "God is the Lord," in Tone 2, and we say the Troparion of Saint Theodore, twice:* "Great are the accomplishments of faith." *Glory, and both now and ever, Theotokion, Tone 2:* "All beyond comprehension." *And the customary stichology.*

*After the first stichology, we sing the Sessional Hymns (Martyrika) of the Oktoechos of the occurring Tone, with the Theotokion.*

*After the second stichology, we sing the present Sessional Hymns of Saint Theodore:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃їѧ вѣ́ры):*
Boiling with the orthodoxy of the faith, thou didst quench the deception of evil doctrine, abolishing the godlessness of idols; and having become a divine whole-burnt offering, with miracles thou dost bedew the ends of the earth, O most glorious martyr: pray unto Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Another Sessional Hymn of the Saint, the same melody:*
As a divine gift for salvation unto all the inhabited earth did the Lord, Who strengthened thee in thy martyrdoms, give thee, healing the diseases of our souls and overcoming the passions of the body, O Martyr Theodore: pray unto Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Theotokion, the same melody:*
O all-pure Virgin who didst carry in thy womb the uncontainable God, Who before the ages shone forth unhidden from the Father, the hypostatic Word and consubstantial Son: Him do thou entreat, together with prophets and martyrs, venerable ones, fasters, and righteous ones, to grant us the remission of our sins.

*Then we read the Martyrdom of the Saint.*
*Then Psalm 50. And the canon of the patron saint of the monastery with the Heirmoi on 6,*
*and of Saint Theodore on 8; and we sing the biblical canticles and "Sing ye to the Lord":*
*The Canon, of which the acrostic is:*[^358]
*"Most glorious Tyro, the glory of the first Saturday."*
*And in the first troparia of the canon, another acrostic.*
*Canon, the work of John, Metropolitan of Euchaita, Tone 4:*[^359]

*Ode 1. Heirmos: "I shall open my mouth":*

*Troparion:*
The Most High of all, having set thee who didst ineffably bear Him above all His chosen ones: therefore today, singing of this crowned champion, O All-pure One, we begin with thee.

*Troparion:*
The great one among martyrs, the most radiant champion, renowned and most glorious in miracles, celebrated from the ends of the earth unto the ends, in hymns will I sing of Theodore!

*Troparion:*
A joyful day has shone forth in sorrows and quenched the mourning thereof, the pre-festal harbingers having from afar illumined us through the grace of the divine martyr.

*Troparion:*
The holy slaughter with blood sprinkles the cleansing of the Church, which He Who was sacrificed and accepted as a sacrifice received from him who contended for the sake of His divine glory.

*Theotokion:*
Fair, even if unseasonable, is that which is established by law to celebrate; for the Virgin who adorned all things has now shown him who mixed his blood with this memorial to be an advocate.

*Another Canon, by the same [John] of Euchaita:*
*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Traversing the deep as on dry land":*

*Troparion:*
Marvelous art Thou truly in the saints, O God! Make marvelous Thy mercies in me, O Christ, granting me utterance, that I may praise the wonders of Thy martyr.

*Troparion:*
Steadfastness in martyrdom and patience in pains didst thou show, O passion-bearer of Christ; and swift again unto us dost thou show thy help, O Theodore.

*Glory:*
Wickedly rejecting from his soul the pious faith of the fathers, the tyrant, rushing upon an impious counsel against God, wished to go continually.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
The prophesies of the men of old have manifestly been fulfilled in thee, O Virgin Mother; for thou didst bear the true God and the true Man, above nature and awe-inspiringly.

*Katavasia:*
*Tone 4:* I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen and Mother; and I will appear radiantly keeping festival, and I will sing rejoicing of her wonders.

*Ode 3. First Canon of St. Theodore, Tone 4. Heirmos: "Thy hymn-singers":*[^360]

*Troparion:*
Desiring to see the day of Thy Son, the people named after Christ, in the image of this day, O Sovereign Lady, now celebrate its Forefeast, bearing Thee as proof, and Thy glorious passion-bearer.

*Troparion:*
The best things overcome by all means; for the great one among sufferers, unto a more excellent joy, has turned into relaxation the temporary sorrow of the exceedingly holy fast.

*Troparion:*
A proper and suitable gift unto thee: as from thine own, O sufferer, whom thou thyself didst settle in this thy portion, joyfully do we bring gifts of praise unto thee.

*Troparion:*
An excellent renewal hast thou established by thine excellencies, O excellent one, having overthrown through supernatural valor the all-destroying beast, breathing forth fire and fury, as of no account.

*Troparion:*
The new apostate, having raged in a manner equal to the ancient cruel one, mixed sacrifices with the provisions as with poison; but the false wretch was outwitted by the martyr.

*Theotokion:*
All the earth, O Virgin, has been filled with the Spirit to know the Lord Who was revealed unto the world from thee; but great grace belongs unto the divine beams that have confirmed the truth by themselves.

*Another Canon [Second Canon of St. Theodore, Tone 6]. Heirmos: "There is none holy":*

*Troparion:*
He who deceitfully whispered into the soul of Eve in the Church, and who by the same deceit deceived Adam, he also now poured out the venom of wickedness unto deceit.

*Troparion:*
He possessed the deceiver against him, he nurtured fury against those who live uprightly, he sought an opportune time in which the most lawless one might study how to commit an abomination.

*Glory:*
The beginning of the days of the holy fasts has arrived, in which every Christian, having cleansed himself by much abstinence, has departed from all things.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
O mighty Hope of the world, protection and intercession, all-holy Virgin: do not despise, O Good One, the supplications of thy servants who ever bless thee from love.

*Katavasia:*
*Tone 4:* Establish thy hymn-singers, O Theotokos, thou living and plentiful fountain, who have assembled a choir, spiritually in thy divine glory, vouchsafing them crowns of glory.

*Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of the Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва):*
Having taken up the divine armor, and overthrown the deceit of idols, thou didst raise up angels to praise thy struggles; for having inflamed thy mind with divine desire, thou didst courageously despise death in the fire; therefore, unto us who honor thee in a manner named after gifts, grant divine gifts, the bestowing of healings, O Passion-bearer Theodore: pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love!

*Glory, the same melody:*[^361]
Not swayed by the flatteries of Maximian, but showing courage in the words of Christ, thou didst burn the idol temple unto ashes in the fire, having well conquered the adversary in suffering; prophetically didst thou pass through fire as through water; therefore, as a worthy reward of thy struggles, thou pourest forth healings, O Passion-bearer Theodore: pray unto Christ God that remission of transgressions be granted unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion, the same melody:*
As the all-immaculate Bride of the Creator, as the unwedded Mother of the Redeemer, as the Receptacle of the Comforter, O All-holy One: since I am an unclean dwelling of iniquity and in my mind have become a plaything of demons, make haste to deliver me from their evil deeds, O thou who by virtue hast acquired a radiant dwelling, O incorruptible bearer of light: dispel the cloud of passions, and vouchsafe me heavenly radiance and the unwaning light, through thine intercessions.

*Ode 4. First Canon, Tone 4. Heirmos: "He Who sits in glory":*

*Troparion:*
O new and most glorious commingling and blending! The God-Man Who was born united childbearing unto virginity; and, honoring His sufferer, He introduced gladness into the radiant days.

*Troparion:*
Behold how he who served as a soldier unto Christ loved Christ! For he destroyed His enemies with zeal and fire, and fervently offered himself unto Him as an ultimately good and acceptable sacrifice.

*Troparion:*
The victor was released from the courageous acts of martyrdom, he spoke with boldness, and, having astonished the spectators with his contempt of tortures, he became a new and most glorious spectacle unto all creation.

*Troparion:*
The bone of my God and Master was not broken, O the longsuffering of Him Who suffered after being put to death! Fire having received his horse, and the wheel was ashamed to touch the dead one.

*Troparion to the Trinity:*
The ministers who died with the Master of life and rose with Him live with Him eternally outside; and they glorify the Mother together with the Son, blessing Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.

*Another Canon [Second Canon], Tone 6. Heirmos: "Christ is my power":*

*Troparion:*
A most ungodly counsel does he devise, to see which is truly fearful; for the wretched one intended to defile the people of Christ with abominable provisions.

*Troparion:*
He brought the lawless counsel into action; for having commanded that the provisions of pure foods be withheld, he ordered those to be set forth which he had defiled with blood.

*Glory:*
The lawlessness became evident and published unto all Christians; therefore, as they learned of it, they all ran together unto the holy temple of the Lord.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Thee who didst bear Him Who was begotten of the Father before the ages do we, the faithful, now set forth as an intercessor: moved by her prayers, O Christ, be well-pleased unto Thy servants.

*Katavasia:*
*Tone 4:* He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine, has come upon a light cloud, with an incorruptible palm, and has saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Ode 5. First Canon, Tone 4. Heirmos: "All things were amazed":*[^362]

*Troparion:*
Today the portals of grace are opened, and rest itself now prefigures the resurrection: thus radiantly do we honor the radiant Sovereign Lady and the glorious martyr.

*Troparion:*
God bestows a gift named after Theodore, revealing unto those who love Him the revelation of the beloved one, a maker of most glorious deeds, working as a personal minister of divine powers.

*Troparion:*
Thy bows and arrows are of no avail against the martyr, O enemy of the salvation of man; for even having died he lives, and resisting thy craft and wickedness, he saves every Orthodox one.

*Theotokion:*
A marvelous miracle was wrought of old, O Maiden of God, and through the testimonies of martyrdom it received unhesitating faith; and that which is believed ever abides wondered at and faithfully worshiped.

*Another Canon [Second Canon], Tone 6. Heirmos: "By Thy divine light":*

*Troparion:*
The archbishop of that time was then wholly struck with terror when he learned the counsel of the ungodly one, and with all-night hymns he prayed unto God not to utterly despise the people of His inheritance.

*Troparion:*
But the speedy Helper of those who call upon Him in truth and fervor truly did not despise any of their supplications, but quickly works for them the dissolution of the enterprise.

*Glory:*
From the heights a helper is sent swiftly unto the earth, the Passion-bearer Theodore; and he appears unto the chief pastor, teaching the dissolution of the lawless counsel.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
No one who flees unto thy protection, O Sovereign Lady, will ever be put to shame; but he asks for grace, and receives a most speedy gift of request for that which is profitable.

*Katavasia:*
*Tone 4:* All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O unwedded Virgin, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst bear the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who sing thy praise.

*Ode 6. First Canon, Tone 4. Heirmos: "This divine and all-honorable":*

*Troparion:*
The friends and sons of Thy bridal chamber, O Savior, cannot fast; for they entertain Thee, the Bridegroom, with Thy Mother and with Thy friend standing by, and they minister.

*Troparion:*
The ill-named one departed from God Who saves, he departed also from understanding, and in pride he rose against the faith; but instead of this, he found the greatly-named one.

*Troparion:*
Having suffered, the wise one stood forth, and the valiant, fervent one engaged in battle with the tormentors; and he was well crowned with victory, nor did he depart from righteousness even in death.

*Theotokion:*
Having been delivered today from soul-destroying calamity, O Sovereign Lady, by the miracles of thy passion-bearer, according to thy divine providence we offer thanksgiving unto both.

*Another Canon [Second Canon], Tone 6. Heirmos: "The sea of life":*

*Troparion:*
Struck with terror at the strange sight of thee, the archbishop spoke in response: Who art Thou, O Lord, Who speakest unto me? Give me understanding and teach me how we may find most speedy help!

*Troparion:*
Theodore said: I am plainly a passion-bearer; and give ear: boil wheat and distribute it unto the people, and thus will they be saved from the defiled provisions of the deceiver.

*Glory:*
How great is thy miracle and thy most glorious protection, O Theodore! Therefore, taking courage, all of us purely flee unto thee, and we pray: Save thy servants!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Thou wast a dwelling of light and a pure receptacle of the Word: through the good pleasure of the Father and through the visitation of the Holy Spirit unto thee; therefore enlighten me.

*Katavasia:*
*Tone 4:* Celebrating this divine and all-honorable festival of the Mother of God, come, ye godly-minded, let us clap our hands, glorifying God Who was born of her.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*[^363]
Having received the faith of Christ within thy heart as a shield, thou didst trample down the opposing powers, O much-suffering one; and thou hast been crowned eternally with a heavenly crown, O Theodore, as one invincible!

*Ikos:*
Him Who is borne upon the throne of light do we thankfully praise in faith, for Thou hast bestowed a divine gift, the good sufferer Theodore, who in life was thrice-blessed, as a champion of truth: who having acquired Christ with godly thoughts, became a mighty victor over the deceiver, as one invincible.

*Synaxarion.*
*Verses:*
*With food of kolyvo, Tyro nourishes the city,*  
*Rendering the defiled food useless.*

On this day, on the first Saturday of the fasts, we celebrate the most glorious miracle wrought with kolyvo by the holy and glorious Great-Martyr Theodore the Recruit [Tyro], the beginning of which occurred in this manner: Julian the Apostate, having seized the scepters of the empire after Constantius, the son of Constantine the Great, and turned away from Christ unto idol-worship, raised up an exceedingly great persecution against Christians, both openly and secretly. The impious one refrained from torturing cruelly, as well as miserably and inhumanly wounding Christians in this manner; but, being ashamed and holding back lest a greater number join them, the deceitful and defiled one devised secretly to defile them in some way; and knowing from this that our Christian people especially cleanses itself during the first week of the holy fasts and attends unto God, he summoned the prefect of the city and commanded that the usual foods offered for sale be removed from the midst, and that other foods be set forth in the market, namely loaves of bread and drinks, having first mingled them with blood from his sacrifices and defiled them by the mingling: so that those who bought them on account of the fast might become defiled instead of attaining purity. The prefect indeed carried out the command, and set forth the foods and drinks defiled with sacrifices and blood throughout all the markets. But the all-seeing Eye of God, which catches the wise in their craftiness and ever provides for us His servants, dissolved the abominable plots of the apostate against us. For unto the city's archbishop, Eudoxius, even though he was not upright and not orthodox, He sent His great passion-bearer Theodore, of the rank of the recruits, called Tyro. And standing before him visibly and not in a dream, he spoke in this manner: Rise up as quickly as possible, assemble the flock of Christ, and command strictly that no one buy anything of the things set forth in the market; for they have been defiled with the blood of sacrifices by the impious emperor. And when the archbishop was perplexed and asked how this might easily be done, since those who have want in their homes cannot refrain from buying from the goods set forth in the market, the saint said unto him: Give them kolyvo and relieve their want. And when he was at a loss, inquiring what this kolyvo might be, the Great Theodore said: Boiled wheat; for thus are we accustomed to speak in Euchaita. And the patriarch examined him: Who then art thou, who carest for the people named after Christ? And the holy martyr of Christ Theodore said again: I am now sent from Him as a helper unto you. The patriarch immediately arose, announced unto the multitude the things that had been seen, and having acted accordingly, he preserved the flock of Christ unharmed from the craftiness of the enemy and apostate. And when the tyrant found his cunning exposed as nothing and having accomplished nothing, and was sufficiently put to shame, he commanded that the customary goods for sale be set forth again in the market. Therefore, the people of Christ, when the week had already ended, rendering thanksgiving unto the Benefactor and martyr, on this Saturday celebrated a memorial for him with kolyvo...

...rejoicing. From that time, therefore, the faithful, even unto this day, renewing the miracle so that so great a deed of the martyr may not be forgotten by time, honor the great Theodore with kolyvo.[^364] This great martyr was tortured beforehand under Maximian by the impious Bryngas, after having been chosen earlier; and having burned their temple with fire and distributed its adornments unto the poor, he did not endure those who came unto him with words, wishing to turn him unto evil and offering counsel. Having suffered many things, he was cast into a great furnace that was heated; and having suffered nothing from it, he yielded His spirit unto God in the midst of it. Through His prayers, O Christ God, have mercy on us and save us. Amen.

*Ode 7. First Canon, Tone 4. Heirmos: "The youths did not serve the creature":*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst say that the law does not apply unto Thy righteous ones, O righteous Master; therefore Thy Mother and this Thy marvelous servant today dissolve the laws of burial by the radiance of their memory.

*Troparion:*
How sweet, and great, and joyful is the present solemnity! Which the most valiant and most good one among martyrs has restored unto us, having made the people of Christ clean from unclean things by a single miracle.

*Troparion:*
Him who opposed the cruel serpents and prevailed over the soul-destroying and defiling one of embodied beings, do we crown with songs of victory and praises of crowns.

*Theotokion:*
The forefather indeed did not escape the soul-destroying food of the waking; but thy faithful people, instructed beforehand by thy martyr and having escaped the all-destructive food, offer saving sacrifices unto thee with him, O Sovereign Lady.

*Another Canon [Second Canon], Tone 6. Heirmos: "O Thou Who didst make the furnace dewy":*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown to be a dispenser of dew in sorrow and a cloud in burning heat, O most glorious one, and thou didst save from the furnace of torture those who cry, all ye faithful: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Be put to shame, O most lawless emperor, and let thy mouth be stopped, which instructed against us in lawless counsel; for the reigning King and Lord of all has destroyed thy strength!

*Glory:*
O foolish Julian, thy heart was filled with all wickedness, but that which was deceitfully devised against us is condemned by our most wise God, as we cry unto Him: Thou bestowest grace upon us!

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Thy conception was beyond word, and thy childbearing beyond understanding, O all-immaculate one; for thou didst bear in the flesh God, Who saved all from corruption, O blessed one; therefore now do we glorify thee, O Theotokos.

*Katavasia:*
*Tone 4:* The godly-minded did not serve the creature rather than the Creator, but having courageously trampled down the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers, praised above all!

*Ode 8. First Canon, Tone 4. Heirmos: "O Redeemer of all":*

*Troparion:*
She who filled heavenly and earthly things with joy through her childbearing, by a temporal shining bestows timely grace upon the eternal ones, weaving together radiant festive celebrations.

*Troparion:*
Let us keep sabbath today, O men, resting from yesterday's labors, for the sake of Him Who blessed the present day with rest, and now with the festival of the martyr.

*Troparion:*
Truly this is the first among sabbaths, and it might be called the sabbath of sabbaths: filled with the grace of martyrdom and divine power, a commemoration of great salvation.

*Troparion:*[^365]
With clean provisions, the pure King and God abolished the uncleanness of the lawless kingdom, and in a most glorious manner of wonderworking sanctified the holy week.

*Theotokion:*
The fervent death of the martyrs confirms thy mystery, O Maiden; for having believed in God Who was born, even though He willingly endured passion, the valiant ones sacrificed themselves.

*Another Canon [Second Canon], Tone 6. Heirmos: "Out of the flame for the venerable ones":*

*Troparion:*
As thou wast delivered out of the flame, O Martyr Theodore, by divine power, so also now hast thou thyself saved us from the insidious lawless counsel by thy divine intercession.

*Troparion:*
Weeping lodged at evening for thy servants from the deceitful hunting of our adversary; but in the morning joy shone forth, O patient-minded martyr, through thy warm help.

*Glory:*
Having found thee as a deliverer when we fell into perils, and a deliverer from the snare of the enemy, O martyr of Christ and passion-bearer Theodore, we glorify thee unto all ages.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
The human mind is at a loss to tell the mystery of thy childbearing, O Pure One; and for this cause do we honor thee in faith devoutly as the Theotokos, and we glorify thee unto all ages.

*Katavasia:*
*Tone 4:* The childbearing of the Theotokos saved the godly youths in the furnace, then prefigured, but now in reality; it moves the whole inhabited earth to sing unto Thee: Sing ye to the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Ode 9. First Canon, Tone 4. Heirmos: "Thy childbearing was incorrupt":*

*Troparion:*
The multitude assembles from every side unto the tabernacle of testimony; and the living grace, which pre-glorified him in the glory of all martyrs, do we magnify together with the Sovereign Lady of all.

*Troparion:*
Take courage, O Church of Christ, and hold sway over those who fight against thee; for the friends and attendants of Christ take care of thee, and the defenders as valiant men, unto Whom thou dost accomplish thanksgiving.

*Troparion:*
They were thought of old to have died, but the men of faith show that they live even after the end, and the men of faith again intercede with equal zeal, enriched by which we magnify the Benefactor.

*Theotokion:*
Bearing thee as a divine seal, the sealed book of God, I set thee upon my songs, and I gather praises in thee, O Virgin, thus glorifying thy martyr exceedingly.

*Another Canon [Second Canon], Tone 6. Heirmos: "For man to see God is impossible":*

*Troparion:*
Well wast thou revealed as a champion, O most glorious one, and a mighty protection unto us who now honor thy honorable and eternal memory, and the accomplishments of thy sufferings, and the divine protection that came upon us.

*Troparion:*[^366]
Thy most blessed miracle, O Theodore, accomplished by holy showers, is sung in all the world, O blessed one; therefore, gathering annually, we send up glory unto Christ Who glorified thee.

*Glory:*
Beholding the love of those who praise thee, O martyr of Christ, bestow greater grace; and knowing faith, how great it is, O most glorious one, cease not, O warmest intercessor and martyr, showing thyself as an advocate unto our God.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
We cease not to sing of thee, O Virgin, our helper, protection, speedy shelter, and unashamed intercessor: preserve thy servants, ever freeing them from every affliction and from every snare of the deceitful one.

*Katavasia:*
*Tone 4:* Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy in spirit, being enlightened, and let the nature of immaterial minds celebrate, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let it cry: Rejoice, all-blessed Theotokos, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Exapostilarion of the Saint, Tone 2, Podoben: "With the disciples" (Со ᲂу҆чени́ки):*
O holy bearer of the crown, who now standest with the angels before the throne of Christ, and art filled with light from thence, O passion-bearer: unceasingly intercede for peace for the world, and salvation for us who devoutly celebrate thy radiant memory, O all-opulent Theodore, most beautiful martyr.

*Theotokion, the same melody:*
The Lord, wishing to call back the perishing world from corruption, dwelt in thy womb, O Theotokos, as He knew; having all found salvation, therefore, let us cry aloud unto thee the angelic word: Rejoice, O All-praised One, blessed among all women; for thou didst bear joy unto all the inhabited earth!

*At the Praises, we set 4 verses, and we sing stichera, Tone 1:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Of the celestial orders" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Having assembled in harmony, ye faithful, let us praise with mystical songs the excellent man of the heavenly army, as a patient soldier of our piety, saying: O worthy of marvel, Martyr Tyro, pray for those who honor thee!

*Sticheron 2:*
By thy name named after gifts, O gift of God in truth, thou hast given comfort unto all who sorrow, O thrice-blessed Theodore; for everyone who draws near in truth unto thy temple, receiving with gladness the rewards of thy miracles, honors Christ.

*Sticheron 3:*
Having treasured up for thyself the wealth of piety and radiance by thy suffering struggles, thou didst offer all thy strength as an acceptable gift unto God, fulfilling with diligence in thy struggles thy gift-bestowed calling.

*Sticheron 4:*
Let us all be nourished by the all-radiant festival of the divine martyr, and let us, lovers of festivals, rejoice faithfully, honoring the radiant solemnity of his end, singing in hymnody Jesus Who glorified his memory.

*Glory, Tone 6:*[^367]
A gift of sanctification and a wealth of divine life hast thou been shown to the world, O Theodore; for Christ, O wise one, has glorified thy memory, in which we the faithful, rejoicing in harmony, sing the struggles of thy pains.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion, the same tone:*
O Theotokos, thou art the true vine that didst blossom forth for us the Fruit of life: thee do we entreat, intercede, O Sovereign Lady, together with the passion-bearer and all the saints, that our souls may be shown mercy.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Saint, Tone 2: Idiomela:*
The choirs of martyrs rejoice today with joy in thy temple, O Passion-bearer Theodore, and the angelic orders applaud the endurance of thy suffering; and the Crown-giver Christ Himself stands by, with His right hand granting rich-producing gifts unto thy hymn-singers: Whom having loved, thou didst seek, and having found, didst converse with, Whom thou didst desire: Whom do thou beseech, to save and enlighten our souls.

*Verse:* The righteous man shall flourish like the palm tree, and like a cedar in Lebanon shall he be multiplied.

The pure and spotless fast that has now come unto us has brought with it the perfection of the martyr's miracles; for having cleansed the soul's defilements by the fast, and having acted manfully against passions with martyrs' signs and by suffering, we are enlightened from this by the grace of sacred abstinence, and by the wonderworkings of the Martyr Theodore we are confirmed in faith in Christ, asking Him to grant salvation unto our souls.

*Verse:* Those that are planted in the house of the Lord shall flourish in the courts of our God.

By thy martyr's boldness before God, O Theodore, thou hast made vain the apostate's snare against the faith of Christ, having become a champion for the people who practice piety: by an awesome appearance thou didst deliver them from the defiled foods of the idol sacrifice; therefore, honoring thee as a destroyer of idols, and as the savior and guardian of Christ's flock, and as our attentive intercessor, in songs do we beseech thee that cleansing and illumination be granted through thee unto our souls.

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Having devoutly armed thyself with suffering courage, O passion-bearer of Christ, and having spiritually subjected rational service through His power, thou didst show the ungodliness of idols and the threat of the tyrants to be impotent, despising tortures and the temporal fire; but, O divine in gifts and deeds and name! from every assault do thou save by thine intercession those who celebrate thy memory.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady, receive the prayers of thy servants, and deliver us from every need and sorrow.

*And the rest of the order of Matins.*

*We also join the First Hour.*

*At the Liturgy, the Typika, and from the Canon of the Saint, Odes 3 and 6.*[^368]

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:* The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.  
*Verse:* Hear, O God, my voice, when I make supplication unto Thee.

*The Epistle of the day, to the Hebrews, pericope 313 (Heb. 3:12–16). For the Saint, the Epistle to Timothy, pericope 292 (2 Tim. 2:1–10).*
