*Alleluia, Tone 4:* The righteous man shall flourish like the palm tree, and like a cedar in Lebanon shall he be multiplied.  
*Verse:* Those that are planted in the house of the Lord shall flourish in the courts of the house of our God.

*The Gospel from Mark, pericope 7 (Mark 1:35–44). And for the Saint, from John, pericope 52 (John 15:17–16:2).*

*Communion Hymn:* In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings.

*At the trapeza, we read the Encomium of Saint Theodore by Saint Gregory of Nyssa. And we eat cooked beans, with white and black olives. We also partake of cooked food with available oil, and kolyvo; and we drink wine in moderation, for the sake of the saint. This have we received from the lavra of our venerable Father Sabbas, and from our God-bearing Father Euthymius.*

*It is proper to know that we have received permission to eat fish twice in this Great Fast: on the feast of the Annunciation, and on Palm Sunday.*

*[Engraving: An allegorical maiden representing Faith, seated upon clouds, crowned with a diadem, holding a cross in her right hand and a palm branch in her left, symbolizing Christian victory over pagan persecution].*

*[Woodcut Headpiece: The Holy Eucharist—a sacred chalice surmounted by a crucifix, set upon open sacred Scriptures, emitting radiant rays of divine light, within an elaborate Renaissance foliage border].*

*FIRST SUNDAY OF THE GREAT FAST: SUNDAY OF ORTHODOXY:*
*On which Orthodoxy is commemorated.*[^369]

*The customary Vigil.*

*At Little Vespers, we set 4 verses, and we sing prosomoia stichera. The work of Nicholas, Tone 1:*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "Of the celestial orders" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Now the radiance of piety has spread over all, dispelling the deceit of ungodliness like a cloud, and enlightening the hearts of the devout: come, all ye Orthodox, let us fall down, venerating with godly wisdom the honored icons of Christ.

*Sticheron 2:*
With sacred-figured icons is the Church of Christ now adorned, like a bride adorned, and she summons all to celebrate spiritually: let us come together in unity of mind and faith, joyfully magnifying the Lord.

*Sticheron 3:*
Rejoice, all the world! For behold, by His ineffable providence, the Lord has cast down the dominion of ungodliness from on high, and we the humble have been exalted today unto the faith of Orthodoxy, guided by the pious empress.

*Sticheron 4:*
The honor of the icon passes unto the Prototype, says Basil: for this reason, with love do we honor the icons of Christ the Savior and of all the saints, that having gathered them together, we may never now be led astray by ungodliness.

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion, the same melody:*
Invisible by Thine unoriginate and divine nature, Thou didst become visible man, O Maiden, from thy pure blood, through Thine extreme tender mercy: whose likeness of flesh having depicted, all we the faithful honorably venerate, and devoutly magnify.

*At the Aposticha, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Еѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
Having fled the corrupting destruction of the evil-minded, by the radiance of Orthodoxy let us the faithful be enlightened in heart, glorifying God.[^370]

*Verse:* Bring unto the Lord glory and honor.  
The unclean delusion of Arius has been brought to nought, with that of Macedonius, Peter, Severus, and Pyrrhus; and the Tri-radiant Light shines forth.

*Verse:* Worship Him in His holy court.  
No longer is the murky doctrine of the ungodly boasted; for unto the Orthodox, through the goodwill of Theodora, God gives the rod of strength.

*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion, the same melody:*  
Thou hast exalted the horn of the humble, O Mother of God, who didst give birth to God the Word; therefore, faithfully honoring thee, we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:* **"Thy pure image..."** *And the dismissal takes place.*

## At Great Vespers
*After the customary recitation of the Psalter, at "Lord, I have cried," in the Tone of the Octoechos, we set 10 verses: and we sing 2 Resurrection stichera, and 3 Anatolian stichera, and 4 prosomoia of the Triodion, Tone 6:*[^371]

*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*  
Thee, the Incomprehensible, Who before the morning star didst shine forth without beginning from the immaterial and bodiless womb of the Parent, the Prophets, O Lord, inspired by Thy Spirit, foretold to be a Child incarnate of the Virgin Unwedded, united with men and seen by those upon the earth; through whom, as the Merciful One, make us worthy of Thine illumination, as we hymn Thine ineffable and honorable Resurrection.

*Podoben:*  
The God-proclaiming prophets, having preached Thee in word and honored Thee in deeds, brought forth unending life; for not enduring to serve the creature rather than Thee the Creator, they turned away apostolically from the whole world, and were conformed unto Thy Passion which they foretold; by whose prayers make us worthy to pass through the course of abstinence blamelessly, O only Abundantly-Merciful One.

Though by Thy divine nature Thou art uncircumscribed, O Master, having become incarnate in the latter days, Thou didst deign to be circumscribed by the assumption of flesh; and Thou didst take upon Thyself all the properties thereof; therefore, depicting the likeness of Thy form, we lovingly kiss it, being raised unto Thy love, and we draw forth the grace of healing from it, following the apostolic and divine traditions.

The Church of Christ has received an all-honorable adornment: the radiant restoration of the precious and holy icons of Christ the Savior, and of the Mother of God, and of all the saints; by which she is enlightened and flourishes with grace, and putting to flight the assembly of heretics, she casts it out; and rejoicing, she glorifies God the Lover of mankind, Who for her sake endured voluntary Sufferings.

*Glory...*

*Glory, Tone 2:*  
The grace of truth, which of old was prefigured by the shadow, has now visibly found its fulfillment; for behold, the Church is robed with the incarnate image of Christ as with a most fair ornament, setting forth the pattern of the Tabernacle of Testimony and holding fast the Orthodox faith; so that, holding fast the image of Him Whom we honor, we are not led astray; may those who do not believe thus be clothed in shame! For our glory is the form of Him Who became incarnate, which is devoutly venerated, not deified; kissing it, we the faithful cry aloud: O God, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance![^372]

*Both now and ever, Theotokion, the first of the Tone.*

*At the Litiya, as usual, the sticheron of the temple.*

*Glory, Tone 2:*  
Rejoice, O honorable Prophets, who well established the Law of the Lord, and who by faith were shown to be invincible, unyielding pillars; for ye were shown to be mediators of the New Covenant of Christ; and having passed over unto the heavens, pray Him to bring peace unto the world, and to save our souls.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion, the same Tone:*  
All my hope I place upon thee, O Mother of God: preserve me under thy shelter.

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos. Glory, Tone 2:*  
Having passed from impiety unto piety, and having been enlightened by the light of knowledge, let us clap our hands like the psalmist, bringing thanksgiving praise unto God; and let us devoutly venerate the sacred images inscribed upon walls, boards, and sacred vessels—of Christ, of the All-Pure One, and of all the saints, casting aside the impious belief of the evil-minded; (for the honor of the image, as Basil says, passes unto the Prototype); beseeching Him, through the prayers of Thine all-pure Mother, O Christ our God, and of all the saints, to grant us great mercy.[^373]

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:* **"O wonder of new wonders..."**

*Troparion:* **"O Birth-giver of God and Virgin, rejoice..."** *twice; and of the feast:* **"Thy pure image do we venerate..."** *once. And the rest of the order of the Vigil.*

*At Matins:*
*At "God is the Lord," the troparion of the Resurrection, twice.*

*Glory, the present troparion, Tone 2:*  
Thy pure image do we venerate, O Good One, asking forgiveness of our sins, O Christ God; for of Thine own will Thou wast pleased to ascend the Cross in the flesh, that Thou mightest deliver those whom Thou didst create from the bondage of the enemy; therefore, with thanksgiving we cry unto Thee: Thou hast filled all things with joy, O our Savior, Who didst come to save the world.

*Both now and ever:*  
All beyond comprehension, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Mother of God; in purity sealed and in virginity preserved, thou wast known to be truly the Mother, having given birth to the true God; implore Him that our souls may be saved.

*And the customary recitation of the Psalter, and the rest of the order.*

*And the customary recitation of the Psalter: and the rest of the order.*

*Canons: of the Resurrection, and of the Mother of God, and of the Triodion to 6. The work of Lord Theophanes, whose acrostic is:*[^374]  
*Today the all-radiant dawn of piety has arrived.*

*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:*  
**"The Red Sea deep..."**

*Troparia:*  
Clapping with gladness today, let us the faithful cry aloud: How marvelous are Thy works, O Christ, and great is Thy power, Who hast made unity of mind and concord to surpass all!

Godly-minded people, come, let us celebrate a joyful day; now heaven and earth make merry, and the ranks of angels and assemblies of men exceptionally feast.

Beholding the supreme beneficence, let us clap our hands: the sundered members of Christ are gathered into unity, and let us praise God Who has bestowed peace.

Triumphant victory has been given today unto the Church through the divine movement and counsel of Michael and Theodora, our sovereigns who piously hold the faith.[^375]

*Theotokion:*  
The weapons of impious heresies have visibly vanished; for beholding thy temple, O All-Pure, Holy Lady, adorned in all-godly beauty with icons, we now rejoice in all-sacred manner.

*Katavasia:*  
Having traversed the watery deep of the Red Sea with dry feet, ancient Israel of old conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness by the crosswise outstretched hands of Moses.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
**"Thy Church rejoices in Thee..."**

*Troparia:*  
No longer is the brow of the ungodly heretics lifted up; for the divine power has sustained Orthodoxy.

Let the prophetic clouds drop down upon us today from heaven the life-giving dew, for the rising up of the faith!

Let the secret trumpets of the Apostles of Christ cry aloud tunefully beyond nature, proclaiming the restoration of the precious icons.

Let us hymn Christ, Who has shown us a pious and Christ-loving empress, together with her God-crowned Offspring.

*Theotokion:*  
Having reached thy sacred habitation, O All-Pure Lady, we the faithful pray that we may now be illumined with light-bearing grace.

*Katavasia:*  
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge, and my firm foundation.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскій):*  
Depicting Thy divine form in an icon, O Christ, we clearly proclaim Thy Nativity, Thine ineffable miracles, and Thy voluntary Crucifixion; whence demons are driven away in fear, and the evil-minded weep in shame, as partakers with them.

*Glory...*

*Glory:*  
With the forms of the prophets, the countenances of the apostles, the icons of the sacred martyrs and of all the saints, the mother Sion on high is sacredly adorned; and she is made beautiful with the spiritual beauties of the noetic Bridegroom and the Bride.

*Both now and ever, Theotokion:*  
Unto those who honor thy holy icon with love, O Pure One, and who with one accord proclaim thee to be truly the Mother of God, and who faithfully venerate thee, thou hast appeared as a guardian and a mighty protection, turning away every cruel affliction far from them, as thou art able to do all things.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
**"Beholding Thee lifted up..."**[^376]

*Troparia:*  
By the divine visitation of the Comforter, sanctify Thy temple, and by His coming drive away the heretical deceit, O all-merciful Word of God.

Having delivered Thy people from the most grievous impiety, show them kindled with the zeal of piety, and crying aloud with faith: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Beholding the radiant, divine habitations adorned in sacred beauty with the sacred figures of the icons of Christ and of the Mother of God, we sacredly rejoice.

Adorned in royal beauty, the empress, having desired the true Kingdom of Christ, inscribed His all-pure image and the depictions of the saints.

*Theotokion:*  
Having given birth to the incarnate Divine Word, O Full of Grace, thou didst show Him to be the God-befitting sanctification; therefore do we renew thy light-bearing temple.

*Katavasia:*  
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
**"Thou, my Lord, a Light..."**

*Troparia:*  
Grant, O Lord, a firm foundation to Thy Church, that she may remain unshaken unto ages of ages from the disturbances of heresies.

Unto all the earth the radiant brightness of the Tabernacle has shone forth, giving gladness and divine protection unto the faithful.

O only Good One, and Wellspring of goodness, do Thou exalt the horn of Orthodox sovereigns who honor Thine image.

The unwaning light of piety has shone forth upon us by the God-inspired command of faithful sovereigns and by their God-wise beckoning.

*Theotokion:*  
Renew for us our ancient beauty, O all-pure Mother of God, and sanctify this house of thine by thy grace.

*Katavasia:*  
Thou, my Lord, a Light into the world art come, a holy Light, turning from murky ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith.

*Ode 6.*

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
**"I will sacrifice unto Thee..."**

*Troparia:*  
The faithfully venerated image of the Master is depicted and honored, and the Church again takes boldness, piously glorifying the Savior.

The Church of Christ is stripped of mourning and of the darkness of heresies, and wears the garment of gladness, and is covered with divine and light-bearing grace.

The assembly of the Orthodox has obtained the ancient garment of goodly glory by the beckoning of Empress Theodora and of her son, the pious Autocrat Michael.

*Theotokion:*  
He Who commanded the Tabernacle of Testimony to be made of old, as in a spiritual Tabernacle dwells in thee, the only Most-Glorified One, glorifying thy temple, O Virgin, with miracles.

*Katavasia:*  
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demons' blood by the blood which flowed from Thy side for mercy's sake.

*Kontakion, Tone 6, Automelon:*  
The uncircumscribed Word of the Father was circumscribed when He became incarnate of thee, O Mother of God; and having restored the defiled image to its ancient dignity, He united it with divine beauty; but confessing salvation in deed and in word, we depict this in icons.[^377]

*Ikos:*  
This mystery of the dispensation the prophets of old, having been inspired in God-beholding vision, foretold for our sake, who have reached the ends of the ages; having obtained the radiance thereof, and having received from it divine understanding, we know the one Lord God, Who is worshipped in three Persons; and serving Him alone, having one faith and one baptism, we have been clothed in Christ; but confessing salvation in deed and in word, we depict this in icons.

*Synaxarion.*[^378]  
*Verses:*  
*Unbecomingly were the icons cast out;*  
*I rejoice, seeing them becomingly venerated.*

On this day, the first Sunday of the Holy Fasts, the Church of Christ received to celebrate Orthodoxy, that is, the restoration of the holy and venerable icons, which took place through Michael and Theodora, the holy and most blessed sovereigns, and through Saint Methodius, Patriarch of Constantinople; and it happened on this wise:  
Leo the Isaurian, who from being a tender of sheep and a swineherd had seized the scepters of the empire by God's permission, when Saint Germanus was then holding the helm of the Church, immediately summoned him and said: "It seems to me, Master, that the holy icons differ in no way from idols; therefore command them to be removed from our midst as quickly as possible. Even if they are the true figures of the saints, let them be hung higher up, lest wallowing in sins we should ever defile them by kissing them." But the Patriarch turned the emperor away from such loathsomeness, saying: "Hast thou not heard, O emperor, that someone would once rage against the holy icons, and that his surname would be Conon?" The emperor replied: "Why, that was my name while I was still an infant." When the Patriarch would not obey him, the emperor drove him into exile, and set up Anastasius in his place, who was of one mind with him; and thus at that time the war against the holy icons began to blaze. It is said that the Jews previously instilled such hatred in him, by a certain magical prophecy of his elevation to the throne, when in his poverty he was pleased to live with them as a tender of sheep. When he had wickedly departed this life, that most ungodly whelp, Constantine Copronymus, became his successor in authority, and especially in madness against the holy icons. And what need is there to recount how many and what great evils this lawless man committed? Yet when he too had come to a most shameful end, his son by the Khazar woman was placed upon the throne; but since he also lived wickedly, Irene and Constantine became heirs of the empire. Guided by the most holy Patriarch Tarasius, they assembled the Seventh Council, and the Church of Christ again received the holy icons. When they had laid aside the imperial rule, Nicephorus the General was elevated, then Stauracius, and after that Michael Rangabe, who honored the divine icons. But Michael was succeeded by the beast-like Leo...

...the Armenian; who, corrupted by the deceit of a certain recluse and ungodly monk, stirred up the second iconoclasm, and again the Church of God was shown without splendor. Him Michael the Amorian succeeded; and his son again, Theophilus, added fury against the icons to all the rest. This Theophilus, having delivered many of the holy fathers to torture and various torments on account of the venerable icons, then above all sought after justice, so that if anyone in the city was in dispute with another, not a single one could be found for many days, as they say. Having ruled autocratically for twelve years, he was seized with a bowel disease, and was about to burst from his belly; his mouth was opened exceedingly wide, so that his internal entrails were visible. Empress Theodora, grieving over what had happened, with difficulty fell asleep; and in a dream she saw the All-Pure Mother of God embracing the Pre-Eternal Infant, attended by radiant angels, while Theophilus her husband was being beaten and humiliated by them. When sleep departed from her, Theophilus, having come slightly to himself, cried aloud: "Woe is me, wretched one! For the sake of the holy icons am I beaten!" Immediately the empress placed an icon of the Mother of God upon him, praying for him with tears. And Theophilus, being in such a state, saw one of those standing by wearing an enkolpion, snatched it from him, and kissed it. And immediately his lips that had raved against the icons, and his throat that had shamelessly gaped open, returned to their proper condition; and he was delivered from the surrounding affliction and distress, and fell asleep, having confessed that it is good to honor the holy icons. Then the empress, bringing forth the holy and venerable icons from her caskets, brought Theophilus to kiss them and to honor them with all his soul. Shortly thereafter, Theophilus vanished from this life. And Theodora, recalling all the sacred persons who were in exiles and in prisons, commanded them to live in freedom. Then John, also called Jannes, who was the chief of magicians and demon-worshippers rather than a patriarch, was cast down from the patriarchal throne; and Methodius, the confessor of Christ, ascended the throne, having suffered many things before and having been shut up alive in a tomb.[^379]

While things were in this state, a certain divine visitation came upon Joannicius the Great, who was flourishing upon Mount Olympus: the great faster Arsacius came to him and said: "God has sent me unto thee, that we should go unto the venerable man Isaiah the Recluse in Nicomedia, and learn from him what is pleasing unto God and suitable for His Church." And having come unto the venerable Isaiah, they heard from him: "Thus says the Lord: Behold, the end of the enemies of My depiction has drawn near. Go ye unto Empress Theodora and unto Patriarch Methodius, and say thus: Put away all the unholy, and thus with the angels bring sacrifice unto Me, and honor the icon of My form and of the Cross." Having heard these things, they immediately reached the city of Constantine, and proclaimed what had been spoken unto Patriarch Methodius and unto all the elect of God. And having gathered together, they went unto the empress, and found her submissive unto all things, for she was God-loving. Immediately the empress, taking off an icon of the Mother of God hung upon her neck, kissed it in the sight of all, saying: "If anyone does not venerate these, and kiss them lovingly—not in service, not as gods, but as icons for the sake of the love of the prototypes—let him be anathema!" And they rejoiced with great joy. In return she also asked this of them, that they would offer supplication for her husband Theophilus. Seeing her faith, though they hesitated, they nevertheless complied. For Methodius the Patriarch, having gathered all the people, the entire clergy, and the hierarchs, came thither into the Great Church of God. Among them were these chosen ones: from Olympus, Joannicius the Great, and Arsacius; and Naucratius, and the disciples of Theodore the Studite; and from the Great Village, Theophanes, and Theodore the Branded, the confessors; Michael of the Holy City, the Syncellus, and very many others. They made an all-night supplication unto God for Theophilus, all praying with tears and prolonged supplication; and this was done throughout the entire first week of the fasts, the Empress Theodora herself doing likewise with the women and other people. While things were thus, Empress Theodora, falling asleep at the dawning of Friday, looked and saw the cross-pillar opening, and saw certain ones walking along the way with noise, carrying various instruments of torture; and in their midst Emperor Theophilus was being led bound, with his hands tied behind him. Recognizing him, she followed with those who were leading him. When they reached the Brazen Gate, she saw by a supernatural vision a certain Man sitting before an icon of Christ, opposite Whom they placed Theophilus; and the empress touched His feet, praying for the emperor. But He, scarcely opening His mouth, said: "Great is thy faith, O woman! Understand therefore that for the sake of thy tears and thy faith, and also for the sake of the petition and prayer of My servants and My priests, I grant forgiveness unto thy husband Theophilus." Then He said unto those who were leading him: "Untie him, and give him unto his wife." And she, taking him, departed rejoicing and glad; and immediately sleep left her. This therefore the Empress Theodora saw. But Patriarch Methodius, while prayers and supplications were being made for him, took a new parchment, wrote thereon the names of all the heretical emperors, including Theophilus the culprit, and taking it, placed all under the holy Table. On Friday he also saw a certain fearful, great angel entering into the temple, and coming unto him, saying: "Thy prayer has been heard, O bishop, and Emperor Theophilus has received forgiveness; no longer therefore trouble the Godhead concerning this." He, wishing to test whether what was seen was true, descended from his place, and took the parchment, and opening it, found—O divine judgments!—the name of Theophilus completely erased by God. When the empress learned this, she rejoiced exceedingly, and sent unto the Patriarch to gather all the people with precious crosses and holy icons in the Great Church, so that the adornment of holy icons might be restored unto it, and the new divine miracle might be made known unto all. And when all quickly assembled in the church with tapers, the empress also came with her son. And a Litiya was held there with the holy icons, and the divine and precious woods of the Cross, and the sacred and divine Gospel, and they went out as far as the place called the Hippodrome, crying: "Lord, have mercy!" And having returned thus again into the church, they celebrated the divine Liturgy. The holy and precious icons having been restored by the aforementioned holy men, the pious and Orthodox were proclaimed, and those who opposed in ungodliness and did not accept the honor of holy icons were rejected and delivered unto anathema. And from that time these holy confessors ordained that this sacred celebration should take place annually, lest we should ever fall again into the same impiety. O unchangeable Image of the Father, through the prayers of Thy holy confessors, have mercy on us. Amen.[^380]

*Ode 7.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
**"In the furnace of Abraham..."**[^381]

*Troparia:*  
Let the angelic army join with divine love in the joyful chorus of the Church's splendor, godly-mindedly singing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

The Church of the firstborn and their assembly rejoice, beholding now the divine people singing in oneness of mind: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Delivered from murky heresy of old by the beckoning of Theodora the praiseworthy empress, we sing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*  
Above the celestial choirs hast thou been exalted, O All-Praised One, having alone become the Mother of the Creator of all; rejoicing therefore, we cry aloud: Blessed art thou among women, O all-blameless Sovereign Lady!

*Katavasia:*  
In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, kindled by love of piety rather than by flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
**"Spreading out his hands..."**

*Troparia:*  
Preserving the ancestral laws of the Church, we depict the icons of Christ and of His saints, and kiss them with lips, heart, and desire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Clearly referring the honor and veneration of the icon unto the Prototype, we honor them, following the teachings of the God-proclaiming fathers, and with faith we cry unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Having illumined her mind with the enlightenment of the Divine Spirit, the honorable empress, possessing godly-minded fruits, loved the goodly dignity and beauty of the Church of Christ, blessing together with the faithful Jesus the God-man.

*Theotokion:*  
Illumined with the rays of noetic light, this thy divine house now overshadows all with the cloud of the Spirit, and sanctifies the faithful who sing with one accord: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Katavasia:*  
Spreading out his hands, Daniel stopped the mouths of lions in the pit; and girt with virtue, the children, lovers of piety, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
**"A stone cut without hands..."**

*Troparia:*  
Beholding the honorable Church again adorned with the sacredly depicted icons, with reverence let us all run thither, and let us cry aloud unto God: Thee do we magnify, O Thrice-Holy!

Having acquired Thy Cross, Thy precious icons, and the depictions of the saints as her honor and glory, the Church magnifies Thee with gladness and joy, O Master!

Illumine our sovereigns with Thy divine glory, O Merciful One, and surround them with angelic armies and with the armor of protection, subduing the fierceness of the nations before them, O Master!

*Theotokion:*  
The condemnation of Eve our foremother has been lifted, O Mother of God, for thou, O Pure One, didst ineffably give birth unto the Master of all; Whose likeness we now kiss in icons.

*Katavasia:*

*Katavasia:*  
A stone cut without hands from the unquarried mountain—which art thou, O Virgin—a cornerstone, was cut off, even Christ, Who has joined together the sundered natures; therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*The morning Resurrection Exapostilarion, once. And the present one:*  
*Podoben: "With the disciples" (Со у҆чени́ки взы́демъ):*  
Leap for joy, clap your hands, sing with gladness: How marvelous and strange are Thy works, O Christ, crying aloud; and who can recount Thy mighty acts, O Savior, Who hast joined together into one Church unity of mind and concord surpassing all?

*Theotokion:*  
The weapons of hostile heresy have now failed, and its memory has perished with a noise; for beholding thy temple, O All-Praised One, adorned in all-godly beauty with the graces of precious icons, we are all filled with joy.

*At the Praises, 4 Resurrection stichera, 1 Anatolian sticheron, and 3 prosomoia of the Triodion, Tone 4:*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́меніе):*  
In Thee now rejoices the Church, O Lover of mankind, in her Bridegroom and Creator, Who by this God-befitting will didst deliver her from idol deceit, and didst betroth her unto Thyself by Thy precious Blood, brightly receiving the sacred restoration of icons; and rejoicing, she faithfully hymns and glorifies Thee.[^382]

*Verse:* I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart, I will tell of all Thy marvelous works.  
Restoring the depiction of Thy flesh, O Lord, we lovingly kiss it, setting forth the great mystery of Thy dispensation; for not in mere appearance, as the God-fighting children of Manes say, didst Thou appear unto us, O Lover of mankind, but in truth and by the nature of flesh, being led by Thee unto Thy love and longing.

*Verse:* I will be glad and rejoice in Thee, I will sing unto Thy name, O Most High.  
A joyful day, full of gladness, has appeared today; for the brightness of the truest dogmas flashes and shines forth: the Church of Christ is adorned now with the restorations of holy icons and with the splendors of depictions, and there is a God-honored oneness of mind among the faithful.

*Then we say the verse, Tone 6:*  
Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up, forget not Thy poor unto the end.  
*And we sing the idiomelon of the day, Tone 6:*[^383]  
Moses at the time of abstinence received the Law and drew the people; Elijah by fasting shut the heavens; and the three children of Abraham overcame the lawless tyrant by fasting; through which make us also worthy, O Savior, to attain the Resurrection, crying thus: Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us!

*Glory, the same again. Both now and ever:* **"Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Mother of God..."**  
*The Great Doxology. And the Dismissal.*  
*Glory, and now and ever, the Gospel Sticheron, and the First Hour.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone, on 6; and from the Canon of the day, Ode 6, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4. Song of the Fathers:*[^384]  
Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name unto the ages.  
*Verse:* For righteous art Thou in all that Thou hast done for us.  
*The Epistle to the Hebrews, section 324 from the half (Heb. 11:24–26, 32–12:2).*  
*Alleluia, Tone 4:* Moses and Aaron among His priests, and Samuel among them that call upon His name.  
*Verse:* They called upon the Lord, and He gave ear unto them.  
*The Gospel from John, section 5 (John 1:43–51).*  
*Communion Hymn:* Praise the Lord from the heavens:  
*Another:* Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous:  
*In the refectory we eat cooked food with oil, but not fish. And if it happens, we drink wine appointed unto the glory of God, two cups; likewise also toward evening two cups.*

## On the First Sunday of the Great Fast at Vespers
*At the Lamplighting service, we set 10 verses: and we say 4 compunctionate penitential stichera of the Octoechos: and prosomoia of the Triodion, the work of Lord Joseph, Tone 4:*[^385]

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́меніе):*  
Grant me compunction, and estrangement from evil, and complete amendment, unto me who am now plunged in bodily passion and estranged from Thee, O God, King of all, having nowhere any hope; and save me, a prodigal, according to Thy manifold goodness, O all-powerful Jesus, Savior of our souls.

Marvelous Moses, having cleansed himself by fasting, beheld the Desired One; striving to emulate him, O my humble soul, make haste in the day of abstinence to cleanse thyself from evils, so that thou mayest behold the Lord Who gives thee remission, and cleansing, and deliverance, Who is good and the Lover of mankind.

*Another, by Lord Theodore, Tone 6, Podoben: "In archangelic manner" (А҆рха́гг҃льски):*  
Let us now brightly begin the second week of the fasts, completing day by day, O brethren; having made for ourselves a chariot of fire, like Elijah the Tishbite, the four great virtues; let us lift up the mind through dispassion, let us arm the flesh with purity, running and conquering the enemy.

*And of the Menaion, 3.*  
*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion.*  
*Entrance. "O gladsome Light:"*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*  
Thou hast given an inheritance unto them that fear Thee, O Lord.  
*Verse 1:* From the ends of the earth have I cried unto Thee.  
*Verse 2:* I will be sheltered under the covert of Thy wings.  
*Verse 3:* So will I chant unto Thy name unto ages of ages.  
*And again:* Thou hast given an inheritance unto them that fear Thee:  
*It is proper to know that these two Great Prokeimena, namely: "Turn not away Thy face:" and "Thou hast given an inheritance:" with their verses, are sung alternately one after another throughout the entire Holy Fast: we begin from Cheesefare Sunday unto the Fifth Sunday of the Fast.*

*And at "Vouchsafe, O Lord:" The bows begin.*

*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 8:*[^386]  
Come, let us cleanse ourselves by almsgiving and compassion for the poor, not sounding a trumpet, nor showing off our benevolence, lest the left hand know what the right hand does, lest vainglory scatter the fruit of almsgiving; but in secret let us cry unto Him Who knows secrets: Father, forgive us our trespasses, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Martyrikon:*  
O Martyrs of the Lord, ye sanctify every place, and cure every disease; and now pray that our souls may be delivered from the snares of the enemy, we beseech you.

*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion:*  
The heavenly hosts hymn thee, O Mother full of grace, who hast not known wedlock; and we glorify thine unsearchable childbearing, O Mother of God; pray that our souls may be saved.

*And the rest of the order, as usual.*

## On the First Sunday of the Great Fast at Compline
*We sing the Canon to the holy Prophets, whose acrostic is:*[^387]  
*Let the choir of the Prophets clap tunefully.*  
*The work of Theophanes, Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
**"The horse and the rider into the Red Sea..."**

*Troparia:*  
Implore Christ, the Cause of all, to grant me the grace of the Holy Spirit, as I hymn your venerable memory, and remission of transgressions, as ye are the prophets of divine grace.

Harmonious vessels of God-inspired words, and mirrors of divine flashing, receiving divine revelation, let us hymn all the God-wise prophets.

Receiving the words of the Law from God upon Mount Sinai, Moses the God-beholder was enlightened in face with the vision of God, marvelous by grace with signs.

*Glory:*  
With showers of the knowledge of God, the clouds of God by grace have noisily cast down the sea of polytheism among the gentiles, godly-mindedly proclaiming the One Sovereign Power.

*Both now and ever...*

*Both now and ever: Theotokion.*  
As God-proclaiming trumpets, the prophets proclaim thee, O All-Pure One, as the Mother of God, who at the end of the ages gavest birth, having been taught from on high the divine mysteries concerning thee.[^388]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
**"Who didst establish upon nothing..."**[^389]

*Troparia:*  
As spiritual harps struck by the plectrum, all the prophets, sounding forth in God-inspired voice the fitting sound of the knowledge of Thee, O Christ, sing unto those who love God, enriching the radiance from on high.

Flashing forth as weapons at Thy command, all the prophets cut down the tumultuous deceit, O Christ, devoutly clothed with Thy divine power and with Thine unsearchable might.

*Glory:*  
As divine and noetic chalices of understanding, the prophets inebriated the whole earth, clearly prefiguring the hard-to-comprehend mystery of Thy dispensation, each in a different manner proclaiming Thee.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
The mystery commanded of old, O Virgin, and foreknown before the ages by God Who knows all things, having reached its fulfillment in the latter time within thy womb, O All-Immaculate One, has been revealed.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
**"Thy divine..."**[^390]

*Troparia:*  
Speaking by the Spirit, ye appeared as undeceived beacons of truth, O all-wise prophets, enlightening all the nations with the brightness of your manner of life.

Beholding now the fulfillment of the things spoken by you, O divine prophets, we glorify Him Who was proclaimed by you and Who fulfilled the prophecies in deed.

*Glory:*  
Enlightened by divine wisdom, O divine ones, by God's grace ye foresaw the bringing in of the Gentiles, the fall of Israel, and the protection of all good things.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Beholding God incarnate, having stood upon the divine watchtower, ye were accounted worthy, O all-opulent ones, to see Him coming from the Mountain, the Virgin.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
**"Thou that clothest Thyself with light..."**[^391]

*Troparia:*  
Thy spirit rose early at dawn unto Him Who sits upon the exalted throne, O all-wise Isaiah, and with the choirs on high thou didst sing praise.

Moving your tongues by the Spirit, O holy ones, steadfastly preaching His words unto the inhabited earth, ye made luminous the dawn of the knowledge of God.

*Glory:*  
Having become dwellings of God, Who works things surpassing nature, ye have Him within yourselves; and standing now before Him, pray ye for us.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
His all-wise counsel and ancient decree has God revealed unto you, O prophets of truth, being born of the Virgin, as He alone is merciful.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
**"Raging with the tempest..."**[^392]

*Troparia:*  
Having acquired a godly manner of life and the true word of piety, ye became most true preachers of the divine incarnation, O holy ones.

Possessing divine power, ye curbed the unrestrained onset of kings and stopped the mouths of wild beasts, and unharmed were delivered from the belly of the whale.

*Glory:*  
Shining forth from the womb upon the whole world, O holy ones, ye were teachers and planters of virtue, all ye most opulent prophets.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Possessing thoughts that behold God, the prophets, O Sovereign Lady, foreannounced thy childbirth in manifold depictions and types, O Pure One.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Automelon:*[^393]  
The prophetic choir with Moses and Aaron rejoices today with gladness, as the end of prophecy arrives: the Cross shines forth, whereby Thou hast saved us; through their prayers, O Christ God, have mercy on us.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
**"The supremely exalted God of our fathers..."**[^394]

*Troparia:*  
The unsearchable power of the Lord's bidding bridled the lions in the pit, as the prophet cried: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Glory:*  
Shining as beacons, having perfumed the earth with the blossoms of virtue, O offspring of Abraham, the prophets sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Thou didst transcend the laws of nature, O Sovereign Lady, having given birth unto the Lord Who rules over the laws, unto Whom we all sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
**"Unto Thee, the Creator of all..."**[^395]

*Troparia:*  
Being within the delight of Paradise, O God-welcoming prophets, having formed a choir, implore Christ to deliver from tribulations all who praise you with faith.

Let us the faithful offer song unto all the God-proclaiming prophets, all of us having filled out the choir, having been strengthened by their words, that through their prayers we may be justified.

*Glory:*  
Ye all clearly became noetic vessels of sweet fragrance for the Spirit, O prophets, instructing all the nations unto understanding, and supremely exalting Christ unto the ages.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
The unoriginate Dawn from the Father hast thou borne, O Virgin, through the assumption of flesh; therefore: Rejoice! we unceasingly cry unto thee, and we supremely exalt thee, O Pure One, unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
**"Rejoice, O Isaiah..."**[^396]

*Troparia:*  
In oneness of mind, come, let us now praise all the all-venerable prophets of God, workers of supernatural wonders, the honorable branch, the chosen race of Jacob, as they who proclaimed Christ.

*Troparion:*  
Heralds of worldwide joy, manifest preachers, and treasures of understanding: ye were divine beacons of the most true wisdom, venerable lips, all-honored tongues of the Holy Spirit.

*Glory:*  
As all-wise prophets, beholding the Creator and God of all as mighty, and illuminated with light from Him, deliver from tribulations those who devoutly honor your memory with faith.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Thou becamest the mediatress of salvation unto men, O Pure One, having bodily given birth unto the Creator and Lord, Who for thy sake grants cleansing unto all who magnify thee, O Virgin.

*Then stichera to the Prophets, Tone 1. Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мч҃нцы):*[^397]  
All-glorious prophets, ye rebuked kings and withstood insolent peoples, preaching the One God in Trinity, and ardently driving away polytheistic deceit from the earth; and now pray ye that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

All-glorious prophets, devoutly wandering in deserts and mountains, and suffering affliction in hunger and thirst as pleasing servants of God, ye now dwell with Christ in the heavens; and now pray ye that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

All-praised prophets, patiently sawn asunder, pelted with stones, and put to death by the stroke of the sword, for piety's sake ye became radiant sufferers; and now pray ye that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

*Glory, and now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Truly beyond comprehension are the marvelous splendors of thy Childbirth, O Bride of God, which all the prophets proclaimed: the all-glorious Conception and all-hymned, inscrutable, and ineffable Childbirth, whereby the world has been saved.

## On Monday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Matins[^398]

*After the 1st Kathisma-verse, the compunctionate Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos, and the Theotokion.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma-verse, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^399]  
Me who am slain in soul by the fruit of transgression, make alive, O Christ, through true fasting and repentance, I pray Thee, as Thou art compassionate; and grant me ever to walk in the right and good paths of Thy venerable commandments, that I may attain Thy divine glory, and with all who love Thee glorify Thy goodness in all things, O Jesus.

*Glory, the same. Both now and ever: Theotokion. Podoben:*  
Surged about by the billows of passions, obscure and tempest-tossed, O Pure One, I warmly call upon thee: despise me not, a wretched one, that I perish not, O thou who gavest birth unto the abyss of mercy; for apart from thee I have gained no hope, lest I who trust in thee should become an object of rejoicing and laughter unto enemies; for thou canst do whatsoever thou desirest, being the Mother of the God of all.

*After the 3rd Kathisma-verse, Sessional Hymn, Tone 6. Podoben: "Lord, have mercy on us" (Гдⷭ҇и поми́лꙋй на́съ):*  
O Lord, guide us who are repeating the fast, shine forth upon us the sanctification and illumination of Thy statutes; and account us worthy to offer unto Thee acceptable prayer, as we bend our knees; for Thou art our Father, and we are Thy sons, and with fear we hymn Thee, and we call upon Thy name.

*Glory, the same. Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
The beginning of salvation was Gabriel's greeting unto the Virgin; for hearing "Rejoice!", she rejected not the greeting, nor did she doubt like Sarah in the tent, but thus she spoke: Behold the handmaid of the Lord, be it unto me according to thy word.

*Canon of the Menaion, and the Triodion three-canto ode by Lord Joseph.*[^400]  
*And the canticles are chanted.*

*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:*  
**"The mighty captains..."**[^401]

*Troparia:*  
O Thou Who alone art good, Fount of mercy, Lamb of God Who as God takest away the sins of the world: save me who am submerged in the surging waves of sins, guiding me unto the haven of repentance.

A pure fast, departure from sin, estrangement from passions, love toward God, diligence in prayer, tears with compunction, and care for the poor, Christ has enjoined in the Scriptures.

Wounded in soul by the weapon of sin, and stricken in heart with bitter sores, O Physician of our souls, heal me as Benefactor, applying unto me the remedies of Thy wise commandments, O Lover of mankind.

*Theotokion:*  
O undefiled tabernacle, most holy Virgin, cleanse me who am defiled by the assaults of grievous passions with the fountain of thy mercy; and grant me the shower of compunction, O Sovereign Lady, consuming the flame of sin.

*Another canon, by Lord Theodore, Tone 2. Heirmos:*[^402]  
**"He was my helper and protector..."**

*Troparia:*  
Having received as festal joy also the second week of the light-bearing fasts, O brethren, let us sing with love unto Christ, divinely making merry.

Having found the fasting season now to be a time of compunction, let us weep bitterly and sigh, and let us lift up our hands unto the only Deliverer, that He may save our souls.

*Trinity Hymn:*  
O Tri-hypostatic Unity, most sovereign Lord of all, all-perfect Principle, pre-originate Principle: do Thou Thyself save us, O Father, Son, and Most Holy Spirit!

*Theotokion:*  
Who has borne an unwaning Son by the Father's law? Him indeed the Father begets without a mother: O all-glorious wonder! For thou hast given birth, O Pure One, unto Him Who is at once God and man.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
How shall I endure Thy wrath, O my Christ, when Thou comest unto judgment? What word shall I find to offer there, having neither accomplished nor fulfilled Thy will, O Christ? Therefore grant me remission before the end.

*Katavasia:*  
He was my helper and protector unto salvation; He is my God, and I will glorify Him, the God of my father, and I will exalt Him; for gloriously has He been glorified.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
**"O Redeemer of all..."**[^403]

*Troparia:*  
Passing through the instability of life, I fell among thievish thoughts and, being wounded, my sores grew foul: O Physician of the ailing, give me Thy hand, through the supplications of all Thy saints.

The storm of grievous sin troubles my mind; as Peter, save me, O Jesus, as I sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works, sing praise unto the Lord.

*Troparion:*  
By abstinence let us mortify the passions, by fasting let us wing the spirit unto heaven, and let us cry out with compunction of heart: We have sinned against Thee, O God, as Thou art compassionate, forgive!

*Theotokion:*  
The Most High, having taken up His abode within thy womb, showed thee to be more spacious than the heavens, and an unconquerable intercession for those who cry aloud, O Virgin: Bless the Lord, all ye works, sing praise unto the Lord.

*Another canon. Heirmos:*  
**"Whom the heavenly hosts glorify..."**[^404]

*Troparia:*  
Show my soul not to be enslaved unto the tyranny of passions, O Lord, that freely doing Thy will I may rejoice, and glorify Thy power unto the ages.

Abhor the intemperance of Esau, O my soul, and emulate the good deeds of Jacob; impoverish Belial through abstinence, enrich thyself with divine things, and sing praise unto the ages.

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
One God according to essence do I revere; three Hypostases do I sing distinctively, another and another, yet not different in nature, since the Godhead is One, and one is the dominion in the Three: the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Coming forth from thy radiant womb as a bridegroom from his chamber, Christ shone forth, a great light unto those in darkness; for as the Sun of Righteousness He flashed forth and enlightened the world, O Pure One.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
No one who drank water became demented, yet Noah appeared naked, being drunken with wine; and the seeds of evil Lot hence brought forth: flee, O my soul, from imitating them, as thou singest unto Christ.

*Katavasia:*  
Whom the heavenly hosts glorify, and Cherubim and Seraphim tremble before, let every breath and creation sing, bless, and supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
**"Eve, indeed, through the disease..."**[^405]

*Troparia:*  
Let us fast from the passions of wrath, let us delight in unfeigned love, having fed the poor with bread, being nourished with divine grace; and with tears let us quench the tears of future condemnation.

O wretched soul, sigh, lament, show forth types of repentance; the day is now at the doors, the Judge sits without respect of persons, rendering unto each what is worthy of his deeds, as it is written.

I have not acquired good deeds, I have defiled the garment which I received of divine baptism; I walk in the clothing of darkness and ignorance: with the light of Thy countenance, O Lord, set me upright now, despairing though I am.

*Theotokion:*  
O Mother of the Lord of all, set me free who am enslaved by every sin, O Virgin, who hast given birth unto the Deliverer of our souls; for beyond word hast thou borne the substantial Goodness.

*Another canon. Heirmos:*  
**"The ineffable Childbirth of a seedless conception..."**[^406]

*Troparia:*  
Let us suffer hardship now as soldiers of Christ; for as time passes away, crowns are woven for those who strive, which Christ will bestow when He comes with angelic glory to judge all the earth.

*Troparion:*  
All wounded am I, all covered with scabs, O my Christ; heal my festering sores, which are the passionate bites of sin, that like the leper I may hear Thy holy voice: "I will; be thou cleansed!"

*Trinity Hymn:*  
O Begetter of the Only-Begotten, Father of the Only-Begotten Son; and O sole Light of the One, Radiance of Light; and O sole Holy Spirit of the One God, truly being Lord of the Lord: O Holy Trinity, Unity, save me who theologize Thee!

*Theotokion:*  
The wonder of thy Childbirth astounds me, O All-Immaculate One: how dost thou conceive the Incomprehensible One without seed? Tell me, how remainest thou a virgin, having given birth as a Mother? Receiving that which is beyond nature with faith, let us worship Him Who was born; for whatsoever He wills, He also can do.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Fasting made Moses a God-beholder, and of old made Elijah a fiery charioteer, and Paul, O my soul, an awesome heaven-traverser; therefore let us fast from evils, and let us also be caught up unto the height of dispassion.

*Katavasia:*  
The ineffable Childbirth of a seedless conception, the incorrupt fruit of a husbandless Mother: for the birth of God renews natures; therefore all generations, as the Mother of God and unwedded Bride, orthodoxly magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion of the Tone:*  
*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon sticheron, Tone 5:*[^407]  
A marvelous weapon is prayer and fasting: this showed Moses to be a writer of the Law, and Elijah a zealot in sacrifices; abiding therein, O faithful, let us cry aloud unto the Savior: Against Thee only have we sinned, have mercy on us!  
*Twice.*

*Martyrikon:*  
O blessed army of the King of Heaven! For even though the passion-bearers were earthborn, yet they hastened to attain the angelic dignity, taking no care for their bodies, and through their sufferings were accounted worthy of the honor of the incorporeal ones; through their prayers, O Lord, save our souls.

*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion:*  
Unto thee do we pray as unto the Mother of God, O Blessed One: pray for the salvation of our souls.

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 5:*[^408]  
O Thou Who alone didst fashion our hearts, remember that we are dust; condemn us not unto the nethermost parts of the earth, O Sinless One!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 21 (LXX), Tone 6:*[^409]  
Ye that fear the Lord, praise Him.  
*Verse:* O God, my God, attend unto me; why hast Thou forsaken me?

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isa. 4:2–5:7):*[^410]  
Thus says the Lord: In that day God will shine forth in counsel with glory upon the earth, to exalt and glorify the remnant of Israel. And the remnant in Zion, and the remnant in Jerusalem: holy shall all be called that are written unto life in Jerusalem. For the Lord will wash away the filth of the sons and daughters of Zion, and will purge the blood of Jerusalem from their midst by the spirit of judgment and by the spirit of burning. And the Lord will come, and every place of Mount Zion and all that is round about it will be overshadowed by a cloud by day, and as of smoke and of the light of a burning fire by night, and by all the glory shall it be covered. And it will be for a shade from the heat, and for a shelter and a hiding place from harshness and rain. Now will I sing unto the Beloved a song of my Beloved concerning my vineyard: My Beloved had a vineyard on a horn, in a fertile place. And I made a hedge about it, and dug a trench, and planted a choice vine, and built a tower in the midst thereof, and dug a wine vat therein; and I waited for it to bring forth grapes, and it brought forth thorns. And now, ye that dwell in Jerusalem, and men of Judah, judge between Me and My vineyard. What more should I have done for My vineyard that I have not done for it? Because I waited for it to bring forth grapes, and it brought forth thorns. And now I will declare unto you what I will do to My vineyard: I will take away the hedge thereof, and it shall be for a plunder; and I will break down its wall, and it shall be for a trampling. And I will forsake My vineyard, and it shall not be pruned, nor dug, and thorns shall spring up upon it as upon a waste; and I will command the clouds that they rain no rain upon it. For the vineyard of the Lord of Sabaoth is the house of Israel, and the man of Judah is a new, beloved planting.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 22 (LXX), Tone 6:*[^411]  
Thy rod and Thy staff, they have comforted me.  
*Verse:* The Lord is my shepherd, and I shall want nothing.

## On Monday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Vespers[^412]

*At the Lamplighting service, after the introductory psalm, we chant the Kathisma: "Unto the Lord..." (Kathisma 18, Pss. 119–133).*  
*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and sing the prosomoia stichera of the Triodion.*

*The work of Lord Joseph, Tone 8. Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что́ вы́ нарече́мъ):*[^413]  
Unto whom hast thou become likened, O wretched and wretched soul, doing no works, but ever loving to abide in evils? The Judge is at the doors; repent, cleanse thyself by fasting and prayer, and cry aloud unto thy Master: I have sinned against Thee, forgive as Thou art tender-hearted, and grant me amendment!

*Troparion:*  
Possessing an abyss of goodness, dry up the surging abysses of my sins, O Christ, as Thou art the sinless God, and grant compunction unto my heart, washing away the torrents of lawlessness, that I may sing and glorify Thine ineffable tender-heartedness, O longsuffering Benefactor of our souls.

*Another sticheron, by Lord Theodore, Tone 8. Podoben: "In Eden of old" (Въ Е҆де́мѣ ра́й):*[^414]  
O God, Who hast brought us again to complete the cycle of time of the all-venerable fast, and hast granted us to enter upon the holy stadium of the second week: do Thou Thyself account us worthy, O Lord, also to run well into the future, benefiting and strengthening both our body and soul, so that having manfully finished our course, we may all attain unto the Lord's Day of Thy Resurrection in joy, and bearing crowns, may unceasingly praise Thee.

*And of the Menaion, 3.*  
*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 23 (LXX), Tone 6:*[^415]  
The Lord is strong and mighty, the Lord mighty in battle.  
*Verse:* The earth is the Lord's, and the fullness thereof.

*Reading from Genesis (Gen. 3:21–4:7):*[^416]  
The Lord God made for Adam and for his wife coats of skins, and clothed them. And God said: Behold, Adam is become as one of Us, to know good and evil; and now, lest he put forth his hand, and take also of the tree of life, and eat, and live unto the ages. And the Lord God sent him forth from the paradise of delight, to till the ground from which he was taken. And He cast out Adam, and caused him to dwell over against the paradise of delight; and He stationed the Cherubim, and a flaming sword that turned about, to keep the way of the tree of life. And Adam knew Eve his wife, and she conceived and bore Cain, and said: I have gotten a man through God. And again she bore his brother Abel. And Abel was a keeper of sheep, but Cain was a tiller of the ground. And in process of time it came to pass that Cain brought of the fruits of the ground a sacrifice unto God. And Abel also brought, even he, of the firstlings of his sheep, and of their fat. And God had respect unto Abel and unto his gifts; but unto Cain and unto his sacrifices He had no regard. And Cain was exceedingly sorrowful, and his countenance fell. And the Lord God said unto Cain: Why art thou sorrowful, and why has thy countenance fallen? If thou hast offered rightly, but hast not rightly divided, hast thou not sinned? Be silent: unto thee is his turning, and thou shalt rule over him.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 24 (LXX), Tone 6:*[^417]  
Look upon me, and have mercy on me.  
*Verse:* Unto Thee, O Lord, have I lifted up my soul.

*Reading from Proverbs (Prov. 3:34–4:22, beginning):*[^418]  
The Lord resists the proud, but gives grace unto the humble. The wise shall inherit glory, but the ungodly have exalted dishonor. Hear, ye children, the instruction of a father, and attend that ye may know understanding. For I give you a good gift; forsake ye not my law. For I also was an obedient son unto my father, and beloved before the face of my mother; who spoke and taught me: Let our word be established in thine heart; keep commandments, forget them not. Acquire wisdom, acquire understanding; forget not, neither disregard...

...the sayings of my mouth, neither turn aside from the words of my mouth. Forsake her not, and she will cleave unto thee; love her, and she will keep thee. The beginning of wisdom is: acquire wisdom, and in all thine acquiring acquire understanding. Enclose her, and she will exalt thee; honor her, that she may embrace thee; that she may give unto thine head a crown of graces, and with a crown of delight she will shield thee. Hear, O son, and receive my words, and the years of thy life shall be multiplied, that the paths of thy life may be many. For I teach thee the ways of wisdom, and I guide thee into right tracks. For if thou walkest, thy steps shall not be hindered; and if thou runnest, thou shalt not be weary. Take hold of my instruction, forsake it not, but keep it for thyself unto thy life. Go not in the ways of the ungodly, neither envy the paths of the lawless. In whatever place they assemble, go not thither; turn aside from them, and pass away. For they sleep not, unless they have done evil; sleep is taken away from them, and they sleep not. For they eat the bread of wickedness, and are drunken with the wine of lawlessness. But the paths of the righteous shine like light: they go forth and shine, until the day is established. But the ways of the ungodly are dark: they know not how they stumble. My son, attend unto my speech, and unto my words incline thine ear; that thy fountains fail thee not, keep them in thine heart; for they are life unto all that find them, and healing unto all their flesh.

*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon of the day, Tone 3, twice:*[^419]  
Let us fast with a spiritual fast, let us tear asunder every perversity, let us depart from the stumbling blocks of sin, and let us forgive our brethren their debts, that our trespasses may also be forgiven us; for so shall we be able to cry aloud: Let our prayer be set forth as incense before Thee, O Lord!

*Martyrikon:*  
The prophets, and apostles of Christ, and martyrs taught men to sing of the Consubstantial Trinity, and enlightened the wandering nations, and made the sons of men partakers with the angels.

*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion, Tone 3:*  
Forget not Thy poor unto the end, O Sovereign Lady: but by thy prayers deliver us from future condemnation, and from present harm, and snatch thy servants away from the wrath of the Lord, O Mother of God.

*Mark this: Be it known that from this Monday of the second week of the fast, there are sung at Compline the canons from the Menaion of the saints whose commemorations occur from Lazarus Saturday until Thomas Sunday, together with the canons to the Mother of God. First the canon of the Mother of God is sung, and after it that of the Menaion. After Ode 3, the Kontakion and Sessional Hymn of the Menaion; and after Ode 6, the Sessional Hymn of the Mother of God. And after the canon, the stichera of the Menaion.*[^420]

## On Tuesday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Matins[^421]

*The Trinity Hymns.*  
*After the first reading of the Psalter, the penitential sessional hymns of the Octoechos, with their Theotokia.*  
*After the second reading of the Psalter, we sing the following sessional hymns, Tone 8.*  

*Tone 8, Podoben: "Thou hast risen" (Воскре́слъ є҆сѝ):*[^422]  
Restraining the passions with the bridle of the blameless fast, let us all strive to wing the mind unto honorable divine visions with most perfect faith, that we may despise the earthly sweetness of life, and obtain heavenly life and divine radiance.

*Glory: the same. Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Into a grievous abyss of despondency have I fallen, from the multitude of my deceitful and lawless deeds; I have come into perplexity, and am now held fast by sorrow. O Sovereign Lady, Mother of God, do thou save me, do thou help me; for thou art the propitiation of sinners and saving cleansing.

*After the third reading of the Psalter, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "The shepherd's pipe" (Па́стырскаѧ свирѣ́ль):*[^423]  
Having set forth the fast as a mystical table, He urges us richly to be filled: let us eat the ever-living gifts of the Spirit as food; let us drink the God-flowing stream of tears as drink, and rejoicing, let us unceasingly offer praise unto God.

*Glory: the same. Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
From every threat and affliction of man save us, O all-hymned Virgin; for we who flee unto thee, O Mother of God, and unto our God Who was born of thee, possess thee as protection and intercession: do thou entreat Him that we may be delivered from tribulations and afflictions.

*The Canon from the Menaion, and the Triodion of Lord Joseph.*  
*And the biblical ode from the Psalter is read.*  

*Ode 2, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^424]  
**"See, see that I am your God..."**  

*Troparia:*  
Sigh, weep, O my soul, turn back, repent: the day draws near, the Judge is at the doors; make ready for the defense, and cry aloud: I have sinned against Thee, O Compassionate, Merciful, and Good One, have compassion on me!

*Troparia:*  
Having come to hate the all-destroying frenzy of sin, O my soul, take delight diligently in the fullness of the fast; make the saving commandments thy food, which procure through faith the delight of eternal good things.

Ye Angels, Powers, Principalities, Archangels, Dominions, Thrones, Authorities, Cherubim, and Seraphim: pray ye to the Giver of grace and God, that He grant unto us forgiveness of debts and turning away of passions.

*Theotokion:*  
O all-immaculate one, who alone didst bear the unoriginate Word incarnate of thee without change, remaining after childbirth the unwedded Virgin: pray thou for all, that our life may be delivered from corruption.

*Another canon, by Lord Theodore, same Tone. Heirmos: "See, see:"*[^425]  
We have seen, we have known, that the season which Thou hast appointed for us is good for repentance: but in this season, O God, receive our prayers, and direct them before Thee, O Compassionate One, as good incense, as an acceptable sacrifice.

I am terrified and afraid when I consider how much I have sinned: how shall I behold Thee? How shall I appear before Thee, O Terrible One? And how shall I stand before Thy tribunal which cannot be bribed? Therefore spare me, O Compassionate One, in the hour when Thou shalt judge all the earth!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
O All-Perfect Unity, Supremely Divine, in Three Hypostases: Unbegotten Father, and Only-Begotten Son, and Spirit proceeding from the Father, yet made manifest through the Son: One Substance and Nature, Dominion, Kingdom, save us all!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
The wonder of thy childbearing is ineffable, O Mother and Virgin: for how dost thou both give birth, and yet remainest pure; how dost thou bear an Infant, and knowest no experience of man at all? Even as He knows Who was incarnate of me surpassing nature: the Word of God Who was born.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Thou didst deliver the Ninevites who repented and fasted from the threatened wrath; but the Sodomites who strayed in satiety Thou didst burn with fire: yet deliver me, O Christ, from their condemnation!

*Heirmos:*  
See, see that I am your God, Who established for you an acceptable first-fruits, a tithing of the days of the whole year; and this have I given unto My people for the deliverance from passions, as the beginning of salvation.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^426]  
**"The God-proclaiming children..."**  

*Troparia:*  
O Thou Who didst justify the publican when he sighed, Who didst have compassion on the harlot weeping from her heart: save and have compassion, O Savior, on the souls of them that sing to Thee!

Let us bring forth fruitful deeds of virtue unto the Lord by fasting, departing from harmful thoughts, that we may obtain divine sweetness.

The choirs of angels, the assemblies of apostles, and the ranks of martyrs entreat Thee, O Jesus: grant Thou forgiveness unto Thy people!

*Theotokion:*  
The unquarried mountain, the Virgin, the light-bearing lampstand, the easily traversed ladder, the dwelling-place of God: let us the faithful bless her!

*Another canon [by St. Theodore]. Heirmos: "Upon the holy mountain:"*[^427]  
On the appointed day of abstinence, come, let us all sound the trumpet in songs: and as those feasting upon divine sweetness, let us sing in hymns unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all the ages!

Who consumed the man of God of old? A fierce wild beast, when through the deceit of the false prophet he partook of the food of disobedience: take heed therefore, O my soul, lest the serpent of gluttony deceive thee!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
As Unity in essence I sing of Thee; as Trinity in Persons I honor Thee, O Father, and Son, and Most Holy Spirit: the unoriginate dominion of Thy Kingdom I glorify unto the ages!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Thou hast appeared as the Mountain of God, O Mother of God, in which Christ dwelt and made those who sing divine temples: Sing ye unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all the ages!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Honoring the fast as the guardian of purity and the measure of dispassion, come, ye people, let us embrace it with love, singing unto the Lord, and supremely exalting Him unto all the ages!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord.*  
*Heirmos:*  
Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and in the bush through fire revealed the mystery of the Ever-Virgin unto Moses: sing ye unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^428]  
**"That which was prefigured on the mountain..."**  

*Troparia:*  
The good fast that enriches the heart makes the ripe ear of virtues divinely spring forth: let us love it, receiving the gathering of the holy days.

Beholding me defiled in many sins, bound fast and condemned, O Merciful One, have compassion through Thy mercy, and save me by the prayers of Thy saints!

Woe is me, O all-wretched soul! How wilt thou answer? What fear shall seize thee when the Judge sits and ten thousand times ten thousand angels stand before Him? Strive therefore: before the end, repent!

*Theotokion:*  
Chariot of the King, bright cloud, fruitful mountain, curdled mountain, O thou who knewest not wedlock: we pray thee, O Virgin, heal the passions of our souls!

*The same Heirmos.*  
*Troparia:*  
Let us fast with a true fast unto the Lord, as we abstain from foods, keeping ourselves distant from the tongue, wrath, falsehood, and every other passion, that we may purely behold Pascha.

Thou didst cause Samuel to blossom as fruit, O fast; thou didst nurture Samson the great and valiant hero; thou makest priests and prophets complete: do thou sanctify us, O honorable fast!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
O Lord, with the bright radiances of Thy Thrice-Radiant Monarchy shine upon our mind, that from manifold deceit we may turn unto the unitive deification.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Thou art our weapon and wall, O Mother of God; thou art the protection of those who flee unto thee; thee do we now move unto prayer, that we may be delivered from our enemies.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Possessing the Holy Spirit as the rich host of the fast, let us be filled with His gifts and richly take delight, praising Him as our God.

*Heirmos:*  
The Childbirth of the Ever-Virgin, which was prefigured on the mountain unto the Lawgiver in the fire and the bush, for the salvation of us the faithful, with unceasing hymns we magnify.

*Exapostilarion of the Tone.*  

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, Tone 5:*[^429]  
Why dost thou despond, O my soul, serving sin? And why, being sick, dost thou not hasten unto the Physician? Behold the acceptable time, behold now the true day of salvation: arise, wash thy face with tears of repentance, and illuminate thy lamp with the oil of well-doing, that thou mayest find cleansing from Christ God, and great mercy.  
*(Twice.)*  

*For the Martyrs:*  
Thy passion-bearers, O Lord, having imitated the angelic ranks as if bodiless, endured tortures, possessing a unanimous hope, the enjoyment of promised good things: by their prayers, O Christ our God, grant peace unto Thy world, and unto our souls great mercy.

*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion:*  
We bless thee, O Virgin Mother of God, and we the faithful glorify thee as is due: the unshakable city, the impregnable wall, the firm protectress, and the refuge of our souls.

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 6:*[^430]  
We are not able worthily to hymn Thee; but we pray and entreat: destroy us not with our lawlessnesses, O Lord, Who art easily reconciled!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 25 (LXX), Tone 4:*[^431]  
O Lord, I have loved the beauty of Thine house, and the place where Thy glory dwells.  
*Verse:* Judge me...

*Verse:* Judge me, O Lord, for I have walked in my blamelessness.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isa. 5:7–16):*[^432]  
Thus says the Lord: The vineyard of the Lord of Sabaoth is the house of Israel, and the man of Judah a new and beloved plant: I waited for it to execute judgment, but it executed lawlessness, and not righteousness, but a cry. Woe unto those who join house to house, and bring field near to field, that they may take away something from their neighbor: shall ye dwell alone upon the earth? For these things were heard in the ears of the Lord of Sabaoth: For though houses be many, into desolation shall great and good houses become, and there shall be none dwelling in them. For where ten yoke of oxen plow, it shall yield one jar; and he that sows six artabae shall reap three measures. Woe unto those that rise up early in the morning and follow strong drink, who wait until evening: for the wine shall inflame them. For with harp, and psaltery, and timbrels, and pipes they drink wine; but upon the works of the Lord they look not, and the works of His hands they consider not. Therefore My people have been taken captive, because they know not the Lord; and there was a multitude of dead because of famine and thirst for water. And Hades enlarged its soul, and opened its mouth without ceasing; and down shall go their glorious, and their great, and their rich, and their destroyers, and he that rejoices therein. And man shall be brought low, and a man shall be dishonored, and the eyes that look high shall be humbled. And the Lord of Sabaoth shall be exalted in judgment, and the holy God shall be glorified in righteousness.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 26 (LXX), Tone 3:*[^433]  
The Lord is my illumination and my Savior; whom shall I fear?  
*Verse:* The Lord is the defender of my life; of whom shall I be afraid?

## On Tuesday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Vespers[^434]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of Lord Joseph, Tone 1. Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*[^435]  
Having stretched out Thine all-pure hands upon the Cross, O Christ, gathering the ends of the earth, therefore I cry unto Thee: gather my scattered mind, taken captive and dragged along by the passions, and show me to be a partaker of Thy sufferings, cleansed of all through abstinence.

Fasting sharpened the children of old, and showed them stronger than the burning fire, as it is written: O my humble soul, fast thou, kindling within thyself the Master's love, whereby thou shalt be able to escape the future Gehenna and burn up all-destructive passions.

*Another sticheron, of Lord Theodore, Tone 3. Podoben: "O valiant martyrs" (До́блїи мꙋ́ченицы):*[^436]  
The time of the fast is cheerful; therefore, having been richly filled with light-like purity, and pure love, radiant prayer, and every other virtue, let us brightly cry aloud: O Cross of...

...Christ, the honorable one, that hast made the sweetness of life to grow: account us all worthy to worship thee with a pure heart, granting unto us cleansing and great mercy.

*And of the Menaion, 3.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 27 (LXX), Tone 6:*[^437]  
Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance.  
*Verse:* Unto Thee, O Lord, will I cry, O my God.

*Reading from Genesis (Gen. 4:8–15):*[^438]  
Cain said unto Abel his brother: Let us go out into the field. And it came to pass, when they were in the field, that Cain rose up against Abel his brother, and slew him. And the Lord God said unto Cain: Where is Abel thy brother? And he said: I know not; am I my brother's keeper? And the Lord said: What hast thou done? The voice of thy brother's blood cries unto Me from the ground. And now cursed art thou from the earth, which has opened its mouth to receive the blood of thy brother from thy hand. When thou tillest the ground, it shall not add to give its strength unto thee: groaning and trembling shalt thou be upon the earth. And Cain said unto the Lord God: My guilt is greater than can be forgiven me. If Thou drivest me out this day from the face of the earth, and from Thy face I shall be hidden, and I shall be groaning and trembling upon the earth, then it will come to pass that everyone that finds me will kill me. And the Lord God said unto him: Not so; whosoever slays Cain, vengeance shall be taken on him sevenfold. And the Lord God set a mark upon Cain, lest anyone finding him should kill him.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 28 (LXX), Tone 7:*[^439]  
The Lord will give strength unto His people; the Lord will bless His people with peace.  
*Verse:* Bring unto the Lord, ye sons of God.

*Reading from Proverbs (Prov. 5:1–15):*[^440]  
My son, attend unto my wisdom, and apply thine ear unto my words, that thou mayest keep good counsel; and the sense of my lips gives commandment unto thee. Give no heed unto an evil woman: for honey drops from the lips of an harlot woman, who for a season pleases thy palate; but afterward thou shalt find her more bitter than gall, and sharper than a two-edged sword. For the feet of folly lead down those who deal with her with death into Hades, and her steps are not established; for she enters not upon the paths of life, and her ways are straying and unwise. Now therefore, my son, hear me, and make not my words hidden: remove thy way far from her, and come not near unto the doors of her houses, lest she give thy life unto others, and thy living unto the unmerciful; lest strangers be filled with thy strength, and thy labors enter into the houses of strangers; and thou repent in thine end, when the flesh of thy body is consumed, and say: How have I hated instruction, and mine heart declined from reproofs? I heard not the voice of him that instructed me, and inclined not mine ear unto him that taught me; I was well-nigh in all evil in the midst of the church and congregation. My son, drink waters out of thine own vessels, and from the spring of thine own wells.

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 3:*[^441]  
O Lord Who wast crucified in the flesh, and didst crucify with Thyself our old man, and wast pierced with a spear in Thy side, and didst pierce through the serpent, the slayer of men: nail my flesh unto Thy fear, and wound my soul with Thy love, that contemplating Thy Passion, I may with abstinence accomplish the appointment of the fast, restraining not only the belly, but also the other entrances of sin; and sacrifice unto Thee contrition of heart and humility of spirit for my former transgressions, from which do Thou deliver me, O Lover of mankind!

*For the Martyrs:*  
The prophets, and apostles of Christ, and martyrs taught men to sing of the Consubstantial Trinity, and enlightened the wandering nations, and made the sons of men partakers with the angels.

*Glory, and now and ever, Stavrotheotokion. Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy martyrs" (Ве́лїѧ мꙋ́ченикъ):*[^442]  
Beholding Him Who was born of thee, O All-Immaculate One, hanging upon the Tree, thou didst lament, crying aloud: O my most longed-for Child, whither has Thy light-bearing beauty departed, which adorned human nature?

## On Wednesday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Matins[^443]

*The Trinity Hymns.*  
*After the first reading of the Psalter, the sessional hymns of the Cross from the Octoechos.*  
*After the second reading of the Psalter, the following sessional hymns, Tone 1:*
