O Christ, stretched out upon the Cross, Thou wast put to death, Who didst put to death the enemy and origin of evil, the serpent, and didst make alive those who died through his bite: therefore I pray: make alive my deadened soul, O Savior, which looks unto Thee in prayer and fasting.  
*(Thrice.)*  

*Stavrotheotokion, Podoben:*[^444]  
When the Ewe-lamb beheld Thee, the Lamb, hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, in the midst of evildoers, she cried with tears, and bitterly lamented: O most beloved Child, what is this wonder that is seen? O most pure Mother, universal Life, this which thou didst foretell is made known!

*After the third reading of the Psalter, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^445]  
With the trumpet of songs let us sound, as we pass through the appointed day, the season of abstinence, and let us call out, crying aloud: Make this our life to blossom forth in peace, having bridled the passions of intemperance, by the power of Thy Cross, O Christ Word, in this season preserve Thy servants!  
*(Twice.)*  

*Stavrotheotokion:*  
A reproachful death by crucifixion didst Thou willingly endure, O Compassionate One; beholding which, she who bore Thee, O Christ, was wounded in her womb, and beating her breast as a mother, lamented: by her prayers, for the sake of the tender-heartedness of Thy mercy, have compassion, and save the world, O Thou Who takest away the sin of the world!

*The Canon from the Menaion and the present Triodia according to their order, and the biblical ode is read.*  

*Ode 3, of Lord Joseph, Tone 1. Heirmos:*[^446]  
**"Let my heart be established..."**  

*Troparia:*  
Upon the Cross Thou didst stretch out Thy palms in place of Adam's hands stretched out of old unto the sensible garden, taking away sin because of the loving-kindness of Thy compassions, O All-Good Word of God!

Establish my thoughts, which are shaken by the envy of the serpent: drive away the assaults of the passions through Thy Passion, O Thou Who didst suffer and didst grant dispassion unto the faithful!

The grace of the fast has shone forth, driving away the darkness of intemperance: behold the acceptable time, and the day of salvation; let us show forth fruits of repentance, and we shall live!

*Theotokion:*  
O restoration of the fallen, who fell by the precipice of pleasures and fell into the abyss through grievous sins: raise me up, O Pure One, and guide me unto life!

*Another canon, of Lord Theodore, Tone 3. Heirmos: "Establish, O Lord, our hearts:"*[^447]  
Establish, O Lord, our hearts by Thy Cross, that they turn not away from Thee unto evil words and defiled deeds.

Beholding Thy Passion, O Master, heaven and earth were visibly changed, showing Thee truly to be the King of all.

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
O Equal in honor, Unoriginate, All-Holy Trinity, Life-Creating and Primeval Light Unity: O Father, and Son, and Spirit, save me!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
O Mother of God who knewest not man, thou didst bear the One God, not having lost the purity of virginity, but thou remainest pure even as thou wast before childbirth.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Thy purple robe, the nails, and the Cross, the sponge, and the spear, O Jesus, I worship, and I sing of Him Who made all things alive.

*Katavasia:*  
Establish, O Lord, our hearts, and enlighten them unto Thy hymnody, that we may glorify Thee, O Savior, unto ages of ages.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^448]  
**"O hymn-singers..."**  

*Troparia:*  
O Thou Who wast pleased to be numbered with the lawless through the multitude of mercy: blot out my sins, that in faith I may glorify Thee unto all the ages!

*Troparia:*  
Tear asunder with Thy spear the handwriting of my sins, and heal the diseases of my heart, O Lord, wounded by the arrows of the malice of deceitful demons!

Wash my heart, darkened by sin, with Thy redemption, with the blood that flowed from Thy ribs, O my Christ, that I may glorify Thee unto all the ages!

*Theotokion:*  
O Virgin who untilled didst shoot forth the heavenly ear of grain, Who feeds all things by divine power: fill my hungry and humble soul!

*Another canon [by St. Theodore]. Heirmos: "Into the flame unto the children:"*[^449]  
Rejoice, O Cross, whereby the thief in a single instant was recognized as a theologian, crying aloud: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom! Show us to be partakers of his portion.

Being pierced, Thou dost turn aside the flaming sword, O Christ, and openest Paradise again unto men: entering into which, we unceasingly enjoy Thy deathless life.

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
With the Father let us worship the Son, and the Holy Spirit in one Essence, unceasingly crying with clay lips: Glory in the highest unto God in Trinity!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Thou givest birth to a Child while remaining a virgin, O only One full of divine grace, a great mystery, a terrible wonder: for thou didst bear God incarnate, the Savior of the world!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
O Cross of Christ, hope of the ends of the earth: account us worthy through thy pilotage calmly to cross the abyss of the good fast, saving us from the tempest of transgressions.

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord.*  
*Heirmos:*  
Him Who descended by divine power into the flame unto the Hebrew children, and was revealed as Lord: praise, ye priests, and supremely exalt Him unto all the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^450]  
**"Life-receiving spring..."**  

*Troparia:*  
Elisha, having fasted, raised the dead youth, as it is written: and we, fasting, let us the faithful strive to put to death the carnal mind, that we may obtain life there!

Woe is me, O my soul! A fearful tribunal, and the Judge's fearful sentence! Strive, repent, be reconciled unto Christ, Who for thy sake was suspended upon the Cross, and delivered the faithful from condemnation.

Open unto me the doors of repentance, close the entrances of my passions, O Christ, Who by Thy crucifixion didst show the entrance into Paradise easy to enter for the thief, that I may glorify Thy goodness!

*Theotokion:*  
Since I have been cast into the depth of malice, and am tempest-tossed by opposing thoughts, obeying demons, and enslaved to pleasures: help me, O pure Virgin Mother, guiding me unto the paths of salvation!

*Another canon [by St. Theodore]. Heirmos: "On Mount Sinai:"*[^451]  
Thou didst deliver Thyself willingly, and didst draw Thyself unto murderers; Thou didst stand before the judgment seat, beaten by the hands which Thou didst fashion; Thou wast crucified and mocked, Thou wast pierced, O Lord; Thou didst suffer in the flesh, enduring all things mercifully, that Thou mightest save us.

The angelic hosts trembled, seeing Thee upon the Cross; the luminaries hid their light, and the earth shook; all things were shaken at Thy reviling, O Lord: by Thy divine Passions Thou hast wrought salvation for us, O God.

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
In essence the Trinity is an Undivided Unity, Supremely Divine, united in nature, divided properly in Persons: for being undivided, It is divided, and being one, It is threefold: this is Father, Son, and Living Spirit, keeping all things.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Who has heard of a Child-bearing Virgin, and a Mother without a husband? Mary, thou accomplishest a wonder: but tell me, how? Search not into the depth of childbirth: this is truly beyond the comprehension of the human mind.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
By Thy wound we have all been healed from the passions of sins: for being lifted up upon the Cross, Thou didst wound the author of evil, the enemy: but account us uncondemned, having completed the time of the fast, also to attain unto the Resurrection.

*Heirmos:*  
On Mount Sinai Moses beheld thee in the bush, having conceived in thy womb without being burned the Fire of the Godhead; and Daniel beheld thee as the unquarried mountain; a rod that budded forth, Isaiah cried, from the root of David.

*Exapostilarion of the Tone.*  

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon, Tone 7:*[^452]  
O Lord Who by Thy Cross didst destroy death, which was brought into the world on account of the eating of the tree, which Thou didst forbid in Paradise: do Thou Thyself by the present fast deliver us from every soul-corrupting pleasure, and account us worthy to labor for the meat that abides unto eternal life, that we may obtain the sweetness of incorruptible crowns, which Thou hast prepared for true fasters, we pray Thee.  
*(Twice.)*  

*For the Martyrs:*  
Ye appeared as luminaries of the universe, all-praised passion-bearers, crying unto Christ: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory, and now and ever, Stavrotheotokion. Podoben: "No longer are we forbidden" (Не ктомꙋ̀ возбранѧ́еми):*[^453]  
When the All-Immaculate One beheld Thee nailed willingly upon the Tree, weeping, she sang of Thy dominion, O Lord.

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 6:*[^454]  
O Thou Who dwellest on high in the heavens, Who hast created every breath: save us, O God, for in Thee have we hoped, O our Savior.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 29, Tone 5:*[^455]  
**Reader:** Unto Thee, O Lord, will I cry, and unto my God will I make supplication.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* I will exalt Thee, O Lord, for Thou hast lifted me up.

*Reading of the Prophecy of Isaiah (5:16–25):*[^456]  
**Reader:** The Lord of hosts shall be exalted in judgment, and the holy God shall be glorified in righteousness. And they that were plundered shall feed like wolves, and the lambs shall feed in the desolate places of the captives. Woe unto them that draw sins to themselves as with a long cord, and iniquities as with the thong of a chariot yoke, who say: Let that which He will do draw near quickly, that we may see it, and let the counsel of the Holy One of Israel come, that we may know it! Woe unto them that call evil good, and good evil; that put darkness for light, and light for darkness; that put bitter for sweet, and sweet for bitter! Woe unto them that are wise in their own eyes, and prudent before themselves! Woe unto your mighty men that drink wine, and to the nobles that mix strong drink; who justify the ungodly for gifts, and take away the righteousness of the righteous from him! Therefore, as stubble is burned by a coal of fire, and shall be consumed by a burning flame, their root shall be as dust, and their blossom shall ascend like chaff; for they would not have the law of the Lord of hosts, but provoked the word of the Holy One of Israel. And the Lord of hosts was stirred with anger against His people, and He laid His hand upon them and smote them: and the mountains were provoked, and their corpses became as dung in the midst of the way. And in all these things His wrath is not turned away, but His hand is still high.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 30, Tone 6:*[^457]  
**Reader:** In Thee, O Lord, have I hoped, let me not be put to shame unto the age.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* In Thy righteousness deliver me, and rescue me.

## On Wednesday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Vespers[^458]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and we sing the present idiomelon twice, the martyrion, and 3 prosomoia.*

*Tone 1. Idiomelon:*[^459]  
Having taken up the spiritual fast, brethren, speak not deceitful things with your tongue, nor put a stumbling block before your brother for an offense; but having enlightened your soul through repentance with tears, let us cry aloud unto Christ: forgive us our transgressions, as Lover of mankind!

*Martyrion:*

*Martyrion:*[^460]  
All-lauded martyrs, neither has the earth hidden you, but heaven has received you; the gates of paradise have been opened unto you, and being within, you delight in the tree of life. Entreat Christ that He grant peace unto our souls, and great mercy.

*Other stichera by Lord Joseph, Tone 3. Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy Cross" (Ве́лїа крⷭ҇та̀):*[^461]  
By the prayers of the divine apostles, O Lord, vouchsafe us to accomplish well the season of the fast with compunction of thought, as Thou art good, O Tender-Hearted One, that being saved, we all may glorify Thee.

*Podoben:*  
Great and terrible is Thy coming, O Lord, wherein sitting Thou shalt execute righteous judgment: therefore condemn me not who am already condemned, but as God spare me, by the acceptable prayers of Thine apostles!

*Another sticheron by Lord Theodore, Tone 6. Podoben: "Having laid aside all" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^462]  
O apostles of Christ, luminaries of the universe, world-enriching treasures of the all-wise understanding of our God: deliver from temptations those who sing of you, by your holy prayers, and granting us youthfully to pass through the season of the fast, direct our life in peace, that having worthily anticipated the Passion of Christ, we may with boldness offer hymns unto our God.

*And 4 from the Menaion. Glory, Both now and ever: Theotokion.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 31, Tone 6:*[^463]  
**Reader:** Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous!  
**Reader:** *Verse:* Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven.

*Reading from Genesis (4:16–26):*[^464]  
**Reader:** Cain went out from the presence of God, and dwelt in the land of Nod, over against Eden. And Cain knew his wife, and she conceived and bore Enoch; and he was building a city, and called the name of the city after the name of his son, Enoch. And unto Enoch was born Irad, and Irad begot Mehujael, and Mehujael begot Methushael, and Methushael begot Lamech. And Lamech took unto himself two wives: the name of the one was Adah, and the name of the second Zillah. And Adah bore Jabal: he was the father of those who dwell in tents, feeding cattle. And his brother's name was Jubal: he was the one who showed the psaltery and the harp. And Zillah, she also bore Tubal-Cain: he was a hammerer, an artificer of brass and iron; and the sister of Tubal-Cain was Naamah. And Lamech said unto his wives: Adah and Zillah, hear my voice, ye wives of Lamech; give ear unto my words: for I have slain a man to my wounding, and a young man to my hurt. For sevenfold vengeance was taken on Cain, but of Lamech seventy times seven. And Adam knew Eve his wife, and she conceived and bore a son, and called his name Seth, saying: For God has raised up for me another seed instead of Abel, whom Cain slew. And unto Seth there was born a son, and he called his name Enos: this man hoped to call upon the name of the Lord God.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 32, Tone 1:*[^465]  
**Reader:** Let Thy mercy, O Lord, be upon us, according as we have hoped in Thee.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* Rejoice in the Lord, ye righteous!

*Reading of Proverbs (5:15–6:3):*[^466]  
**Reader:** My son, drink waters out of thine own vessels, and from the spring of thine own wells; let waters from thy fountain overflow for thee, and let thy waters go forth into thy streets; let them be for thee alone to have, and let no stranger partake with thee; let the spring of thy water be thine own, and rejoice with the wife of thy youth. Let the hind of love and the foal of thy graces converse with thee, and let her go before thee, and be with thee at all times; for abiding in her friendship, thou shalt be multiplied. Be not much with a strange woman, neither be embraced in the arms of one that is not thine own; for the ways of a man are before the eyes of God, and He watches all his paths. Iniquities ensnare a man, and each is held fast in the cords of his own sins. This man dies with the undisciplined, and from the abundance of his life he is cast out, and perishes through folly. My son, if thou become surety for thy friend, thou shalt deliver thine hand unto the enemy. For a strong snare unto a man are his own lips, and he is captured by the lips of his own mouth. Do, my son, what I command thee, and save thyself!

*And immediately the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.*[^467]

## On Thursday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Matins[^468]

*The Trinity Hymns of the Tone.*  
*After the first reading of the Psalter, the apostolic sessional hymns of the Octoechos, with their Theotokion.*  
*After the second reading of the Psalter, we say the following sessional hymns, Tone 3.*

*Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^469]  
Christ, the beautiful Vine, brought you forth as clusters of grapes, watering the earth with saving sweetness, O God-bearing apostles: therefore deliver me from the drunkenness of pleasures, I pray, granting a stream of compunction to my soul in the divine days of fasting, that being saved, I may obtain life.

*Glory: the same. Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Taking to mind the unmeasured wealth of thy tender mercy and the invincible might of thy dominion, I flee unto thy protection, held fast by sorrow and sorely at a loss, crying from the depth of my heart with tears: O Virgin Mother of God, help me, the sole intercession of the world!

*After the third reading of the Psalter, Sessional Hymn, Tone 6. Podoben: "The Lord stood before" (Гдⷭ҇и предстоѧ́ше):*[^470]  
O Lord, be gracious unto us in this divine season; vouchsafe us ever to pour forth tears from the heart unto Thee, for the washing away of spiritual defilements and for the blossoming of holy commandments, that having fasted in this manner, we may worthily be well-pleasing unto Thee, and graciously grant unto all to behold Thy most honorable Passion, by the prayers of Thine apostles who save the world: glory to Thee!

*Glory: the same. Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
O most immaculate Virgin, Mother of God, richly pour out upon us, O pure one, the fountain of mercy, and grant unto us forgiveness of transgressions; for thou art our intercession and divine protection.

*The Canon from the Menaion, and the present Triodion of Lord Joseph.*  
*Tone 3. Ode 4. Heirmos: "When Thou art angry at the rivers" (Е҆гда̀ въ рѣка́хъ прогнѣ́ваешисѧ):*[^471]  
**"When Thou art angry at the rivers..."**

*Troparia:*  
Extinguishing the soul-destroying drunkenness of the pleasures of those who cast off restraint, let us be enriched by the fast: fast, O soul, from the food of intemperance, and take delight in the contemplation of good things, that thou mayest attain the banquet on high!

Being coals of the immaterial fire, O divine apostles of the Savior, burn up the brushwood of my passions!

*Theotokion:*  
O pure Virgin, who gavest birth to Life, who didst lend flesh unto Christ from thine own: mortify the passions of my flesh!

*Another Triodion by Lord Theodore. Tone 6. Heirmos: "The prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord" (Оу҆слы́ша проро́къ прише́ствїе твоѐ Гдⷭ҇и):*[^472]  
**"The prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord..."**

*Troparia:*  
O twelve-fold band of apostles: Peter, Paul, James, and John, Andrew, Bartholomew, Philip, Thomas, Matthew, and James, with Simon and Jude: make ye supplications unto Christ, that we may be saved!

Casting the nets of teachings strangely into the sea of life, O holy apostles, ye mystically caught the nations like fish, in number an hundred and fifty-three, and brought them unto the Lord.

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
I glorify the Son from the Father and the Spirit, as light and ray from the sun: the One by generation, since He is the Begotten; the Other by procession, since He is the Proceeding: the co-unoriginate divine Trinity, worshiped by every creature.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Thou didst appear pure, having given birth to the Infant and preserved thy virginity, having borne God and man, the same One in both forms: thy wonder, O Virgin Mother, terrifies every ear and thought!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Ye are the light of the world, as Christ said unto you, by the radiance of the word: but pray ye, O apostles, that we may magnanimously pass through the course of abstinence, and worship His Resurrection!

*Heirmos:*  
The prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid, that Thou wast about to be born of a Virgin and appear unto men, and he said: I heard the report of Thee and was afraid: glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "In the flame unto the Children" (Въ пла́мени въ ѻ҆трокѡ́мъ):*[^473]  
**"In the flame unto the Children..."**

*Troparia:*  
Slain by many sins, I bring a dead soul into life: therefore have compassion on me through your life-bearing prayers, O eyewitnesses of God, disciples of the Savior!

I have been enveloped by the gloom of sin; truly have I drawn near to the hell of despair: therefore enlighten me, being eyewitnesses of God, ye mystical rays of the Sun of glory!

Abstain from passions, and thou shalt be saved, O passionate soul: for setting aside meats is a fast unacceptable to those who seek righteousness, unless there also be amendment of transgressions.

*Theotokion:*  
Moses foresaw thee as the bush burning with fire, O Virgin Mother of God: therefore quench the furnace of my passions, and snatch me from the fire of Gehenna, and save me!

*Another. Heirmos: "Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify" (Е҆гѡ́же во́инства):*[^474]  
**"Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify..."**

*Troparia:*  
As the twelve-tribed inheritance from Jacob, so another twelve-fold choir of apostles from Thee appeared in the world, O Christ, through whom every believer was born unto the word, singing of Thee unto all ages.

Having mystically drowned the chariots of the noetic Pharaoh, as in the Red Sea, ye led forth the people of the Lord into the land of divine knowledge, singing, O apostles, unto all ages.

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*

*Trinity Hymn:*  
I worship the one God according to Substance, and I sing of Three Hypostases distinctively: other, yet not of another nature; for the Godhead is One in Three Persons: as Father, and Son, and the divine Spirit.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Going forth from thy most radiant womb, as a bridegroom from a bridal chamber, Christ shone forth, a great light unto those in darkness: as the Sun of righteousness having shone, O Pure One, He enlightened the world.

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Mystics and fellow-enthroned ones of Christ, praying diligently for us sinners at all times: pray ye also now, that we may pass through the entire fast!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*

*Heirmos:*  
Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify, and Cherubim and Seraphim tremble before: let every breath and creature praise, bless, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "On Mount Sinai" (На Сїна́йстѣй горѣ̀):*[^475]  
**"On Mount Sinai..."**

*Troparia:*  
Having become rivers of the Noetic Spring, O apostles, and having well poured forth living waters from a pure heart: dry up the streams of my sin by your prayers, and guide me unto the paths of salvation!

A storm of sin besets my wretched soul: stretch forth Thy right hand unto me, as unto Peter, O Good Pilot, that the depth of despair may not cover me, nor let me become food for the soul-destroying sea monster, O most merciful Christ!

*Troparia:*[^476]  
The apostolic choir entreats Thee, O Jesus, King of all; the angelic ranks entreat Thee: according to the multitude of Thy compassions, grant unto Thy people deliverance from evil things, amendment of life, and the inheritance of Thy Kingdom!

*Theotokion:*  
Virginity and childbirth came together in thee beyond nature, O ark of honorable sanctification, pure Virgin Mother: therefore with faith I sing of thee: sanctify me wholly; deliver me wholly from every working of passions that afflict me!

*Another. Heirmos: "Of the Seedless Conception" (Безсѣ́меннагw Зача́тїа):*[^477]  
**"Of the Seedless Conception..."**

*Troparia:*  
In the world as bodiless ones, and in the flesh as angels, bearing the one Word in your heart, ye drew the nations unto the one faith of Christ, having by understanding convicted the proud-minded and the wise as fools, O all-blessed apostles.

O Peter, rock and foundation of faith; Paul, preacher and teacher of the nations; ye sons of Zebedee, with the remaining eight: entreat Christ diligently, that we may pass through the arena of fasting with good strength!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
O Sole Begetter of the Only-Begotten, Father of the Only-Begotten Son, and Thou the One Light, Radiance of the One Light, and Thou the One Holy Spirit of the One God, truly being the Lord of the Lord: O Trinity, Holy Unity, save me who theologize Thee!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
The wonder of thy Childbirth astonishes me, O all-immaculate one: how dost thou conceive without seed Him Who cannot be contained? Tell me, how dost thou remain a virgin, having given birth as a Mother? Receiving in faith that which is beyond nature, worship the One Who is born: for whatever He wills, He also can do!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
Going forth by the power of the Spirit unto kings and princes, ye were in no way afraid of fire or sword: ye conquered every deceit, and ye save mankind, being generals of the Lord, O all-blessed apostles.

*Heirmos:*  
The ineffable childbirth of the Seedless Conception, the incorruptible Fruit of the unwedded Mother: for the Childbirth of God renews natures; therefore all generations orthodoxly magnify thee as the Bride-Mother of God.

*The Photogogic of the Tone.*  
*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon twice, Tone 8:*[^478]  
The season of repentance and mediator of eternal life is the labor of the fast unto us, if we stretch forth our hands in well-doing; for nothing so saves the soul as giving to the needy: almsgiving mingled with fasting delivers a man from death. Let us embrace this, than which nothing is equal, for it is sufficient to save our souls.

*Martyrion:*[^479]  
Clad well in the breastplate of faith, and having armed yourselves with the sign of the Cross, ye proved to be valiant warriors, manfully resisting the tormentors, and ye destroyed the deceit of the devil, having become victors, ye were vouchsafed crowns: pray ye unto Christ for us, that our souls may be saved![^480]

*Glory, Both now and ever: Theotokion, same Tone:*  
I, O holy pure Virgin, flee unto thy protection; I know that through thee I shall find salvation: for thou, O pure one, art able to help me.

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 1:*[^481]  
Raise up us who are fallen, turn back unto Thyself us who have turned aside, O God, as Lover of mankind, Who holdest the ends of the earth!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 33, Tone 6:*[^482]  
**Reader:** I sought the Lord, and He heard me, and delivered me from all my tribulations.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* I will bless the Lord at all times.

*Reading of the Prophecy of Isaiah (6:1–12):*[^483]  
**Reader:** In the year that King Uzziah died, I saw the Lord sitting on a high and exalted throne, and the house was full of His glory. And Seraphim stood round about Him: each one had six wings; with two he covered his face, and with two he covered his feet, and with two he flew. And one cried unto another, and they said: Holy, holy, holy is the Lord of hosts: the whole earth is full of His glory! And the lintel was lifted up at the voice wherewith they cried, and the house was filled with smoke. And I said: Woe is me, for I am pricked to the heart; for being a man, and having unclean lips, I dwell in the midst of a people that have unclean lips; and mine eyes have seen the King, the Lord of hosts! And there was sent unto me one of the Seraphim, and he had a live coal in his hand, which he had taken with tongs from the altar. And he touched my lips, and said: Lo, this has touched thy lips, and shall take away thine iniquities, and shall purge thy sins. And I heard the voice of the Lord saying: Whom shall I send, and who will go unto this people? And I said: Here am I, send me! And He said: Go, and say unto this people: Hearing ye shall hear, and shall not understand; and seeing ye shall see, and shall not perceive. For the heart of this people has grown gross, and their ears are dull of hearing, and their eyes have they closed, lest they should see with their eyes, and hear with their ears, and understand with their heart, and should turn back, and I should heal them. And I said: Lord, how long? And He said: Until the cities be desolate without inhabitant, and the houses without men, and the land be left desolate. And after this God shall remove men far away, and they that are left upon the earth shall be multiplied.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 34, Tone 6:*[^484]  
**Reader:** Arise, O Lord, and attend unto my judgment.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* Judge Thou, O Lord, them that wrong me.

## On Thursday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Vespers[^485]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and we sing the present three prosomoia of the Triodion by Lord Joseph.*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^486]  
Pierced in the ribs and hanged upon the Tree, Thou didst pour forth life unto me who am slain through the eating of the tree by the malice of the serpent; therefore I glorify Thee, O Christ, and beseech Thy tender compassion: show me who accomplish the course of the fast with compunction to be a partaker of Thy Passion and Resurrection!

Having found afflictions from the garden, Thou didst cast out the first-fashioned man from paradise; but being nailed as a man upon the Tree, O Savior, Thou leadest him in: therefore I cry unto Thee, O Redeemer: snatch me now from my afflictions, having cleansed me by the fast, and through repentance and tears, O most good Jesus, Savior of our souls!

*Another sticheron prosomoion, Tone 5, by Lord Theodore. Podoben: "O Lord, at the time of Moses" (Гдⷭ҇и при Мѡѷсе́и):*[^487]  
O Lord, we sing of Thy life-creating Cross: for this has blossomed life in the world, having put death to death; which we now bring unto Thee in prayer: strengthen us who fast in these holy days, and grant us power against passions and the victory of peace, for the multitude of Thy mercy, O Lover of mankind!

*And 3 from the Menaion. Glory, Both now and ever: Theotokion.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 35, Tone 8:*[^488]  
**Reader:** O Lord, Thy mercy is in the heavens, and Thy truth reaches unto the clouds.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* Thy righteousness is as the mountains of God; Thy judgments are a great deep.

*Reading from Genesis (5:1–24):*[^489]  
**Reader:** This is the book of the generations of men, in the day that God made Adam: in the likeness of God He made him. Male and female He made them, and blessed them; and He called his name Adam, in the day when He made them. And Adam lived two hundred and thirty years, and begot a son according to his form, and according to his image, and called his name Seth. And the days of Adam which he lived after he begot Seth were seven hundred years; and he begot sons and daughters. And all the days of Adam which he lived were nine hundred and thirty years: and he died. And Seth lived two hundred and five years, and begot Enos. And Seth lived after he begot Enos seven hundred and seven years, and he begot sons and daughters. And all the days of Seth were nine hundred and twelve years: and he died. And Enos lived an hundred and ninety years, and begot Cainan. And Enos lived after he begot Cainan seven hundred and fifteen years, and he begot sons and daughters. And

**Reader:** all the days of Enos were nine hundred and five years: and he died. And Cainan lived an hundred and seventy years, and begot Mahalalel. And Cainan lived after he begot Mahalalel seven hundred and forty years, and he begot sons and daughters. And all the days of Cainan were nine hundred and ten years: and he died. And Mahalalel lived an hundred and sixty-five years, and begot Jared. And Mahalalel lived after he begot Jared seven hundred and thirty years, and he begot sons and daughters. And all the days of Mahalalel were eight hundred and ninety-five years: and he died. And Jared lived an hundred and sixty-two years, and begot Enoch. And Jared lived after he begot Enoch eight hundred years, and he begot sons and daughters. And all the days of Jared were nine hundred and sixty-two years: and he died. And Enoch lived an hundred and sixty-five years, and begot Methuselah. And Enoch was well-pleasing unto God; and Enoch lived after he begot Methuselah two hundred years, and he begot sons and daughters. And all the days of Enoch were three hundred and sixty-five years. And Enoch was well-pleasing unto God, and he was not found, for God translated him.[^490]

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 36, Tone 6:*[^491]  
**Reader:** Wait on the Lord, and keep His way.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* Fret not thyself because of evildoers, neither be envious against the workers of iniquity.

*Reading of Proverbs (6:3–20):*[^492]  
**Reader:** Do, my son, what I command thee, and save thyself: for thou art come into the hands of evil men for thy friend: be not slack; spur on also thy friend for whom thou art become surety. Give not sleep to thine eyes, nor slumber to thine eyelids, that thou mayest be saved as a roe from the snare, and as a bird from a net. Go to the ant, O sluggard! And be envious, seeing his ways, and be wiser than he: for having no husbandry, nor one compelling him, nor being under a master, he prepares food in harvest, and makes abundant provision in summer. Or go to the bee, and learn how industrious she is, and how noble a work she produces, whose labors kings and common people use for health; and she is beloved by all and renowned, though being weak in strength, yet she is advanced, honored through wisdom. How long, O sluggard, wilt thou lie down? When wilt thou arise from sleep? A little indeed thou sleepest, a little thou sittest, a little thou slumberest, a little thou foldest thy hands upon thy breast: then shall poverty come upon thee like an evil traveler, and want like a swift runner; but if thou be not sluggish, thy harvest shall come as a fountain, and want shall flee away from thee like an evil runner. A foolish and lawless man walks in paths that are not good: the same winks with the eye, and makes a sign with the foot, and teaches with a motion of the fingers. A perverse heart devises evils: at all times such an one causes tumults in a city. Therefore suddenly comes his destruction, severing and incurable crushing. Because he rejoices in all things which God hates, and is crushed for the uncleanness of his soul: the eye of the insolent, an unrighteous tongue, hands shedding innocent blood, a heart devising wicked thoughts, and feet hastening to do evil, shall be destroyed. An unrighteous witness kindles lies, and sends lawsuits in the midst of brethren. My son, keep the laws of thy father, and reject not the instruction of thy mother!

*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon of the day, Tone 5:*[^493]  
Not having fasted according to the commandment of the Creator, the first-fashioned brought forth death from disobedience through the garden of knowledge, and estranged themselves from the tree of life and paradisiacal delight: therefore let us the faithful fast from corruptible foods and all-destroying passions, that from the divine Cross we may lay hold on life, and with the good-minded thief return unto the primal Fatherland, receiving great mercy from Christ God.[^494] *Twice.*

*Martyrion:*[^495]  
Having disregarded all earthly things, and manfully dared to endure torments, ye missed not your longed-for hopes, but became heirs of the heavenly Kingdom, O all-lauded martyrs: having boldness before God the Lover of mankind, entreat peace for the world, and great mercy for our souls!

*Glory, Both now and ever: Stavrotheotokion. Podoben: "Rejoice, ascetic of old" (Ра́дуйсѧ по́стникwмъ):*[^496]  
The Ewe beholding of old her Lamb hastening unto the slaughter, diligently followed, crying aloud unto Him: Whither goest Thou, my sweetest Child Christ? Why dost Thou run this swift course without slackness, O Long-Suffering One? O Jesus most desired, sinless, most merciful Lord: grant a word unto Thy handmaid, O my Son most beloved; despise me not, O Compassionate One, in silence—me who strangely gave birth to Thee, O God all-compassionate, grant great mercy unto the world!

## On Friday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Matins[^497]

*The Trinity Hymns of the Tone.*  
*After the first reading of the Psalter, the sessional hymns of the Cross from the Octoechos, with their Theotokion.*  
*After the second reading of the Psalter, we say the following sessional hymns, Tone 8.*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "Quickly anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^498]  
Being lifted up through fasting from earthly and zealous passions, let us exalt the Lord Who was lifted up upon the Cross and Who exalted the world; let us drink within our belly the drink of compunction; let us receive the day and the hour into our mind, wherein we shall stand before the eternal Judge!

*Stavrotheotokion. Podoben:*  
Thy Mother who knew not wedlock, when she beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, weeping and lamenting spoke in this manner: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How does the lawless people nail Thee to the Cross—Thee the Life of all, my sweetest Light?

*After the third reading of the Psalter, Sessional Hymn by Lord Theodore, Tone 5. Podoben: "Today the light" (Свѣ́тъ дне́сь):*[^499]  
The light of the grace of Thy Cross, O Lord, shining upon all the universe, destroys demonic strength, and now beautifully adorns the course of the fast: strengthening us thereunto, have mercy on us! *Twice.*

*Stavrotheotokion, Tone 5. Podoben: "Co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное):*[^500]  
When the Mother who bore Thee without travail beheld Thee lifted up upon the Tree, she lamented with weeping, and cried aloud: Woe is me, my sweetest Son! I am now wounded in soul, beholding Thee upon the Cross between two malefactors, nailed as a malefactor!

*The Canon from the Menaion, and the present triodia according to their order, by Lord Joseph; and the Biblical Ode is chanted. Tone 8:*[^501]

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ" (Нечести́вїи не ᲂу҆́зрѧтъ сла́вы твоеѧ̀ Хрⷭ҇тѐ):*[^502]  
**"The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ..."**

*Troparia:*  
Of Thine own will, O Master, Thou wast lifted up upon the Cross, and didst cast down the enemy: therefore, by Thy tender mercy, set me upright who by mine own will am cast down into the abyss by passions.

Enlighten my soul darkened by unseemly passions, O Jesus, Who didst darken the sun when stretched out upon the Cross, and didst enlighten all the fullness of the universe.

Vouchsafe me, O Christ, peacefully to sail across the ocean of the fast, stilling the billows of my thoughts, and guide me unto the haven of the Resurrection.

*Theotokion:*  
Let us honor the pure one with a pure mind, the beauty of Jacob; adorning ourselves with divine deeds, let us piously sing of her, as the Mother of our God.

*Another triodion by Lord Theodore, Tone 5. Heirmos:*[^503]  
*Ode 5. Heirmos: "Rising early from the night" (Ѿ но́щи ᲂу҆тренюющыѧ):*  
**"Rising early from the night..."**

*Troparia:*  
By being crucified Thou deliverest me from corruption, and having been pierced, Thou makest me immortal: I glorify Thine unutterable mercy which Thou didst accept, O Christ, that Thou mightest save me.

Stretching out Thy palms upon the Cross, Thou didst slay the spiritual Amalek, saving Thy people, O Lord: therefore do we sing of Thy dominion.

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
In three Persons one Godhead, one all-transcendent Principle: let us sing of the unoriginate Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Whom heaven could not contain, Thou, having conceived in thy womb, didst bear. O strange and unutterable wonder! Therefore all of us sing of thee, O all-pure one.

*Refrain:* *Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Thou Who for my sake didst endure crucifixion, drinking vinegar, and saying: "It is finished": bring to completion my course of the fast, and vouchsafe me to behold Thy Resurrection!

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
*Heirmos:*  
Have mercy on those who from the night rise early unto Thee, O Christ, and grant peace: for the light of Thy commandments has become healings unto Thy servants, O Lover of mankind.

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "The pious Children" (Ѻ҆́троки бл҃гочести́выѧ):*[^504]  
**"The pious Children..."**

*Troparia:*  
Nailed upon the Cross for Thy tender mercy, Thou didst open Paradise unto the thief: but now heal me who am crushed by demonic fury and have received a whole-flesh wound in my soul, opening unto me the doors of repentance through Thy love for mankind.

Having enlightened the flesh through fasting, let us enrich the soul with virtues, feeding the poor, purchasing inexhaustible wealth in the heavens, and let us cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all forever!

At Thy Passion the creation was shaken, seeing Thee nailed upon the Cross, O all-good Savior: therefore I pray Thee, who am ever shaken by the assaults of the serpent: establish my mind, O Compassionate One, upon the firm rock of Thy desires.

*Theotokion:*  
O closed gate of God, through which the Lord alone passed, direct me unto the divine paths, and open unto me the gates of salvation, O full of God's grace: for unto thee do I flee, O Virgin, the sole intercessor of the human race.

*Another Triodion by Lord Theodore. Ode 8. Heirmos: "The Creator of creation" (Творца̀ тва́ри):*[^505]  
**"The Creator of creation..."**

*Troparia:*  
All-holy Tree, whereon Thou, my Christ, wast crucified: singing, I bless Thee unto all ages.

On Golgotha the lawless, having crucified Thee, O Christ, slew Thee: yet Thou livest, and savest us forever.

*Verse:* *We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
In marvelous manner the Trinity is divided, yet remains undivided, as God, Whom we exalt above all forever.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Make supplication for those who sing of thee, O all-pure one, that we may be delivered from all temptations and tribulations.

*Refrain:* *Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Having quickened me by the power of Thy Cross, O Lord, vouchsafe me with good courage to finish the course of the fast.

*Verse:* *We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*  
*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
*Heirmos:*  
The Creator of creation, Whom angels dread: praise Him, ye peoples, and exalt Him above all forever.

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "A stone cut without hands from the unquarried mountain" (Ка́мень нерꙋкосѣ́чный ѿ несѣко́мыѧ горы̀):*[^506]  
**"A stone cut without hands from the unquarried mountain..."**

*Troparia:*  
The prophet foresaw Thee, O Savior, as a stone cut off from the mountain of the Virgin, Who didst shatter the stones by Thy crucifixion: therefore, as Thou art all-compassionate, deliver me who am weighed down by the stones of insensibility.

Fast from malice, O my soul, taking delight in divine love; open the doors unto all kinds of good things, barring the entrance of wickedness by self-control and supplication.

Save me who am condemned, O Master, my Savior, Who by Thy Cross didst condemn enmity, and show me not guilty of Gehenna, defiled by passions and darkened by sins.

*Theotokion:*  
Spare me, O Christ, when Thou comest to judge the world with glory; disperse the mist of my vices by the entreaties of her who bore Thee, and make me an heir of Thine eternal kingdom.

*Another Triodion by Lord Theodore. Ode 9. Heirmos: "Rejoice, O Isaiah" (И҆са́їе ликꙋ́й):*[^507]  
**"Rejoice, O Isaiah..."**

*Troparia:*  
Once crucified, Thou openest Paradise again, wherein partaking of life I rejoice, being delivered from the eternal death of disobedience: therefore, O Lover of mankind, as my God do I magnify Thee.

An instrument of curse, Thy life-giving Cross, O Lord, is shown to be a seal of blessing, beholding Thee upon which, we who were formerly dead are made alive, and singing of Thee, as Master do we magnify Thee.

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
One in essence I sing of Thee, O unoriginate Trinity, honorable, life-originating, indivisible Unity: unbegotten Father, and begotten Word and Son, and Holy Spirit: save us who sing of Thee!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Beyond understanding is thy childbearing, O Mother of God: for without man was the conception in thee, and virginal was the birth, since God is He Who was born: Whom magnifying, thee, O Virgin, do we bless.

*Refrain:* *Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
O precious Cross, my strength and refuge, be Thou now enlightenment unto me in self-control, gladdening and cleansing me, delivering me from temptations: that glorifying thee, I may magnify Christ the Master.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
*Heirmos:*  
Rejoice, O Isaiah: the Virgin held in womb, and bore a Son, Emmanuel, both God and man; Orient is His name: Whom magnifying, we bless the Virgin.

*Photagogicon of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon twice, Tone 8:*[^508]  
The present fast, slayer of passions, promises to heal those who are afflicted by sin: let us honor it as a God-given helper; having received the God-inscribed tablets by Moses, let us not choose the one who broke them through intemperance; let us not be partakers with those whose bodies fell in the wilderness; let us not feast in Jewish fashion, but let us be enlightened ecclesiastically; let us not play the hypocrite in Pharisaic fashion, but let us adorn ourselves evangelically, glorying in the Cross of Christ, the Redeemer of our souls.

*Martyrion:*[^509]  
What shall we call you, O saints? Cherubim? For upon you Christ rested. Seraphim? For unceasingly ye glorified Him. Angels? For ye turned away from the body. Powers? For ye work miracles. Many are your names, and greater are your gifts: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory, Both now and ever: Stavrotheotokion, the same Tone:*[^510]  
What strange and marvelous thing is this! cried the Virgin Mother of the Lord: Sharp pangs, which I had not when bearing Thee, O Son, touch my heart! I cannot endure beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Light of mine eyes: hasten therefore to arise, that with the world I may glorify Thine awesome dispensation!

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 1:*[^511]  
By night and by day we fall down before Thee, O Lord, that Thou mayest grant remission of sins unto our souls, that in peace we may worship Thee and glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 37, Tone 4:*[^512]  
**Reader:** O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger, neither chasten me in Thy wrath.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* For Thine arrows are fastened in me.

*Reading of the Prophecy of Isaiah (7:1–14):*[^513]  
**Reader:** And it came to pass in the days of Ahaz the son of Jotham, the son of Uzziah, king of Judah, that Rezin king of Aram and Pekah son of Remaliah, king of Israel, came up against Jerusalem to make war against it, and they were not able to breach it. And it was reported to the house of David, saying: Aram has confederated with Ephraim. And his soul was terrified, and the soul of his people, as a tree in a forest is shaken by the wind. And the Lord said unto Isaiah: Go forth to meet Ahaz, thou and Shear-Jashub thy son who was left behind, at the pool of the upper way, of the fuller's field. And thou shalt say unto him: Take heed to be silent, and fear not, neither let thy soul be faint because of these two smoking firebrands; for when the anger of My wrath shall be, I will heal again. But as for the son of Aram and the son of Remaliah, because they have taken evil counsel against thee, saying: Let us go up against Judea, and having conferred with them, let us turn them unto us, and let us make the son of Tabeal king over it; thus says the Lord of hosts: This counsel shall not stand, neither shall it come to pass. But the head of Aram is Damascus, and the head of Damascus is Rezin; yet sixty-five years more, and the kingdom of Ephraim shall fail from being a people. And the head of Ephraim is Samaria, and the head of Samaria is the son of Remaliah; and if ye believe not, neither shall ye understand. And the Lord spoke again unto Ahaz, saying: Ask for thyself a sign from the Lord thy God, in the depth or in the height. And Ahaz said: I will not ask, neither will I tempt the Lord. And he [Isaiah] said: Hear ye now, O house of David! Is it a small thing for you to weary men, and how do ye weary the Lord? Therefore the Lord Himself shall give you a sign:

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 38, Tone 6:*[^514]  
**Reader:** Hear my prayer, O Lord, and give ear unto my supplication.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* I said: I will take heed to my ways, that I sin not with my tongue.

## On Friday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Vespers[^515]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses: and we sing the idiomelon twice; and 6 martyr-stichera of the Octoechos of the occurring tone; and 4 of the Menaion. Glory, of the saint, if there be one; if not, Glory: the dead sticheron of Damascene from the Octoechos. Both now and ever: Theotokion of the 1st tone.*

*Idiomelon, Tone 8:*[^516]  
Now is the acceptable time, now is the day of salvation! In the multitude of Thy mercy visit my soul, and ease the burden of mine iniquities, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 39, Tone 4:*[^517]  
**Reader:** Let Thy mercy and Thy truth, O Lord, continually preserve me.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* Waiting I waited for the Lord, and He gave ear unto me.

*Reading from Genesis (5:32–6:8):*[^518]  
**Reader:** Noah was five hundred years old, and he begot three sons: Shem, Ham, and Japhs. And it came to pass, when men began to multiply on the earth, and daughters were born unto them, that the sons of God saw the daughters of men that they were fair, and they took for themselves wives of all whom they chose. And the Lord God said: My Spirit shall not abide in these men forever, because they are flesh; yet their days shall be an hundred and twenty years. Now there were giants upon the earth in those days; and after that, when the sons of God went in unto the daughters of men, and they bore children to them, those were the giants who were of old, men of renown. And the Lord God saw that the wickednesses of men were multiplied on the earth, and that everyone ponders in his heart diligently on evils all the days. And God considered that He had made man on the earth, and He reflected. And God said: I will blot out man whom I have made from the face of the earth, from man even to cattle, and from creeping things even unto the fowls of heaven; for I have reflected that I made them. But Noah found grace before the Lord God.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 40, Tone 6:*[^519]  
**Reader:** I said: O Lord, have mercy on me, heal my soul.  
**Reader:** *Verse:* Blessed is he that considers the poor and needy.

*Reading of Proverbs (6:20–7:2):*[^520]  
**Reader:** My son, keep the laws of thy father, and reject not the instruction of thy mother: bind them upon thy soul continually, and tie them about thy neck. When thou walkest, lead it, and let it be with thee; and when thou sleepest, let it keep thee, that when thou awakest it may talk with thee. For a commandment of the law is a lamp, and a light, and a way of life, and reproof, and instruction: to preserve thee from a married woman, and from the slander of a strange tongue. My son, let not the desire of beauty conquer thee, neither be thou caught by thine eyes, neither be thou captivated by her eyelids: for the price of a harlot is as much as of one loaf of bread; but a woman hunts for the precious souls of men. Can anyone bind fire in his bosom, and not burn his garments? Or will anyone walk on coals of fire, and not burn his feet? So he that goes in to a married woman shall not be held guiltless, neither anyone that touches her. It is no wonder if anyone be caught stealing: for he steals that he may satisfy his hungry soul; but if he be caught, he shall repay sevenfold, and giving all his possessions, he shall deliver himself. But an adulterer through lack of understanding procures destruction to his own soul: he shall bear grief and dishonor, and his reproach shall not be wiped away forever. For the rage of her husband is full of jealousy: he will not spare in the day of judgment; he will not change his enmity for any price, neither will he be appeased by many gifts. My son, keep my words, and hide my commandments within thyself: my son, honor the Lord, and thou shalt be strong; and besides Him fear none other.

*And the rest of the Order of the Presanctified.*[^521]

## On the Second Saturday of the Great Fast at Matins[^522]

*Alleluia, and Troparia, Tone 2:*[^523]  
*Troparion:*  
Apostles, martyrs, and prophets, hierarchs, venerable and righteous ones, who have finished the good fight and kept the faith, having boldness before the Savior: pray to Him as He is good on our behalf, we pray, that our souls may be saved! *Twice.*

*Glory: For the departed:*[^524]  
Remember, O Lord, as Thou art good, Thy servants, and forgive whatever they have sinned in life: for none is sinless except Thee, Who art able to give rest also to the departed.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*[^525]  
Holy Mother of the unutterable Light, honoring thee with angelic hymns, we piously magnify thee.

*And we chant one Kathisma: "The Lord said unto my Lord..."*[^526]  
*Then, three sessional hymns to the martyrs from the Octoechos, and one for the departed, and a Theotokion. And the reading in the Hexaemeron.*  
*Then, "Blessed are the undefiled," chanted in two stases; and the priest commemorates the departed, as is customary.*  
*And at the conclusion of the Kathisma we chant: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord," "The choir of the saints..." and the rest.*  
*After "Glory, Both now and ever," the litany for the departed. The prayer: "O God of spirits and of all flesh..."*  
*And immediately, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*[^527]  
Give rest with the righteous unto Thy servants, O our Savior, and settle them in Thy courts, as it is written, overlooking as Thou art good their offenses, voluntary and involuntary, and all things done in knowledge and in ignorance, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory: the conclusion. Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth from the Virgin upon the world, and through her didst show forth sons of light: have mercy on us!

*The Canon from the Menaion with the Heirmos to 6, and of the holy monastery to 4: for the Heirmos and troparion are said without verses, and we hold the verses from: "Then the leaders were in haste." For we do not chant the Biblical Odes from the beginning unto the 6th ode on all Saturdays of the Holy Forty Days until the Akathist; but from the sixth ode the four-ode canons begin, and the Menaion of the day of the saint is sung, and the four-ode canons: and the Biblical Odes are chanted.*  
*The Four-Ode Canon by Lord Joseph, whose acrostic is: "This singing is of Joseph."*  
*Ode 6, Tone 8. Heirmos: "I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord" (Моли́твꙋ пролїю̀ ко Гдⷭ҇ꙋ):*[^528]  
**"I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord..."**

*Troparia:*  
By manifold torments the holy ones were vouchsafed, O Savior, to overthrow many myriads of foes, and they obtained many of Thy good things: by their prayers, as Thou art good, cleanse my many offenses!

Let us sing of the passion-bearers of Christ, and with one mind let us cry aloud unto them: Imitators of the Master's Passions, heal the passions of our souls, and strengthen us to fast from evil habit!

Thou didst change death into sleep, painfully didst Thou sleep in the tomb, and didst grant life unto the dead: vouchsafe therefore the departed, O Savior, to stand with the elect, through the entreaties of the holy passion-bearers!

*Theotokion:*  
Strengthen me, O Maiden, to fast and depart from all sin, who didst bear the mighty and sinless Word ineffably incarnate, and grant unto me a tear of cleansing from defilements, O all-immaculate one!

*Another canon by Lord Theodore. Tone 3. Heirmos: "The abyss of passions" (Глꙋбина̀ страсте́й):*[^529]  
**"The abyss of passions..."**

*Troparia:*  
We divinely celebrate the memory of the martyrs: come, ye lovers of the martyrs, let us rejoice, crowning them with hymns, and let us bless Christ the Ordainer of the contest!

Having first received trial of wounds, and then having been beaten with stones, having been sawn asunder, having been given as food to wild beasts, and having been slaughtered like lambs of Christ: yet ye live forever, O passion-bearers!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
Uniting the Three in one essence, yet dividing into three Persons the One which is the Godhead: I avoid Sabellius and Arius, equal precipices.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
A Virgin thou didst appear even after childbearing, keeping pure in both, and bearing: a marvelous vision, spoken and understood, wrought in thee, O Mother of God!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Or the verse: Marvelous is God in His saints:*  
*Martyrion:*  
The blood of your endurance unceasingly prays unto the Lord for us, O martyrs: therefore pray even now, that we may fast from shameful passions!

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*  
*For the departed:*  
Thou Who art about to judge all the world, while myriads of angels stand by: vouchsafe the departed faithfully to stand then before Thee uncondemned, O all-compassionate One!

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
*Heirmos:*  
The abyss of passions has risen up against me, and the tempest of contrary winds: but anticipating me, do Thou save me, O Savior, and deliver me from corruption, as Thou didst save the prophet from the beast!

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*  
*"With the saints give rest..."*[^530]

*Ikos:*  
*"Thou alone art immortal..." (see fol. 17).[^531]*

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "The Hebrew Children in the furnace" (Ѻ҆́троцы є҆вре́йстїи въ пещѝ):*[^532]  
**"The Hebrew Children in the furnace..."**

*Troparia:*  
With showers of blood, O most glorious ones, ye quenched the flame of deceit: from the coming fire therefore deliver us, O passion-bearers of the Lord, by your prayers unto Christ!

Having muzzled the mouths of lions, and having endured the burning of torments, O passion-bearers, ye inherited the better delight: pray that we also may obtain it unto ages.

Enlightened with all the light of the Spirit, O valiant passion-bearers, ask for relief, and entrance into Paradise, and communion in life for the departed faithful!

*Theotokion:*  
A hymn I offer unto thee, O Virgin: despise me not who am held fast by evils, but grant, O pure one, complete amendment through fasting and through all goodly conduct.

*Another canon by Lord Theodore. Heirmos: "The flame of the furnace moist with dew" (Пла́мень ѡ҆роси́вый пе́щный):*[^533]  
**"The flame of the furnace moist with dew..."**

*Troparia:*  
Fire, and sword, and wild beasts, O passion-bearers, having accounted as sweetness when suffering, ye astonished your murderers, singing unto the God of our fathers.

Cut into members, and burned, ye were offered unto God as a sweet-smelling sacrifice, O martyrs of Christ: therefore unceasingly pray unto Him for us!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
In the Trinity I glorify the one God, Father, Son, and Spirit: simple Unity, venerable Trinity, Principle pre-beginning and consubstantial.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
All-holy Sovereign Lady, Mother of God, accepting the prayers of thy servants, bring them before all unto God, that He may save us from all temptation!

*Verse: Unto the saints that are in His earth, the Lord hath been marvelous in all His desires in them.*  
*Martyrion:*  
Assemblies of martyrs, standing from heaven, bless and sanctify those who sing of you, that we may eagerly finish the season of the fast!

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord.*  
*For the departed:*  
Them that have passed away in faith unto Thee, Thou Who knowest the works of men, forgiving offenses voluntary and involuntary, give rest as God!

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
*Heirmos:*  
The flame of the furnace moist with dew, and Who didst save the Children unconsumed: blessed art Thou forever, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "The conquerors of the tyrant" (Побѣди́тели мꙋчи́телѧ):*[^534]  
**"The conquerors of the tyrant..."**

*Troparia:*  
Having endured forms of torments by the weakness of the flesh, ye were shown to be physicians unto the sick, O passion-bearers: therefore I cry: heal my ailing soul by repentance during the time of the fast!

Woe is me, how all my days have passed away in sloth! And lo, the end draws near, anticipating to seize me unperfected in good deeds: O martyrs who have happily finished your course, pray that a good end may be mine!

With drops of your blood ye quenched the burning of godlessness, O divine passion-bearers: and for those who have departed from this life, ask in truth for divine rest and forgiveness of things committed, unto ages!

*Theotokion:*  
Ezekiel foresaw thee, O pure one, as the gate through which none may pass, opening unto all the despairing the gates of repentance: therefore I pray thee: open unto me the paths leading unto their rest!

*Another canon by Lord Theodore. Heirmos: "From the unoriginate Father" (И҆зъ безна́чальна Ѻ҆ц҃а̀):*[^535]  
**"From the unoriginate Father..."**

*Troparia:*  
Having variously endured much-suffering wounds, some having burned up, others having been consumed by fire, and others being cut down: rejoice, ye sufferers, singing unto Christ: Praise, bless, and exalt Him above all forever!

With your blood ye sanctify the ends of the earth, by small sprinklings pouring forth streams of healings unto all, O all-lauded martyrs: therefore cry ye continually: Praise, bless, and exalt Him above all forever!

*Verse:* *We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
Tri-hypostatic Unity, Father, Son, and living Spirit, one Godhead, one Kingdom: the angelic hosts praise Thee, the Light never setting, and we who are upon earth sing, bless, and exalt Thee above all forever!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Lo, all generations call thee blessed, O all-pure one, beholding thy great majesty: for supernatural to all dost thou bear God Who is Creator and man: therefore we bless thee and exalt thee above all forever!

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*  
*Martyrion:*  
Choir of passion-bearers, pray unto the Savior to save us, making supplications even now that we may serve Him purely by self-control: Praise, bless, and exalt Him above all forever!

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*  
*For the departed:*  
Vouchsafe the departed piously in hope of resurrection, O Lord, to rise unto eternal life, to praise Thee purely, and to glorify Thee in psalmody: Praise, bless, and exalt Him above all forever!

*Verse:* *We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*  
*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
*Heirmos:*  
God, begotten of the unoriginate Father before the ages, and in the last times clothed in flesh from the Mother of God, as perfect man and true God: praise, bless, and exalt Him above all forever!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Thou hast surpassed the ordinances" (Оу҆ста́вы прешла̀ є҆сѝ):*[^536]  
**"Thou hast surpassed the ordinances..."**

*Troparia:*  
Ye appeared as divine coals, having burned up the evil substance of godlessness, and having cut down the demonic legions with the sword, O valiant passion-bearers, guides to the light of our hearts!

Ye passed through the darkness of torments, and migrated unto the spiritual light, O valiant passion-bearers: therefore enlighten my humble soul, darkened by transgressions!

Bearing pains of the body by their own will, the most glorious passion-bearers now ask painless rest and the delight of Paradise for the departed faithful.

*Theotokion:*  
Appear as a divine helper unto thy worthless servants, O pure Sovereign Lady, in the time of self-control, offering our prayers unto the Lord Who reigns unto ages!

*Another canon by Lord Theodore. Ode 9. Heirmos: "Thee, the immortal Fountain" (Тебѐ безсме́ртный И҆сто́чника):*[^537]  
**"Thee, the immortal Fountain..."**

*Troparia:*  
Having been cast like fire by God upon the earth, ye burned up every idol deceit, kindling a luminary of piety in the ends of the earth, O passion-bearers of Christ!

Neither flame, nor slaughter, nor wheels, nor tortures on the rack, nor burning, nor any other bitter torment separated you from the love of Christ, O passion-bearers!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
In pious manner do I sing of the one and threefold Godhead in aspect, uniting the Persons and dividing Them: the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit; These are one!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Thee, the Rod that budded forth from the root of Jesse and of David the forefather, O all-immaculate, pure one, do we magnify: for thou savest our souls.

*Verse: Unto the saints who are in His earth.*[^538]  
*Martyrion:*  
O honorable multitude of martyrs, entreat Christ, as we in peace complete the course of the fast, that we may behold and worship also His Passions.

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.*  
*For the departed:*  
As God Who didst put death to death and Who by Thy Resurrection bestowest life upon all, O Christ: give rest also unto Thy servants whom Thou hast translated.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
*Heirmos:*  
Thee, the immortal Fountain, Who bestowest healings of the saints upon the human race, do we ever magnify: for Thou savest our souls.

*Exapostilarion:*  
Thou Who as God didst adorn heaven with stars, and by Thy saints didst enlighten all the earth, O Creator of all: save those who sing unto Thee.

*Glory:*  
Thou Who rulest over the living and the dead as God, give rest unto Thy servants in the dwellings of the elect: for even if they have sinned, O Savior, yet they did not depart from Thee.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
O sweetness of the angels, joy of the afflicted, protectress of Christians, O Virgin Mother of the Lord: intercede for us, and deliver us from eternal torments.

*At the Praises: Martyria of the Tone.*  
*Glory: Idiomelon for the departed. Both now and ever: Theotokion.*  
*At the Aposticha: Memorial stichera of Lord Theophanes, of the occurring Tone.*  
*Glory, and both now and ever: Theotokion.*  
*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Tone, to 6. After the Entrance, Troparia: "Apostles, martyrs": "Remember, O Lord": Glory: "With the saints give rest": Both now and ever: "Thee as a wall":*[^539]  
*Prokeimenon: "Be glad in the Lord":*  
*Verse: "Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven":*  

*Another [Prokeimenon]: "Their souls shall dwell among good things."*  
*Epistle of the day, Pericope 314 [Heb. 3:12-16]. And for the departed, to the Thessalonians, Pericope 270 [1 Thess. 4:13-17]. Alleluia: "The righteous cried":*  
*Verse: "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen":*  
*Gospel from Mark, Pericope 6 [Mark 1:35-44]. And from John, Pericope 16 [John 5:24-30].*  
*Communion Hymn: "Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous":*  
*Another: "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord."*  
*Thus is performed the service for the departed on the three Saturdays of the Holy and Great Fast, when there is none of the three feasts: of the Forerunner, of the Holy Forty Martyrs, or of the Annunciation.*[^540]

[Depiction: Woodcut engraving of the Crucifixion of our Lord Jesus Christ upon the Cross, enclosed within an ornamental foliate frame. The Savior is crucified upon the Cross; above hovers an angel, and below at the foot of the Cross kneels the All-Holy Mother of God in lamentation.][^541]

[Headpiece Ornament: Icon of the All-Holy Mother of God with the Pre-Eternal Infant Christ.]

## SECOND SUNDAY OF THE GREAT FAST[^542]

*On Saturday evening, we set 10 verses. And we sing 3 Resurrection stichera, and 4 Anatolian, and 3 for the occurring Saint from the Menaion. [In the margin: Anatolian]. Glory, and both now and ever: The first Theotokion of the Tone.*  [^542]
*At the Aposticha: Resurrection stichera of the Octoechos. Glory, and both now and ever: Theotokion: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart": Trisagion, and after "Our Father": Resurrectional Troparion; Glory: to the Saint; Both now and ever: Theotokion according to the Tone.*  

*At Matins:*  
*After the Six Psalms, at "God is the Lord": Resurrectional Troparion, twice. Glory: to the Saint; Both now and ever: Theotokion according to the Tone.*  
*And the customary stichology.*  
*Songs of Ascents of the Tone.*  
*"Let every breath": Morning Gospel. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ": Psalm 50. Glory: "Open unto me the doors of repentance": Both now and ever, Podoben: "Guide me onto the paths of salvation" (На спасе́нїѧ стєзѝ): Then, in Tone 6: "Have mercy on me, O God": Then: "The multitude of evil things committed by me":*  
*Canons: Resurrectional of the Octoechos, with the Theotokion to 6; and of the Triodion to 4; and of the occurring Saint to 4.*[^543]  

*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos: "The chariot-driver Pharaoh" (Колесницегони́телѧ фараѡ́на):*[^544]  
**"The chariot-driver Pharaoh..."**

*Troparia:*  
Having taken up with warm tears the cry of the prodigal, unto Thee, the Father of all and God, do we fall down, crying aloud: for we have sinned, having wandered away from Thee, and having served prodigality, accept our repentance!

I have held in contempt the royal nobility of birth which Thou didst bestow upon me,[^545] O Word Who for my sake becamest man: grievously did I go astray to please swine, the demons of sin; but spare me, O Savior, by Thy tender mercy!

Unto me who fall upon my knees, even as formerly did the prodigal, O Master and Lord, hasten Thou and receive me, and embracing me with Thine arms, grant me the signs of Thy salvation, making me once again Thy son instead of a hireling, O Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*  
Through thee, O full of God's grace, human nature was vouchsafed a divine manifestation; for thou alone, O Virgin, hast been an intercessor between God and men; therefore, as the Mother of God do all of us worthily glorify thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos: "The vault of heaven" (Нбⷭ҇нагѡ крꙋ́га):*[^546]  
**"The vault of heaven..."**

*Troparia:*  
I have been terrified by my deeds, and by my departure afar off, and by Thy scattered wealth which I spent in prodigal strivings; in repentance I cry unto Thee, my Father and God: I have sinned, save me!

Sinning upon earth, I was afraid of heaven; for thus shall the judgment then convict me, when all things stand before Thee, O Word, judged by Thy righteous judgment.

Dark and defiled thoughts nourished me when I, the prodigal, was far from Thee, O Savior; but now I cry unto Thee: I have sinned, I have sinned, warmly save him who flees unto Thy tender mercy!

*Theotokion:*  
Adam's compound nature was deified, O Virgin; for from thy womb God appeared bearing flesh, through Whom we were recalled from ancient wandering, having of old been deceived by the hope of deification.

*Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos. And for the occurring Saint, Glory: Sessional Hymn; Both now and ever: Theotokion.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos: "Thou art my strength, O Lord" (Ты̀ моѧ̀ крѣ́пость Гдⷭ҇и):*[^547]  
**"Thou art my strength, O Lord..."**

*Troparia:*  
The wealth of Thy fatherly commandments, which Thou didst grant unto me to put away, I spent in voluptuousness, and I became poor and wretched, bereft of divine gifts; therefore, cast me not away, O Master and Lord, who repent with confession!

Divinely taking upon Thyself poverty, Thou dost unite unto Thyself me who of old was far from Thee, and Thou sanctifiest me unto gladness, O Lover of mankind: Thy divine Body, O Word, effecting my restoration and gladness.

That with unceasing tears we may be delivered afar from eternal torments, which God prepared for the wicked spirits, let us cry aloud as the prodigal: We have sinned against Thee, O Father, but receive all of us who flee unto Thy mercy!

*Theotokion:*  
The Word Who is co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit is born of the Virgin who knew not man, beyond the laws of nature: remaining that which He was, and taking upon Himself that which He received from us; for in two natures He is one Son, preserving in both that which is natural.

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "Why hast Thou cast me off" (Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ):*[^548]  
**"Why hast Thou cast me off..."**

*Troparia:*  
I have been deprived of wealth and divine gifts, and I arrived into a country melting with hunger for good and living things; but do Thou, O Father, as Thou art good, fill me with former glory and sweetness for the sake of Thy tender-heartedness!

Having taken to heart the thought of the prodigal who spent his life in prodigality, let us hasten unto the merciful Father with unhesitating faith and contrition of heart, that we may receive forgiveness of transgressions.

Linger not, O my soul, dwelling in a far country, but run quickly, confessing unto God and Father, that thou mayest receive the release of the evils thou hast committed, having found true life.

*Theotokion:*  
We know thee, O all-immaculate Virgin, as a radiant Cloud bearing the Sun of righteousness, borne within thee, putting to flight the darkness of idolatrous ignorance, and enlightening with true knowledge.

*Ode 6. Heirmos: "I will pour out my supplication" (Моли́твꙋ пролїю̀):*[^549]  
**"I will pour out my supplication..."**

*Troparia:*  
I became Thy younger son, and I evilly spent Thy wealth by departing into an evil life; and having been deprived of Thy good things, O Lover of mankind, I come unto Thee, my Father and God, and I ask forgiveness.

I obtained an inhuman estrangement, and I was condemned to pasture swine, because I scattered my wealth which Thou didst mercifully bestow quickly, and I became stripped of all; but do Thou, as my God, have compassion upon me!

I have no boldness, O Lover of mankind, having sinned against heaven's boundless height, to look up and see, nor to be called Thy son, prodigal that I am; but have compassion upon me freely, Thou Who possessest boundless mercy!

*Theotokion:*  
Unutterable is thy giving-birth, and ineffable is the manner of thy childbearing, O Maiden, for beyond understanding dost thou bear God, possessing undefiled virginity; therefore, as the true Mother of God do all of us worthily glorify thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*[^550]  
Now the time for action has appeared, judgment is at the doors; let us arise, therefore, in fasting, let us bring tears of contrition, calling out in almsgiving: We have sinned more than the sand of the sea, but pardon us, O Creator of all, that we may receive incorruptible crowns!

*Ikos:*  
I loved earthly and temporal things, and was deprived of eternal good things, O Christ Jesus, sole Lover of mankind, inexhaustible hope of sinners, Who hast poured out mercy upon the repentant; and despise me not who repent, O Jesus, for I know that the thief was saved by confession, the publican was cleansed by mercy, and I consider the harlot weeping: for in all these Thou didst set forth images of repentance; for Thou dost reprove mercifully, and art tenderly compassionate, Thou foreseest the repentant, and runnest forward as a Father: if Thou wilt, Thou art able to forgive also my sins, wherewith after baptism I defiled myself, and after the promise of an undefiled life; but pardon us, O Creator of all, that we may receive incorruptible crowns!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "The youths who arrived from Judea" (Ѿ І҆ꙋде́и доше́дше):*[^551]  
**"The youths who arrived from Judea..."**

*Troparia:*  
I have no boldness to be called Thy son, O Father, Lover of mankind, praying that I may become as one of Thy hirelings; abhor me not as I call aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Having defiled our life by our former conduct, and having destroyed our first nobility of birth, let us hasten unto our only Father and God, to receive salvation through warm repentance.

A harsh citizen was he to whom I, wretched one, was condemned to work; and the famine which I served, gratifying swine, was severe and unbearable; but turning back I entreat, O Savior: have mercy on me!

*Theotokion:*  
Thou hast quickened deadened nature, O Virgin Mother of God, who alone didst bear Life; therefore, we faithful know thee as salvation, who didst bear in the flesh the God of our fathers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Sevenfold the furnace" (Седмери́цею пе́щь):*[^552]  
**"Sevenfold the furnace..."**

*Troparia:*  
We who have believed in Thee know the wealth of Thine immeasurable and great tender mercy, O Master; therefore, with the prodigal do all of us warmly fall down before Thee: Receive those who have sinned and fled unto Thee: for there is no sin, there is no sin, O Compassionate One, that is able to overcome Thy love for mankind!

Having humbled Thyself for the sake of tender mercy, O Master, Thou hast drawn near unto Thy fallen sons; for Thou Thyself, O Lover of mankind, didst go to meet the fallen, and dost kiss them, and bestowest salvation: and if anyone falls down, being merciful Thou art not angered, as a Lover of mankind.

The most dreadful judgment terrifies me, O Master! For seeing Thee to be a Lover of mankind and free of malice, I do not hasten unto Thee, calling with the voice of the prodigal, but I spend my life in slothfulness; be Thou therefore merciful unto me, and snatch me from that condemnation, O Compassionate One, for the sake of repentance!

*Trinity Hymn:*  
Not three gods do we glorify, but one Godhead; yet three Hypostases do we truly honor: the Father unbegotten, and the Son begotten of the Father, and the Holy Spirit proceeding from the Father; one God in three, even though each name is faithfully glorified as God.

*Theotokion:*  
Bent down under many assaults, O all-immaculate one, and submerged by the tempest of sorrows, deliver me through thy prayer unto the haven of salvation, O all-pure one, and snatching me from all things, save me: that worthily, as a fervent protectress, I may glorify thee, the Mother of God, unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Heaven was dismayed at this" (Оу҆жасе́сѧ ѡ҆ се́мъ нб҃о):*[^553]  
**"Heaven was dismayed at this..."**

*Troparia:*  
With the joy and gladness of Thy fatted calf slain now for my sake, fill my soul that awaits this from Thee, O God; receive me who have gone astray, and lead me back unto life, and clothe me with the divine garment of salvation unto incorruption.

O souls distanced from God and bereft of divine gifts, come ye warmly, and taking up the conversion of the prodigal, let us cry: O good Father Who art in the heavens, we have all sinned against Thee: cleanse and save those who flee unto Thy mercy!

As Thou hast a great multitude of tender mercy, abhor me not, O Lord, Lord, who have spent my wealth in many pleasures, and who now flee and call with the voice of the prodigal: I have sinned against Thee, save him who flees unto Thy mercy!

*Theotokion:*  
Thee as a haven of salvation, a guide, and a protectress do I, thy servant, possess against all cruel obstacles, that as thou art good, by thine entreaties unto God, O Sovereign Lady, I may be delivered: for upon thee have I placed all my hope with love, that in faith I may magnify thee.

*Resurrectional Exapostilarion.*  
*At the Praises: We set 8 verses, and we sing 4 Resurrection stichera, and 4 Anatolian.*  
*Then we say the verse: "Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor unto the end."*  
*And we sing the idiomelon in Tone 6:*
