Unto those walking in the darkness of sins, Thou hast made light to shine, O Christ, through the season of self-control; and show unto us the fragrant day of Thy Passion, that we may cry unto Thee: Arise, O God, have mercy on us!  
*Glory: the same. Both now and ever: "Most blessed art thou":*  
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*  
*At the Liturgy: The Typika. The Beatitudes of the Octoechos of the Tone.*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 5:*  
Thou, O Lord, shalt keep us, and shalt preserve us from this generation and forever.  
*Verse: Save me, O Lord, for the godly man hath failed.*  
*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 304 [Heb. 1:10 - 2:3]. Alleluia of the Tone. Gospel from Mark, Pericope 7 [Mark 2:1-12].*  
*Communion Hymn: "Praise the Lord from the heavens":*[^554]  

[Ornamental Divider]

## Sunday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Vespers[^555]

*At the Lucernarium, we set 10 verses: and we sing 4 penitential stichera of the Octoechos, and the prosomoia of the triodion by Lord Joseph.*  [^555]
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Ineffable" (Неизрече́нъ):*  
Having sinned against Thee without number, I await unnumbered torments: gnashing of teeth, and inconsolable weeping, the Gehenna of fire, and darkness, and Tartarus, O most righteous Judge; grant me therefore tears, whereby I may find remission, and release of my evils, fasting and calling unto Thee: O Master Christ, have compassion upon me according to Thy great and rich mercy!

*Podoben:*  
Me who have gone astray upon the mountains of cruel transgressions, seek out, O Word, and call back unto Thyself, driving far away the wicked habits of my mind; quicken me again who am dead, and purify me by fasting, as I cry aloud and say in unceasing weeping: O Master Christ, have compassion upon me according to Thy great and rich mercy!

*Another sticheron by Lord Theodore, the same Tone. Podoben: "Most marvelous" (Пресла́вное):*[^556]  
Having begun the third week of the fast, let us, the faithful, praise the honorable Trinity, joyfully passing through the remainder; and having withered the fleshly passions from our souls, let us reap divine flowers, having woven crowns for the queen of days, that all of us, wearing crowns, may sing unto Christ as the Conqueror!

*And 3 from the Menaion. Glory, and both now and ever: Theotokion.*  
*"Gladsome Light":*  
*After the Entrance, Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*[^557]  
Turn not away Thy face from Thy servant, for I am afflicted; quickly give ear unto me: attend unto my soul, and deliver it!  
*Verse 1: Let Thy salvation, O God, receive me.*  
*Verse 2: Let the poor see and be glad.*  
*Verse 3: Seek God, and your soul shall live.*  
*And again in the highest voice:*  
Turn not away Thy face:  

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 8:*[^558]  
I have spit upon the fatherly furrows with an unstable mind, and I lived with beast-like sinful thoughts, spending all my life in prodigality, wretched that I am; deprived of food that strengthens the heart, I was filled with pleasure that temporally fattens; but, O good Father, shut not against me Thy bowels of loving-kindness, but opening them, receive me as the prodigal son, and save me!

*For the Martyrs:*  
O martyrs of the Lord, ye sanctify every place, and ye heal every disease; and now entreat, we pray, that our souls may be delivered from the snares of the enemy!

*Glory, and both now and ever: Theotokion, the same Tone:*  
The heavenly hosts sing of thee, O joyful Mother unwedded, and we glorify thine unsearchable childbearing, O Mother of God; pray that our souls may be saved!

*And the remainder of the office, as has been handed down.*  

[Headpiece Ornament: Icon of Christ the Savior Blessing.]

*THIRD WEEK OF THE GREAT FAST:*
## On Monday of the Third Week of the Great Fast
*At Matins:*  
*Trinity Hymns of the Tone.*  
*After the first stichology, we say the penitential Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos, of the occurring Tone.*  
*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn of Lord Joseph, Tone 8. Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*[^559]  
With the fire of self-control let us all burn up the brushwood uprisings of the passions, and that unquenchable flame let us now quench with streams of tears, crying unto Him Who is to judge all the earth: O Savior, tender-hearted Lord, preserve us uncondemned, granting us forgiveness of transgressions! *Twice.*

*Theotokion, Podoben:*  
O full of God's grace, pure and blessed one, for the tender mercy of the compassions of Him Who was born of thee, with the hosts on high, and with the archangels, and with all the bodiless powers, entreat unceasingly for us, that before the end He may grant us forgiveness, and cleansing of sins, and correction of life, that we may find mercy.

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn, of Lord Theodore, Tone 8. Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*[^560]  
O holy and honorable Trinity, preserve whole and uncondemned those who complete the fast during the three weeks, vouchsafe us to run well also unto the remainder, and to accomplish all Thy commandments: that thus we may attain uncondemned to behold the most glorious Resurrection, offering a hymn of praise! *Twice.*

*Theotokion, the same Tone:*  
O protection of the faithful, Mother of God, joy of the sorrowful, and great consolation of those who mourn: with the Angels and Principalities, unceasingly entreat Him Who was supernaturally born of thy most holy womb, O Virgin, that we may be delivered in the hour of examination from cruel condemnation.

*Canon from the Menaion, and the present triodion, by Lord Joseph.*  
*And the biblical ode is chanted.*  
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos: "Let us send up a hymn, ye peoples" (Пѣ́снь возсле́мъ лю́дїе):*[^561]  
**"Let us send up a hymn, ye peoples..."**

*Troparia:*  
Possessing as God that which is merciful, receive me who repent, who have spent my life in prodigality, as I cry unto Thee: I have sinned; I require no accusers, bearing the shame of mine own deeds!

Having abandoned angelic food, I was likened unto the beasts, when feeding on defiled malice; but receive me who turn back, as one of Thy hirelings, O heavenly Father!

Having abandoned the night of passions which increases from food, let all of us, the faithful, diligently draw near unto the light of truth, that we may be vouchsafed the radiant banquet.

*Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, O most holy Temple, Dew-covered Fleece, sealed Fountain of the immortal stream: preserve thy flock unravaged from all adversaries, O Sovereign Lady!

*Another canon by Lord Theodore, the same Tone. Ode 1. Heirmos: "Let us sing unto God" (Пои́мъ Бг҃ꙋ):*[^562]  
**"Let us sing unto God..."**

*Troparia:*  
Now in three weeks, even as Israel of old on the third day, having cleansed ourselves, O brethren, let us attain unto the mountain of prayers, and having heard from thence the divine voice, let us sing unto Christ!

Strengthened by fasting as was Samson, come, let us fiercely strike down the gluttonous demon, as another wild beast, and the Delilah of passions, which seduces us, will not laugh us to scorn!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
In cherubic manner do I sing of three, the holy Godhead in Thee: lights and Light, and lives and Life, God the Begetter, God the Begotten, God the living Spirit proceeding from the Father!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, O joy of Eve, for by thy childbearing her grief ceased, O pure one; rejoice, O radiant Cloud of the unwaning light, from which Christ God shone forth!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
By faith as David, let us also, having armed ourselves with humility of mind, cast down proud thought as another Goliath, and let us together cut down also the darkness of passions!

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
*Heirmos:*  
Let us sing unto God, Who delivered Israel from the bitter bondage of Pharaoh, and guided them with a pillar of fire and a bright cloud: for He hath been glorified![^563]

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Divinely-speaking [youths]" (Бг҃оглаго́ливи):*[^564]  
**"Divinely-speaking [youths]..."**

*Troparia:*  
I spent my fatherly wealth with harlots, and was cast out from the portals of grace, wretched that I am; but receive me, O Father, according to Thy boundless mercy!

Having been enlightened in the most radiant illumination of prayers of the fast, let us become light-bearing, that we may escape the darkness of sin.

The choir of the bodiless entreats Thee, the Cherubim entreat, and the ranks of the saints: save our souls, O Christ, abundant in mercy!

*Theotokion:*  
O Virgin Mother of God, hope of our souls, who didst conceive the Cluster of Grapes that was not tilled: make me chaste who am darkened by the drunkenness of sin!

*Tone 8. Ode 8. Another canon [by Lord Theodore]. Heirmos: "Who coverest with waters" (Покрыва́ѧй вода́ми):*[^565]  
**"Who coverest with waters..."**

*Troparia:*  
Come, O brethren, let us delight in the fast, the fatherly treasure and marvelous possession, the mother of all who have served the Master Christ; for it strengthens the body and enlightens the mind and heart.

Emulating marvelous Gideon, O my soul, bearing the virtues—faith, hope, and the love of Christ—go forth slaying foreign passions, even as he with three hundred destroyed the nation of Midian.

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
I glorify Thee as Trinity, and I praise Thee as Unity, one Godhead, O Father Almighty, and co-beginningless Son, and Holy Spirit: one sovereign Dominion, one Nature, one Kingdom, adored in three persons.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
She who gave Thee birth, having supernaturally escaped pangs at Thy Nativity, came to know pain at Thy precious Passion, and was in travail seeing Thee, Who didst establish the earth upon the waters, willingly nailed to the Cross by the Jews.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
When Thou shalt come in Thy glory to judge all the earth, with thousands and myriads of Thy hosts: then spare me, O Lord, remit and deliver me from condemnation; put me not to shame, nor condemn me to eternal fire.

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him unto all ages.*  
*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Who coverest Thine upper chambers with waters, Who settest the sand as a bound for the sea, and Who upholdest all things: Thee the sun hymns, Thee the moon glorifies, unto Thee all creation brings a hymn, as the Creator of all unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "That which was prefigured" (Проꙗвле́нное):*[^566]  
**"That which was prefigured..."**

*Troparia:*  
Having distanced myself from Thy love, O Father, and having been enslaved by willful assaults of pleasures, receive me who turn back as the prodigal; for Thou alone art abundant in mercy.

I have appeared as a barren fig tree, producing only wickedness, wretched that I am, having prepared fire for myself; do Thou, therefore, O Lord, show me forth as fruitful, bearing good deeds for Thee.

Fast, O my soul, from wickedness and deceit; refrain from wrath, anger, and every sin: for such is the fast Jesus desires, our God Who loves mankind.

*Theotokion:*  
Thou art our weapon and wall, O Theotokos; thou art the protection of those who flee unto thee; thee do we now move to prayer, that we may be delivered from our enemies.

*Another canon [by Lord Theodore], the same Tone. Ode 9. Heirmos: "We magnify thee" (Велича́емъ тѧ̀):*[^567]  
*Troparion:*  
Let us offer pure fasting and the putting away of evils, as acceptable gifts unto Christ our God.

Having learned how Elisha fed the prophets of old, O my soul, feed thyself kindly, giving thanks unto Christ.

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
I hymn Thee as the single tri-hypostatic Godhead, in persons the form of the unoriginate Father, and the Son, and the living Spirit.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
O Virgin Mother of God, bridal chamber of the heavenly King Christ: save by thy prayers those who hymn thee with love.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Daniel tamed the wild beasts in the den with the bridle of abstinence; let us also by fasting afflict the passions.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
We magnify thee, the all-immaculate Mother of Christ our God, whom the Holy Spirit overshadowed.

*Exapostilarion of the Tone:*  
*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 4:*  
I clothed myself in the cruel defilement of my transgressions, and was driven out from the joy of the bridal chamber; have compassion on me in Thine ineffable loving-kindness, as on the prodigal son, O God, and have mercy on me.

*Martyricon:*  
Who does not marvel, O holy martyrs, beholding the good contest wherein ye struggled? How, being in the body, ye conquered the bodiless foe, having confessed Christ and armed yourselves with the Cross; therefore ye worthily appeared as expellers of demons and chastisers of barbarians: pray unceasingly that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, and now and ever: Theotokion, the same Tone:*  
O unquenchable lamp, righteous throne, all-pure Sovereign Lady: pray that our souls may be saved.

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 4:*  
Since we are weak and paralyzed by sins, heal our brokenness, O Physician of our souls, Who knowest the mind of man, O Lover of mankind.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 41, Tone 4:*  
**Reader:** A prayer unto the God of my life, I will say unto God: Thou art my protector.[^568]  
*Verse: As the hart pants after the fountains of waters.*

*Prophecy of Isaiah, Reading:*  
**Reader:** Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (8:13–9:7).[^569]  
"Sanctify the Lord of hosts Himself, and He shall be thy fear. And if thou trust in Him, He shall be unto thee for sanctification, and not as a stumbling upon a rock of offence, nor as upon a stone of falling; yet the houses of Jacob are in a snare, and they that dwell in Jerusalem in a pit. Therefore many among them shall become weak, and fall, and be broken; and they shall draw near, and men shall be taken, being in security. Then shall they be made manifest that seal up the law, that they may not learn. And he shall say: I will wait for God Who turns away His face from the house of Jacob, and I will trust in Him: behold, I and the children whom God hath given me; and they shall be for signs and wonders in the house of Israel from the Lord of hosts, Who dwells on Mount Zion. And if they say unto you: Seek out the belly-diviners, and those that cry out from the earth, the ventriloquists that speak from the belly: shall not a nation seek unto their God? Why do they consult the dead concerning the living? For He gave the law for a help, that they might speak not according to this word, concerning which there is no giving of gifts. And a severe famine shall come upon you; and it shall come to pass, when ye shall hunger, ye shall be grieved, and ye shall speak evil of the prince and of your fathers. And they shall look up to heaven above, and shall look down upon the earth below: and behold, distressing straitness and darkness, affliction and anguish and gloom, so that they cannot see; and he that is in anguish shall not fail until the time. Drink this first, do it quickly, O land of Zebulun, and land of Naphtali, and the rest that dwell by the seacoast, and beyond the Jordan, Galilee of the Gentiles: the people that walked in darkness have seen a great light; ye that dwell in the land and shadow of death, a light shall shine upon you. The greater part of the people, whom Thou hast brought down in Thy gladness: they shall rejoice before Thee, even as they that rejoice in harvest, and as they rejoice that divide the spoils. For the yoke that lay upon them hath been taken away, and the rod that was upon their neck: for the Lord hath broken the rod of the exactors, as in the day of Midian. For every garment gathered by deceit, and robe with reconciliation they shall restore, and they shall wish to have been burned with fire. For unto us a Child is born, unto us a Son is given; Whose government was upon His shoulder, and His Name is called: Angel of Great Counsel, Marvelous Counselor, Mighty God, Ruler, Prince of Peace, Father of the age to come: for I will bring peace upon the rulers, peace and health unto Him. And great is His government, and of His peace there is no end upon the throne of David and upon His kingdom, to order it and to defend it with judgment and righteousness, from henceforth and unto the ages: the zeal of the Lord of hosts will perform this."

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 42, Tone 6:*  
**Reader:** The salvation of my countenance, and my God.[^570]  
*Verse: Judge me, O God, and plead my cause.*

## On Monday of the Third Week of the Holy Great Fast, at Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 stichera, and chant the following prosomoia of the Triodion, and 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Prosomoia Stichera by Lord Joseph.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "All-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃еницы):*[^571]  
Let us love the fast, which by the operation of the Spirit withers the fiercest passions of the soul, strengthens us to perform divine works, sends the mind up to heaven, and mediates forgiveness for those things wherein we have sinned, granted by the merciful God.

*Tone 1, Podoben: "All-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃еницы):*  
All my life have I shamefully squandered, O Lord, wretched that I am, with harlots; like the prodigal I cry in compunction: O heavenly Father, I have sinned, cleanse and save me, and cast not me away who of mine own accord have estranged myself from Thee, and have become utterly impoverished of divine deeds.

*Another sticheron, prosomoion by Lord Theodore, Tone 3, Podoben: "Come, all ye" (Прїиди́те всѝ):*[^572]  
Come, all ye, greeting precious abstinence with cymbal-clash of psalms, let us cry aloud: for in it we spiritually tear apart the serpent, the origin of evil; therefore let us boldly cry aloud unto Christ: grant us, O Savior, without condemnation to behold Thy all-pure Cross, and to venerate it, brightly celebrating in psalms and hymns.

*And 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Glory, and now and ever: Theotokion.*  
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 43, Tone 4:*  
**Reader:** In God will we boast all the day, and in Thy Name will we confess unto ages.[^573]  
*Verse: O God, with our ears have we heard.*

*Genesis, Reading:*  
**Reader:** Reading from Genesis (6:9–22).[^574]  
"Noah was a righteous man, being perfect in his generation; Noah pleased God. And Noah begat three sons: Shem, Ham, and Japhs. But the earth was corrupted before God, and the earth was filled with iniquity. And the Lord God saw the earth, and it was corrupt: for all flesh had corrupted its way upon the earth. And the Lord God said unto Noah: The time of every man is come before Me, for the earth is filled with iniquity from them; and behold, I will destroy them and the earth. Make therefore for thyself an ark of squared (rot-resistant) timbers; cabins shalt thou make in the ark, and thou shalt coat it within and without with pitch. And thus shalt thou make..."

"...make the ark: three hundred cubits the length of the ark, and fifty cubits the breadth, and thirty cubits the height thereof. Gathering it together with a vault shalt thou make the ark, and to a cubit shalt thou finish it above; and the door of the ark shalt thou make from the side; two-story and three-story chambers shalt thou make in it. And behold, I bring a flood, water upon the earth, to destroy all flesh wherein is the breath of life under heaven; and all things that are upon the earth shall die. And I will establish My covenant with thee; and thou shalt enter into the ark, thou, and thy sons, and thy wife, and thy sons' wives with thee. And of all cattle, and of all reptiles, and of all wild beasts, and of all flesh, two by two of all shalt thou bring into the ark, that thou mayest nourish them with thee; male and female shall they be. Of all winged birds after their kind, and of all cattle after their kind, and of all reptiles that crawl upon the earth after their kind, two by two of all shall enter unto thee, to be nourished with thee: male and female. And take thou unto thee of all food that ye eat, and thou shalt gather it unto thee, and it shall be food for thee and for them. And Noah did all things whatsoever the Lord God commanded him, so did he."

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 44, Tone 6:*  
**Reader:** I will remember Thy Name in every generation and generation.[^575]  
*Verse: My heart hath poured forth a good word.*

*Proverbs, Reading:*  
**Reader:** Reading from the Proverbs (8:1–21).[^576]  
"Son, preach thou wisdom, that understanding may give ear unto thee: for she is upon the high peaks, and stands in the midst of the pathways; for she sits at the gates of the mighty, and is sung at the entries. Unto you, O men, do I appeal, and I utter my voice to the sons of men. Understand craftiness, ye simple ones, and apply your heart, ye untaught. Give ear unto me, for I will speak honorable things, and from my lips I will bring forth right things. For my throat shall meditate truth, and lying lips are an abomination before me. All the words of my mouth are with righteousness; there is nothing crooked or perverse in them. They are all straight to them that understand, and right to them that find knowledge. Receive instruction and not silver, and knowledge rather than tested gold; choose knowledge rather than pure gold. For wisdom is better than precious stones, and nothing that is honorable is worthy of her. I, wisdom, have dwelt with counsel, and have called upon knowledge and understanding. The fear of the Lord hates unrighteousness, insolence, pride, and the ways of the wicked; and I have hated the perverse ways of evil men. Mine are counsel and steadfastness, mine is understanding, mine is strength. By me kings reign, and the mighty decree justice. By me princes are magnified, and rulers hold the earth. I love them that love me, and they that seek me shall find grace. Riches and glory are mine, and the possession of many things and righteousness. It is better to bear my fruit than gold and precious stones, and my fruits are better than chosen silver. I walk in the ways of righteousness, and live in the midst of the paths of justification, that I may distribute substance to them that love me, and fill their treasuries with good things."

*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 2:*  
O good Father, my folly hath stripped me of all that Thou gavest me; I distanced myself from Thee, and became enslaved to a strange citizen, pasturing abominable beasts, and I was not even filled with their food; therefore I have fled unto Thee, knowing Thy loving-kindness: cover my nakedness with Thy love for mankind, and save me.

*Martyricon:*  
When the holy martyrs pray for us and hymn Christ, all deception ceases, and the human race is saved by faith.

*Glory, and now and ever: Theotokion, the same Tone:*  
From thy virginal womb light shone forth unto the world, the Word through the word; entreat Him, O pure and all-praised Virgin, that our souls may be delivered from the snares of the enemy.

## On Tuesday of the Third Week of the Holy Great Fast, at Matins

*Trinity Hymns of the Tone.*  
*After the first stichology, penitential sessional hymns from the Octoechos.*  
*After the second stichology, sessional hymn of Lord Joseph, Tone 1, Podoben: "The stone having been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*[^577]  
By fasting and prayer let us cleanse ourselves, and by caring for the poor let us please God, let us sigh, let us weep fervently: for we have time for conversion, that we may be delivered from eternal weeping in the flame of Gehenna, sending up glory unto Christ Who hath provided repentance for men, unto all that turn with uprightness of mind.  
*Twice.*

*Theotokion:*  
Thy divine hands, wherewith thou didst bear the Creator, O all-pure Virgin, incarnate in the Godhead, stretch forth and entreat, that we may be delivered from temptations, passions, and tribulations, who praise thee with love and cry aloud unto thee: Glory to Him Who made His abode in thee; glory to Him Who came forth from thee; glory to Him Who hath set us free by thy Nativity!

*After the third stichology, sessional hymn of Lord Theodore, Tone 3, Podoben: "Let [the heavens] rejoice" (Да веселѧ́тсѧ):*[^578]  
Rejoicing in fasting, glad in hymns, and renewing through prayers our mastery over all passions, let us trample underfoot the snares of Belial, crying aloud unto Christ as we run our course: Vouchsafe us to behold Thy Cross, as Thou art compassionate, granting us great mercy!  
*Twice.*

*Theotokion, Tone 3, Podoben: "The beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^579]  
O divinely blessed unwedded Mother, heal my ailing soul, for I am grievously held fast by transgressions, O Theotokos; therefore I cry unto thee with sighing of my heart: receive me, O all-immaculate one, who have sinned much, that with boldness I may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O dwelling-place of God!

*Canon from the Menaion, and the present triodion by Lord Joseph. And the biblical ode is chanted.*  
*Ode 2, Tone 1. Heirmos: "See, see, that I am God" (Ви́дите ви́дите, ꙗ҆́кѡ Бг҃ъ а҆́зъ є҆́смь):*[^580]  
See, see, that I am God, Who commanded Moses to lead the enslaved people of Israel in the wilderness, and saved them as mighty by My power!

*Troparia:*  
Thou hast renewed me by the bath of baptism, O most good Father, adorning me with all wealth of good things; but I willfully became enslaved to hostile thoughts, therefore I have become impoverished.

Be merciful, be merciful, be merciful unto me, O Lord; judge my judgment for me, O righteous Judge, accepting my small sighing in place of great mercy, O Christ, and despise me not.

Having stripped off the defiled garment of incontinence, let us clothe ourselves in the bright robe of abstinence, and having become radiant, let us attain unto the radiant Resurrection of the Redeemer.

*Theotokion:*  
Save me, save me, O thou who gavest birth to the Savior! Behold my affliction, O pure one, which the multitude of my measureless evils causes all the days to my wretched soul from despair.

*Another canon by Lord Theodore, Tone 3. Ode 2. Heirmos: "See, see, that I am He" (Ви́дите ви́дите, ꙗ҆́кѡ а҆́зъ є҆́смь):*[^581]  
*Troparia:*  
See, see, that I am He, Who gushed forth salvation for you as God: which, through abstinence, all ye earthly draw forth!

See, see, that I am He, Who hath defined for you the sacred time, the holy course of the fast for the spiritual banquet of your soul!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
Let us glorify the Trinity, let us worship the Unity: the unoriginate only-begotten Son of the unoriginate Father, the Spirit co-enthroned, and the Father co-eternal!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Entreat the Savior unceasingly, O all-immaculate one, that those who confess thee as Theotokos with lips and soul may be delivered from every tribulation.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Great art Thou, O Lord, and marvelous are Thy works, for in the den Thou didst of old show forth Daniel uneaten by the lions, protected by fasting!

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
See, see, that I am the Savior of the world, the true Light, the Fountain of life, the Son of God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Him before Whom tremble" (Е҆гѡ́же ᲂу҆жаса́ютсѧ):*[^582]  
**"Him before Whom tremble..."**

*Troparia:*  
I weep and lament, reflecting on the hour wherein I must stand before the dread throne, guilty of punishment for my many evils, which I committed in ignorance and with knowledge.

Moses having found fasting to be a cleansing, perceived God, the only pure One; O my soul, cleanse thyself by fasting, that thou mayest draw near unto God Who loves mankind.

Let us love the fast, the mother of virtues; let us hate food, the parent of passions, and let us cry aloud: Our Father Who art in heaven, save us, save us by the prayers of Thy saints!

*Theotokion:*  
Entreat the Judge Whom thou didst bear, O Virgin Mary, to have compassion upon me in the hour of judgment, O pure one, and to save me who am condemned, O sole protectress of the human race.

*Another canon [by Lord Theodore]. Tone 3. Ode 8. Heirmos: "Into the flame unto the youths" (Въ пла́мень ко ѻ҆трокѡ́мъ):*[^583]  
*Troparia:*  
Having received the all-radiant grace of the fasts, let us shine forth with virtues, showing gentle countenances and gentle habits of soul's disposition.

Having fasted, Elisha gave the child alive to the Shunammite woman; let us understand concerning this, O brethren: how good is fasting! How great! What a God-given grace!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
With the Father let us worship the Son, and the Holy Spirit in the one Trinity, crying with the Angels with strong lips: Glory in the highest unto God, Who is in Trinity!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Thou bearest a child while remaining a virgin, O only one full of divine joy; a great mystery, a terrible wonder: for thou didst bear God incarnate, the Savior of the world!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Let none of us be encompassed with despondency and sluggishness, O brethren! It is the time of labor, the hour of triumph: who is wise then to gain all ages in a single day?

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord:*  
*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Who descended into the flame unto the Hebrew youths by divine power, and appeared as Lord: bless Him, ye works of creation, and exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "The light-bearing cloud" (Свѣтоно́сный ѻ҆́блакъ):*[^584]  
**"The light-bearing cloud..."**

*Troparia:*  
Having fallen into the abyss of sin, into the depth of despair, into the surge of thoughts and the tempest of passions, I cry aloud: O Abyss of mercy, help me, and by Thy loving-kindness rain down upon me cleansing of sins!

With fasting the body, with abstinence the spirit, with tears the soul let us cleanse, ye faithful; and pure, let us brightly behold the Pure One, seeing the saving Passion which the Redeemer endured for our sake.

Like the publican I groan, like the harlot I shed tears, like the thief I cry aloud: Remember me, O Compassionate One! As the blind man I cry unto Thee: O Son of God, open the eyes of my soul, blinded by the malice of the deceitful supplanter!

*Theotokion:*  
The uncut mountain, the impassable door, higher than the heavens and all creation, the beauty of Jacob, the all-golden jar, the bridge, and the Mother of the Creator: the Virgin let us bless, ye faithful!

*Another canon [by Lord Theodore], Tone 3. Ode 9. Heirmos: "On Mount Sinai" (На Сїна́йстѣй горѣ̀):*[^585]  
*Troparia:*  
Having understood the present days, sacred above all others, let us send up prayers unto God with a pure conscience, frequently bowing our knees and saying: O Lord, receive the supplications and prayers of Thy servants unceasingly!

The people sat down, as it is written, to eat and to drink, and rose up to play, serving the idol Baal-peor; see the cruel waves of gluttony! But let us fast, and let us be glorified, like great Moses!

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
In essence the Unity is indivisible, the transcendent Trinity, united in Nature, is divided personal-wise: for indivisibly It is divided, being One It is threefold: this is Father, Son, and living Spirit, preserving all things!

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Who hath heard of a virgin mother, and a mother without a husband? Mary, thou workest a wonder! But tell me, how? Search not into the depth of my divine childbearing; this is entirely true, surpassing the comprehension of human mind.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Since Thy wrath is unbearable against sinners, who henceforth shall endure, O Lord, this threatening? Having measureless mercy, save me by Thy loving-kindness, not according to my many and cruel deeds!

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
On Mount Sinai Moses saw thee in the bush, having conceived in thy womb without burning the Fire of the Godhead; and Daniel saw thee as the uncut mountain; a rod that budded, Isaiah cried aloud, from the root of David.

*Exapostilarion of the Tone.*  
*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 7:*  
I have sinned, I confess unto Thee, O Lord, prodigal that I am; I dare not look up to heaven with mine eyes, for having fallen from thence, I became wretched; I have sinned against heaven and before Thee, and am not worthy to be called Thy son; I renounce myself, I require no accusers, nor witnesses again: I have fornication that convicts me, I have an evil life that convicts me, I have shame that holds fast my nakedness, and the pits unto disgrace wherewith I am clothed; O compassionate Father, only-begotten Son, Holy Spirit, receive me who repent, and have mercy on me.

*Martyricon:*  
In the midst of the tribunal of transgressors, rejoicing, Thy passion-bearers cried aloud: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory, and now and ever: Theotokion, Tone 7, Podoben:*  
Thou wast born of the Virgin ineffably, O Christ, and didst enlighten those who were in darkness, crying: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 7:*  
Thou art our salvation, O Lord, and defender in the day of sorrow; have mercy on us according to Thy great mercy, O Lover of mankind.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 45, Tone 4:*  
**Reader:** The Lord of hosts is with us, our protector is the God of Jacob.[^586]  
*Verse: God is our refuge and strength.*

*Prophecy of Isaiah, Reading:*  
**Reader:** Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (9:8–10:4).[^587]  
"Thus says the Lord: All the people of Ephraim and they that dwell in Samaria shall know, speaking in insolence and a proud heart: The bricks are fallen down, but come, let us hew stones, and let us cut down sycamores and cedars, and build for ourselves a tower. And God shall destroy them that rise up against Mount Zion, and shall scatter His enemies: Syria from the rising of the sun, and the Greeks from the setting of the sun, devouring Israel with open mouth; in all this His anger is not turned away, but His hand is stretched out still. And the people turned not back until they were smitten, and they sought not the Lord. And the Lord took away from Israel head and tail, great and small, in one day: the elder, and they that admire faces, this is the head; and the prophet that teaches lawless things, he is the tail. And they that call this people blessed shall lead them astray, and they lead them astray that they may swallow them up. Therefore the Lord shall not rejoice over their young men, neither shall He have mercy on their orphans and their widows: for all are lawless and deceitful, and every mouth speaks unrighteousness; in all this His anger is not turned away, but His hand is stretched out still. And lawlessness shall burn as fire, and as dry couch grass shall it be devoured by fire; and it shall burn in the thickets of the forest, and shall devour all that is round about the hills. For the wrath of the fury of the Lord the whole land is burned up, and the people shall be as men burned by fire: a man shall not spare his brother. But he shall turn to the right hand, for he shall be hungry, and shall eat from the left hand, and a man shall not be satisfied eating the flesh of his arm: for Manasseh shall eat Ephraim, and Ephraim Manasseh, for together they shall war against Judah. In all this His anger (His) is not turned away, but His hand is stretched out still. Woe unto them that write wickedness: for writing they write wickedness: turning aside the judgment of the needy, seizing the judgment of the poor of My people, that widows may be their prey, and an orphan for robbery. And what will they do in the day of visitation? For affliction shall come upon you from afar; and unto whom will ye flee for help? And where will ye leave your glory, that ye fall not into captivity? And they shall fall under the slain; and in all this His anger is not turned away, but His hand is stretched out still."

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 46, Tone 3:*  
**Reader:** Sing praises to our God, sing praises; sing praises to our King, sing praises.[^588]  
*Verse: All ye nations, clap your hands.*

## On Tuesday of the Third Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 stichera, and chant 3 prosomoia of the Triodion, and 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Prosomoia of the Triodion, by Lord Joseph, Tone 3. Podoben: "They weighed thirty pieces of silver" (Поста́виша три́десѧть сре́бреникъ):*[^589]  
O Lord, Who by the Cross didst slay the deceiver, deliver me from his delusion, me who sin and am deceived; having cleansed me by the fast, grant me to accomplish Thy desires, that I may see, O Master, rejoicing, Thy precious Sufferings.

*Podoben:*  
I was wounded by the weapon of pleasure, O Lord, and became utterly dead; heal and revive my humble soul, O Master Who wast pierced with a spear; and me, who am wounded by the arrow of the enemy, heal as Thou art compassionate, and show me forth as a partaker of Thy precious Sufferings.

*Another sticheron by Lord Theodore, Tone 8. Podoben: "Let Him be crucified" (Да ра́спнетсѧ):*[^590]  
Having enlightened our souls by fasting, O Lord, make us worthy without condemnation to behold Thy Cross in joy, and to venerate it in fear and love; for it enlightens Thy praiseworthy Sufferings, which do Thou deign that we may reach, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*And 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Glory, both now and ever: Theotokion.*  
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 47, Tone 3:*  
**Reader:** Great is the Lord, and greatly to be praised, in the city of our God.[^591]  
*Verse: In the city of the Lord of hosts, in the city of our God.*

*Reading from Genesis (7:1–5):*[^592]  
**Reader:** The Lord God said unto Noah: Enter thou and all thy house into the ark, for I have seen thee righteous before Me in this generation. And of clean cattle bring in unto thee seven and seven, male and female; and of unclean cattle two and two, male and female. And of clean fowls of the heaven seven and seven, male and female, and of all unclean fowls two and two, male and female, to nourish seed upon all the earth. For yet seven days, and I will bring rain upon the earth forty days and forty nights; and I will destroy all that hath arisen which I made from the face of all the earth. And Noah did all things whatsoever the Lord God commanded him.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 48, Tone 2:*  
**Reader:** My mouth shall speak of wisdom, and the meditation of my heart shall be of understanding.[^593]  
*Verse: Give ear unto this, all ye nations.*

*Reading from the Proverbs (8:32–9:11):*[^594]  
**Reader:** Son, listen unto me, and blessed are they that keep my ways. Hear wisdom, and be wise, and reject it not. Blessed is the man who listens unto me, and the man who keeps my ways, watching daily at my gates, waiting at the posts of my doors. For my issues are the issues of life, and desire is prepared from the Lord. But they that sin against me act impiously against their own souls, and they that hate me love death. Wisdom has built a house for herself, and set up seven pillars. She has killed her sacrifices, and mingled her wine in the bowl, and prepared her table. She has sent forth her servants, calling with a high proclamation unto the bowl, saying: Whosoever is simple, let him turn aside unto me; and to them that lack understanding she says: Come, eat of my bread, and drink the wine which I have mingled for you. Forsake folly, and ye shall live, that ye may reign unto the ages; and seek understanding, that ye may live, and establish understanding in knowledge. He that instructs the evil shall receive dishonor to himself, and he that rebukes the ungodly shall defame himself (for rebukes to the ungodly are wounds to him). Rebuke not the evil, lest they hate thee; rebuke a wise man, and he will love thee. Give occasion to a wise man, and he will be wiser; instruct a righteous man, and he will add to receiving it. The beginning of wisdom is the fear of the Lord, and the counsel of the holy is understanding: for to know the law is the property of a good mind. For in this manner thou shalt live a long time, and years of thy life shall be added unto thee.

*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 2:*  
I dare to call Thee my Father and Creator, O Lord, being an earthly creature; partaking of Thine image, though I have sinned against the likeness, having lived prodigally, and squandered the riches in forgetfulness of Thy gifts: do not then disown me, Thou Who wast well pleased for Thy only-begotten Son to endure in the flesh the Cross and death for my sake: but having pronounced me blessed, unite me unto Thyself, O Lover of mankind.

*For the Martyrs:*  
The ranks of the martyrs opposed the tyrants, saying: We are soldiers of the King of hosts; though ye deliver us to fire and torments, we reject not the power of the Trinity.

*Glory, both now and ever: Stavrotheotokion, the same Tone. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*  
When the lawless people, O Savior, hung Thee, the Life of all, upon the Tree, then the all-immaculate Ewe-lamb and Thy Mother, standing by, sobbed weeping: O my sweetest Child, light of mine eyes! Woe is me, how hast Thou endured to be nailed to the Tree in the midst of evildoers, Thou Who hast hung the earth upon the waters?

## On Wednesday of the Third Week of the Great Fast, at Matins

*Trinity Hymns.*  
*After the first stichology, the sessional hymns of the Cross from the Octoechos.*  
*After the second stichology, we chant the present sessional hymn by Lord Joseph, Tone 3. Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^595]  
By the Tree Thou didst quench the flame of transgression, O Master, being lifted up upon the Cross, and Thou didst slay the enemy, being slain of Thine own will; therefore I pray Thee: slay the desires of my flesh, and revive my wretched heart by the passion-slaying fast, cleansing me from every defilement, as Thou art tender-hearted.

*Glory, the same. Both now and ever: Stavrotheotokion. Podoben:*  
A shameful death, O Compassionate One, Thou didst willingly endure by crucifixion; beholding which, she who bore Thee, O Christ, was wounded, was torn in her womb, and maternal-wise sobbed; by whose prayers, for the loving-kindness of Thy mercy, show compassion, and save the world, Thou Who takest away its sin.

*After the third stichology, sessional hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Having risen from the tomb" (Воскр҃съ и҆з̾ гро́ба):*[^596]  
Passing through the bright table of the fast, we cry aloud: Preserve all in peace, O Lord, delivering us from every craftiness of the enemy, and make us worthy, as Thou art all-good, to kiss Thy precious Cross in love, whereby Thou hast granted Thy mercies to the universe, O only Abundantly-merciful One.

*Glory, the same. Both now and ever: Stavrotheotokion, the same Tone. Podoben: "Being the wellspring of loving-kindness" (Милосе́рдїѧ):*[^597]  
The Virgin and Thy Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee stretched out dead upon the Tree, weeping bitterly, cried aloud and said: What is this fearful mystery, O Thou Who grantest eternal life unto all? How diest Thou willingly on the Cross by a shameful death?

*Canon from the Menaion, and the present triode by Lord Joseph.*  
*And the biblical ode is chanted.*  
*Ode 3, Tone 3. Heirmos: "The Affirmation" (Оу҆твержде́нїе):*[^598]  
**"The Affirmation..."**

*Troparia:*  
Being crucified together for our sake with Him Who was crucified, let us put to death all bodily members in fasts, in prayers, and in supplications.

Thou Who by Thy Cross didst uproot the thorns of sin, O Lord, tear up the thorny reasonings of my mind.

Having armed our thoughts with the fast, let us conquer the alien demonic hosts, shielded by the power of the Cross.

*Theotokion:*  
The Word, being incarnate, came forth from thee, O Most Pure One, correcting the fall of the forefathers in the loving-kindness of His compassions.

*Another canon by Lord Theodore, Tone 8. Heirmos: "Establish us in Thee, O Lord" (Оу҆твердѝ на́съ въ тебѣ̀ Гдⷭ҇и):*[^599]  
**"Establish us in Thee, O Lord..."**

*Troparia:*  
Thy life-creating Cross, O Lord, is a seal unto me for salvation; for having thereby abolished the adversary, I hymn Thee as the mighty God.

The Tree of the Cross brought forth unto the world the fruit of everlasting life; tasting whereof, O Christ, we are delivered from death.

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
I glorify three Persons of one form, Father, Son, and Spirit, one dominion of the Godhead; for It reigns and rules as sole Principle, as God.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Thy childbearing, O Pure One, is fearful; for God is He Who became man, being begotten timelessly of the Father, and born from thee in the last times without a husband.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
The grace of the Cross, having shone forth in advance upon the world, summons us all unto Thy divine Sufferings, O Compassionate One; make us worthy faithfully to venerate them.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who hymn Thee.

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Unto the unbearable fire" (Нестерпи́момꙋ ѻ҆гню̀):*[^600]  
**"Unto the unbearable fire..."**

*Troparia:*  
Thou Who by the Cross didst vanquish the principalities and the dark powers, O Giver of light, when Thou shalt come with power to judge all the world, expose not my secret deeds, O Word, that I may glorify Thine abundant loving-kindness.

Thou, the Judge, didst stand being judged before the judgment seat, condemning enmity by Thy Cross, O longsuffering and righteous Judge; therefore deliver from eternal condemnation those who cry in fear and exalt Thy love for mankind.

Sharpened by the fire of the fast, the godly youths truly quenched with divine dew the flame raised up of old; and we who fast, let us quench the furnace of all passions, that we may escape the flame of Gehenna.

*Theotokion:*  
Divine Wisdom, having built for Herself a temple from thee, became incarnate by ineffable condescension, O Maiden unwedded; for thou alone from all generations hast been chosen, incorrupt one, to be the dwelling of the incorrupt Word.

*Another canon [by Lord Theodore]. Heirmos: "In the bush unto Moses" (Въ кꙋпинѣ̀ Мѡѷсе́ю):*[^601]  
**"In the bush unto Moses..."**

*Troparia:*  
Bearing all things by Thy word, Thou endurest all things for my sake: buffeting, and spitting, and crucifixion, O Christ; I praise the greatness of Thy love for mankind unto all ages.

Thou art slain, O Christ, as a lamb, pierced in Thy side, that Thou mayest save me, the straying sheep, from the snare of the devil, and number me in Thy good fold unto ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
One Godhead in Trinity, indivisible Nature, yet divided in Persons, ever-existing Dominion, O Father, Son, and Holy Spirit: we hymn Thee in song unto all ages.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
O pure Mother of God, heavenly door, saving gate, receive the supplication of all Christians who bless thee unto all ages.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
In Thy Cross, O Christ, priests boast, kings are strengthened, and every faithful one is enlightened; make me worthy to see it, and to venerate it, and to hymn it unto ages.

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him unto all ages.*  
*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Unto Him Who of old prefigured the wonder of the Virgin in the bush unto Moses on Mount Sinai, sing, bless, and exalt Him unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "In the shadow of the law" (Въ зако́нѣ сѣ́ни):*[^602]  
**"In the shadow of the law..."**

*Troparia:*  
Moses, having lifted up the serpent on the tree, prefigured Thee, O Jesus, willingly lifted up on the Cross, Who didst drive away the venomous malice of the evil one, and didst draw men unto Thyself, O Lover of mankind.

Cleanse me with the fire of Thy fear, kindling in my soul Thy divine love, and surround me with Thy Cross, which the ancient supplanter seduced, having darkened my reasoning with pleasures, O Christ.

Let us fast, O brethren, from defiled thoughts and evil deeds, let us cleanse our hearts, let us be winged with divine virtues, and let us turn aside from earthly assaults of malice, that we, light-bearing, may behold the great Pascha.

*Theotokion:*  
Wishing to clothe Himself in our poverty from thee, O Virgin, He Who is rich in nature, and Who is hymned by the heavenly ranks, became visible to us, the Invisible One, renewing the shattered image in His goodness.

*Another canon [by Lord Theodore]. Heirmos: "Thee, the noetic ladder" (Тебѐ мы́сленнꙋю):*[^603]  
**"Thee, the noetic ladder..."**

*Troparia:*  
Thee, Who didst stretch out Thy hands upon the Cross and gather the four ends together, whereby we found access unto the Father, O Son of God, all of us do magnify.

Having crowned Thee with thorns, O Christ, the lawless beat Thee and crucified Thee, for Whose sake all things under heaven trembled; but we magnify Thee Who hast saved us.

*Glory: Trinity Hymn:*  
Thee, the thrice-radiant Holy Godhead, Who upholdest all things and preservest them ever, Father, Son, and living Spirit, with unceasing hymns do we magnify.

*Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
Thee, the bright cloud of the Spirit, from whom shone forth unto us the unapproachable Light, Christ, the great Sun of righteousness, with hymns, O Mother of God, do we magnify.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Thou art our light, holy wood, victory-bearing conquest, O Cross of Christ; do Thou make smooth our abstinence, and make us worthy to venerate Thee.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Thee, the noetic and living ladder, whereon our God was established, whereby we found an ascent unto heaven, with hymns, O Theotokos, do we magnify.

*Exapostilarion of the Tone.*  
*At the Aposticha, the idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 2:*  
Being in the honor of salvation, in foolishness I understood not the good Father; but I deprived myself of glory, evilly squandering the riches of grace; and being in want of divine food, I became a flatterer to the vile citizen, by whom I was sent into his soul-captivating field, living prodigally, I fed with the swine, and serving pleasures I was not filled; but turning back, I cry unto the tender-hearted and compassionate Father: I have sinned against heaven and before Thee, have mercy on me.

*For the Martyrs:*  
The multitudes of Thy saints pray unto Thee, O Christ: Have mercy on us, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Glory, both now and ever: Stavrotheotokion. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*  
Having endured many sufferings at the crucifixion of thy Son and God, O Most Pure One, thou didst groan with tears, and didst cry out bitterly: Woe is me, sweetest Child! How sufferest Thou unjustly, wishing to deliver the earthborn from Adam? Therefore, all-immaculate Virgin, we pray thee in faith: make Him merciful unto us.

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 8:*  
There is no substance of deeds in us, O Lord; Thou art merciful, O Lover of mankind; despise not the works of Thy hands, O Sinless One.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 49, Tone 4:*  
**Reader:** Sacrifice unto God a sacrifice of praise, and pay thy vows unto the Most High.[^604]  
*Verse: The God of gods, the Lord, hath spoken, and called the earth.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (10:12–20):*[^605]  
**Reader:** It shall come to pass, when the Lord shall finish doing all things on Mount Zion and in Jerusalem, He will bring judgment upon the great mind, upon the Prince of Assyria, and upon the high glory of his eyes. For he said: By the strength of my hand will I do it, and by the wisdom of my understanding will I take away the bounds of the nations, and will plunder their strength. And I will shake inhabited cities, and with my hand will I grasp the whole world as a nest, and will take them as abandoned eggs; and there is none that shall escape me, or say anything against me, or open his mouth and murmur. Shall the axe boast itself without him that hews therewith? Or shall the saw magnify itself without him that draws it? In like manner if one should lift up a rod or wood: and it is not so. But the Lord of hosts shall send dishonor upon thine honor, and upon thy glory a burning fire shall be kindled. And the light of Israel shall be for a fire, and He shall sanctify him with burning fire, and it shall devour the matter as hay. In that day the mountains, and the hills, and the forests shall be extinguished, and it shall devour from soul even to flesh; and he that flees shall be as one fleeing from a burning flame. And they that are left of them shall be a small number, and a little child shall write them down. And it shall come to pass in that day, that the remnant of Israel shall no more be added, and the saved of Jacob shall no more trust in them that wronged them; but they shall trust in God, the Holy One of Israel, in truth.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 50, Tone 6:*  
**Reader:** Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy.[^606]  
*Verse: Create in me a clean heart, O God.*

## On Wednesday of the Third Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 stichera, and chant the idiomelon twice, and the Martyricon, and 3 prosomoia of the Triodion.*  
*Idiomelon, Tone 4:*  
Prodigally have I squandered my fatherly wealth; I became desolate, I dwelt in the country of wicked citizens, and I was likened unto the irrational dumb beasts, and became stripped of all divine grace; therefore turning back, I cry unto Thee, the tender-hearted and compassionate Father: I have sinned; receive me who repent, O God, and have mercy on me.

*For the Martyrs:*  
Living sacrifices, rational whole burnt offerings, martyrs of the Lord, perfect oblations of God, sheep that know God and are known of God, whose sheepfold cannot be entered by wolves: pray that we also may be pastured with you beside the water of rest.

*Other stichera by Lord Joseph, Tone 6. Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^607]  
Having appeared as rays of the spiritual Sun, O apostles who beheld God, ask enlightenment for our souls, and deliver us from the dark gloom of passions; and pray that we may see the saving day, having cleansed our hearts with fasting and prayers, which the deceitful one wounded: that being saved by faith, we may ever honor you who saved the world by your all-wise preaching.

*Podoben:*  
Having departed as a prodigal into the land of malice, I squandered the riches which Thou, O compassionate Father, gavest me; and I melt away with hunger for good deeds, and behold, I am clothed with the shame of transgression, being stripped of the grace of God, and I cry unto Thee: I have sinned, for I know Thy goodness; receive me as one of Thy hired servants, O Compassionate Christ, by the supplications of the apostles who loved Thee.

*Another sticheron, prosomoion by Lord Theodore, the same Tone. Podoben: "At the right hand of the Savior" (Ѡдеснꙋ́ю Сп҃са):*[^608]  
Apostles of the Savior, luminaries of the universe, benefactors and saviors, tellers of God's glory as the heavens, adorned with stars of miracles and signs of healings: earnestly offer prayers unto the Lord for us, to receive our prayers unto the end in purity, and that all of us may be accounted worthy to kiss and behold with fear the life-bearing Cross; and through the veneration thereof, send down Thy mercies upon us, O Savior, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*And 4 from the Menaion.*  
*Glory, both now and ever: Theotokion.*  
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 51, Tone 4:*  
**Reader:** I have hoped in the mercy of God unto ages, and unto ages of ages.[^609]  
*Verse: Why boastest thou in malice, O mighty man?*

*Reading from Genesis (7:6–9):*[^610]  
**Reader:** Noah was six hundred years old, and the flood of waters came upon the earth. And Noah went in, and his sons, and his wife, and his sons' wives with him into the ark, because of the waters of the flood. And of clean fowls, and of unclean fowls, and of clean cattle, and of unclean cattle (and of beasts), and of all reptiles that crawl upon the earth, two and two went in unto Noah into the ark, male and female, even as God commanded Noah.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 52, Tone 4:*  
**Reader:** When the Lord turns back the captivity of His people.[^611]  
*Verse: The fool hath said in his heart: There is no God.*

*Reading from the Proverbs (9:12–18):*[^612]  
**Reader:** Son, if thou be wise, thou shalt be wise for thyself and for thy neighbors; but if thou prove evil, thou alone shalt draw out evil. A son that is instructed shall be wise, but the foolish shall be used as a servant. He that leans upon lies feeds upon the winds, and he pursues birds that fly; for he has left the pathways of his own vineyard, and has wandered from the paths of his own husbandry; and he passes through a waterless desert, and a land...

**Reader:** ...assigned to thirst, and gathers in his hands unfruitfulness. A foolish and brazen woman comes to lack bread; she knows no shame, she sits at the doors of her house, upon a stool openly in the streets, calling unto passers-by that go right on their ways: Whosoever is the most foolish among you, let him turn aside unto me; and to them that lack understanding I command, saying: Touch secret bread with pleasure, and drink stolen sweet water. But he knows not that the earthborn perish with her, and that they are in the depths of Hades. But spring away, tarry not in her place, neither fix thine eye upon her; for so shalt thou go through strange water, and pass over a strange river; but keep away from strange water, and drink not of a strange fountain, that thou mayest live a long time, and years of life may be added unto thee.

*And immediately the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified.*

## On Thursday of the Third Week of the Great Fast, at Matins

*After the first stichology, we say the sessional hymns of the Apostles from the Octoechos.*  
*After the second stichology, we chant the present sessional hymn by Lord Joseph, Tone 6. Podoben: "O hope of the world" (Наде́ждо мі́ра):*[^613]  
Unerring luminaries of the world, disciples of the Savior, enlighten my soul blinded by sin, and show me forth as a partaker of the divine day, keeping the saving commandments, and deliver me from that unillumined darkness, that I may glorify you.

*Glory, the same. Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
O holy Sovereign Lady Theotokos, Mother of Christ our God, who ineffably gavest birth to the Creator of all: pray ever with the holy apostles unto His goodness, to deliver us from passions, and to grant us remission of sins.

*After the third stichology, another sessional hymn by Lord Theodore, Tone 6. Podoben: "The Angelic Powers" (А҆́гг҃льскїѧ):*[^614]  
Luminaries shining upon the world, divine apostles, enlighten your chanters, paying recompense for the time of the fast, worthily praying that all may behold the Tree of the life-bearing Cross, and kiss it with undefiled lips and eyes, crying aloud in psalmody: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory, the same. Both now and ever: Theotokion:*  
O good Theotokos Virgin, hope of the world, we bring thy sole and fearful intercession; show mercy upon helpless people, and pray unto the merciful God, that our souls may be delivered from every threatening, O only blessed one.

*Canon from the Menaion, and the present triode by Lord Joseph.*  
*And the biblical ode is chanted.*  
*Ode 4, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Christ is my power" (Хрⷭ҇то́съ моѧ̀ си́ла):*[^615]  
**"Christ is my power..."**

*Troparia:*  
Fasting, tears, prayers, almsgiving, a well-contrite disposition, righteous thought, and purity of life let us show forth, ye faithful, that we may enjoy glory.

As shining luminaries on the firmament of the precious Church, ye appeared most brightly, O apostles; therefore enlighten our hearts with the Divine Spirit.

As divine coals ignited with immaterial fire, burn up the passionate matter of our hearts, O wise apostles of our God.

*Theotokion:*  
O unquenchable lampstand, all-bright bridal chamber, higher than the Seraphim, all-immaculate Cherubic chariot: free me from grievous sins and tribulations.

*Another canon by Lord Theodore, the same Tone. Heirmos: "The prophet heard" (Оу҆слы́ша проро́къ):*[^616]  
**"The prophet heard..."**

*Troparia:*  
Having appeared as luminaries of divine manifestation, O apostles of the Savior, ye dispersed the night of ignorance, and having made bright the Church, ye enlighten the universe with your fiery-bright teachings.

Having traversed the world, one unto one land, and another unto another, ye gathered all into the one faith, O apostles; and having attained heaven, ye exult, praying unceasingly to Christ that all of us may be saved.

*Trinity Hymn:*  
I glorify the Son from the Father, and the Spirit, as light and ray from the sun: the One by begetting, since He is the Begotten; the Other by proceeding, since He is the Proceeding: the co-unoriginate divine Trinity, adored by all creation.

*Theotokion:*  
A Virgin bearing a Child and preserving purity, O Pure One, thou didst appear, having given birth to God and man, the one and the same in both forms; thy wonder, O Virgin Mother, strikes with awe every hearing and thought.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
By your holy prayers, O apostles of the Lord, may we be accounted worthy to kiss with pure lips and eyes the life-creating Cross, set forth for those in the world and adored.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
The prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid, how Thou wouldst be born of a Virgin and appear unto men, and he said: I heard the report of Thee and was afraid; glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "From the flame unto the righteous" (И҆з̾ пла́мене преподо́бнымъ):*[^617]  
**"From the flame unto the righteous..."**

*Troparia:*  
Fasting showed Moses as a beholder of God, and Elias was taken up in a fiery chariot; be zealous, O soul, refrain from harmful thoughts, that thou mayest be delivered from the downward-dragging deceit.

*Troparia:*  
Having stumbled, I fell into worse evils, and I add a wound unto my sore; heal, O Christ, the petrified hardness of my heart, O Compassionate One, through the prayers of the Apostles.

O God-radiant luminaries of the world, disperse the clouds of our passions, and show us to be worshipers of the radiant Resurrection, singing the praises of the Sun of glory.

*Theotokion:*  
Generations of generations call thee blessed, O divinely blessed one, as of old thou didst foretell; for unto mankind thou alone hast ineffably given birth to the blessed Word, incarnate of thee.

*Another canon by Lord Theodore, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Thy holy children" (Преподо́бнїи твоѝ ѻ҆́троцы):*[^618]  
**"Thy holy children in the furnace..."**

*Troparia:*  
Having caught with the rod of faith those swimming in the abyss of deceit, O apostles, ye brought them unto the Lord, blessing, chanting, and exalting the Lord unto the ages.

Great is Thy power in the Apostles, for their shadows and handkerchiefs heal the sick, as they bless, chant, and exalt the Lord unto the ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
By unoriginate being, by begetting, and by proceeding: I worship the Father Who begat, I glorify the Son Who was begotten, and I praise the Holy Spirit Who co-shone forth unto the Father and the Son.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Cease not ever to pray Him Whom thou hast ineffably borne, O Virgin, since He is the Lover of mankind, that He may save from perils those who flee unto thee.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
O choir of twelve holy apostles, make me worthy to bow down in worship without condemnation before the precious Cross, blessing, chanting, and exalting Him unto all ages.

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord.*  
*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Thy holy children in the furnace imitated the Cherubim, chanting the thrice-holy hymn: Bless, sing, and exalt Him unto all ages.

*Ode 9, Tone 6. Heirmos: "It is not possible for men to see God" (Бг҃а человѣ́кѡмъ):*[^619]  
**"It is not possible for men to see God..."**

*Troparia:*  
O God and Lord of great mercy, cleanse the wounds of my heart, applying unto me the remedies of repentance: I have sinned, I have sinned against Thee; have compassion, save me by the prayers of Thine Apostles, for Thou art abundant in mercy.

I have followed unknown impulses with an injured mind, and I was wholly outside of myself, nourished on harsh pleasures, and deprived of saving food, prodigal that I am; O Christ, have compassion and save me.

O soul that hast not cleansed thyself of malice, and hast not distanced thyself from corruptive pleasures, why dost thou untimely rejoice in fasting? For not this fast hath the Lord chosen, Who alone desires our amendment.

*Theotokion:*  
Enlighten me with divine Light, O Virgin Mother of God, Mother of the Light, and drive away the gloom of my soul, that I may call thee blessed, whom all human generations call blessed, even as thou didst foretell.

*Another canon. Heirmos:*  

*Another canon by Lord Theodore, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Of seedless conception the birth" (Безсѣ́меннагѡ Зача́тїѧ):*[^620]  
**"Of seedless conception the birth..."**

*Troparia:*  
Untaught in speech, yet exceedingly wise in understanding did ye appear, having destroyed the woven subtleties of the philosophers' words, the sophisms of rhetoricians, and the counting of the astrologers; therefore, O apostles of Christ, ye alone were revealed as teachers of the whole universe.

Peter discourses eloquently, and Plato is struck dumb; Paul teaches, and Pythagoras is put to shame; the rest of the company of apostle-theologians buries the dead Greek utterance, and gathers the world unto the service of Christ.

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O Father of the Only-begotten Son, the Only-Begetter of the One; and Thou, O Only One of the One, O Light, Radiance of the Light; and Thou, the One Holy Spirit of the one God, of the Lord, being truly the Lord: O Holy Trinity, the Unity, save me who speak in theology of Thee.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
The wonder of thy childbearing astonishes me, O All-Immaculate One: how dost thou conceive without seed Him Who cannot be comprehended? Tell how thou remainest a virgin after giving birth as a Mother? Having received with faith that which is above nature, bow down in worship before Him Who was born; for whatsoever He wills, He is also able to do.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
May we all be accounted worthy to behold and venerate the precious Cross, the all-holy Wood that made salvation blossom for the world, with pure lips and soul, through the prayers of all you holy apostles of Christ.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
The ineffable childbearing of seedless conception, the incorrupt Fruit of an unwedded Mother: for God's Nativity renews natures; therefore all generations, as the Bride-unwedded Mother of God, do orthodoxly magnify thee.

*Exaposteilarion of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 6:*  
Having fallen away from adoption as a son, I, a prodigal, fed slavishly with the swine, and was not even satisfied with their food; I come unto Thee, the tender-hearted Father: whence I wickedly went forth, thither I return rightly in repentance, crying out: I have sinned against heaven and before Thee, O Father; grant me great mercy in my turning back.

*For the Martyrs:*  
Our God hath made marvelous His chosen and holy ones; rejoice and be glad, all ye His servants, for He hath prepared for you crowns and His Kingdom: but we pray you, forget not us also.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
O Mother of God, thou art the true vine that hast blossomed for us the Fruit of life; unto thee we pray: Intercede, O Sovereign Lady, with the holy Apostles [and all the saints], that He have mercy on our souls.

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 3:*  
If Thou mark iniquities, O Lover of mankind, what hope of salvation is there for us? But send down Thy help from on high unto Thy people, O Lord, as Thou art compassionate.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 53, Tone 4:*  
**Reader:** Save me, O God, by Thy Name, and judge me in Thy power.[^621]  
*Verse: O God, hear my prayer, give ear to the words of my mouth.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (11:10–12:2):*[^622]  
**Reader:** Thus saith the Lord: In that day there shall be a root of Jesse, and He that arises to rule the nations, in Him shall the Gentiles hope; and His rest shall be honor. And it shall come to pass in that day, that the Lord shall set His hand again to be zealous for the remnant of the people that is left from the Assyrians, and from Egypt, and from Babylon, and from Ethiopia, and from the Elamites, and from the rising of the sun, and from Arabia, and from the islands of the sea. And He shall set up a banner for the nations, and shall gather the lost ones of Israel, and gather together the dispersed of Judah from the four corners of the earth. And the jealousy of Ephraim shall be taken away, and the enemies of Judah shall perish: Ephraim shall not envy Judah, and Judah shall not afflict Ephraim. And they shall fly in the ships of the aliens, together they shall despoil the sea, and those from the rising of the sun, and Idumaea: and upon Moab they shall first lay their hands, and the children of Ammon shall first be subject unto them. And the Lord shall desolate the sea of Egypt, and shall lay His hand upon the river with a mighty wind: and He shall smite it into seven channels, so that one may pass through it dry-shod: and there shall be a highway for My people that are left in Egypt, and it shall be unto Israel as in the day when he came up out of the land of Egypt. And in that day thou shalt say: I will bless Thee, O Lord, for Thou wast angry with me, and hast turned away Thy fury (from me), and hast pitied me. Behold, God is my Savior, I will trust in Him, and will not be afraid; for my glory and my praise is the Lord, and He is become my salvation.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 54, Tone 7:*  
**Reader:** Give ear, O God, to my prayer, and despise not my supplication.[^623]  
*Verse: Attend unto me, and hear me.*

## On Thursday of the Third Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and chant these prosomoia, three times, and 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Stichera of Lord Joseph, Tone 5. Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*[^624]  
O Savior, the true sweetness, Who of old didst sweeten the bitter waters of Marah by the figure of the wood, and then of the divine Cross, having stretched out Thyself upon it as Thou wast well pleased; and Thou didst taste of gall, pierced in Thy side, from which water of forgiveness poured forth unto the world for the renewal of earthly mold; therefore we glorify Thine ineffable power, and pray: Grant us Thy fear, O Lord, during the time of fasting, and forgiveness of transgressions, and great mercy.

O Word Who wast stretched upon the Cross, gathering those who were far from Thee: having lifted up my mind from the mire of passions, enrich it with every virtue, granting unto my heart Thy purest fear, and unto my soul perfect love, severing me from the love of the flesh; that by abstinence unto Thee, by prayer and supplication, I may be well-pleasing unto Thee in these present days, and may behold Thy radiant day of Resurrection, receiving great mercy.

*Another sticheron by Lord Theodore, Tone 1. Podoben: "A radiant cloud" (Ѻ҆́блакъ тѧ̀ свѣ́тлый):*[^625]  
Having traversed the third week of the precious fasts, O Christ the Word, account us worthy to behold the wood of Thy life-bearing Cross, and reverently to venerate it, and to sing worthily, and to glorify Thy dominion, to hymn Thy Passions, and in purity to reach the glorious and holy Resurrection, the mystical Pascha, through which Adam entered again into Paradise.

*And 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*  
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 55, Tone 6:*  
**Reader:** Have mercy on me, O God, for man hath trodden me down.[^626]  
*Verse: Mine enemies have trodden me down all the day long.*

*Reading from Genesis (7:11–8:3):*[^627]  
**Reader:** In the six-hundredth year of Noah's life, in the second month, on the twenty-seventh day of the month, on that day all the fountains of the abyss were burst open, and the floodgates of heaven were opened. And there was rain upon the earth forty days and forty nights. On that very day entered Noah, Shem, Ham, and Japhs, the sons of Noah, and Noah's wife, and the three wives of his sons with him into the ark; and all the wild beasts of the earth according to their kind, and all cattle according to their kind, and every creeping thing that moves upon the earth according to its kind, and every winged fowl according to its kind. They went in unto Noah into the ark, two and two of all flesh wherein is the breath of life, male and female. And they that entered in, male and female of all flesh, went in as the Lord God had commanded Noah; and the Lord God shut the ark from without. And the flood was forty days and forty nights upon the earth; and the water increased, and lifted up the ark, and it was raised high above the earth. And the water prevailed, and increased exceedingly upon the earth, and the ark was borne upon the face of the water. And the water prevailed exceedingly, exceedingly upon the earth, and covered all the high mountains that were under heaven. Fifteen cubits upward did the water prevail, and covered all the high mountains. And all flesh died that moved upon the earth, of fowls, and of cattle, and of beasts, and every creeping thing that moves upon the earth, and every man. And all things whatsoever have the breath of life, and all that was upon the dry land, died. And every living thing was blotted out that was upon the face of all the earth, from man even unto cattle, and creeping things, and fowls of the heavens; and they were blotted out from the earth: and Noah only remained, and they that were with him in the ark. And the water prevailed upon the earth an hundred and fifty days. And God remembered Noah, and all the wild beasts, and all the cattle, and all the fowls, and all the creeping things that crawled, as many as were with him in the ark; and God brought a wind upon the earth, and the water abated. And the fountains of the abyss were stopped, and the floodgates of heaven; and the rain from heaven was restrained. And the water returned from off the earth after an hundred and fifty days.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 56, Tone 7:*  
**Reader:** Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me, for my soul hath trusted in Thee.[^628]  
*Verse: And in the shadow of Thy wings will I hope.*

*Reading from Proverbs (10:1–13):*[^629]  
**Reader:** A wise son makes a glad father, but a foolish son is a sorrow to his mother. Treasures of the lawless shall not profit, but righteousness delivers from death. The Lord will not slay the righteous soul with hunger, but the life of the ungodly He overthrows. Poverty brings a man low, but the hands of the manly make rich. An instructed son will be wise, but the foolish will be used as a servant; a prudent son is saved from the heat, but a lawless son is blighted by the wind in harvest. The blessing of the Lord is upon the head of the righteous, but untimely weeping shall cover the mouth of the ungodly. The memory of the righteous is with praises, but the name of the ungodly is extinguished. The wise in heart will receive commandments, but he that is uncovered in his lips, being perverse, shall be tripped up. He that walks simply, walks trustingly; but he that perverts his ways shall be known. He that winks with his eye deceitfully heaps up sorrows for men; but he that reproves with boldness makes peace. A fountain of life is in the hand of the righteous, but destruction shall cover the mouth of the ungodly. Hatred stirs up strife, but love covers all that are not contentious. He that brings forth wisdom from his lips beats a heartless man with a rod.

The wise hide perception, but the mouth of the rash draws near to destruction. The wealth of the rich is a strong city, but the destruction of the ungodly is poverty. The works of the righteous produce life, but the fruits of the ungodly are sins. Instruction keeps the ways of life, but he that is unchastened by instruction wanders astray. Righteous lips cover enmity, but they that utter reviling are most foolish. From many words thou shalt not escape sin, but sparing lips, thou shalt be prudent. The tongue of the righteous is refined silver, but the heart of the ungodly shall vanish away. The lips of the righteous know high things, but the foolish die in want. The blessing of the Lord is upon the head of the righteous; it makes rich, and sorrow shall not be added to it in his heart.

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 6:*  
O Christ God, the Life of all, Who wast hanged upon the tree of the Cross: revive my soul that is deadened by transgressions, and leave not Thy sheep to perish utterly, O Good Shepherd! I have departed from Thy commandments, and the wealth of sinlessness which Thou gavest me, I squandered by sin-loving choice; I have melted away and become loathsome in prodigal enterprises: but renew me, drawing me unto repentance, O Thou Who alone art abundant in mercy.

*For the Martyrs:*  
Thy Martyrs, O Lord, denied Thee not, nor departed from Thy commandments: by their prayers have mercy on us.

*Glory... Both now... Cross-Theotokion, Tone 6. Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ):*[^630]  
Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, she who bare Thee cried aloud: What is this strange mystery that I behold, O my Son? How diest Thou in the flesh, hanged upon the tree, O Giver of life?

## On Friday of the Third Week of the Great Fast, at Matins

*Trinity Hymns of the Tone.*  
*After the first stichology, we say the Cross Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos.*  
*After the second stichology, we say this present Sessional Hymn, by Lord Joseph, Tone 5. Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное):*[^631]  
We men, who of old died by the eating from the tree, have been brought to life by Thy Cross, O Compassionate One; by its power strengthen us, O Good One, to pass through the season of abstinence in compunction, doing Thy will, and to behold the day of the radiant Resurrection.

*Glory, the same. Both now... Cross-Theotokion:*  
Beholding Thee, O Christ, hanging willingly upon the Cross between thieves, Thy Mother, wounded in her maternal womb, said: O sinless Savior, how art Thou unjustly fastened to the Cross like a malefactor, desiring as the tender-hearted One to bring to life the human race?

*After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn of Lord Theodore, Tone 1. Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*[^632]  
Having purified our flesh through abstinence, and having enlightened our souls in prayers, O Lord, account us worthy to behold Thy precious and holy Cross, and with fear to venerate it, singing in hymns and saying: Glory to the life-bearing Cross, glory to Thy divine spear, whereby we have been brought to life, O only Lover of mankind!

*Glory, the same. Both now... Cross-Theotokion:*  
The Ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb hanging upon the tree, crucified with thieves, O Long-suffering Word, and pierced in the side with a spear, lamented, crying out maternally: What is this strange and fearful mystery, O my Jesus?
