How is the uncircumscribed God covered by a tomb? Ineffable is that which is done: leave me not alone who bare Thee, O my sweetest Jesus!

*Canon of the Menaion, and these triodes by Lord Joseph. And the biblical ode is chanted.*  
*Ode 5, Tone 5. Heirmos: "Thou that clothest Thyself with light as with a garment" (Ѡ҆дѣ́ѧйсѧ свѣ́томъ ꙗ҆́кѡ ри́зою):*[^633]  
**"Thou that clothest Thyself with light as with a garment..."**

*Troparia:*  
Heal me, O Lord, and I shall be healed by Thy wound, having cleansed the wounds of my soul, O Christ Who in the flesh didst receive the Passions.

When the sun beheld Thee hanging in the flesh upon the tree, O Christ, it turned its light into darkness, and the earth was shaken, and the rocks were split asunder.

My soul overgrown with thorns and hardened with every kind of passion do Thou cleanse, O Lord, Who wast crowned with thorns for Thine exceeding goodness.

*Theotokion:*  
Newly hast thou borne as an Infant my Creator, O All-Immaculate One; implore Him, O Sovereign Lady, to renew me, who have grown old in much malice.

*Another canon by Lord Theodore, Tone 1. Heirmos: "From night early waking" (Ѿ но́щи оу҆́тренююще):*[^634]  
**"From night early waking..."**

*Troparia:*  
At the place of a skull the Jews crucified Thee, O Christ; they wagged their heads, mocking and deriding; but Thou didst endure, in order to save us.

Writing threefold upon the title of Thy Cross, Pilate made manifest that Thou, One of the Trinity, didst willingly suffer for the salvation of all, O Christ.

*Trinity Hymn:*  
Let us faithful hymn the three-sunned splendor of the All-Radiant Trinity, honoring the Father as Light, glorifying the Son as Light, and proclaiming the Spirit also as Light.

*Theotokion:*  
He Who was born of thee hath shown thee higher than the Angels, O Pure One; for Him Whom they tremble to behold as God, thou bearest in thine arms as thy Son, O Pure One.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
O all-precious, saving Cross, keeper of the world: preserve me as I fast, and show me worthy of venerating thy precious self.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Rising early from the night, we hymn Thee, O Christ God, Who becamest poor for our sake, and in the flesh didst endure the Cross and death.

*Ode 8, Tone 5. Heirmos: "Unto Thee, the Creator of all" (Тебѣ̀ Вседѣ́телю):*[^635]  
**"Unto Thee the Creator of all..."**

*Troparia:*  
Thou hast stretched out Thy palms upon the Cross, O Christ, doing away with the intemperance of the forefather's hand; and by the tree Thou hast healed the curse of the tree; therefore we sing of Thee unto all ages.

Unto Thee, the Guide of salvation, O Word, I fall down: quickly remove from my heart wicked desires, O Thou Who didst willingly endure the Cross and suffering.

Having desired a prodigal life and a defiled conduct, I, the prodigal, became darkened: shine forth upon me the ray of turning back, O Word, Who in Thy suffering didst darken the shining of the sun.

*Theotokion:*  
Calm all the painful malice of my heart, O Mother of Christ God; shatter the arrows and bows of the demons that fight against my humble soul.

*Another canon, Tone 1. Heirmos: "The hymn-singers in the furnace" (Пѣсносло́вцы въ пещѝ):*[^636]  
**"The hymn-singers in the furnace..."**

*Troparia:*  
What shall we, wretched ones, offer unto Thee Who didst suffer, O Master of all? For for our sake Thou hast endured the Cross, O Christ; we sing the grace of Thy measureless mercy.

The lawless Jews who slew Thee through envy lifted Thee up upon the tree, O Christ, yet they diminished not the majesty of Thy glory; for Thou art Master of all, Who didst suffer willingly.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
I venerate the three Persons of the Super-essential Godhead: the unoriginate Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit; I glorify unto the ages the Essence undivided in nature.

*Theotokion:*  
Higher than the heavens and more exalted than the Cherubim, the golden-winged and undefiled Maiden of the God of all do we hymn and exalt unto all ages.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
O most blessed Cross of Christ, heaven-inscribed victory, Thou hast sprouted forth unto us from the earth: show us all, who are cleansed by fasting, worthy to venerate Thee.

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord.*  
*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Chant unto Christ God, Who saved the children singing hymns in the furnace, and turned the roaring flame into dew, and exalt Him unto all ages.

*Ode 9, Tone 5. Heirmos: "Rejoice, O Isaiah" (И҆са́їе ликꙋ́й):*[^637]  
**"Rejoice, O Isaiah..."**

*Troparia:*  
Wash away the petrified hardness of my soul, O tender-hearted Savior, and grant me, O God, a fountain of divine compunction, Thou Who from Thy side didst pour out life for me, consuming the torrents of mine iniquity.

I have cast myself into the depths of sins, having abandoned the heights of virtues; but bend down and save me, O Lord Who didst ascend the Cross and draw mankind unto Thyself.

Being sweetness and delight, as Creator Thou didst taste of gall, O Christ, setting right the falling away from delight that came from Adam; therefore we who are saved by Thy Passions sing of Thee.

*Theotokion:*  
O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, my hope and refuge: heal the sores of my soul, bring peace unto my mind, that I also rejoicing may hymn thy great deeds, O Mother of God, Ever-Virgin.

*Another canon, Tone 1. Heirmos: "Thee whom Moses saw" (Тѧ̀ ю҆́же ви́дѣ):*[^638]  
**"Thee whom Moses saw..."**

*Troparia:*  
Thee Whom the lawless, O Christ, clothed insultingly in purple and a crown, mocking Thee as King, striking Thy head with a reed, crucifying Thee through envy, and giving Thee gall to drink: with hymns do all of us faithful magnify.

Thee, at Whose suffering upon the Cross the sun hid its light, all creation bowed down together in mournful sorrow, and the rocks were split asunder: with hymns, O Christ the Savior, do we magnify.

*Trinity Hymn:*  
Light, and Life, and Creator of all, the Trinity of three Suns, God and Lord, shining with a single light, radiating together in three hypostases in one Godhead: Him do all of us faithful magnify.

*Theotokion:*  
Thee whom David the chanter proclaimed as Zion, wherein He Who cannot be contained by the heavens made His abode, and fashioned from thy womb cleansing for the world: with hymns, O pure Mother, do we magnify.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*  
*Troparion:*  
Account those running the course of abstinence worthy, O Lord, to reach, behold, and venerate Thy saving Cross, the enlightener of the faithful, unto our sanctification, that with it we may magnify Thee.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Thee whom Moses saw as the unburnt bush, and the animate ladder which Jacob saw, and the heavenly gate through which Christ our God passed: with hymns, O pure Mother, do we magnify.

*Exaposteilarion of the Tone.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 6:*  
I squandered the wealth of the paternal gift; I spent my life in prodigality, conscious of my sin through evil thoughts; and taking pleasure in the sweetness of passion, I was likened unto the senseless beasts through the transgression of saving commandments: but Thou Who wast well pleased to be hanged upon the Cross for my sake, O Christ God, cast me not away from Thine adoption, but receive me as I turn back, like the prodigal son, and save me.

*For the Martyrs:*  
O Lord, at the memory of Thy saints all creation keeps festival, the heavens rejoice with the Angels, and the earth makes merry with men: by their prayers have mercy on us.

*Glory... Both now... Cross-Theotokion, the same Tone. Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ):*[^639]  
The All-Immaculate One, when she beheld Thee hanging upon the Cross, wept and cried out maternally: O my Son and my God, my sweetest Child, how endurest Thou reproachful suffering?

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 8:*[^640]  
By Thy Word and by Thy power hast Thou accomplished all things, O God, and brought us from non-being into being; deliver us not over unto our iniquities, O sinless Lord, we pray Thee.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 57, Tone 6:*  
**Reader:** The righteous man shall rejoice when he sees vengeance.[^641]  
*Verse: If ye indeed speak righteousness.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (13:2–13):*[^642]  
**Reader:** Thus saith the Lord: Lift ye up a banner upon the mountain of the plain, exalt the voice unto them, fear not: beckon with the hand, open, ye rulers! I give command, they are consecrated: and I lead them, giants come to fulfill My wrath, rejoicing together and insulting. The voice of many nations upon the mountains, like as of many nations, the voice of kings and nations gathered together! The Lord of Sabaoth hath given command to a warrior nation, to come from a far country, from the uttermost foundation of heaven, the Lord and His warriors, to destroy the whole world. Wail ye! for the day of the Lord is near, and destruction from God shall come: therefore shall every hand become feeble, and every soul of man shall be afraid; and the ambassadors shall be troubled, and pangs shall take hold on them as of women in travail: they shall mourn one to another, and be amazed, and their faces shall change like a flame. For behold, the day of the Lord comes, incurably, of wrath and fury, to make the whole world desolate, and to destroy the sinners out of it. For the stars of heaven and Orion and all the adornment of heaven shall not give their light; and the sun rising shall be darkened, and the moon shall not give her light. And I will command evil upon the whole world, and upon the ungodly their sins: and I will destroy the reviling of the lawless, and will humble the insolence of the proud. And they that are left shall be more precious than unsmelted gold, and a man shall be more precious than the stone from Ophir. For the heaven shall be enraged, and the earth shall be shaken from its foundations, because of the wrath of the anger of the Lord of Sabaoth in the day wherein His fury shall come.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 58, Tone 7:*  
**Reader:** O God, Thou art my helper, and Thy mercy shall go before me.[^643]  
*Verse: Deliver me from mine enemies, O God.*

## On Friday of the Third Week of the Great Fast, at Vespers

*The usual stichology.*  
*On "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and sing the idiomelon of the day twice, Tone 7:*  
Like the prodigal I departed from Thy grace, O Lord, and I squandered the wealth of goodness; unto Thee I flee, O Tender-hearted One, crying aloud unto Thee, O God: I have sinned, have mercy on me.

*Then the 4 Sticheron Martyrs of the Octoechos of the occurring Tone; and 4 in the Menaion:*  
*Glory... Sessional hymn for the departed of the Tone. Both now... The first Theotokion of the Tone.*  
*Entrance: "O gladsome Light..."*  
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 59, Tone 4:*[^644]  
Give us help from tribulation, and vain is the salvation of man.  
*Verse: O God, Thou hast cast us off and destroyed us.*  
*Reading from Genesis (8:4–21):*[^645]  
**Reader:** And the ark rested in the seventh month, on the twenty-seventh day of the month, upon the mountains of Ararat. And the water continued to decrease until the tenth month; and in the tenth month, on the first day of the month, the tops of the mountains were seen. And it came to pass after forty days, Noah opened the window of the ark which he had made. And he sent forth a raven (to see if the water had subsided from the face of the earth); and going forth, it did not return until the water had dried up from off the earth. And after it he sent forth a dove, to see if the water had subsided from the face of the earth. And the dove, finding no rest for her feet, returned unto him into the ark, for water was upon the face of all the earth; and stretching forth his hand, he took her, and brought her in unto himself into the ark. And having waited yet seven days, he again sent forth the dove out of the ark. And the dove returned unto him toward evening, having an olive sprig with leaves in her mouth; and Noah knew that the water had subsided from the face of the earth. And having waited yet another seven days, he again sent forth the dove, and she did not return unto him any more. And it came to pass in the six hundred and first year of Noah's life, on the first day of the first month, the water had dried up from off the face of the earth; and Noah uncovered the roof of the ark which he had made, and saw that the water had dried up from off the face of the earth. And in the second month, on the twenty-seventh day of the month, the earth was dry. And the Lord God spoke unto Noah, saying: Go forth out of the ark, thou, and thy wife, and thy sons, and thy sons' wives with thee. And all the wild beasts that are with thee, and all flesh from birds even unto cattle, and every creeping thing that moves upon the earth, bring forth with thee; and increase and multiply upon the earth. And Noah went forth, and his wife, and his sons, and his sons' wives with him:

And all the wild beasts, and all cattle, and all birds, and all creeping things that move upon the earth according to their kind, went forth out of the ark. And Noah built an altar unto the Lord; and he took of all clean cattle, and of all clean birds, and offered a whole-burnt offering upon the altar. And the Lord smelled a sweet savor.

*Second Prokeimenon, Psalm 60, Tone 6:*[^646]  
Hear my supplication, O God, attend unto my prayer.  
*Verse: From the ends of the earth have I cried unto Thee.*  
*Reading from Proverbs (10:31–11:12):*[^647]  
**Reader:** The mouth of the righteous drops wisdom, but the tongue of the unrighteous shall perish. The lips of righteous men drop grace, but the mouth of the ungodly is perverse. Deceitful balances are an abomination before the Lord, but a just weight is pleasing unto Him. Wheresoever insolence enters, there also is dishonor; but the mouth of the humble meditates on wisdom. The righteous man dying leaves repentance, but the destruction of the ungodly is swift and brings derision. The perfection of the upright shall guide them, but the stumbling of the rebellious shall plunder them. Possessions shall not profit in the day of wrath, but righteousness delivers from death. The righteousness of the blameless makes his ways straight, but ungodliness falls into unrighteousness. The righteousness of upright men delivers them, but the lawless are caught in their own counsel-lessness. When a righteous man dies, his hope does not perish, but the boast of the ungodly perishes. The righteous man escapes from the snare, and in his stead the ungodly is delivered up. In the mouth of the ungodly is a snare for citizens, but the discernment of the righteous is prosperous. In the good things of the righteous a city is set upright, and at the destruction of the ungodly there is rejoicing. In the blessing of the upright a city is exalted, but by the mouth of the ungodly it is overthrown. A man void of understanding mocks his citizens, but a wise man keeps silence.

*And the rest of the Liturgy of the Presanctified.*

## On the Third Saturday of the Great Fast, at Matins

*Troparion on "Alleluia":*  
**"Apostles, martyrs, and prophets..."** *Glory...* **"Remember, O Lord..."** *Both now...* **"Holy Mother..."**  
*After the first stichology, we say the Sessional Hymns for the Martyrs of the Octoechos, of the occurring Tone, three; and the Theotokion.*  
*After the second stichology, we say the Sessional Hymn for the departed, Tone 5:*  
**"Give rest, O our Savior..."** **"Thou Who didst shine forth from the Virgin..."**  
*Concerning all these, direction was given on the past Saturday (see fol. 209).[^648] And a reading in the Hexaemeron.*  
*We sing the canon of the Menaion, and of the saint of the monastery, and the four-ode canon according to their order. Here we do not chant the biblical canticles "Let us sing unto the Lord," but immediately say the Heirmos of the Menaion.*

*Four-ode canon, whose acrostic is:*  
**Joseph chants these praises.**  
*Composition of Lord Joseph. And the biblical odes are chanted. Ode 6, Tone 4. Heirmos: "I entered into the depths of the sea" (Прїидо́хъ во глꙋбины̑ морскї̑ѧ):*[^649]  
**"I entered into the depths of the sea..."**  
*Troparia:*  
With drops of your blood ye watered the hearts of the pious, but the ranks of the impious ye drowned as it were therein, O praiseworthy passion-bearers.

Having glorified the Master of all with your members, ye are now glorified unceasingly, being a glory and great defenders unto the faithful, O sufferers.

Thou wast laid in the lowest pit: unto those who have died in the faith, O Word, grant rest through the supplications of the holy sufferers, and the remission of transgressions.

*Theotokion:*  
That with unceasing voices we may praise thee, O Virgin, receive these our voices, and entreat Thy Son that we may receive cleansing of our debts.

*Another canon by Lord Theodore, Tone 8. Heirmos: "Take hold of me, O Lover of mankind" (Содержи́ма мѧ̀ прїимѝ Человѣколю́бче):*[^650]  
**"Take hold of me, O Lover of mankind..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Now is the solemn feast of the martyrs: let us assemble, and blessing their all-honorable sufferings, let us hymn Christ Who crowned them.

Kindled with divine love for Christ, ye walked upon coals of fire as upon dew, chanting unto Him, O blessed martyrs.

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O Trinity transcendent in being without beginning, and divine Unity: I sing of light and lights, life and lives, Mind, Word, and Holy Spirit, holy, the one God.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
From thy root has blossomed forth the Flower of life, O forefather Jesse; leap for joy, for Christ God, saving the world, is born of the pure Maiden.

*Verse: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.*  
Ye feared not fire, nor sword, nor death, and preserved the confession of salvation, strengthened by Christ, O blessed ones.

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*  
Free among the dead, as Thou art the Author of life and death, do Thou give rest unto those whom Thou hast taken, and make them dwell in Thy courts, O Lover of mankind.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Take hold of me, O Lover of mankind, who am held fast by many sins, and who fall down before Thy compassions, as Thou didst the Prophet, O Lord, and save me.

*Kontakion: With the saints give rest...*  
*Ikos: Thou alone art immortal... (see fol. 17).[^651]*  
*Ode 7, Tone 4. Heirmos: "Three youths" (Ю҆́ношы трѝ):*[^652]  
**"Three youths in Babylon..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Unto whom, O my soul, loving passions, hast thou truly become like unto the irrational beasts? Whom hadst thou who surpassed thee in sins? Cry aloud unto Christ: Save me, O Good One!

O holy ones, fervently entreat Him Who rests in the saints, that in these holy days He may encompass the minds of all the pious.

O Most High, rich in mercy and Good, grant remission unto Thy servants who have fallen asleep beforehand, numbering them among the choirs of the righteous, forasmuch as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Theotokion:*  
Thou hast borne beyond the laws of nature the Creator Who is above man, O Pure One; Him do thou therefore entreat, to overlook now the lawlessness and transgressions of all.

*Another canon. Heirmos: "Thou Who by an angel savedst the youths" (А҆́гг҃ломъ ѻ҆́троки):*[^653]  
**"Thou Who by an angel savedst the youths..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Thou Who hast shown the memorials of the honorable martyrs to be the joy of Thy Church and spiritual consolation: blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers.

We lie not unto Thee, O Christ, nor do we deny Thee, the martyrs cried aloud in the midst of tortures, astounding the lawless judges: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
In three Persons we honor one nature, Father, Son, and Spirit, prophetically crying aloud: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
How dost thou give birth as a Mother, and yet hast remained a Virgin? Inasmuch as I have given birth to God, inquire not of me the manner: for whatsoever He wills, He does, the Maiden of God cries aloud.

*Verse: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.*  
O choir of martyrs chosen of God, make thy servants worthy to be worshipers of the divine and life-giving Cross of the Savior.

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord.*  
O Treasure of life, immortal King, account worthy of Thine eternal life Thy servants whom Thou hast chosen in faith and hope.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
Thou Who by an angel savedst the youths from the fire, and turned the roaring furnace into dew: blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Ode 8, Tone 4. Heirmos: "O Redeemer of all" (И҆зба́вителю всѣ́хъ):*[^654]  
**"O Redeemer of all, the Almighty..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Going with painless intent unto painful labors, O martyrs, ye gave over bodily sufferings; and having passed over unto painless delight, ye lighten every sickness of the heart.

Having exchanged corruptible things for things on high, save me by fasting, who have been corrupted by bodily passions, O divine martyrs, praying unto the God of all with fervent prayers.

Through the prayers of Thy martyrs, O Christ, send down Thy mercies upon all; and unto those who have departed from this life unto Thee, the Tender-hearted, grant release of debts and divine rest.

*Theotokion:*  
As thou hast borne the sinless God, blot out our sins by thy maternal prayers, O Theotokos, and save those who cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another canon. Heirmos: "The God-proclaiming youths" (Бг҃оглаго́ливі́и ѻ҆́троцы):*[^655]  
**"The God-proclaiming youths in the furnace..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Having endured fiery torments like dew, O sufferers, ye cried aloud with joy: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

The God-warring host of martyrs, having conquered deception, cried triumphantly: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
With the Son let us worship the Father, and unto the All-Holy Spirit let us all faithfully cry aloud: O Trinity Who art in Unity, save our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thou hast been shown both Virgin and Mother, having borne without a husband the God of all, O All-Pure One; but entreat Him, to save thy servants.

*Verse: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.*  
By the prayers of the passion-bearers, O Christ Savior, vouchsafe Thy servants both to venerate and to behold the life-giving Cross of Thy goodness.

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*  
Thou Who didst destroy the dominion of death by Thy rising from the dead, give rest unto those who have departed, together with Thine elect, that they may praise Thee, the Lord.

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord.*  
*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
The God-proclaiming youths in the furnace, trampling upon the fiery flame together with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9, Tone 4. Heirmos: "Eve indeed by the disease" (Е҆́ва ᲂу҆́бѡ недꙋ́гомъ):*[^656]  
**"Eve indeed by the disease of disobedience..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Having established your understanding firmly upon the noetic Rock, O passion-bearers, without perversion ye conquered all the wiles of the enemy; therefore, establish me toward God by your prayers, who am perverted toward soul-destroying passions.

O divine and truly radiant assembly of martyrs, entreat the All-Good Master, during this season of abstinence, to grant unto us all the remission of transgressions and everlasting rejoicing.

O Lover of mankind, Who alone art Good: vouchsafe Thy servants who have fallen asleep in hope of the resurrection to partake of Thine eternal, never-setting Light and the food of delight, that we may magnify Thee with fear.

*Theotokion:*  
Thy most holy womb was made the dwelling-place of Light, O All-Pure One; therefore faithfully I cry unto thee: Enlighten the pupils of my soul, and show the right path unto me who ever bless thee.

*Another canon. Heirmos: "We magnify thee, the Mother of God" (Велича́емъ тѧ̀ Мт҃рь Бж҃їю):*[^657]  
**"We magnify thee, the Mother of God..."**  
*Troparia:*  
We the faithful celebrate the memory of the martyrs, that we may become partakers of their glory, praising the suffering endurance of their struggles.

The wild beasts greatly feared your courage, the fire was quenched, the wheels were shattered; in you, O sufferers, God is marvelous!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O Nature transcendently unoriginate, Tri-hypostatic Unity: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, Light and Life divine in principle, preserve those who glorify Thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
We bless Thee, the God of Israel, Who didst appear from the Virgin unto the world, and hast raised up a horn of salvation for us.

*Verse: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.*  
O intercessors of the world, passion-bearers of Christ, vouchsafe all to behold His Cross and to bow down before it, by your prayers.

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord.*  
Where the fountain of life wells forth, O Savior and God, and the enlightenment of the visitation of Thy countenance shines, there place those who have departed unto Thee.

*Katavasia (Heirmos):*  
We magnify thee, the Mother of God, and glorify thee, O Virgin Theotokos, as having given birth to the Savior of our souls.

*Then, "It is truly meet..." Exapostilarion: "O Thou Who adornedst heaven with stars..." Glory... "Thou Who rulest over both the living..." Both now... Theotokion: "O sweetness of the angels..."*  
*Likewise the direction for the Praises and for the Aposticha is as on the Second Saturday of the Fast (see fol. 107).[^658]*  
*Then, "It is good to give praise..." and the First Hour. And the Dismissal.*  
*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Tone. Prokeimenon of the day, and for the departed. Epistle to the Hebrews, section 325 (10:32–38).[^659] Another, for the departed. Alleluia, Tone 6. Gospel according to Mark, section 8 (2:14–17).[^660] And for the departed. Communion hymn of the day, and for the departed.*

*(Engraving: In an ornate rectangular floral border, the precious Cross of Calvary is exalted upon a stone mount of Golgotha among pine trees and wild foliage, commanding the landscape.)*

## THIRD SUNDAY OF THE GREAT FAST: VENERATION OF THE PRECIOUS CROSS

*Wherein we celebrate the Veneration of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross.*  
*On Saturday evening, at Little Vespers, stichera on 4.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "The angelic hosts" (А҆́гг҃льскїѧ):*[^661]  
Now the angelic hosts bear as lances the precious Tree, reverently surrounding it and summoning all the faithful unto veneration; come, therefore, ye that have been enlightened by fasting, let us fall down before it with joy and fear, faithfully crying aloud: Rejoice, O precious Cross, foundation of the world! *(Twice)*

That Thou mightest destroy the curse of Adam, Thou didst accept our flesh without defilement, and art crucified and diest, O all-good Jesus; therefore Thy Cross and spear, the sponge and the reed, and the nails do we faithfully venerate, and we beseech to behold Thy Resurrection.

The serpent of old enclosed Eden through a tree, but the Tree of the Cross opens it unto all that desire to be cleansed through fasting and tears; come, therefore, beholding it set forth, let us the faithful fall down before it with fear, crying aloud: Open the heavenly gates, O Cross, unto them that love thee!

*Glory... Both now... Same Podoben:*  
When She who without seed bare Thee, O Word of God, beheld Thee fastened with nails unto the Cross, she cried aloud weeping: Woe is me, O my sweetest Child! What is this Thy humility, O my God? How dost Thou Who art impassible suffer passion by an unjust judgment? I hymn Thy fearful and utmost condescension!

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^662]  
Having been released from the bonds of the ancient condemnation by the Tree of the Cross, we the faithful glorify Christ Who was fastened with nails thereon.

*Verse: Exalt ye the Lord our God, and bow down before His footstool, for it is holy.*  
Come now with us, O David, tune thy psaltery, singing: Exalt Christ, ye faithful, bow down before His footstool!

*Verse: But God is our King before the ages; He has wrought salvation in the midst of the earth.*  
Let us, O peoples, exalt the set-forth Tree, whereby Christ grants salvation unto us, and let us kiss it with faith.

*Glory... Both now... The same. Stavrotheotokion:*  
Beholding Thy Son, O Virgin, lifted up upon the Cross for the sake of men, she cried aloud weeping: Glory to Thy tender compassion!

*Then, "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." Trisagion. After "Our Father...": Troparion, Tone 1:*  
Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance, granting victories to our right-believing Emperor over adversaries,[^663] and preserving Thy commonwealth by Thy Cross.  
*And the Dismissal takes place.*  
*And after the Dismissal, the priest enters into the vestry with the ecclesiarch, and they take the precious Tree, preceded by a candle-bearer and a censer, chanting the troparion:* **"Save, O Lord, Thy people..."** *and they bring it upon the holy table, and light a lamp for the entire night.*

*At Great Vespers:*  
*The priest gives the blessing, and we sing the introductory psalm as usual. Then the stichology, the first kathisma.*  
*On "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and sing 3 Resurrectional stichera of the Octoechos, and 3 Eastern [Anatolian] stichera, and 4 of the precious Cross, Tone 5.*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*[^664]  
Shine forth, O Cross of the Lord, the bright lightning-flashes of Thy grace into the hearts of them that honor thee, and receive thee with God-pleasing love, O desired of the whole world! Whereby the sorrow of tears has been destroyed, and we have been delivered from the snares of death, and have come unto perpetual gladness; show forth the beauty of Thy splendor, grant the reward of abstinence unto Thy servants, who faithfully entreat Thy rich protection and great mercy.

Rejoice, O life-bearing Cross, fair paradise of the Church, tree of incorruption, which blossomed forth for us the enjoyment of eternal glory; whereby the hosts of demons are driven away, and the ranks of angels rejoice together, and the assemblies of the faithful keep festival; invincible weapon, unbroken foundation, triumph of kings, praise of priests: grant us now to attain unto the Passions of Christ, and great mercy.

Rejoice, O life-bearing Cross, invincible victory of piety, gate of paradise, confirmation of the faithful, rampart of the Church; whereby corruption was destroyed and abolished, and the dominion of death was trampled down, and we were raised from earth unto heavenly things; invincible weapon, adversary of demons:

...adversary of demons, glory of martyrs, true adornment of the venerable, haven of salvation, granting great mercy unto the world.

Come, first-created pair, who fell from the heavenly choir through the envy of the manslayer, by the bitter pleasure of the tree through tasting of old: behold, the all-honorable Tree truly comes forth! Running unto it, embrace it with joy, and cry aloud unto it with faith: Thou art our restoration, O all-honorable Cross, tree blessed of God, heavenly plant! Partaking of whose fruit, we have attained the incorruption of the first Eden, having received it assuredly, and great mercy.

*Glory... Tone 3:*[^665]  
O Christ our God, Who didst accept a voluntary crucifixion for the common resurrection of the human race, and with the reed of the Cross, dipping Thy fingers in crimson dye, didst vouchsafe in Thy love for mankind to sign our imperial decrees of pardon: disdain us not in our perils, nor our distance again from Thee; but have compassion, O Thou Who alone art Long-suffering, upon Thy people in distress, and arise, fight against them that fight against us, forasmuch as Thou art almighty.

*Both now... Theotokion, the first of the Tone.*  
*Entrance: "O gladsome Light..."*  
*Prokeimenon: "The Lord is King..." Litany, and the rest as usual.*  
*At the Litiya, sticheron of the temple.*  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 5:*[^666]  
Beholding Thee, the Maker and Creator of all, hanging naked upon the Cross, all creation was transformed with fear and wailed; the sun darkened its light, the earth shook, the rocks were split, the splendor of the temple was rent in twain, the dead arose from their tombs, and the angelic powers were filled with awe, saying: O marvel! The Judge is judged, and suffers willingly for the salvation and renewal of the world!

*At the Aposticha, the stichera of the Octoechos, according to the alphabet.*  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*[^667]  
O Lord Who didst help the meek David to conquer the foreigner: fight alongside our faithful Emperor,[^668] and by the weapon of the Cross cast down our enemies; show forth upon us, O Tender-hearted One, Thine ancient mercies, that they may truly know that Thou art God, and that we who hope in Thee conquer, customary intercessors being offered by Thy most pure Mother, that great mercy may be granted unto us.

*Troparion: "Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos..." twice; and the troparion of the Cross, once, Tone 1:*  
**"Save, O Lord, Thy people..."**  
*And the rest of the order of the Vigil, as usual.*

*At Matins:*  
*After the Six Psalms, "God is the Lord," in the Tone of the Octoechos, and we say the Resurrectional troparion, twice. Glory... troparion of the Cross:* **"Save, O Lord, Thy people..."** *Both now... Theotokion:* **"When Gabriel announced..."** *And the usual stichology.*  
*We say the Resurrectional Sessional Hymns.*  
*After the Polyeleos [if anywhere there is a temple of the precious Cross], Sessional Hymn of the Cross, Tone 8. Podoben: "Having received the command mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*[^669]  
In paradise of old, through a tree the enemy stripped [us], by tasting bringing about putting to death; but the Tree of the Cross, bearing the garment of life unto men, was brought forth upon the earth, and the whole world was filled with all joy; seeing it venerated, let us the people cry aloud unto God in faith with one accord: The temple is full of His glory! *(Twice)*

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi] of the Tone. Matins Gospel: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50.*  
*Glory... Tone 8:* **"Open unto me the doors of repentance..."** *Both now...* **"Guide me in the paths of salvation..."** *Then Tone 6:* **"Have mercy on me, O God..."** **"The multitude of evil deeds done by me..."**  
*Canon of the Resurrection, and of the Theotokos, on 6; then of the Triodion on 8, composition of Lord Theodore the Studite.*  
*Tone 1, Ode 1. Heirmos: "It is the day of Resurrection, let us be radiant, O ye peoples" (Воскрⷭ҇нїя де́нь, просвѣти́мся лю́дїе):*[^670]  
**"It is the day of Resurrection, let us be radiant, O ye peoples..."**  
*Troparia:*  
It is the day of celebration: by the rising of Christ, death has been shown to have vanished, the dawn of life has shone forth, Adam having arisen dances for joy; therefore let us cry aloud, singing the song of victory!

It is the day of the veneration of the precious Cross: come unto it, all ye people! For radiating light, it now sets forth the rays of Christ's Resurrection; let us embrace it, spiritually rejoicing!

Appear, O great Cross of the Lord, show me now the divine form of thy beauty, making me a worthy worshiper of thy praise; for as unto one that has a soul, I cry aloud unto thee and embrace thee![^671]

Let heaven and earth praise in unison, for the all-blessed Cross is set forth before all, whereon Christ was sacrificed, being nailed in the flesh; let us embrace it, spiritually rejoicing!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
O Trinity in hypostatic forms! O Unity in essence! Father, Son, and Spirit, Unity of equal power, one in counsel and will, and in sovereignty of rule: preserve Thy world, granting peace!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Having known no experience of man, O Virgin, without seed thou givest birth to a Child, purely carrying Thy Offspring, Christ God, the Creator of the world: Him do thou entreat, to make peace with all things.

*Katavasia: "Moses of old most divinely prefigured" (Бж҃е́ственнѣйшїй проѡбразѝ дре́вле Мѡѷсе́й):*[^672]  
Moses of old most divinely prefigured [it], leading Israel through the Red Sea by Thy Cross, having cleft the water with his rod, singing an exodus song unto Thee, O Christ God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos: "Come, let us drink a new drink" (Прїиди́те, пи́во пїе́мъ):*[^673]  
**"Come, let us drink a new drink..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Come, let us sing a new song, celebrating the destruction of Hades; for Christ is risen from the tomb, having taken death captive and saved all things.

Come, let us draw forth, O faithful, not from a spring pouring out corruptible water, but from the fountain of enlightenment through the veneration of the Cross of Christ, in which also we boast.

Kissing now Thy Cross, which of old Moses prefigured with his palms,[^674] we conquer the intelligible Amalek, O Master Christ, by which also we are saved.

Singing a song of rejoicing with pure eyes and lips, let us, the faithful, bow down in worship with joy before the Cross of the Lord, clapping our hands with songs.

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
I revere the unoriginate God, One in three Hypostases: the Father, the Son, and the Living Spirit, undivided in form of essence, into Whom we were baptized.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Of old, O Pure One, Moses beheld in the bush the mystery of thy figure; for as the flame thereof, the fire of the Godhead did not burn thy womb.

*Katavasia: "Establish me, O Master Christ" (Оу҆твердѝ Влдко Хрⷭ҇тѐ):*[^675]  
Establish me, O Master Christ, upon the rock of the faith by the Cross, that my mind be not shaken by the assaults of the evil enemy; for Thou alone art holy.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 6:*[^676]  
Thy Cross, O Lord, is sanctified, for in it healings are granted unto those ailing in sins; falling down before Thee through it, have mercy on us.

*Verse: Exalt ye the Lord our God, and bow down in worship before the footstool of His feet, for He is holy.*[^677]

Today the prophetic word has been fulfilled; for lo, we bow down in worship at the place where Thy feet stood, O Lord; and having tasted of the tree of salvation, we have obtained freedom from sinful passions, through the intercessions of the Theotokos, O Thou Who alone lovest mankind.

*Glory...*  
Scarcely had the tree of Thy Cross been set up, O Christ, when the foundations of death were shaken, O Lord; for Hades released with trembling him whom he had swallowed with longing; Thou hast shown forth unto us Thy salvation, O Holy One, and we glorify Thee, O Son of God: have mercy on us!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
O Virgin Theotokos, entreat Thy Son, Christ our God, Who of His own will was nailed upon the Cross and is risen from the dead, that our souls may be saved.

*Ode 4. Heirmos: "Upon the divine watch" (На бж҃е́ственнѣй стра́жи):*[^678]  
**"Upon the divine watch..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Lo, Christ is risen, the Angel said unto the myrrh-bearing women: Weep not; go, tell the Apostles: Rejoice, today salvation is come unto the world, the tyranny of the enemy has been destroyed through death!

Meeting today with joy of adoration Thy life-bearing Cross, O Christ, we make the forefeast of Thy most pure Passion, which Thou didst accomplish for the salvation of the world, O Savior, as the Almighty.

Today joy comes to pass in heaven and upon earth, for the thrice-blessed Cross is revealed to the world as the sign of Christ; for being set forth, it pours out ever-flowing joy unto them that bow down in worship before it.

What shall we offer Thee, O Christ, in that Thou hast granted us to bow down in worship before the precious Cross, whereon Thy most holy blood was poured out, and where Thy flesh was affixed with nails? Embracing it now, we give thanks unto Thee.

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
I sing of the three Hypostases of the one Godhead, simply proclaiming in essence, undivided: the unoriginate Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit enthroned together; one Lordship, and one Kingdom, one ever-existing dominion.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Alone among women hast thou appeared as a most marvelous wonder, O Pure One, an awesome thing to hear; for thou hast renewed nature, having given birth without seed, yet remaining a Virgin as before; for He that was born of thee is the true God.

*Katavasia: "Seeing Thee upon the Cross, O Mighty One" (На крⷭ҇тѣ́ тѧ Си́льне):*[^679]  
Seeing Thee upon the Cross, O Mighty One, the great luminary was seized with trembling, and drawing together its rays it hid them; and all creation sang of Thy longsuffering with fear; for the earth was filled with Thy praise.

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "Let us rise early in the deep dawn" (Оу҆́тренюемъ ᲂу҆́трꙋ глꙋбокꙋ̀):*[^680]  
**"Let us rise early in the deep dawn..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Thou hast shone forth from the tomb, O unwaning Light, flashing forth incorruption upon the world; Thou hast abolished the weeping of death from the ends of the earth, O Lord, as the Tender-hearted One.

Having been cleansed by self-control, let us draw near, warmly embracing in praise the all-holy Tree whereon Christ, being crucified, saved the world, as the Tender-hearted One.

The angelic ranks dance in gladness today at the veneration of Thy Cross; for by it Thou hast cast down the demonic hosts, O Christ Who hast saved mankind.

The Church has been revealed as another Paradise, possessing as before the life-bearing tree, Thy Cross, O Lord, by touching which we have partaken of immortality.

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
I glorify the three co-unoriginate Ones of one essence: God the Father, the Son, and the Spirit, one three-radiant Light, together possessing sovereign Rule in unconfused sameness.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thou hast given birth according to the law of nature, but above nature; for unto thee alone was childbearing without seed, an awesome manner both to contemplate and to speak of concerning thy birthgiving, O All-Immaculate One.

*Katavasia: "Rising early we sing unto Thee, O compassionate Savior" (Оу҆́тренююще тѧ̀ воспѣва́емъ):*[^681]  
Rising early we sing unto Thee, O compassionate Savior, having found peace through Thy Cross, by which Thou hast renewed the human race, leading us unto the unwaning light.

*Ode 6.*

*Ode 6. Heirmos: "Thou didst descend into the deepest parts of the earth" (Снизше́лъ є҆сѝ):*[^682]  
**"Thou didst descend into the deepest parts of the earth..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Having risen and abolished death, O Christ, as the Great King, Thou hast summoned us from the vaults of Hades into the enjoyment of the Heavenly Kingdom, into the land of immortality.

Clapping our hands with divine songs, O faithful, let us shout aloud unto God, kissing the Cross of the Lord; for it pours forth a fountain of sanctification unto all that are in the world.

The prophetic voice of the psalmist is fulfilled; for lo, we bow down in worship before the footstool of Thy most pure feet, O Almighty One, before Thy precious Cross, the thrice-desired Tree.

The mourner among the prophets saw the tree cast into Thy bread;[^683] embracing Thy Cross, O Compassionate One, we praise Thy bonds, and burial, the spear and the nails.

Embracing the holy Cross which Thou didst choose to bear upon Thy shoulders, O Christ, and upon this to be lifted up and crucified in the flesh, we gather strength against the invisible enemies.

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
I sing of the Unity in three characters, and the Trinity worshiped in one nature: God, threefold together, the three-sunned Light: Father, and Son, and Holy Spirit.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
A great marvel of marvels has appeared in thee, O undefiled Ewe-lamb: thou hast given birth unto the Lamb Who takes away the sin of the world; Him do thou entreat fervently on behalf of them that sing of thee.

*Katavasia: "Jonah prefigured the image of the divine Cross" (Ѻ҆́бразъ бж҃е́ственнагѡ крⷭ҇та̀):*[^684]  
Jonah prefigured the image of the divine Cross in the belly of the whale with outstretched palms, and emerged saved from the beast by Thy power, O Word.

*Kontakion, Tone 7. Idiomelon:*[^685]  
No longer does the flaming sword guard the gate of Eden, for upon them has come the marvelous bond, the tree of the Cross; the sting of death and the victory of Hades have been driven away; for Thou hast stood forth, my Savior, crying unto them that were in Hades: Enter ye again into Paradise!

*Ikos:*[^686]  
Pilate set up three crosses upon Golgotha, two for the robbers, and one for the Giver of Life; whom Hades saw, and said unto them below: O my servants and my powers! Who has driven nails into my heart? A wooden spear has suddenly pierced me through; and I am torn asunder, I ache in mine inward parts, I am wounded in my belly, my senses confuse my spirit, and I am constrained to cast out Adam and them that came from Adam, who were given unto me by the tree; for lo, this Tree leads them again into Paradise!

*Synaxarion.*[^687]  
*Verses:*  
Let all the earth bow down in worship before the Cross:  
Through which it learned to bow down in worship before Thee, O Word.

On this same day, the Third Sunday of the Fasts, we celebrate the veneration of the precious and life-creating Cross, for this reason: Since during the forty-day fasts we also are in a certain manner crucified, being put to death from the passions, and we experience a sense of bitterness, falling into despondency and growing faint: the precious and life-creating Cross is set forth, both refreshing and strengthening us, and reminding us of the Passions of our Lord Jesus Christ, and comforting us. If our God was crucified for our sake, how much how much does it behoove us to labor for His sake, lightening our afflictions by the setting forth of the Master's sorrows, and by the remembrance and expectation of the Cross of glory? For as our Savior, having ascended the Cross, was glorified through an ignominious life and through embitterment, so it behooves us also to do, that we may be glorified together with Him, even if at any time we suffer something sorrowful. And furthermore, just as those traversing a long and rough path, weighed down by labor, if they find somewhere a leafy tree offering good shade, rest for a little while by sitting down under it, and being as it were rejuvenated, finish the remainder of the way: so also now in this time of fasting, in this sorrowful way and ascetic struggle, the life-bearing Cross was planted in the midst by the holy Fathers, granting us respite and refreshment, and rendering those who have labored courageous and light-hearted for the remaining labor. Or as at the arrival of kings, their standards and scepters go before them, and then the king himself arrives, rejoicing and making merry over the victory, and his subjects rejoice together with him: so also our Lord Jesus Christ, desiring to show forth all the more His victory over death, and to go forth with glory on the day of the Resurrection, sent forth beforehand His scepter, the royal standard, the life-creating Cross, comforting and refreshing us as ones filling up the excessive measure, and preparing us to be ready to receive the King Himself as far as possible, and to praise Him radiant in victory. Moreover, in the midst of the week of the Holy Forty Days: because the Holy Forty Days is like unto a bitter spring, on account of compunction and the bitterness and grief that befall us from the fast; as therefore the divine Moses cast the tree into the midst of that spring and sweetened it,[^688] so also God, Who brought us across the spiritual Red Sea and delivered us from Pharaoh, through the life-creating tree of the precious and life-creating Cross sweetens the bitterness of the fasts of the Forty Days, and comforts us as ones dwelling in the wilderness, until He brings us up unto the spiritual Jerusalem through His Resurrection. Or, since the Cross is called and is the tree of life, and that tree was planted in the midst of the Paradise of Eden: fittingly also did the divine Fathers plant the tree of the Cross in the midst of the Holy Forty Days, both commemorating the greed of Adam and depicting his redemption through the present Tree; for tasting of this, we no longer die, but rather are made alive. By its power, O Christ God, preserve us also from the temptations of the evil one; and account us worthy to bow down in worship before Thy divine Passions and life-bearing Resurrection, having brightly passed through the forty-day course; and have mercy on us, as Thou alone art good and the Lover of mankind. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "He Who delivered the Children from the furnace" (Ѻ҆́троки ѿ пе́щи):*[^689]  
**"He Who delivered the Children from the furnace..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Thou hast arisen from the tomb on the third day as from sleep, O Lord, having struck down the gatekeepers of Hades by divine power, and raised up the ancient forefathers, O Thou only-blessed and exceedingly glorified God of our fathers!

Dancing to the lute of songs, let us, the people, rejoice today, glorifying through the veneration of the Cross Christ Who was nailed upon it, the only-blessed and exceedingly glorified God of our fathers!

O Thou Who hast shown forth the instrument of putting to death to be the workshop of life, worthy of the world's embrace, Thy Cross, O All-Merciful One: sanctify them that bow down in worship before it, O Thou only-blessed and exceedingly glorified God of our fathers!

O Thou Who alone art merciful and tender-hearted, enlighten and sanctify, O Jesus, them that faithfully bow down in worship before Thy Cross and divine Passions, O Thou only-blessed and exceedingly glorified God of our fathers!

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
I sing of the Godhead, a Unity in three Hypostases: for the Father is Light, the Son is Light, the Spirit is Light; the Light remaining undivided, shining by unity of nature and by the three rays of the Persons.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thou art the multifold proclamation of all the prophets; for the Gate of God, the Golden Urn, the Holy Land hast thou appeared, O Virgin Bride of God, who hast in the flesh given birth unto Jesus Christ, the God of our fathers, Who is exceedingly glorified!

*Katavasia: "He Who delivered the Children from the flame" (И҆з̾ пла́мене ѻ҆́троки и҆зба́влей):*[^690]  
He Who delivered the Children from the flame, having taken flesh, came upon the earth, and being nailed upon the Cross, granted us salvation, O Christ, the only-blessed and exceedingly glorified God of our fathers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "This chosen and holy day" (Се́й нарече́нный):*[^691]  
**"This chosen and holy day..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Why hold ye myrrh in your hands at all? Whom do ye seek? the youth who now appeared in the tomb cried aloud: Christ our God is risen, having raised the nature of mankind from the vaults of Hades!

Rejoice, O thrice-rich and divine Tree, O Cross, light unto them that are in darkness, having revealed the rays of Christ's rising unto the four-cornered world by thy radiance; account all the faithful worthy to attain unto Pascha!

On this day the divine myrrh-bearing vessels pour out the fragrance of myrrh, the life-bearing Tree, the Cross of Christ; let us inhale its God-inspired scents, faithfully bowing down in worship before it unto the ages.

Come, O prophet Elisha, tell us plainly: What was that tree which thou didst cast into the water?[^692] It was the Cross of Christ, by which we were drawn forth from the depth of corruption, faithfully bowing down in worship before it unto the ages.

Of old Jacob, prefiguring Thy Cross, O Christ, bowed down in worship before the top of Joseph's staff,[^693] having foreseen this awesome scepter of Thy Kingdom, before which we now faithfully bow down in worship unto the ages.

We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.  
*Trinity Hymn:*  
I glorify the one Essence in three characters; I do not confound the Three in a single Person, nor do I divide the Form: the Father, and the Son, with the Spirit; for God is one over all in the Trinity.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Alone among mothers hast thou appeared as a Virgin, O Mary Bride of God, having without a man given birth unto Christ the Savior, preserving the sign of purity: we, the faithful, call thee blessed unto the ages.

*Katavasia: "Daniel, great among the prophets, of old stretched out his hands" (Рꙋ́цѣ въ ро́въ ве́рженый львѡ́въ):*[^694]  
Daniel, great among the prophets, having of old stretched out his hands cruciform when cast into the den of lions, was saved unhurt from being eaten by them, blessing Christ God unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Shine, shine, O New Jerusalem" (Свѣти́сѧ, свѣти́сѧ):*[^695]  
**"Shine, shine, O New Jerusalem..."**  
*Troparia:*  
Thou hast descended into the tomb, O Giver of Life and God, and Thou hast broken all the bars and bolts, and raised up the dead, who cry: Glory to Thy rising, O Christ, Almighty Savior!

Thy tomb poured forth life for me, O Christ; for standing forth, the Container of life cried aloud unto them that dwelt in the tombs: Ye that are in bonds, be loosed; for I, the Redemption of the world, am come!

Let all the trees of the forest skip with songs, beholding the namesake Tree of the Cross embraced today, whereon Christ lifted up His head, as the divine David prophesies.

Having died by a tree, I found thee, O my Christ-bearing Cross, as the Tree of life, my unbreakable guardian, mighty dominion against demons; bowing down in worship before thee today, I cry: Sanctify me by thy glory!

Be glad, rejoice, O Church of God, today bowing down in worship before the thrice-rich Tree of the all-holy Cross of Christ, which the angelic ranks serve, and before which they stand with fear.

*Glory... Trinity Hymn:*  
I bow down in worship before the Trinity in Persons, the Unity in nature: unto Thee, O Holy Godhead, Father, and Son, with the Holy Spirit: one Beginning, one Kingdom, holding dominion over all.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
A great Mountain wherein Christ took up His abode hast thou appeared, O Virgin, as the divine David cries aloud; through which we were lifted up unto heaven, having been made sons by the Spirit, O All-Blessed One!

*Katavasia:*

*Katavasia: "O Mother Virgin and true Theotokos" (Ѽ Мт҃и Дв҃о):*[^696]  
O Mother Virgin and true Theotokos, who without seed hast borne Christ our God, Who was lifted up in the flesh upon the Cross: thee, all the faithful, worthily magnify now together with Him.

*Exapostilarion of the Resurrection. And of the Cross. Tone 2, Podoben: "With the disciples" (Со ᲂу҆чени́ки):*[^697]  
Beholding today the precious Cross of Christ set forth, let us bow down in worship, and faithfully rejoice, embracing it with love, asking the Lord Who of His own will was crucified thereon to account all of us worthy also to bow down in worship before the precious Cross, and to attain uncondemned unto the Resurrection.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thy Son, O All-Pure One, stretched forth His most pure hands on our behalf upon the Tree, and was nailed thereto: grant peace unto us who now piously bow down in worship before it, and grant us to anticipate the world-saving, most pure Passions, and to bow down in worship before the radiant, world-rejoicing, Lord-named Pascha, and the light-bearing day.

*At the Praises, we set 8 verses, and sing 4 Resurrectional stichera of the Octoechos, and 3 Prosomoia of the Triodion, repeating the first, Tone 4. Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^698]  
Let us shout aloud with voices, let us magnify in songs, kissing the precious Cross, and let us cry out unto it: O all-honorable Cross, sanctify our souls and bodies by thy power, and preserve unhurt from all harm of adversaries them that piously bow down in worship before thee.

Draw near and draw from inexhaustible waters, poured forth by the grace of the Cross; seeing the holy Tree set forth, the fountain of gifts, watered by the blood and water of the Master of all, Who was voluntarily lifted up thereon, and raised up earthly men.

*Verse: Exalt ye the Lord our God, and bow down in worship before the footstool of His feet, for He is holy.*

The establishment of the Church, the dominion of kings, the boast and salvation of monastics, art thou, O all-honorable Cross; therefore bowing down in worship before thee, both in heart and soul we are enlightened today by the divine grace of Him Who was nailed upon thee, and destroyed the dominion of the deceiver, and abolished the curse.

*Verse: God is our King before the ages, He hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth.*

*And repeating the first sticheron, then we say the verse:*  
*Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor unto the end.*

*And we sing the Idiomelon, Tone 8:*[^699]  
The Lord of all taught by a parable to flee the haughty mind of the wicked Pharisees; and He instructed all not to think more highly of oneself than one ought to think, having Himself become a pattern and an image, even unto crucifixion and death, having emptied Himself; giving thanks therefore with the publican, let us say: O God Who didst suffer for us and didst remain impassible, deliver us from passions, and save our souls!

*Glory... the same again. Both now...* **"Thou art most blessed..."** *Great Doxology.*

*When we begin to sing: "Glory to God in the highest..." then the Priest comes, having vested in all priestly vestments (or if a superior is present, this should be done by the first in rank in their respective places); and having taken the censer, he censes the holy table and the precious Cross; then he takes it upon the diskos, with the veil/case upon his head...*[^700]

*...upon his head, and goes out from the left side of the holy table through the north doors, preceded by two candles and the censer, and proceeds to the Royal Doors.*

*When the Great Doxology is finished and the Trisagion has been sung, the Priest says:*  
**Priest:** Wisdom! Stand upright!  
*And we begin the troparion:*  
**Choir:** **"Save, O Lord, Thy people..."** *(Thrice)*[^701]

*And the Priest comes bearing the precious Cross before the Holy Doors; and there upon the prepared Tetrapod the precious Cross is placed, and having opened the case, he censes it cruciform from the four sides.*

*And immediately we sing the troparion, Tone 6:*  
**Choir:** Before Thy Cross we bow down in worship, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify. *(Thrice)*[^702]

*And they begin to bow down in worship: if anywhere in a monastery, the Abbot, or another presiding among the clergy; likewise also the other priests, bowing down before the precious Cross twice; the seniors kiss it first, and after kissing they bow down again once; likewise do the other priests on the right side and on the left; likewise the brethren also come two by two, bowing down according to their rank. And while the brethren bow down in worship, we sing these Idiomela, Tone 2:*[^703]  
Come, ye faithful, let us bow down in worship before the life-creating Tree, whereon Christ, the King of glory, having voluntarily stretched out His hands, lifted us up unto the first blessedness, whom the enemy of old had stolen away through pleasure and caused to be banished from God; come, ye faithful, let us bow down in worship before the Tree, through which we were accounted worthy to shatter the heads of invisible enemies; come, all ye families of the nations, let us honor the Cross of the Lord with songs: Rejoice, O Cross, complete redemption of fallen Adam! In thee our most faithful rulers boast, as by thy power they mightily subdue the Ismaelite people; thee now with fear we Christians kiss, glorifying God Who was nailed upon thee, saying: O Lord Who wast nailed thereon, have mercy on us, as Thou art good and the Lover of mankind!

*Tone 8:*[^704]  
Today the Master of creation and Lord of glory is nailed upon the Cross, and is pierced in His side, tasting gall and vinegar, the Sweetness of the Church; He is crowned with a crown of thorns, Who covers the heaven with clouds; He is clothed with a garment of mockery, and is buffeted by an earthly hand: He Who with His hand created man; He is beaten upon His shoulders, Who cloths the heaven with clouds; He receives spitting and wounds, reproaches and buffetings; and all things He endures for the sake of me, the condemned, my Redeemer and God, that He may save the world from deception, as the Tender-hearted One.

*Glory... Tone 8:*[^705]  
Today He Who is untouchable in essence becomes touchable unto me and suffers Passions, Who frees me from passions; He Who gives light unto the blind is spat upon by lawless lips, and gives His back unto wounds for the sake of captives; beholding Him upon the Cross, the Pure Virgin and Mother cried out sorrowfully: Woe is me, my Child! What is this that Thou hast done? Thou Who art fair in beauty beyond all men appearest breathless, formless, having neither form nor beauty; woe is me, my Light! I cannot look upon Thee sleeping; I am wounded in my womb, and a cruel sword piercs my heart; I hymn Thy Passions, I bow down before Thy tender-heartedness: O Longsuffering One, glory to Thee!

*Both now... the same Tone:*  
Today the prophetic word has been fulfilled; for lo, we bow down in worship at the place where Thy feet stood, O Lord; and having tasted of the tree of salvation, we have obtained freedom from sinful passions, through the intercessions of the Theotokos, O Thou Who alone lovest mankind.

*Then the litany, and the dismissal.*  
*Glory... Both now... the Gospel Sticheron in the narthex; and the Catechesis of the Studite is read; and the First Hour, and the complete dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy of the Typika: the Beatitudes of the Tone on 6; and of Ode 6 of the Canon of the Cross on 4.*[^706]  
*Instead of the Trisagion, we sing:*  
**"Before Thy Cross we bow down in worship, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify."**  
*Prokimenon of the Epistle, Tone 6:*  
**"Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance."**  
*Verse: Unto Thee, O Lord, will I cry, O my God, be not silent unto me.*  
*Epistle to the Hebrews, pericope 311 [Heb. 4:14-5:6].*[^707] *Alleluia, Tone 1:*  
*Remember Thy congregation, which Thou hast purchased from the beginning.*  
*Verse: God is our King before the ages, He hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth.*  
*Gospel according to Mark, pericope 37 [Mark 8:34-9:1].*[^708] *Communion Hymn:*  
**"The light of Thy countenance is signed upon us, O Lord: Thou hast given gladness into my heart. Alleluia."**

[Headpiece Ornament: Icon of Christ the Savior Blessing.]

## On the Third Sunday of the Great Fast, at Vespers
*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and sing the penitential stichera of the Octoechos, of the Tone; and 3 Prosomoia of the Triodion.*  
*Prosomoia of Lord Joseph, Tone 8. Podoben: "O Lord, though Thou didst stand before the tribunal" (Гдⷭ҇и, а҆́ще и҆ на сꙋди́щи):*[^709]  
O Lord, Who of Thine own will didst stretch out Thy palms upon the Cross, account us also worthy to bow down in worship before it with compunction of heart, having been well enlightened by fasting and prayers, by self-control and good works, as Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Podoben:*  
O Lord, according to the multitude of Thy compassions, cleanse the multitude of my sins, O All-Merciful One, and account me worthy with pure soul to see and to kiss Thy Cross during the present week of self-control, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Another sticheron of Lord Theodore, Tone 3. Podoben: "A great wonder" (Ве́лїе чꙋ́до):*[^710]  
A great wonder, the Tree is beheld whereon Christ was of His own will crucified in the flesh; the world bows down in worship, and being enlightened cries aloud: O power of the Cross! Even when seen, it consumes demons, and being depicted, it appears consuming them! I call thee blessed, O most pure Tree, I honor and bow down in worship before thee with fear, and I glorify God, Who through thee has granted me endless life.

*And in the Menaion 3. Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*  
*Entrance. "O Gladsome Light".*  
*Great Prokimenon, Tone 8:*[^711]  
**"Thou hast given an inheritance unto them that fear Thee, O Lord."**  
*Verse 1: From the ends of the earth have I cried unto Thee.*  
*Verse 2: I will be sheltered under the shadow of Thy wings.*  
*Verse 3: So will I sing unto Thy name.*  

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day, twice, Tone 8:*[^712]  
I dare not gaze with mine eyes into heaven, wretch that I am, because of my wicked deeds; but like the publican groaning, I cry unto Thee: O God, cleanse me a sinner, and deliver me from the hypocrisy of the Pharisees, as Thou alone art tender-hearted!

*Verse 1: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven.*  
*Verse 2: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us.*  

*For the Martyrs:*  
O martyrs of the Lord, every place do ye sanctify, and every disease do ye heal; and now pray ye, we entreat, that our souls may be delivered from the snares of the enemy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, the same Tone:*  
The heavenly hosts sing of thee, O joyful Mother unwedded, and we glorify thine unsearchable childbearing, O Mother of God; pray that our souls may be saved!

*[Headpiece Ornament: Icon of the Holy Cross.]*

---

### Notes

[^230]: Physical Folio 76 (Page_0080.jpg) marks the primary codicological division of the codex: the formal beginning of the Holy and Great Forty Days (*Нача́ло ст҃а́гѡ и҆ вели́кагѡ поста̀*). Physical Leaf p80 = Printed Page 76.
[^231]: The awakening bell on Clean Monday: the paraecclesiarch sounds the klepalo before dawn (*зна́менаетъ... къ свѣ́тꙋ*), allowing extra rest on account of the evening collation (*ко́нѣ ра́ди вече́рнягѡ ᲂу҆тѣше́нїа*). Physical Leaf p80 = Printed Page 76.
[^232]: The monastic discipline of the Six Psalms (*Шестоѱа́лмїе*): Psalms 3, 37, 62, 87, 102, and 142 LXX, during which absolute silence, immobility, and contrition are mandated: no whispering, spitting, or throat-clearing, with arms folded upon the breast and heads bowed. Physical Leaf p80 = Printed Page 76.
[^233]: Isaiah 26:9, 11, 15 LXX. The four solemn verses chanted with Alleluia in the Tone of the Octoechos (*Ѿ но́щи ᲂу҆́тренюетъ дꙋ́хъ мо́й...*) at Lenten Matins, replacing "God is the Lord." Physical Leaf p80 = Printed Page 76.
[^234]: The Triadica (*Трⷪ҇чны*) chanted with slow, compunctious melody (*велегла́снѡ и҆ ко́снѡ съ пѣ́нїемъ*), followed by the first stichology of the Psalter with three prostrations after each antiphon kathisma. Physical Leaf p80 = Printed Page 76.
[^235]: Reading from Saint Ephrem the Syrian, which traditionally begins on Clean Monday and continues through Great Lent until Palm Sunday.
[^236]: Sessional Hymn of the Triodion by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 2, Model: "Being the fountain of tender mercy".
[^237]: Reading from the Lausiac History (*Lavsaik*) of Palladius of Helenopolis, a monastic collection read during Lenten Matins.
[^238]: Rubrics for singing the Biblical Odes (*Господеви поемъ* / *Let us sing unto the Lord*, Exod. 15:1) with the Canon of the Menaion and the Triodia of Joseph and Theodore the Studite.
[^239]: Rubrical prescription concerning the omission of the Octoechos on weekdays throughout the Holy Forty Days (except on Sundays), singing only the Menaion and the Triodia of Joseph and Theodore the Studite.
[^240]: Canon and Triodion by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer, Ode 1, Tone 2, Heirmos: "Come, ye peoples".
[^241]: Triodion, Ode 8, Heirmos: "Into the fiery furnace".
[^242]: Another Triodion, Ode 8, by Theodore the Studite, Heirmos: "Of old Who dewed the Hebrew youths in the flame".
[^243]: The Magnificat rubric (*И стихословится Честнѣйшая*) directing the chanting of the Canticle of the Theotokos ("My soul magnifies the Lord") with the refrain "More honorable than the Cherubim".
[^244]: Triodion, Ode 9, Heirmos: "God the Word from God".
[^245]: Another Triodion, Ode 9, by Theodore the Studite, Heirmos: "Her who supernaturally conceived in her womb".
[^246]: Lenten rubrics for Lauds (*Хвалитны* / *The Praises*): on ordinary Lenten weekdays, stichera are omitted unless the Menaion provides stichera for a second saint, and the three Laudate psalms (Pss. 148, 149, 150) are recited smoothly without chanting.
[^247]: Lenten Matins Aposticha self-governing sticheron (*самогласенъ*), Tone 5: "The Fast has arrived, mother of chastity".
[^248]: Order of the 16 prostrations (metanias) with the Prayer of St. Ephrem the Syrian (*Господи и Владыко живота моего*): three great prostrations, twelve small metanias with "O God, cleanse me a sinner," and one final great prostration reciting the entire prayer.
[^249]: Order of the First Hour in Great Lent: no kathisma on Mondays (or on Fridays at the First and Ninth Hours), with the Lenten troparion of the First Hour sung in Tone 6 ("In the morning give ear unto my voice") with three prostrations to the ground.
[^250]: Rubric on the reading of the Catecheses of St. Theodore the Studite in the narthex on Wednesdays and Fridays, and the rule of monastic silence after dismissal.
[^251]: The Third Hour order in Great Lent: striking the wooden klepalo (*било*), ordinary kathisma, Lenten troparion of the Third Hour ("O Lord, Who didst send down Thy Most Holy Spirit at the third hour upon Thine Apostles"), and reading from the *Ladder of Divine Ascent* of St. John Climacus (*Лествичникъ*).
[^252]: The monitor monk appointed throughout Great Lent to quietly circulate through the church, observe anyone nodding off, and gently rouse them to hear the spiritual readings.
[^253]: The Sixth Hour order in Great Lent: striking the klepalo six times, reciting the kathisma, and chanting the Lenten troparion of the Sixth Hour in Tone 2 ("Who on the sixth day and hour didst nail to the Cross").
[^254]: Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 5: "O Lord, O Lord, at Whom all things do tremble".
[^255]: Prophecy Pericope from Isaiah (Isa. 1:1–20), appointed for the Sixth Hour of Clean Monday, denouncing hollow ritualism and demanding repentance, justice, and purity.
[^256]: Prokeimenon following the Prophecy, Tone 7, Psalm 2: "Serve the Lord with fear, and rejoice unto Him with trembling" (Ps. 2:11).
[^257]: Typikon rubric on the two customs regarding the Ninth Hour: whether the brethren disperse to cells after the Sixth Hour (striking the klepalo nine times to reassemble), or whether the Ninth Hour follows immediately upon the prayer of St. Basil the Great ("O God and Lord of hosts").
[^258]: The Ninth Hour Lenten troparion, Tone 8: "Who at the ninth hour for our sake didst taste death in the flesh," sung with three prostrations to the ground.
[^259]: The Typika (Beatitudes) chanted solemnly and slowly in Tone 8 with the refrain: "Remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom," followed by three great prostrations at "Remember us, O Lord... Remember us, O Master... Remember us, O Holy One...".
[^260]: The Creed (*Вѣрую*) recited during the Typika, followed by the prayer of remission (*Ослаби, остави*), the Lord's Prayer, and the appointed kontakia.
[^261]: The 16 Lenten prostrations concluding the Typika (*изобразительныя поклоны*), following which the paraecclesiarch receives the superior's blessing to ring the bell for Lenten Vespers.
[^262]: The opening of Lenten Vespers: Psalm 103 (*Bless the Lord, O my soul*) read quietly by the reader while the priest recites the Prayers of Lamplighting before the holy doors, followed by the 18th Kathisma (Pss. 119–133, *Ко Господу внегда скорбѣти ми*).
[^263]: Vespers on Monday evening of Clean Week (*Въ понедѣльникъ первыя седмицы святаго Великаго Поста вечеръ*), where at "Lord, I have cried" 6 stichera are appointed: 3 from the Triodion (by Joseph and Theodore the Studite) and 3 from the Menaion.
[^264]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried" by Lord Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 2, Model: "All things have passed away".
[^265]: Another sticheron by Lord Theodore the Studite, Tone 5, Model: "O venerable Father".
[^266]: Prokeimenon at Lenten Vespers, Tone 6, Psalm 3: "Salvation is of the Lord, and Thy blessing is upon Thy people" (Ps. 3:9), Verse: "Lord, why are they multiplied that afflict me?" (Ps. 3:2).
[^267]: The first reading from Genesis (Gen. 1:1–13), appointed for the Vespers of Clean Monday, recounting the creation of the heavens and earth, light, the firmament, the dry land, and plant life during the first three days of creation.
[^268]: Second Prokeimenon at Vespers of Clean Monday, Tone 5, Psalm 4: "The Lord will hear me when I cry unto Him" (Ps. 4:4), Verse: "When I called, the God of my righteousness heard me" (Ps. 4:2).
[^269]: The reading from Proverbs (Prov. 1:1–20), appointed for the Vespers of Clean Monday, establishing the fear of the Lord as the foundation of wisdom and warning the young against the destructive counsels and robbery of sinners.
[^270]: Lenten Vespers Aposticha self-governing sticheron (*самогласенъ* / *idiomelon*), Tone 3: "Let us fast an acceptable fast, well-pleasing unto the Lord".
[^271]: Lenten Vespers dismissal rite: chanting the penitential troparia with three prostrations ("O Virgin Mother of God, rejoice", "O Baptist of Christ", "Pray for us"), 40 repetitions of "Lord, have mercy", and the Prayer of St. Ephrem the Syrian with 16 prostrations.
[^272]: Post-Vespers rubrics: recitation of Psalm 144 (*I will extol Thee, my God*) before the refectory meal, followed by chanting the patronal sticheron and the memorial litya for departed brethren in the narthex.
[^273]: Typikon rubric concerning the omission of the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts on Clean Monday and Clean Tuesday, and the strict monastic fast according to the Athonite Typikon requiring total abstinence from food on the first day of Great Lent.
[^274]: Dietary rule for Tuesday, Wednesday, and Thursday of Clean Week: one litra (approx. 12 ounces) of dry bread with water, and if desired, salt with *ukrop* (a warm infusion of water with dill, fennel, or other herbs permitted on dry-fasting days).
[^275]: Rubrical distinction between the Lavra of St. Sabbas (where monks recited Great Compline individually in their cells) and Palestinian cenobitic monasteries (where Great Compline was chanted communally in church).
[^276]: Clean Monday Great Compline (*на повече́рїи вели́комъ*): summoned by 12 strokes on the wooden klepalo at the tenth hour (approx. 4:00 PM), opening with Psalm 69 before the Great Canon.
[^277]: Division of the Great Penitential Canon of St. Andrew of Crete into four sections, chanted at Great Compline across the first four days of Clean Week (Monday through Thursday), prior to its complete chanting at Matins of Thursday of the Fifth Week.
[^278]: St. Andrew of Crete (c. 660–740), born in Damascus, monk of the Brotherhood of the Holy Sepulchre in Jerusalem, deacon of the Great Church in Constantinople, and Archbishop of Gortyna in Crete, hymnographer and creator of the Great Canon.
[^279]: Great Canon, Ode 2, Tone 6, Heirmos: "Give ear, O heaven, and I will speak" (Deut. 32:1), focusing on Adam's fall, the loss of the original robe of light, and the soul's voluntary submission to the passions.
[^280]: Great Canon, Ode 3, Tone 6, Heirmos: "Upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments", with troparia warning the soul to flee the conflagration of Sodom unto Zoar, and petitioning Christ the Good Shepherd to seek His strayed sheep.
[^281]: Great Canon, Ode 4, Tone 6, Heirmos: "The Prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid" (Hab. 3:2), reflecting on the fleeting nature of earthly life, Jacob's ladder of divine ascent, and the spiritual allegories of Leah (ascetic practice / *praxis*) and Rachel (spiritual contemplation / *theoria*).
[^282]: Great Canon, Ode 5, Tone 6, Heirmos: "Early out of the night waking early unto Thee, O Lover of mankind" (Isa. 26:9), reflecting on Reuben's incestuous transgressions and Joseph's betrayal by his brothers as types of betrayal and of the Lord's burial and resurrection.
[^283]: Great Canon, Ode 6, Tone 6, Heirmos: "I cried with my whole heart unto the merciful God" (Jonah 2:3), recounting the rebellion of Dathan and Abiram, the backsliding heifer Ephraim (Hos. 4:16), and Moses' hand cleansed of leprosy (Exod. 4:6–7).
[^284]: Kontakion of the Great Canon, Tone 6, attributed to St. Romanos the Melodist: "My soul, my soul, arise! Why sleepest thou?", calling the soul to spiritual alertness before the imminent coming of Christ the Judge.
[^285]: Great Canon, Ode 7, Tone 6, Heirmos: "We have sinned, we have transgressed" (Dan. 9:5), with troparia meditating on Saul seeking his father's asses and unexpectedly receiving a kingdom, and David's double sin (adultery and murder) followed by swift, exemplary repentance in Psalm 50.
[^286]: Great Canon, Ode 8, Tone 6, Heirmos: "Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify" (Dan. 3:57), recalling the fiery ascent of Elijah the charioteer, Elisha receiving a double portion of the spirit and parting the Jordan, the hospitality of the Shunammite woman, and the greed and leprosy of Gehazi.
[^287]: Great Canon, Ode 9, Tone 6, Heirmos: "Ineffable is the childbearing of a seedless conception", transitioning from the figures of the Old Witness to the saving economy of Christ in the New Testament: the salvation of the Magi, the Slaughter of the Holy Innocents, and the Lord's forty-day fast in the wilderness.
[^288]: Concluding troparion of the Great Canon addressed directly to the author, St. Andrew of Crete: "O venerable Andrew, thrice-blessed father, shepherd of Crete, cease not to pray for those that hymn thee".
[^289]: Order of the second stasis of Great Compline: Psalms 4, 6, 12, followed by the second stasis (Pss. 24, 30, 90), the solemn chanting of "God is with us" (Isa. 8:8–18), the Hymn of the Bodiless Powers, the Nicene Creed, petitionary verses with alternating bows, and the troparia of the First Tone/Second Tone ("Enlighten mine eyes, O Christ God").
[^290]: Alternate Lenten troparia for Great Compline appointed on Tuesday and Thursday evenings, Tone 8: "The unsleeping vigilance of mine invisible enemies" (*Невидимыхъ врагъ моихъ несыпанїе*).
[^291]: Third stasis of Great Compline: Psalms 50, 101, the Penitential Prayer of King Manasseh (2 Chron. 36 / Odes of Scripture), the troparion "Lord of hosts, be with us" (*Господи силъ съ нами буди*) chanted antiphonally with five psalm verses, the Prayer of St. Ephrem with 16 prostrations, and the Compline bedtime prayers ("O undefiled, unstained" and "And grant unto us, O Master").
[^292]: Rubric concerning the prayer of great intercession ("O Master great in mercy, Lord Jesus Christ our God", *Владыко многомилостиве*) recited by the priest while all clergy and brethren remain prostrate upon the earth.
[^293]: The ancient monastic Rite of Mutual Forgiveness (*Чинъ прощенїя*) concluding Great Compline: the superior makes a full prostration before the brotherhood asking pardon, the brethren respond, and all clergy and monastics exchange mutual prostrations and forgiveness.
