St. Theodore draws a stark biblical warning from 1 Kings 13:11–24, where the unnamed man of God from Judah was devoured by a lion after being deceived into breaking his fast by the old prophet of Bethel, warning the soul against the venomous serpent of gluttony (*чревобѣ́сїѧ ѕмі́й*).
[^428]: Ode 9 of the Triodion Canons by St. Joseph and St. Theodore: Physical leaf p183 (Printed fol. 179). Tone 8, Heirmos: *Проꙗвле́нное на горѣ̀* (Greek: *Τὴν προτυπωθεῖσαν ἐν ὄρει* — "The Childbirth of the Ever-Virgin, prefigured on the mountain unto the Lawgiver in the fire and the bush"). St. Joseph describes fasting as the spiritual cultivator of the soul, yielding the ripe wheat-ear of virtues. St. Theodore extols the biblical ascetics Samuel and Samson, declaring that fasting nurtures and crowns true priests and prophets.
[^429]: Aposticha at Tuesday Matins: Physical leaf p184 (Printed fol. 180). Idiomelon of the day in Tone 5 (*Вскꙋ́ю оу҆ныва́еши дꙋшѐ моѧ̀...*), sung twice. The hymn urges the sluggard soul not to despair in sickness but to run swiftly unto Christ the divine Physician, washing the countenance with tears of repentance and trimming the spiritual lamp with the oil of good deeds (alluding to the Parable of the Ten Virgins, Matt. 25:1–13).
[^430]: Sixth Hour Troparion of the Prophecy: Physical leaf p184 (Printed fol. 180). Tone 6 (*Досто́йнw воспѣ́ти тѧ̀ не мо́жемъ...*). The troparion introduces the Sixth Hour Scripture reading, beseeching the Lord Who is easily propitiated (*бл҃гопремѣни́тель*) not to destroy us along with our manifold transgressions.
[^431]: Sixth Hour First Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p184 (Printed fol. 180). Tone 4, Psalm 25:8 (LXX: *Гдⷭ҇и, возлюби́хъ бл҃голѣ́пїе до́мꙋ твоегѡ̀, и҆ мѣ́сто селе́нїѧ сла́вы твоеѧ̀* / *Κύριε, ἠγάπησα εὐπρέπειαν οἴκου σου καὶ τόπον σκηνώματος δόξης σου*). The verse is Psalm 25:1 (LXX: *Judge me, O Lord, for I have walked in my blamelessness*), which continues onto leaf p185.
[^432]: Sixth Hour Old Testament Reading from Isaiah: Physical leaf p185 (Printed fol. 181). Isaiah 5:7–16. The prophet pronounces God's judgment upon the house of Israel and Judah, the "new and beloved vineyard" that produced only wild thorns and bloodshed instead of justice. Isaiah proclaims woes against greedy land-grabbers who join house to house and field to field, and against drunken carousers who spend their days in music and strong drink while ignoring the works of the Lord, concluding with the expansion of Hades to swallow the proud and unrighteous.
[^433]: Sixth Hour Second Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p185 (Printed fol. 181). Tone 3, Psalm 26:1a (LXX: *Гдⷭ҇ь просвѣще́нїе моѐ и҆ Сп҃си́тель мо́й, когѡ̀ ꙋ҆бою́сѧ;* / *Κύριος φωτισμός μου καὶ σωτήρ μου, τίνα φοβηθήσομαι;*). The verse is Psalm 26:1b (LXX: *The Lord is the defender of my life; of whom shall I be afraid?*).
[^434]: Tuesday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Vespers (*Во вто́рникъ в҃-ѧ седми́цы ст҃а́гw вели́кагw поста̀ ве́чера*): Physical leaf p185 (Printed fol. 181). Preceded by a decorative ornamental frieze. Vespers appoints the evening Kathisma 18 from the Psalter (*Songs of Ascents*), followed at "Lord, I have cried" by 6 stichera: 3 from the Triodion (by St. Joseph and St. Theodore) and 3 from the Menaion.
[^435]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried" by St. Joseph the Hymnographer: Physical leaf p185 (Printed fol. 181). Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (*Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы* / *Πανεύφημοι μάρτυρες*). St. Joseph contemplates Christ stretching forth His hands upon the Cross to gather the dispersed nations, praying that the Savior will gather the sinner's scattered mind and make him a partaker of the divine Passion through fasting, citing the Three Holy Children who proved stronger than Nebuchadnezzar's furnace (Dan. 3).
[^436]: Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" by St. Theodore the Studite: Physical leaf p185 (Printed fol. 181). Tone 3, Podoben: "O valiant martyrs" (*До́блїи мꙋ́ченицы* / *Γενναῖοι μάρτυρες*). St. Theodore extols the joyous nature of the fast as a season enriched with purity, love, and radiant prayer, bidding the faithful to venerate the Precious Cross of Christ. The sticheron crosses the folio break and concludes on leaf p186.
[^437]: Vespers First Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p186 (Printed fol. 182). Tone 6, Psalm 27:9 (LXX: *Спасѝ Гдⷭ҇и лю́ди твоѧ̑, и҆ бл҃гословѝ достоѧ́нїе твоѐ* / *Σῶσον τὸν λαόν σου καὶ εὐλόγησον τὴν κληρονομίαν σου*). The verse is Psalm 27:1 (LXX: *Unto Thee, O Lord, will I cry, O my God*).
[^438]: Vespers First Old Testament Reading from Genesis: Physical leaf p186 (Printed fol. 182). Genesis 4:8–15. The historical narrative of the first murder: Cain lures his brother Abel into the open field and kills him. When interrogated by God, Cain utters his defiant response: "Am I my brother's keeper?" God declares that Abel's blood cries out from the ground, curses Cain to be a fugitive and a vagabond (*стенѧ́й и҆ трѧсы́йсѧ*) upon the earth, and marks him with a protective sign against blood vengeance.
[^439]: Vespers Second Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p186 (Printed fol. 182). Tone 7, Psalm 28:11 (LXX: *Гдⷭ҇ь крѣ́пость лю́демъ свои̑мъ да́стъ, Гдⷭ҇ь бл҃гослови́тъ лю́ди своѧ̑ ми́ромъ* / *Κύριος ἰσχὺν τῷ λαῷ αὐτοῦ δώσει, Κύριος εὐλογήσει τὸν λαὸν αὐτοῦ ἐν εἰρήνῃ*). The verse is Psalm 28:1 (LXX: *Bring unto the Lord, ye sons of God*).
[^440]: Vespers Second Old Testament Reading from Proverbs: Physical leaf p186 (Printed fol. 182). Proverbs 5:1–15. King Solomon instructs the disciple in divine wisdom and prudence, warning against the intoxicating seductions of the adulteress whose lips drop honey but whose end is bitter as wormwood and sharp as a two-edged sword, leading directly down to Sheol. The reading concludes with the counsel to drink pure water from one's own cistern and well.
[^441]: Aposticha at Tuesday Evening Vespers: Physical leaf p187 (Printed fol. 183). Idiomelon of the day in Tone 3 (*Пло́тїю распны́йсѧ Гдⷭи...*), sung twice. The sticheron establishes the theological link between the Crucifixion of Christ and the Christian's ascetic mortification: Christ's pierced side and death on the Cross slay the primordial serpent, inspiring the believer to nail his flesh to divine fear, restrain all the sensory portals of sin, and offer a contrite heart as acceptable sacrifice.
[^442]: Stavrotheotokion at the Aposticha: Physical leaf p187 (Printed fol. 183). Tone 3, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy martyrs" (*Ве́лїѧ мꙋ́ченикъ* / *Μεγάλη τῶν Μαρτύρων σου*). The Mother of God stands at the foot of the Cross, weeping aloud as she beholds her beloved Son stripped of His radiant beauty which once adorned human nature.
[^443]: Wednesday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Matins (*Въ сре́дꙋ в҃-ѧ седми́цы ст҃а́гw вели́кагw поста̀ на оу́трени*): Physical leaf p187 (Printed fol. 183). Display headpiece featuring an ornamental woodcut cartouche enclosing a radiant Latin Cross bordered by acanthus leaves and palmettes. Wednesday Matins centers upon the veneration of the Holy Cross; after the first kathisma, the Cross sessional hymns of the Octoechos are sung, followed after the second kathisma by the Triodion sessional hymns in Tone 1.
[^444]: Sessional Hymn Stavrotheotokion: Physical leaf p187 (Printed fol. 183). Tone 1 Podoben (*Ꙗ҆́кw ви́дѣ на крⷭтѣ̀ А҆́гница...* / Greek: *Ὡς ἑώρακεν ἡ Ἀμνὰς*). The Theotokos laments as she witnesses her Lamb hanging on the Cross between two criminals, recalling the prophetic words of Simeon (Luke 2:35).
[^445]: Sessional Hymn after the Third Psalter Reading: Physical leaf p188 (Printed fol. 184). Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (*Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства* / *Τὴν ὡραιότητα τῆς παρθενίας σου*). The troparion calls the Church to sound the trumpet of hymnody in honor of the season of abstinence, beseeching Christ the Word to preserve His servants in peace by the invincible power of His Cross.
[^446]: Ode 3 of the Triodion Canon by St. Joseph: Physical leaf p188 (Printed fol. 184). Tone 1, Heirmos: *Да оу҆тверди́тсѧ се́рдце моѐ* (Greek: *Στερεωθήτω ἡ καρδία μου* — "Let my heart be established in Thy will, O Christ God"). St. Joseph contrasts Adam's illicit stretching forth of his hands toward the forbidden tree in Eden with Christ's voluntary stretching forth of His pure palms upon the Cross to expiate human guilt.
[^447]: Ode 3 of the Second Triodion Canon by St. Theodore the Studite: Physical leaf p188 (Printed fol. 184). Tone 3, Heirmos: *Оу҆твердѝ Гдⷭи сердца̀ на́ша* (Greek: *Στερέωσον Κύριε* — "Establish, O Lord, our hearts by Thy Cross"). The hymnody venerates the instruments of the Lord's Passion—the scarlet chlamys, the nails, the Cross, the sponge, and the spear—concluding with the appointed Katavasia of the ode.
[^448]: Ode 8 of the Triodion Canon by St. Joseph: Physical leaf p188 (Printed fol. 184). Tone 1, Heirmos: *Пѣсносло́вцы* (Greek: *Ὑμνολόγοι* — "O hymn-singers in the furnace, children of Abraham"). St. Joseph prays that Christ, Who consented to be numbered among the transgressors (Isa. 53:12), will tear up the debt-bond of our sins with His lance and heal the wounded heart with the life-giving blood flowing from His side.
[^449]: Ode 8 of the Second Triodion Canon by St. Theodore the Studite: Physical leaf p189 (Printed fol. 185). Tone 3, Heirmos: *Въ пла́мень ко ѻ҆трокѡ́мъ* (Greek: *Εἰς τὴν κάμινον τοῖς Παισὶ* — "Unto the Hebrew children in the flame"). St. Theodore celebrates the Good Thief who attained theological insight upon his cross in a single instant, crying: "Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom" (Luke 23:42), and extols the Precious Cross as the pilot who navigates the soul safely across the stormy sea of Lent.
[^450]: Ode 9 of the Triodion Canon by St. Joseph: Physical leaf p189 (Printed fol. 185). Tone 1, Heirmos: *Живопрїе́мный и҆сто́чникъ* (Greek: *Ἡ ζωοδόχος πηγή* — "The Life-receiving Spring, ever-flowing and radiant"). St. Joseph cites the miracle of the Prophet Elisha raising the deceased Shunammite's son (2 Kings 4:32–37) as a figure of the soul's resurrection from spiritual death through fasting and bodily mortification.
[^451]: Ode 9 of the Second Triodion Canon by St. Theodore the Studite: Physical leaf p190 (Printed fol. 186). Tone 3, Heirmos: *На Сїна́йстѣй горѣ̀* (Greek: *Ἐν Σιναίῳ τῷ ὄρει* — "On Mount Sinai Moses beheld thee in the bush"). St. Theodore depicts the cosmic horror of creation at the Crucifixion: angels trembling, the sun darkened, and the earth shaken as the Creator suffers mockery, blows, and piercing in the flesh to bestow incorruptible life upon mankind.
[^452]: Aposticha at Wednesday Matins: Physical leaf p190 (Printed fol. 186). Idiomelon of the day in Tone 7 (*Крⷭто́мъ твои́мъ Гдⷭи разрꙋши́вый сме́рть...*), sung twice. The sticheron contrasts the primordial death brought into the world through eating of the forbidden tree in Paradise with the eternal life purchased by the Cross of Christ, exhorting the faithful to labor through fasting for the food that endures unto everlasting life (John 6:27).
[^453]: Stavrotheotokion at the Matins Aposticha: Physical leaf p190 (Printed fol. 186). Tone 7, Podoben: "No longer are we forbidden" (*Не ктомꙋ̀ возбранѧ́еми* / *Οὐκέτι κωλυόμεθα*). The Theotokos weeps as she beholds her Son nailed willingly to the Tree, magnifying His sovereign divine power even in His lowest kenosis. The leaf concludes with the catchword for the Sixth Hour (*На ѕ҃-мъ*).
[^454]: Sixth Hour Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 6: Physical leaf p191 (Printed fol. 187). Tone 6 (*Вы́шнїй на небесѣ́хъ живы́й...*). The troparion introduces the Sixth Hour Scripture reading for Wednesday of the Second Week, invoking God the Creator of all breath to save His people who place their hope in Him.
[^455]: Sixth Hour First Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p191 (Printed fol. 187). Tone 5, Psalm 29:9 LXX (*Къ тебѣ̀ Гдⷭ҇и воззовꙋ̀, и҆ къ Бг҃у моемꙋ̀ помолю́сѧ* / *Πρὸς σέ, Κύριε, κεκράξομαι, καὶ πρὸς τὸν Θεόν μου δεηθήσομαι*). The verse is Psalm 29:2 LXX (*I will exalt Thee, O Lord, for Thou hast lifted me up*).
[^456]: Sixth Hour Old Testament Reading from Isaiah: Physical leaf p191 (Printed fol. 187). Isaiah 5:16–25. The prophet proclaims God's righteousness revealed in judgment, pronouncing woes against those who drag sins along as with ropes, those who invert moral orders by calling evil good and light darkness, greedy nobles intoxicated with wine and strong drink, and those who subvert justice for bribes, concluding with the fiery consuming of the wicked and the Lord's outstretched hand of chastisement.
[^457]: Sixth Hour Second Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p191 (Printed fol. 187). Tone 6, Psalm 30:2a LXX (*На тѧ́ Гдⷭ҇и оу҆пова́хъ, да не постыжꙋ́сѧ во вѣ́къ* / *Ἐπὶ σοί, Κύριε, ἤλπισα, μὴ καταισχυνθείην εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα*). The verse is Psalm 30:2b LXX (*In Thy righteousness deliver me, and rescue me*).
[^458]: Wednesday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Vespers (*Въ сре́дꙋ в҃-ѧ седми́цы ст҃а́гw вели́кагw поста̀ ве́чера*): Physical leaf p191 (Printed fol. 187). Display headpiece featuring an ornamental woodcut band. Vespers appoints at "Lord, I have cried" 10 stichera: the idiomelon of the day sung twice, the martyrion, 3 prosomoia from the Triodion, and 4 from the Menaion.
[^459]: Idiomelon at "Lord, I have cried": Physical leaf p191 (Printed fol. 187). Tone 1 (*Дꙋхо́вный, бра́тїе, взе́мше по́стъ...*). The hymn admonishes the brethren that true fasting is spiritual rather than merely bodily, requiring the restraint of deceitful speech and the removal of stumbling blocks before one's neighbor, accompanied by tears of repentance.
[^460]: Martyrion at "Lord, I have cried": Physical leaf p192 (Printed fol. 188). Tone 1 (*Всехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы, ва́съ ни землѧ̀ потаи́ла є҆́сть...*). The hymn extols the martyrs who were received into heaven, entering the gates of paradise to enjoy the tree of life, and asks their intercessions for peace and mercy.
[^461]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried" by St. Joseph the Hymnographer: Physical leaf p192 (Printed fol. 188). Tone 3, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy Cross" (*Ве́лїа крⷭ҇та̀* / *Μεγάλη τοῦ Σταυροῦ σου*). The first sticheron asks the prayers of the Holy Apostles to pass through the season of fasting in compunction, while the second contemplates the Dread Judgment seat of Christ, praying that the sinner may not be condemned.
[^462]: Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" by St. Theodore the Studite: Physical leaf p192 (Printed fol. 188). Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all" (*Всѐ ѿло́жше* / *Ὅλην ἀποθέμενοι*). St. Theodore invokes the Apostles as cosmic luminaries and treasuries of divine wisdom, beseeching them to grant spiritual youthfulness during Lent so that the faithful may arrive worthily at the Passion of Christ.
[^463]: Vespers First Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p192 (Printed fol. 188). Tone 6, Psalm 31:11 LXX (*Весели́тесѧ ѡ҆ Гдⷭ҇ѣ, и҆ ра́дуйтесѧ пра́ведніи* / *Εὐφράνθητε ἐπὶ Κύριον καὶ ἀγαλλιᾶσθε, δίκαιοι*). The verse is Psalm 31:1 LXX (*Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven*).
[^464]: Vespers First Old Testament Reading from Genesis: Physical leaf p192 (Printed fol. 188). Genesis 4:16–26. Cain settles in the land of Nod east of Eden, builds the first city (Enoch), and the genealogy traces down to Lamech, his two wives Adah and Zillah, the origins of pastoralism (Jabal), music (Jubal), and metallurgy (Tubal-Cain), Lamech's boast of vengeance, and the birth of Seth and Enos, when men began to call upon the name of the Lord.
[^465]: Vespers Second Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p192 (Printed fol. 188). Tone 1, Psalm 32:22 LXX (*Бꙋ́ди Гдⷭ҇и млⷭ҇ть твоѧ̀ на на́съ, ꙗ҆́коже оу҆пова́хомъ на тѧ́* / *Γένοιτο, Κύριε, τὸ ἔλεός σου ἐφ' ἡμᾶς, καθάπερ ἠλπίσαμεν ἐπὶ σέ*). The verse is Psalm 32:1 LXX (*Rejoice in the Lord, ye righteous*).
[^466]: Vespers Second Old Testament Reading from Proverbs: Physical leaf p193 (Printed fol. 189). Proverbs 5:15–6:3. The sage counsels fidelity in marriage, comparing marital love to pure water drawn from one's own cistern, warning that God sees all the paths of man, and concluding with a warning against the perils of becoming surety for a friend's debt.
[^467]: Rubric for the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts: Physical leaf p193 (Printed fol. 189). Following the Old Testament readings, the rubric prescribes the immediate celebration of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts (*И҆ а҆́бїе Преждеѡсвѧще́ннаѧ Лїтꙋргі́а*).
[^468]: Thursday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Matins (*Въ четверто́къ в҃-ѧ седми́цы, ст҃а́гw вели́кагw поста̀ на оу́трени*): Physical leaf p193 (Printed fol. 189). Display headpiece featuring an ornamental woodcut band. The service begins with the Trinity hymns and appoints the apostolic sessional hymns from the Octoechos after the first kathisma.
[^469]: Sessional Hymns after the Second Psalter Reading: Physical leaf p193 (Printed fol. 189). Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (*Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства* / *Τὴν ὡραιότητα τῆς παρθενίας σου*). The troparion extols the Apostles as sweet grape clusters produced by Christ the True Vine, granting spiritual compunction to the fasting Church. The accompanying Theotokion seeks refuge under the protection of the Mother of God amidst deep grief and perplexity.
[^470]: Sessional Hymn after the Third Psalter Reading: Physical leaf p194 (Printed fol. 190). Tone 6, Podoben: "The Lord stood before" (*Гдⷭ҇и предстоѧ́ше* / *Κύριε, παριστάμην*). The hymn beseeches the Lord to grant tears of genuine compunction for the washing of soul defilements and to grant that the faithful may worthily behold His saving Passion.
[^471]: Ode 4 of the Triodion Canon by St. Joseph the Hymnographer: Physical leaf p194 (Printed fol. 190). Tone 3, Heirmos: *Е҆гда̀ въ рѣка́хъ прогнѣ́ваешисѧ* (Greek: *Ὅταν ἐν ποταμοῖς ὀργίζῃ* — "When Thou art angry at the rivers, O Lord, or Thy wrath is against the sea", based on Hab. 3:8). St. Joseph urges the soul to feast upon noetic visions through abstinence, addressing the Apostles as living coals of immaterial fire that consume the thorn-bushes of passion.
[^472]: Ode 4 of the Second Triodion Canon by St. Theodore the Studite: Physical leaf p194 (Printed fol. 190). Tone 6, Heirmos: *Оу҆слы́ша проро́къ прише́ствїе твоѐ Гдⷭ҇и* (Greek: *Εἰσακήκοεν ὁ προφήτης* — "The prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid", Hab. 3:2). St. Theodore explicitly lists the twelve Apostles by name and recalls the miraculous catch of 153 large fish (John 21:11) as a mystery of catching the nations into the Church.
[^473]: Ode 8 of the Triodion Canon by St. Joseph: Physical leaf p195 (Printed fol. 191). Tone 3, Heirmos: *Въ пла́мени въ ѻ҆трокѡ́мъ* (Greek: *Ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ τοῖς Παισὶ* — "Unto the Children in the flame"). St. Joseph laments his spiritual deadness and warns that bodily abstinence from food is unacceptable to God without genuine inner amendment of life, invoking the burning bush (Exod. 3:2) as a type of the Mother of God.
[^474]: Ode 8 of the Second Triodion Canon by St. Theodore the Studite: Physical leaf p195 (Printed fol. 191). Tone 6, Heirmos: *Е҆гѡ́же во́инства нбⷭ҇наѧ сла́вѧтъ* (Greek: *Ὃν στρατιαὶ οὐρανῶν δοξάζουσι* — "Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify and Cherubim and Seraphim tremble before"). St. Theodore compares the twelve Apostles to the twelve tribes of Jacob, who drowned the noetic Pharaoh in the spiritual Red Sea and led the people of God into divine knowledge.
[^475]: Ode 9 of the Triodion Canon by St. Joseph: Physical leaf p195 (Printed fol. 191). Tone 3, Heirmos: *На Сїна́йстѣй горѣ̀* (Greek: *Ἐν Σιναίῳ τῷ ὄρει* — "On Mount Sinai Moses beheld thee in the bush"). St. Joseph portrays the Apostles as life-giving rivers flowing from the Noetic Fountain (Christ), praying to Christ as the Good Pilot to rescue his sinking soul as He once rescued Peter upon the stormy sea (Matt. 14:31).
[^476]: Continuation of Ode 9 of St. Joseph's Canon: Physical leaf p196 (Printed fol. 192). The troparion joins the intercessions of the Apostles and angelic choirs for divine mercy and entrance into the Kingdom, while the Theotokion magnifies the Mother of God as the ark of sanctification in whom virginity and maternity met beyond nature.
[^477]: Ode 9 of the Second Triodion Canon by St. Theodore the Studite: Physical leaf p196 (Printed fol. 192). Tone 6, Heirmos: *Безсѣ́меннагw Зача́тїа ржⷭ҇тво̀ неска́занное* (Greek: *Ἀσπόρου συλλήψεως ὁ τόκος ἀνερμήνευτος* — "The childbirth of the Seedless Conception is ineffable"). St. Theodore extols the Apostles who lived in the flesh as angels and overcame kings and philosophers by the power of the Spirit, hailing Peter as the rock of faith and Paul as teacher of the Gentiles.
[^478]: Matins Aposticha Idiomelon: Physical leaf p196 (Printed fol. 192). Tone 8 (*Покаѧ́нїа вре́мѧ, и҆ жи́зни вѣ́чныѧ хода́тай...*), sung twice. The hymn defines the ascetic discipline of Lent as the mediator of eternal life, emphasizing that fasting is spiritually efficacious only when accompanied by active works of charity and almsgiving, which deliver the soul from death (Tobit 12:9).
[^479]: Martyrion at the Matins Aposticha: Physical leaf p196 (Printed fol. 192). Tone 8 (*Въ броню̀ вѣ́ры ѡ҆болче́шесѧ до́брѣ...*). The sticheron portrays the holy martyrs as valiant soldiers clad in the armor of faith and armed with the sign of the Cross (Eph. 6:11–17), whose courageous confession vanquished the tyrants and the devil. The sticheron crosses the folio break and concludes on leaf p197.
[^480]: Conclusion of Matins Aposticha and Theotokion: Physical leaf p197 (Printed fol. 193). The martyrs are crowned as victors in heaven; the accompanying Theotokion (*А҆́зъ, Дв҃о Ст҃а́ѧ Чтⷭ҇аѧ...*) expresses unshakeable confidence in the saving protection of the Mother of God.
[^481]: Sixth Hour Troparion of the Prophecy: Physical leaf p197 (Printed fol. 193). Tone 1 (*Па́дшыѧ возста́ви ны̀...*). The troparion introduces the Sixth Hour readings for Thursday of the Second Week, praying to the Lover of mankind Who upholds the ends of the earth to restore those who have fallen and turned away.
[^482]: Sixth Hour First Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p197 (Printed fol. 193). Tone 6, Psalm 33:5 LXX (*Взыска́хъ Гдⷭ҇а, и҆ оу҆слы́ша мѧ̀ и҆ ѿ всѣ́хъ скорбе́й мои́хъ и҆зба́ви мѧ̀* / *Ἐξεζήτησα τὸν Κύριον, καὶ ἐπήκουσέ μου καὶ ἐκ πασῶν τῶν θλίψεών μου ἐρρύσατό με*). The verse is Psalm 33:2 LXX (*I will bless the Lord at all times*).
[^483]: Sixth Hour Old Testament Reading from Isaiah: Physical leaf p197 (Printed fol. 193). Isaiah 6:1–12. The grand prophetic inaugural vision of Isaiah: the Lord seated upon a high throne, surrounded by the six-winged Seraphim crying the Thrice-Holy hymn. Isaiah laments his unclean lips until a Seraph purges him with a burning coal from the altar, after which the prophet accepts his mission ("Here am I, send me!"), hearing the tragic prophecy of the judicial hardening of Israel until the land is made desolate.
[^484]: Sixth Hour Second Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p197 (Printed fol. 193). Tone 6, Psalm 34:23 LXX (*Воста́ни Гдⷭ҇и, и҆ вонмѝ сꙋдꙋ̀ моемꙋ̀* / *Ἐξεγέρθητι, Κύριε, καὶ πρόσχες τῇ κρίσει μου*). The verse is Psalm 34:1 LXX (*Judge Thou, O Lord, them that wrong me*).
[^485]: Thursday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Vespers (*Въ четверто́къ в҃-ѧ седми́цы ст҃а́гw вели́кагw поста̀ ве́чера*): Physical leaf p198 (Printed fol. 194). Display headpiece featuring an ornamental woodcut band. Vespers appoints at "Lord, I have cried" 6 stichera: 3 prosomoia from the Triodion (by St. Joseph and St. Theodore) and 3 from the Menaion.
[^486]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried" by St. Joseph the Hymnographer: Physical leaf p198 (Printed fol. 194). Tone 8, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (*Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе* / *Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν*). St. Joseph meditates on Christ pierced in His side upon the Cross to pour forth life unto humanity slain by eating of the forbidden tree in Eden, beseeching the Savior to grant participation in His Passion and Resurrection through the tears of the fast.
[^487]: Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" by St. Theodore the Studite: Physical leaf p198 (Printed fol. 194). Tone 5, Podoben: "O Lord, at the time of Moses" (*Гдⷭ҇и при Мѡѷсе́и* / *Κύριε ἐπὶ Μωϋσέως*). St. Theodore extols the life-giving Cross which brings forth life by destroying death, invoking its protection over the faithful during the holy days of Lent.
[^488]: Vespers First Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p198 (Printed fol. 194). Tone 8, Psalm 35:6 LXX (*Гдⷭ҇и, на нб҃сѝ млⷭ҇ть твоѧ̀, и҆ и҆́стина твоѧ̀ до ѡ҆́блакъ* / *Κύριε, ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ τὸ ἔλεός σου, καὶ ἡ ἀλήθειά σου ἕως τῶν νεφελῶν*). The verse is Psalm 35:7 LXX (*Thy righteousness is as the mountains of God; Thy judgments are a great deep*).
[^489]: Vespers First Old Testament Reading from Genesis: Physical leaf p198 (Printed fol. 194). Genesis 5:1–24. The genealogy of the antediluvian patriarchs from Adam through Seth, Enos, Cainan, Mahalalel, Jared, and Enoch. The passage establishes the generational lifespan formula ("and he lived... and begot sons and daughters... and he died"), concluding across the folio break on leaf p199 with Enoch, who walked with God and was translated without tasting death.
[^490]: Conclusion of the Genesis Reading: Physical leaf p199 (Printed fol. 195). Genesis 5:15–24. The reading culminates in the translation of Enoch: "And Enoch was well-pleasing unto God, and he was not found, for God translated him" (*И҆ оу҆годѝ Е҆нѡ́хъ Бг҃ꙋ, и҆ не ѡ҆брѣта́шесѧ, занѐ преложѝ є҆го̀ Бг҃ъ*, Gen. 5:24 / Heb. 11:5).
[^491]: Vespers Second Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p199 (Printed fol. 195). Tone 6, Psalm 36:34a LXX (*Потерпѝ Гдⷭ҇а, и҆ сохранѝ пꙋ́ть є҆гẁ* / *Ὑπόμεινον τὸν Κύριον καὶ φύλαξον τὴν ὁδὸν αὐτοῦ*). The verse is Psalm 36:1 LXX (*Fret not thyself because of evildoers, neither be envious against the workers of iniquity*).
[^492]: Vespers Second Old Testament Reading from Proverbs: Physical leaf p199 (Printed fol. 195). Proverbs 6:3–20. The reading opens with counsel on escaping surety, directs the sluggard to learn wisdom and industrious foresight from the ant and the bee (the Greek Septuagint addition on the bee is fully preserved in the Slavonic witness), warns against drowsiness, depicts the deceitful behavior of the wicked man, enumerates the seven things an abomination unto the Lord (arrogant eyes, a lying tongue, hands shedding innocent blood, a heart devising wicked plans, feet hurrying to evil, a false witness, and one who sows discord among brethren), and admonishes the disciple to keep the commandments of father and mother.
[^493]: Vespers Aposticha Idiomelon: Physical leaf p199 (Printed fol. 195). Tone 5 (*Не пости́вшесѧ по за́повѣди Созда́вшагw...*). The sticheron contrasts the fall of Adam and Eve through their failure to keep the fast regarding the tree of knowledge with the Christian fast which restores the believer unto the original Fatherland. The hymn crosses the folio break and concludes on leaf p200.
[^494]: Conclusion of Vespers Aposticha Idiomelon: Physical leaf p200 (Printed fol. 196). Tone 5. The hymn concludes by calling the faithful to fast from corruptible meats and destructive passions so that, through the Cross, they may enter Paradise with the Wise Thief.
[^495]: Martyrion at the Vespers Aposticha: Physical leaf p200 (Printed fol. 196). Tone 5 (*Ѡ҆ земны́хъ всѣ́хъ неради́вше...*). The hymn praises the holy martyrs who disdained all earthly goods and courageously underwent torments, winning their desired hope as heirs of the heavenly Kingdom.
[^496]: Stavrotheotokion at the Vespers Aposticha: Physical leaf p200 (Printed fol. 196). Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, ascetic of old" (*Ра́дуйсѧ по́стникwмъ* / *Χαίροις ἀσκητικῶν*). The Virgin Mother laments as she sees her beloved Son and Lamb led forth to slaughter, begging Him not to pass by her in silence as He runs the swift path of the Passion for the salvation of the world.
[^497]: Friday of the Second Week of the Great Fast at Matins (*Въ пѧто́къ в҃-ѧ седми́цы ст҃а́гw вели́кагw поста̀ на оу́трени*): Physical leaf p200 (Printed fol. 196). Display headpiece featuring an ornamental woodcut band with a central Latin cross. Matins centers upon the veneration of the Cross; after the first kathisma, the Cross sessional hymns from the Octoechos are sung.
[^498]: Sessional Hymns after the Second Psalter Reading: Physical leaf p200 (Printed fol. 196). Tone 8, Podoben: "Quickly anticipate" (*Ско́рѡ предварѝ* / *Ταχὺ προκατάλαβε*). The troparion summons the faithful to ascend through fasting above earthly passions to magnify the Lord exalted upon the Cross, drinking the water of compunction as they prepare for the hour of judgment. The Stavrotheotokion records the sorrow of the Mother of God as she beholds her Son nailed to the Tree.
[^499]: Sessional Hymn after the Third Psalter Reading: Physical leaf p200 (Printed fol. 196). Tone 5, Podoben: "Today the light" (*Свѣ́тъ дне́сь* / *Φῶς ἄδυτον*). St. Theodore the Studite praises the radiant grace of the Cross which illuminates the whole cosmos, destroys demonic power, and adorns the pathway of Great Lent.
[^500]: Stavrotheotokion at Friday Matins: Physical leaf p201 (Printed fol. 197). Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-unoriginate" (*Собезнача́льное* / *Τὸ συνάναρχον Λόγον*). The hymn concludes the sessional hymns following the third reading of the Psalter, presenting the lament of the Virgin Mother gazing upon her Son nailed between two thieves upon the Cross.
[^501]: Canons at Friday Matins: Physical leaf p201 (Printed fol. 197). Rubric governing the chanting of the daily canon from the Menaion alongside the triodia of Joseph the Hymnographer and Theodore the Studite, accompanied by the Biblical Odes (Odes 5, 8, and 9). Tone 8.
[^502]: Triodion of Joseph, Ode 5: Physical leaf p201 (Printed fol. 197). Tone 8, Heirmos: "The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ" (*Нечести́вїи не ᲂу҆́зрѧтъ сла́вы твоеѧ̀ Хрⷭ҇тѐ*). The troparia entreat Christ, Who willingly ascended the Cross and darkened the sun, to raise the soul from the abyss of passions and guide it across the sea of the fast to the haven of the Resurrection.
[^503]: Triodion of Theodore the Studite, Ode 5: Physical leaf p201 (Printed fol. 197). Tone 5, Heirmos: "Rising early from the night" (*Ѿ но́щи ᲂу҆тренюющыѧ* / *Ἐκ νυκτὸς ὀρθρίζοντες*). The hymnody meditates on Christ's hands stretched upon the Cross defeating the spiritual Amalek, followed by a Trinity hymn, a Theotokion, the penitential refrain with troparion, and the katavasia heirmos.
[^504]: Triodion of Joseph, Ode 8: Physical leaf p201 (Printed fol. 197). Tone 8, Heirmos: "The pious Children" (*Ѻ҆́троки бл҃гочести́выѧ*). The troparion invokes Christ Who opened Paradise to the Good Thief to heal the soul wounded by demonic fury. The ode continues on leaf p202.
[^505]: Triodion of Theodore the Studite, Ode 8: Physical leaf p202 (Printed fol. 198). Tone 5, Heirmos: "The Creator of creation" (*Творца̀ тва́ри* / *Τὸν ποιητὴν τῆς κτίσεως*). The ode blesses the all-holy Tree of the Cross and exalts Christ slain on Golgotha Who lives forever, concluding with the katavasia heirmos.
[^506]: Triodion of Joseph, Ode 9: Physical leaf p202 (Printed fol. 198). Tone 8, Heirmos: "A stone cut without hands from the unquarried mountain" (*Ка́мень нерꙋкосѣ́чный ѿ несѣко́мыѧ горы̀* / *Λίθος ἀχειρότμητος*). Based on Daniel 2:34, 45, the troparia call upon the Savior Who crushed the stones by His crucifixion to free the soul weighed down by insensibility and to preserve it from Gehenna.
[^507]: Triodion of Theodore the Studite, Ode 9: Physical leaf p203 (Printed fol. 199). Tone 5, Heirmos: "Rejoice, O Isaiah" (*И҆са́їе ликꙋ́й* / *Ἡσαΐα χόρευε*). The troparia celebrate the reopening of Paradise through the Cross and venerate the Wood as the seal of blessing, concluding with the katavasia heirmos.
[^508]: Matins Aposticha Idiomelon: Physical leaf p203 (Printed fol. 199). Tone 8 (*Страстоꙋбі́йца по́стъ настоя́щїй...*). Sung twice. The sticheron personifies the fast as a slayer of passions and a God-given helper, warning against the murmuring and intemperance of the Israelites in the wilderness and exhorting believers to keep the fast spiritually rather than hypocritically.
[^509]: Matins Aposticha Martyrion: Physical leaf p203 (Printed fol. 199). Tone 8 (*Что́ вы нарече́мъ ст҃і́и;* / *Τί ὑμᾶς καλέσωμεν ἅγιοι;*). The classic aposticha martyrion likens the holy martyrs to Cherubim, Seraphim, Angels, and heavenly Powers on account of their endurance and miracles.
[^510]: Matins Aposticha Stavrotheotokion: Physical leaf p203 (Printed fol. 199). Tone 8 (*Что́ чꙋ́дное и҆ и҆зѧ́щное...*). The Virgin Mother laments at the foot of the Cross, expressing sorrow sharper than maternal pangs and longing for Christ's speedy Resurrection.
[^511]: Sixth Hour Troparion of the Prophecy: Physical leaf p204 (Printed fol. 200). Tone 1 (*Въ нощѝ и҆ ко днѝ припа́даемъ тебѣ̀ Гдⷭ҇и...*). The troparion pleads for remission of sins and peace to worship and glorify Christ day and night.
[^512]: Sixth Hour First Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p204 (Printed fol. 200). Psalm 37 (LXX 37:2, 3), Tone 4 (*Гдⷭ҇и, да не ꙗ҆́ростїю твое́ю ѡ҆бличи́ши менѐ...*).
[^513]: Sixth Hour Old Testament Reading: Physical leaf p204 (Printed fol. 200). Isaiah 7:1–14. King Ahaz is confronted by the prophet Isaiah concerning the invasion of Rezin of Aram and Pekah of Israel; Ahaz refuses to test God, prompting the Messianic sign of Emmanuel.
[^514]: Sixth Hour Second Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p204 (Printed fol. 200). Psalm 38 (LXX 38:13, 2), Tone 6 (*Оу҆слы́ши мл҃твꙋ мою̀ Гдⷭ҇и...*).
[^515]: Friday of the Second Week at Vespers: Physical leaf p205 (Printed fol. 201). Ornamental woodcut band displaying symmetrical floral arabesques above the title (*Въ пѧто́къ в҃-ѧ седми́цы ст҃а́гw вели́кагw поста̀ ве́чера*). The rubrics regulate "Lord, I have cried" for the Presanctified Liturgy, appointing the idiomelon twice, six martyr-stichera of the tone from the Octoechos, and four from the Menaion.
[^516]: Vespers Idiomelon of the Day: Physical leaf p205 (Printed fol. 201). Tone 8 (*Ны́нѣ вре́мѧ бл҃гопрїѧ́тное...*). Citing 2 Corinthians 6:2, the idiomelon proclaims the present acceptable time of salvation and begs Christ to lighten the burden of sins.
[^517]: Vespers First Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p205 (Printed fol. 201). Psalm 39 (LXX 39:12, 2), Tone 4 (*Ми́лость твоѧ̀ и҆ и҆́стина твоѧ̀...*).
[^518]: Vespers First Old Testament Reading: Physical leaf p205 (Printed fol. 201). Genesis 5:32–6:8. The genealogy of Noah and his sons, the multiplication of wickedness on earth, the mysterious appearance of the giants (Nephilim), and the divine grief preceding the Flood.
[^519]: Vespers Second Prokeimenon: Physical leaf p205 (Printed fol. 201). Psalm 40 (LXX 40:5, 2), Tone 6 (*А҆́зъ рѣ́хъ: Гдⷭ҇и поми́лꙋй мѧ̀...*).
[^520]: Vespers Second Old Testament Reading: Physical leaf p206 (Printed fol. 202). Proverbs 6:20–7:2. The wise father instructs the son to bind the law upon his heart as a lamp against the snare of the adulterous woman, warning of the inevitable ruin and jealousy of the injured husband.
[^521]: Presanctified Liturgy Conclusion Rubric: Physical leaf p206 (Printed fol. 202). Cinnabar rubric (*И҆ про́чее послѣ́дованїе Преждесвѧще́нныхъ*) directing the continuation of the Presanctified Liturgy according to the Sluzhebnik.
[^522]: Second Saturday of the Great Fast at Matins: Physical leaf p206 (Printed fol. 202). Decorative woodcut headpiece band composed of ten radiating rosettes, introducing the Matins for the departed (*Въ сꙋббѡ́тꙋ в҃-ѧ седми́цы ст҃а́гw вели́кагw поста̀ на оу҆́трени*).
[^523]: Saturday Matins Troparion: Physical leaf p206 (Printed fol. 202). Tone 2 (*А҆пл҃и, мꙋ́ченицы и҆ проро́цы...*). Sung twice after "Alleluia," commemorating the communion of saints who intercede for the salvation of our souls.
[^524]: Saturday Matins Troparion for the Departed: Physical leaf p206 (Printed fol. 202). Tone 2 (*Помѧнѝ Гдⷭ҇и ꙗ҆́кw бл҃гъ рабы̑ твоѧ̑...*). Appointed at "Glory," petitioning the sinless Lord for the forgiveness and eternal rest of His departed servants.
[^525]: Saturday Matins Theotokion: Physical leaf p206 (Printed fol. 202). Tone 2 (*Мт҃и ст҃а́ѧ неизрече́ннагw Свѣ́та...*). Appointed at "Both now and ever," glorifying the Mother of the Light.
[^526]: Saturday Matins Psalter Readings: Physical leaf p207 (Printed fol. 203). Rubrics governing Kathisma 16 (Psalm 109) followed by martyr-sessional hymns from the Octoechos, and Kathisma 17 (Psalm 118, the Amomos / "Blessed are the undefiled") divided into two stases, during which the priest commemorates the departed, concluding with the Evlogetaria for the departed (*Ст҃ы́хъ ли́къ...*), the funeral litany, and the prayer for the dead.
[^527]: Sessional Hymn for the Departed: Physical leaf p207 (Printed fol. 203). Tone 5 (*Поко́й Сп҃се на́шъ съ пра́ведными рабы̑ твоѧ̑...*). Praying for the settlement of the departed in the courts of the righteous, followed by the Theotokion (*Ѿ Дв҃ы возсїѧ́вый мі́рꙋ Хрⷭ҇тѐ Бж҃е...*).
[^528]: Four-Ode Canon of Joseph, Ode 6: Physical leaf p207 (Printed fol. 203). Tone 8, Heirmos: "I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord" (*Моли́твꙋ пролїю̀ ко Гдⷭ҇ꙋ* / *Τὴν δέησιν ἐκχεῶ πρὸς Κύριον*). The acrostic reads: "This singing is of Joseph" (*Пѣ́нїе сїѐ Їѡ́сифово*). The ode commemorates the martyrs' triumphs and prays for the souls of the departed.
[^529]: Canon of Theodore the Studite, Ode 6: Physical leaf p207 (Printed fol. 203). Tone 3, Heirmos: "The abyss of passions" (*Глꙋбина̀ страсте́й* / *Βυθὸς ἁμαρτημάτων*). The ode invokes the lovers of the martyrs to celebrate their memory and honors their diverse sufferings. The ode concludes on leaf p208.
[^530]: Kontakion for the Departed: Physical leaf p208 (Printed fol. 204). Tone 8 (*Со ст҃ы́ми ᲂу҆поко́й* / *Μετὰ τῶν ἁγίων ἀνάπαυσον*). The solemn funeral hymn praying for rest where there is no pain, nor sorrow, nor sighing, but life everlasting.
[^531]: Ikos for the Departed: Physical leaf p208 (Printed fol. 204). Tone 8 (*Са́мъ є҆ди́нъ є҆сѝ безсме́ртный...*). The rubric refers the reader to leaf p21 (Printed fol. 17), the Saturday of the Meatfare Week, where the full text of the Ikos is printed.
[^532]: Four-Ode Canon of Joseph, Ode 7: Physical leaf p208 (Printed fol. 204). Tone 8, Heirmos: "The Hebrew Children in the furnace" (*Ѻ҆́троцы є҆вре́йстїи въ пещѝ* / *Παῖδες Ἑβραίων ἐν καμίνῳ*). The troparia petition the martyrs who extinguished deceit with their blood to deliver believers from eternal fire and obtain Paradise for the departed.
[^533]: Canon of Theodore the Studite, Ode 7: Physical leaf p208 (Printed fol. 204). Tone 3, Heirmos: "The flame of the furnace moist with dew" (*Пла́мень ѡ҆роси́вый пе́щный* / *Ὁ τὴν κάμινον δροσίσας*). The troparia depict the martyrs offering themselves as fragrant victims amid fire and sword, concluding on leaf p209.
[^534]: Four-Ode Canon of Joseph, Ode 8: Physical leaf p209 (Printed fol. 205). Tone 8, Heirmos: "The conquerors of the tyrant" (*Побѣди́тели мꙋчи́телѧ* / *Νικηταὶ τυράννου*). The hymnody entreats the holy martyrs to heal the ailing soul during the fast and intercede for a peaceful end and eternal rest for the departed.
[^535]: Canon of Theodore the Studite, Ode 8: Physical leaf p209 (Printed fol. 205). Tone 3, Heirmos: "From the unoriginate Father" (*И҆зъ безна́чальна Ѻ҆ц҃а̀* / *Τὸν ἐκ Πατρὸς ἀνάρχου*). The ode extols the martyrs who rejoiced while enduring diverse tortures, continuing on leaf p210.
[^536]: Four-Ode Canon of Joseph, Ode 9: Physical leaf p210 (Printed fol. 206). Tone 8, Heirmos: "Thou hast surpassed the ordinances" (*Оу҆ста́вы прешла̀ є҆сѝ* / *Ἐξέστη ἐπὶ τούτῳ ὁ οὐρανός*). The troparia compare the martyrs to spiritual coals burning up godlessness and beseech rest for the departed.
[^537]: Canon of Theodore the Studite, Ode 9: Physical leaf p210 (Printed fol. 206). Tone 3, Heirmos: "Thee, the immortal Fountain" (*Тебѐ безсме́ртный И҆сто́чника* / *Σὲ τὴν ἀθάνατον πηγήν*). Opening troparion celebrating the martyrs as fire sent by God across the earth to burn up idol deceit.
[^538]: LXX Ps. 15:3. Slavonic: 'Ст҃ы̑мъ и҆̀же сꙋ́ть на землѝ є҆гѡ̀' (Masoretic Ps. 16:3).
[^539]: Prescribed dismissal troparia at the Divine Liturgy of Saturday: Troparion of the Apostles and Martyrs ('А҆пⷭ҇ли, мꙋ́ченицы'), memorial troparion ('Помѧнѝ Гдⷭ҇и'), Glory: kontakion for the departed ('Со ст҃ы́ми ᲂу҆поко́й'), Both now: Theotokion ('Тебѐ и҆ стѣ́нꙋ').
[^540]: Codicological and typikon rubric for the general memorial services during the Great Fast: the memorial Liturgy for all departed souls is served on the three Saturdays of the second, third, and fourth weeks of the Fast, unless superseded by the feast of the First and Second Finding of the Head of the Forerunner (February 24), the Forty Martyrs of Sebaste (March 9), or the Annunciation of the Theotokos (March 25).
[^541]: Full-leaf woodcut illustration on physical leaf p212 (printed folio 208) depicting the Crucifixion of Christ, placed after the conclusion of the second week's memorial Saturday and before the entrance into the Second Sunday of the Great Fast.
[^542]: Second Sunday of the Great Fast. In the traditional Ruthenian recension preserved in the 1906 Lviv Stauropegion Triodion, this Sunday preserves the ancient Triodion hymnography of the Prodigal Son alongside the Octoechos and Menaion, rather than the Byzantine/Russian post-fourteenth-century commemoration of St. Gregory Palamas.
[^543]: Typikon canon arrangement for Matins: 6 troparia from the Resurrectional canon of the Octoechos with its Theotokion, 4 troparia from the Triodion (Canon of the Prodigal Son), and 4 troparia from the Menaion.
[^544]: Ode 1 Heirmos: 'Колесницегони́телѧ фараѡ́на' (The chariot-driver Pharaoh), Tone 8 (Exodus 15:1-19).
[^545]: Codicological note: Physical leaf p214 bears printed folio 209 (с҃ѳ), duplicating the folio number assigned to the unnumbered title leaf p213 due to a typographical misnumbering in the Stauropegion edition, which is resolved on leaf p215 (с҃аі = 211).
[^546]: Ode 3 Heirmos: 'Нбⷭ҇нагѡ крꙋ́га' (The vault of heaven / Maker of the vault of heaven), Tone 8.
[^547]: Ode 4 Heirmos: 'Ты̀ моѧ̀ крѣ́пость Гдⷭ҇и' (Thou art my strength, O Lord), Tone 8 (Habakkuk 3:2-19).
[^548]: Ode 5 Heirmos: 'Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ' (Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face), Tone 8 (Isaiah 26:9-20).
[^549]: Ode 6 Heirmos: 'Моли́твꙋ пролїю̀' (I will pour out my supplication unto the Lord), Tone 8 (Jonah 2:3-10).
[^550]: Kontakion of the Second Sunday of the Great Fast in Tone 4: 'Ны́нѣ вре́мѧ дѣ́лательное ꙗ҆ви́сѧ' (Now the time for action has appeared), urging vigilance, fasting, contrition, and almsgiving in view of the approaching judgment.
[^551]: Ode 7 Heirmos: 'Ѿ І҆ꙋде́и доше́дше' (The youths who arrived from Judea), Tone 8 (Daniel 3:26-56).
[^552]: Ode 8 Heirmos: 'Седмери́цею пе́щь' (Sevenfold the furnace), Tone 8 (Daniel 3:57-88).
[^553]: Ode 9 Heirmos: 'Оу҆жасе́сѧ ѡ҆ се́мъ нб҃о' (Heaven was dismayed at this), Tone 8.
[^554]: Divine Liturgy pericopes for the Second Sunday of the Great Fast: Epistle to the Hebrews 1:10 - 2:3 (Pericope 304, on the exalted dignity of the Son of God above angels); Gospel according to St. Mark 2:1-12 (Pericope 7, the healing of the paralytic in Capernaum).
[^555]: Great Vespers on the evening of the Second Sunday, inaugurating the liturgical offices of the Third Week of the Great Fast. The stichera at 'Lord, I have cried' include penitential stichera from the Octoechos and four-stanza prosomoia from the Triodion composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer and St. Theodore the Studite.
[^556]: Tone 8 prosomoion by Lord Theodore to the automelon 'Пресла́вное' (abbreviating the Byzantine model 'Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ' / Ὢ τοῦ παραδόξου θαύματος, 'O most marvelous wonder').
[^557]: Great Prokeimenon of Sunday Evening in Tone 8: 'Не ѿвратѝ лица̀ твоегѡ̀' (Turn not away Thy face from Thy servant, for I am afflicted), taken from LXX Ps. 68:18-19, 30b, 33 (Masoretic Ps. 69:17-18, 29b, 32).
[^558]: Lenten penitential idiomelon at the Aposticha in Tone 8: 'Бразды̑ и҆сплюва́хъ ѻ҆те́ческїѧ' (I have spit upon the fatherly furrows), developing the motif of the prodigal son who neglected his father's fertile fields and squandered his inheritance.
[^559]: Sessional hymn of Lord Joseph in Tone 8, chanted after the second stichology at Third Monday Matins, modeled on the automelon 'Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ' (That which was commanded mystically).
[^560]: Sessional hymn of Lord Theodore in Tone 8, chanted after the third stichology at Third Monday Matins, marking the completion of three weeks of the Great Fast and praying for strength to persevere through the remainder.
[^561]: Ode 1 Heirmos: 'Пѣ́снь возсле́мъ лю́дїе' (Let us send up a hymn, ye peoples), Tone 8 (Exodus 15:1-19).
[^562]: Second canon of Ode 1 by Lord Theodore, Tone 8, to the Heirmos model 'Пои́мъ Бг҃ꙋ' (Let us sing unto God).
[^563]: Katavasia of Ode 1 chanted in full: 'Пои́мъ Бг҃ꙋ, ѡ горькїѧ рабо́ты фараѡ́новы и҆зба́вльшемꙋ Їи҃лѧ' (Let us sing unto God, Who delivered Israel from the bitter bondage of Pharaoh).
[^564]: Ode 8 Heirmos: 'Бг҃оглаго́ливи' (Divinely-speaking [youths]), Tone 8 (Daniel 3:57-88).
[^565]: Ode 8 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon, Tone 8: 'Покрыва́ѧй вода́ми превы́спреннѧѧ своѧ̑' (Who coverest Thine upper chambers with waters), based on LXX Ps. 103:2-3 and Daniel 3:57-88.
[^566]: Ode 9 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's canon, Tone 8: 'Проꙗвле́нное на горѣ̀ законополо́жнику' (That which was prefigured on the mountain to the lawgiver in fire and the bush), evoking Exodus 3:1-6 and Luke 1:46-55.
[^567]: Ode 9 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon, Tone 8: 'Велича́емъ тѧ̀ всенепоро́чнꙋю Мт҃рь Хрⷭ҇та̀ Бг҃а на́шегѡ' (We magnify thee, the all-immaculate Mother of Christ our God), drawing upon Luke 1:46-55.
[^568]: Sixth Hour Prokeimenon in Tone 4 from LXX Ps. 41:9b, 2 (Masoretic Ps. 42:8b, 1).
[^569]: Sixth Hour Old Testament reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah 8:13–9:7, containing the warning against consulting the dead, the promise of the light shining in Galilee of the Gentiles, and the renowned Messianic prophecy of the Child born unto us (Isa. 9:6-7).
[^570]: Sixth Hour concluding Prokeimenon in Tone 6 from LXX Ps. 42:5c, 1 (Masoretic Ps. 43:5c, 1).
[^571]: Vespers stichera at 'Lord, I have cried': prosomoion by Lord Joseph in Tone 1 to the automelon 'Всехва́льнїи мч҃еницы' (All-praised martyrs).
[^572]: Vespers stichera at 'Lord, I have cried': prosomoion by Lord Theodore in Tone 3 to the automelon 'Прїиди́те всѝ' (Come, all ye).
[^573]: Vespers first Prokeimenon in Tone 4 from LXX Ps. 43:9, 2 (Masoretic Ps. 44:8, 1).
[^574]: Vespers first Old Testament reading from Genesis 6:9–22, recounting the righteousness of Noah amid universal human corruption, the decree of the Deluge, and the architectural specifications for the Ark.
[^575]: Vespers second Prokeimenon in Tone 6 from LXX Ps. 44:18a, 2a (Masoretic Ps. 45:17a, 1a).
[^576]: Vespers second Old Testament reading from Proverbs 8:1–21, Wisdom's proclamation at the gates, offering righteous counsel, true riches, and guidance to rulers and seekers.
[^577]: Matins sessional hymn after the second stichology by Lord Joseph in Tone 1 to the automelon 'Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ' (The stone having been sealed).
[^578]: Matins sessional hymn after the third stichology by Lord Theodore in Tone 3 to the automelon 'Да веселѧ́тсѧ' (Let the heavens rejoice).
[^579]: Matins sessional Theotokion in Tone 3 to the automelon 'Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства' (The beauty of thy virginity).
[^580]: Matins Ode 2 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's canon in Tone 1: 'Ви́дите ви́дите, ꙗ҆́кѡ Бг҃ъ а҆́зъ є҆́смь' (See, see, that I am God), drawing on Deuteronomy 32:39.
[^581]: Matins Ode 2 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon in Tone 3: 'Ви́дите ви́дите, ꙗ҆́кѡ а҆́зъ є҆́смь' (See, see, that I am He), drawing on Deuteronomy 32:39.
[^582]: Matins Ode 8 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's canon in Tone 1: 'Е҆гѡ́же ᲂу҆жаса́ютсѧ А҆́гг҃ли' (Him before Whom tremble the angels), based on Daniel 3:57-88.
[^583]: Matins Ode 8 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon in Tone 3: 'Въ пла́мень ко ѻ҆трокѡ́мъ є҆вре́йскимъ' (Into the flame unto the Hebrew youths), based on Daniel 3:57-88.
[^584]: Matins Ode 9 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's canon in Tone 1: 'Свѣтоно́сный ѻ҆́блакъ' (The light-bearing cloud), drawing upon Luke 1:46-55.
[^585]: Matins Ode 9 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon in Tone 3: 'На Сїна́йстѣй горѣ̀ ви́дѣ тѧ̀ въ кꙋпинѣ̀ Мѡѷсе́й' (On Mount Sinai Moses saw thee in the bush), evoking Exodus 3:1-6, Daniel 2:34, 45, and Isaiah 11:1.
[^586]: Sixth Hour Prokeimenon in Tone 4 from LXX Ps. 45:8, 2 (Masoretic Ps. 46:7, 1).
[^587]: Sixth Hour Old Testament reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah 9:8–10:4, warning Ephraim and Samaria of impending judgment and pronouncing woe upon unjust scribes and judges who oppress the poor, widows, and orphans.
[^588]: Sixth Hour concluding Prokeimenon in Tone 3 from LXX Ps. 46:7, 2 (Masoretic Ps. 47:6, 1).
[^589]: Vespers stichera at "Lord, I have cried": prosomoion by Lord Joseph in Tone 3 to the automelon "Поста́виша три́десѧть сре́бреникъ" (They weighed thirty pieces of silver; cf. Zech. 11:12; Matt. 26:15).
[^590]: Vespers stichera at "Lord, I have cried": prosomoion by Lord Theodore in Tone 8 to the automelon "Да ра́спнетсѧ" (Let Him be crucified; cf. Matt. 27:22-23).
[^591]: Vespers first Prokeimenon in Tone 3 from LXX Ps. 47:2, 9 (Masoretic Ps. 48:1, 8).
[^592]: Vespers first Old Testament reading from Genesis 7:1–5, the divine command unto Noah to enter the Ark with his house, clean beasts by sevens, and unclean by twos before the forty days of rain.
[^593]: Vespers second Prokeimenon in Tone 2 from LXX Ps. 48:4, 2 (Masoretic Ps. 49:3, 1).
[^594]: Vespers second Old Testament reading from Proverbs 8:32–9:11, Lady Wisdom's instruction and seven-pillared feast, offering life to the simple and wisdom to the righteous.
[^595]: Matins sessional hymn after the second stichology by Lord Joseph in Tone 3 to the automelon "Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства" (Beholding the beauty of thy virginity).
[^596]: Matins sessional hymn after the third stichology by Lord Theodore in Tone 8 to the automelon "Воскр҃съ и҆з̾ гро́ба" (Having risen from the tomb).
[^597]: Matins sessional Theotokion in Tone 8 to the automelon "Милосе́рдїѧ" (Being the wellspring of loving-kindness).
[^598]: Matins Ode 3 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's canon in Tone 3: "Оу҆твержде́нїе" (The Affirmation; cf. 1 Sam. 2:2 / LXX 1 Kings 2:2).
[^599]: Matins Ode 3 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon in Tone 8: "Оу҆твердѝ на́съ въ тебѣ̀ Гдⷭ҇и" (Establish us in Thee, O Lord; cf. 1 Sam. 2:2 / LXX 1 Kings 2:2).
[^600]: Matins Ode 8 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's canon in Tone 3: "Нестерпи́момꙋ ѻ҆гню̀" (Unto the unbearable fire; based on Dan. 3:57–88).
[^601]: Matins Ode 8 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon in Tone 8: "Въ кꙋпинѣ̀ Мѡѷсе́ю" (In the bush unto Moses; based on Exod. 3:1–6 and Dan. 3:57–88).

[^612]: Vespers second Old Testament reading from Proverbs 9:12–18, contrasting true wisdom with the foolish, brazen woman seated at the gates whose stolen bread leads down to Hades.

[^620]: Matins Ode 9 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon in Tone 6: "Безсѣ́меннагѡ Зача́тїѧ" (Of seedless conception the birth; cf. Luke 1:46–55).

[^624]: Vespers stichera at "Lord, I have cried": prosomoia by Lord Joseph in Tone 5 to the automelon "Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ" (Rejoice, O container of ascetics).
[^625]: Vespers stichera at "Lord, I have cried": prosomoion by Lord Theodore in Tone 1 to the automelon "Ѻ҆́блакъ тѧ̀ свѣ́тлый" (A radiant cloud).

[^628]: Vespers second Prokeimenon in Tone 7 from LXX Ps. 56:2a, 2b (Masoretic Ps. 57:1a, 1b).
[^629]: Vespers second Old Testament reading from Proverbs 10:1–13 (continuing on fol. 242 through verse 22), on the wisdom of sons, the vain treasures of the lawless, the fruit of the righteous, and the blessing of the Lord.

[^634]: Matins Ode 5 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon in Tone 1: "Ѿ но́щи оу҆́тренююще" (From night early waking; based on Isa. 26:9–20).

[^642]: Sixth Hour Old Testament reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah 13:2–13, the oracle of judgment against Babylon, summoning the sanctified warriors and declaring the cosmic terror of the Day of the Lord.
[^643]: Sixth Hour concluding Prokeimenon in Tone 7 from LXX Ps. 58:10b, 11a, 2a (Masoretic Ps. 59:9b, 10a, 1a).

[^646]: Second Prokeimenon of the Presanctified Liturgy at Vespers in Tone 6 from LXX Ps. 60:2, 3a (Masoretic Ps. 61:1, 2a).
[^647]: Second Old Testament reading at the Presanctified Liturgy from Proverbs 10:31–11:12, teaching the fruit of the mouth of the righteous, just weights, humility versus pride, and the preservation of the upright.
[^648]: Direction referencing the rubrics and sessional hymns for the departed given on the Second Saturday of the Great Fast (fol. 209, Physical Leaf p213).
[^649]: Matins Ode 6 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's four-ode canon in Tone 4: "Прїидо́хъ во глꙋбины̑ морскї̑ѧ" (I entered into the depths of the sea; cf. Jonah 2:3–10).
[^650]: Matins Ode 6 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon in Tone 8: "Содержи́ма мѧ̀ прїимѝ Человѣколю́бче" (Take hold of me, O Lover of mankind; cf. Jonah 2:3–10).
[^651]: Matins Ikos for the departed, referencing the full text provided at fol. 17 (Physical Leaf p21).
[^652]: Matins Ode 7 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's canon in Tone 4: "Ю҆́ношы трѝ" (Three youths in Babylon; cf. Dan. 3:26–56).

[^654]: Matins Ode 8 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's canon in Tone 4: "И҆зба́вителю всѣ́хъ" (O Redeemer of all, the Almighty; cf. Dan. 3:57–88).
[^655]: Matins Ode 8 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon in Tone 8: "Бг҃оглаго́ливі́и ѻ҆́троцы" (The God-proclaiming youths in the furnace; cf. Dan. 3:57–88).
[^656]: Matins Ode 9 Heirmos of Lord Joseph's canon in Tone 4: "Е҆́ва ᲂу҆́бѡ недꙋ́гомъ" (Eve indeed by the disease of disobedience; cf. Gen. 3:1–6; Luke 1:46–55).
[^657]: Matins Ode 9 Heirmos of Lord Theodore's canon in Tone 8: "Велича́емъ тѧ̀ Мт҃рь Бж҃їю" (We magnify thee, the Mother of God; cf. Luke 1:46–55).
[^658]: The printed rubric reads "Ли́стъ р҃з" (fol. 107, Physical Leaf p111), an apparent typographical error for the Second Saturday of the Fast located at folios 207–213 (Physical Leaves p211–p217).
[^659]: Divine Liturgy Epistle lection for the Third Saturday of the Great Fast: Hebrews 10:32–38 (section 325), recalling the endurance of affliction by the early Christians.
[^660]: Divine Liturgy Gospel lection for the Third Saturday of the Great Fast: Mark 2:14–17 (section 8), the calling of Levi and Christ eating with tax collectors and sinners.
[^661]: Little Vespers stichera at "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 6 to the automelon "А҆́гг҃льскїѧ" (The angelic hosts), celebrating the angelic veneration of the Cross.
[^662]: Little Vespers Aposticha stichera in Tone 2 to the automelon "До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ" (O house of Ephratha).
[^663]: Diplomatic translation of Habsburg Imperial titulature printed in the 1906 Lviv Stauropegion edition: "побѣ́ды Бл҃говѣ́рномꙋ I҆мпера́торꙋ на́шемꙋ" (granting victories to our right-believing Emperor [Franz Joseph I]).
[^664]: Great Vespers stichera at "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 5 to the automelon "Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ" (Rejoice, O container of ascetics).
[^665]: Great Vespers Doxasticon at "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 3, a classical Byzantine hymn attributed to Emperor Leo the Wise or St. John Damascene, meditating on Christ using the reed of the Cross dipped in His blood to sign the imperial pardon of mankind.
[^666]: Great Vespers Litiya Doxasticon in Tone 5, meditating upon the cosmic convulsion of creation at the Crucifixion of Christ.
[^667]: Great Vespers Aposticha Doxasticon in Tone 4, a supplicatory hymn invoking divine victory through the weapon of the Cross.
[^668]: Diplomatic translation of Habsburg Imperial titulature in the Aposticha Doxasticon: "вѣ́рномꙋ I҆мпера́торꙋ на́шемꙋ" (our faithful Emperor).
[^669]: Matins Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos in Tone 8 to the automelon "Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ" (Having received the command mystically), contrasting the tree of the Fall with the Tree of the Cross which fills the temple with God's glory (cf. Isa. 6:1).
[^670]: Matins Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of the Precious Cross in Tone 1 by St. Theodore the Studite; borrowing the festive Paschal Heirmos of St. John Damascene "Воскресе́нїя де́нь" (It is the day of Resurrection, let us be radiant, O ye peoples).
[^671]: The 1906 Lviv text witnesses a slight typographical dittography: "ꙗ҆́ко ꙗ҆́кѡ ѡ҆дꙋшевленнꙋ̀" (as as unto one that has a soul).
[^672]: Matins Katavasia of Ode 1 in Tone 1: "Бж҃е́ственнѣйшїй проѡбразѝ дре́вле Мѡѷсе́й" (Moses of old most divinely prefigured [it]), celebrating Moses dividing the Red Sea with his staff as a type of the Cross.
[^673]: Ode 3 Heirmos incipit of the Canon of the Cross in Tone 1 by St. Theodore the Studite: "Прїиди́те, пи́во пїе́мъ но́вое" (Come, let us drink a new drink), borrowing the festive Paschal Heirmos of St. John Damascene.
[^674]: Allusion to Moses extending his hands in the shape of a cross on the hill during the battle with Amalek (Exod. 17:11-12), a classical patristic type of the triumph of the Cross.
[^675]: Matins Katavasia of Ode 3 in Tone 1: "Оу҆твердѝ Влдко Хрⷭ҇тѐ, крⷭ҇то́мъ на ка́мени мѧ̀ вѣ́ры" (Establish me, O Master Christ, upon the rock of the faith by the Cross), adapting the heirmos of the Canon of the Elevation of the Cross by St. Cosmas of Maiuma.
[^676]: Matins Sessional Hymn after Ode 3 in Tone 6: "Крⷭ҇тъ тво́й, Гдⷭ҇и, ѡ҆ст҃и́сѧ" (Thy Cross, O Lord, is sanctified), meditating upon the sanctifying power of the Cross and invoking Christ's mercy.
[^677]: Psalm 131:7 LXX (Ps. 132:7 MT): "Се́ бо покланѧ́емсѧ на мѣ́сто, и҆де́же стоѧ́стѣ но́зѣ твоѝ Гдⷭ҇и" (Lo, we bow down in worship at the place where Thy feet stood, O Lord).
[^678]: Ode 4 Heirmos incipit in Tone 1: "На бж҃е́ственнѣй стра́жи" (Upon the divine watch), borrowing the Paschal Heirmos of St. John Damascene based upon the prophecy of Habakkuk (Hab. 3:1-3 LXX).
[^679]: Matins Katavasia of Ode 4 in Tone 1: "На крⷭ҇тѣ́ тѧ Си́льне, свѣти́ло вели́кое ви́дѣвъ" (Seeing Thee upon the Cross, O Mighty One, the great luminary was seized with trembling), adapting St. Cosmas's heirmos on the cosmic darkening of the sun at the Crucifixion (Hab. 3:2-3 LXX).
[^680]: Ode 5 Heirmos incipit in Tone 1: "Оу҆́тренюемъ ᲂу҆́трꙋ глꙋбокꙋ̀" (Let us rise early in the deep dawn), borrowing the Paschal Heirmos of St. John Damascene.
[^681]: Matins Katavasia of Ode 5 in Tone 1: "Оу҆́тренююще тѧ̀ воспѣва́емъ Сп҃се ще́дре" (Rising early we sing unto Thee, O compassionate Savior), based upon Isaiah 26:9.
[^682]: Ode 6 Heirmos incipit in Tone 1: "Снизше́лъ є҆сѝ въ преиспѡ́днѧѧ землѝ" (Thou didst descend into the deepest parts of the earth), borrowing the Paschal Heirmos of St. John Damascene.
[^683]: Typological citation of Jeremiah 11:19 LXX: "Дре́во въ тво́й хлѣ́бъ вложе́ное" (wood cast into His bread), interpreted by the Holy Fathers (such as Justin Martyr, Tertullian, and John Chrysostom) as prefiguring the Crucifixion of Christ's body (the Bread of Life) upon the wood of the Cross.
[^684]: Matins Katavasia of Ode 6 in Tone 1: "Ѻ҆́бразъ бж҃е́ственнагѡ крⷭ҇та̀ І҆ѡ́на" (Jonah prefigured the image of the divine Cross), from St. Cosmas's Canon of the Elevation, seeing Jonah's outstretched arms in the sea-monster as a sign of the Cross.
[^685]: Sunday of the Holy Cross Kontakion in Tone 7 Idiomelon: "Не ктомꙋ̀ пла́менное ѻ҆рꙋ́жїе храни́тъ вра́тъ Е҆де́мскихъ" (No longer does the flaming sword guard the gate of Eden), celebrating the reversal of the Fall and the opening of Paradise.
[^686]: Sunday of the Holy Cross Ikos in Tone 7: dramatic dialogue portraying the torment and defeat of Hades upon Golgotha, constrained by the Cross to surrender Adam and his race.
[^687]: Historical and theological Synaxarion of the Third Sunday of the Great Fast (Sunday of the Holy Cross) by Nicephorus Callistus Xanthopoulos, explaining why the precious Cross is set forth for veneration at the midpoint of the Forty Days to comfort and fortify the fasting faithful.
[^688]: Typological exposition in the Synaxarion: Moses casting the wood into the bitter waters of Marah (Exod. 15:23-25) to make them sweet, prefiguring the Holy Cross sweetening the labor and austerity of the Holy Fast.
[^689]: Ode 7 Heirmos incipit in Tone 1: "Ѻ҆́троки ѿ пе́щи и҆зба́вивый" (He Who delivered the Children from the furnace), borrowing the Paschal Heirmos of St. John Damascene.
[^690]: Matins Katavasia of Ode 7 in Tone 1: "И҆з̾ пла́мене ѻ҆́троки и҆зба́влей" (He Who delivered the Children from the flame), adapting St. Cosmas's heirmos celebrating Christ's voluntary Crucifixion.
[^691]: Ode 8 Heirmos incipit in Tone 1: "Се́й нарече́нный и҆ ст҃ы́й де́нь" (This chosen and holy day), borrowing the Paschal Heirmos of St. John Damascene.
[^692]: Typological reference to the prophet Elisha throwing the stick of wood into the Jordan to make the submerged iron axe head float to the surface (4 Kings / 2 Kings 6:5-7 LXX), prefiguring the Cross raising fallen human nature from the depths of sin.
[^693]: Patriarchal typology: Jacob worshiping leaning upon the top of his staff / bowing down towards the head of Joseph's bed (Gen. 47:31 LXX; Heb. 11:21), venerated by the Fathers as a prophetic type of the royal scepter of the Cross.
[^694]: Matins Katavasia of Ode 8 in Tone 1: "Рꙋ́цѣ въ ро́въ ве́рженый львѡ́въ" (Having of old stretched out his hands cruciform in the den of lions), St. Cosmas's heirmos on the deliverance of Daniel (Dan. 6).
[^695]: Ode 9 Heirmos incipit in Tone 1: "Свѣти́сѧ, свѣти́сѧ, но́вый І҆ерꙋсали́ме" (Shine, shine, O New Jerusalem), borrowing the Paschal Heirmos of St. John Damascene based upon Isaiah 60:1.
[^696]: Matins Katavasia of Ode 9 in Tone 1: "Ѽ Мт҃и Дв҃о, и҆ Бцⷣе нело́жнаѧ" (O Mother Virgin and true Theotokos), concluding the festive katavasiai of St. Cosmas of Maiuma.
[^697]: Matins Exapostilarion of the Cross in Tone 2 to the automelon "Со ᲂу҆чени́ки" (With the disciples), meditating upon the veneration of the Cross and the approach of Holy Pascha.
[^698]: Stichera at the Praises of the Precious Cross in Tone 4, to the automelon "Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ" (As one valiant among martyrs).
[^699]: Matins Praises Doxasticon in Tone 8 Idiomelon: "Высокомꙋ́драгѡ ра́зꙋма ѕлы́хъ Фарїсе́євъ" (The Lord of all taught by a parable to flee the haughty mind of the wicked Pharisees), integrating the penitential gospel parable of the Publican and the Pharisee (Luke 18:9-14) with Christ's voluntary self-emptying and Crucifixion.
[^700]: Rubrics detailing the solemn bringing out and elevation of the Precious Cross during the singing of the Great Doxology, borne upon the diskos upon the Priest's head from the north door to the center of the nave.
[^701]: The solemn elevation of the Precious Cross at the Royal Doors with the intonation "Wisdom! Stand upright!" and the thrice-repeated troparion of the Cross in Tone 1: "Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance."
[^702]: The veneration hymn in Tone 6: "Крⷭ҇тꙋ̀ твоемꙋ̀ покланѧ́емсѧ Влⷣко, и҆ ст҃о́е Воскрⷭ҇нїе твоѐ сла́вимъ" (Before Thy Cross we bow down in worship, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify), sung thrice with profound prostrations.
[^703]: Solemn Idiomelon of the Veneration in Tone 2 by the Byzantine Emperor Leo VI the Wise (886–912): "Прїиди́те вѣ́рнїи, животворѧ́щемꙋ дре́вꙋ поклони́мсѧ" (Come, ye faithful, let us bow down in worship before the life-creating Tree), invoking victory for Christian rulers over the Ismaelites.
[^704]: Veneration Idiomelon in Tone 8: "Дне́сь Влⷣка тва́ри, и҆ Гдⷭ҇ь сла́вы, на крⷭ҇тѣ̀ пригвожда́етсѧ" (Today the Master of creation and Lord of glory is nailed upon the Cross), a solemn contemplation of the paradox of the Creator suffering for His creatures.
[^705]: Veneration Doxasticon in Tone 8: "Дне́сь Неприкоснове́нный сꙋщество́мъ, прикоснове́нъ мнѣ̀ быва́етъ" (Today He Who is untouchable in essence becomes touchable unto me), setting forth the tender and sorrowful maternal lament of the Mother of God standing before the Cross.
[^706]: Rubrics for the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great on the Third Sunday: the Beatitudes with Ode 6 of the Canon of the Cross, and "Before Thy Cross" replacing the Trisagion.
[^707]: Epistle reading for the Sunday of the Holy Cross: Hebrews 4:14–5:6 (pericope 311), presenting Christ as the Great High Priest Who passed through the heavens and is able to sympathize with our infirmities.
[^708]: Gospel reading for the Sunday of the Holy Cross: Mark 8:34–9:1 (pericope 37), proclaiming the Lord's precept: "Whosoever will come after Me, let him deny himself, and take up his cross, and follow Me."
[^709]: Great Vespers on the Third Sunday of the Great Fast: Lord, I have cried Prosomoia of Lord Joseph the Hymnographer in Tone 8, to the automelon "Гдⷭ҇и, а҆́ще и҆ на сꙋди́щи" (O Lord, though Thou didst stand before the tribunal).
[^710]: Vespers Sticheron of the Cross by Lord Theodore the Studite in Tone 3, to the automelon "Ве́лїе чꙋ́до" (A great wonder), extolling the demon-destroying power of the Cross.
[^711]: Great Prokimenon for Sunday evenings in the Fast in Tone 8: "Да́лъ є҆сѝ достоѧ́нїе боѧ́щымсѧ тебѐ Гдⷭ҇и" (Thou hast given an inheritance unto them that fear Thee, O Lord), taken from Psalm 60:5–6 LXX (Ps. 61:4–5 MT).
[^712]: Vespers Aposticha Idiomelon of the day in Tone 8, echoing the humble repentance of the Publican (Luke 18:13): "Воззрѣ́ти ѻ҆чи́ма на нб҃о не смѣ́ю ѻ҆каѧ́нный а҆́зъ" (I dare not gaze with mine eyes into heaven, wretch that I am).
